## 1. Pruning Autonomous Driving VLAs: What to Preserve for Reasoning and Action

- **作者**：Seongchan Kim, Soowon Son, Sangoh Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：45 pages. Project page: https://cvlab-kaist.github.io/MoOP

Reasoning vision-language-action (VLA) models for autonomous driving use chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning to guide action generation, but incur substantial memory and computational costs. We study structured pruning for these models, focusing on preserving both scene understanding and trajectory prediction. Our analysis of Taylor importance in Alpamayo-1.5-10B shows that reasoning and trajectory objectives assign different importance to the same vision-language model (VLM) structures. In the action expert, MLP channels exhibit more stable importance rankings across denoising steps than query heads, and a small fraction of channels accounts for most of the measured importance. Based on these findings, we propose MoOP, which combines importance ranks from both objectives for VLM pruning and aggregates stepwise importance to select a fixed subset of expert MLP channels. At 24.0% total parameter reduction, VLM-only pruning with MoOP performs comparably to or slightly better than the strongest baselines in driving, while retaining 94.0% of the dense model's LingoQA score, far above all other methods. At a higher reduction of 30.0%, MoOP degrades much less than every other method, as it is the only one whose reasoning does not collapse. Removing 93.75% of expert MLP channels increases total parameter reduction to 39.4%, with nearly unchanged open-loop errors relative to VLM-only pruning and competitive closed-loop performance. The resulting model achieves a 1.37x end-to-end inference speedup and 36.6% lower peak memory than the dense model, without post-pruning fine-tuning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31240) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31240)

## 2. Lyris: Layout-Guided Long Video Generation with Persistent 3D Memory

- **作者**：Chen Wang, Jiahui Huang, Riccardo de Lutio et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: http://research.nvidia.com/labs/sil/projects/lyris

Exploring a scene from as little as a single photo requires generating a long, camera-controlled walkthrough that extends far beyond what was captured, yet remains consistent upon return. Memory-based video generation methods preserve what they have already seen, but have no anchor beyond it, where the camera drifts from its path and a text prompt can name objects but not place them. Layout-conditioned methods provide such an anchor and precise control over what appears where, but rely on layouts that are typically authored by hand. Both limitations arise because, beyond what was captured, nothing anchors the video model unless a human authors one. To overcome both, we propose Lyris, a long video generation framework that never leaves the video model without an anchor: a persistent point-cloud memory where the scene has been observed or generated, and a 3D layout that a vision-language model authors where it has not. Our key insight is that the interface between the two models can be surprisingly coarse. A layout of one labeled 3D box per object is simple enough for a vision-language model to author (and for a user to edit), yet expressive enough for a video model to follow once rendered along the camera path. We demonstrate walkthroughs of over three minutes and 50 meters from a single photo, passing through doorways into rooms it never shows. When evaluated on minute-long round trips across four datasets, Lyris outperforms state-of-the-art methods by a wide margin, reducing camera error by 3.8x and improving consistency upon return by 22% on average.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31239) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31239)

## 3. Modern Self-Supervised Vision Transformers Are Pareidolic

- **作者**：Zihang Lin, Huaiyuan Qin, Liangli Zhen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In human perception, pareidolia is the tendency to perceive a meaningful pattern, such as a face, in a random or ambiguous stimulus.We find a similar phenomenon in modern self-supervised Vision Transformers (ViTs): on white noise, which contains neither objects nor spatial structure, a few directions of their features still form spatially coherent patterns across the patches, imposed by the features themselves.We call this behavior pareidolia, and the span of these directions the pareidolic subspace.On natural images, it holds most of the feature variance in DINOv2 and DINOv3 but little in DINO.This excess explains why DINOv2 and DINOv3, despite stronger dense features, fall far behind DINO in object emergence, the hallmark ability of self-supervised ViTs to identify the patches of an object on their own.Our experiments further show that scaling down their pareidolic subspaces steadily restores their ability to discover objects, so their object emergence is hindered rather than lost.These findings naturally lead to a training-free calibration: Pareidolia Subspace Calibration(PSC) measures the pareidolic subspace only on a noise input and then calibrates the features of natural inputs by scaling its directions down to the variance level of the remaining directions.Across diverse object emergence tasks, PSC improves DINOv2 and DINOv3 by up to 31.7 CorLoc on object discovery, 30.2 maxF on saliency detection and 59.9~$\mathcal{J}$ on video object segmentation.To identify the cause of pareidolia, we trace it block by block and find that it forms in the middle blocks of every self-supervised model we examine but stays strong to the last block only in models trained with a patch-level loss; controlled training confirms that an image-level loss alone lets it fade.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31238) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31238)

## 4. Let Vision-Language Models Generate Images through Their Representations

- **作者**：JB Kim, Byungwoo Jeon, Kyungmin Lee et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Preprint. Project page: https://thejb.ai/integral

Recent unified multimodal models aim to combine visual understanding and generation within a single model, yet effectively integrating the two capabilities remains challenging. Leveraging pretrained vision-language models offers a way to build on already learned multimodal knowledge, but making this knowledge effectively accessible to visual generation remains nontrivial. We find that a central challenge lies in the heterogeneous visual representations used for understanding and generation. To address this challenge, we introduce Integral, which converts a pretrained vision-language model into a unified multimodal model by constructing a shared visual representation space across the two capabilities. Integral implements this design through two complementary components. Abstraction Fusion constructs shared visual tokens by aggregating hierarchical representations from the frozen vision encoder, while Block-wise MoT introduces a lightweight generative stream that interacts with the vision-language model only at block boundaries. Our approach effectively preserves the pretrained understanding capability while extending it to strong visual generation. Controlled comparisons further demonstrate that sharing visual representations between understanding and generation substantially improves generative learning compared with using a separate visual representation space, despite comparable reconstruction fidelity. These results suggest that representations learned by pretrained vision-language models already provide an effective foundation for visual generation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31237) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31237)

## 5. TowerBridge: Bridging the Dual-Encoder Modality Gap via Weight-Space Programming

- **作者**：Hani Alomari, Md Atabuzzaman, Chris Thomas
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：42 pages (13 main + 29 supplementary), 11 figures, 35 tables

CLIP-style dual encoders map images and text through two separate towers into a shared embedding space, where the two modalities occupy distinct regions separated by a modality gap. Retrieval fails when wrong matches, or hard negatives, outrank the correct one, and the gap is the usual suspect. Yet removing it post hoc degrades retrieval on every CLIP checkpoint we test. We introduce a paired statistic that identifies directions where the two towers agree more on hard negatives than on matched pairs. Mildly attenuating these directions improves retrieval while largely preserving the gap. We arrive at this correction through weight-space programming: guided by the statistic, an LLM agent inspects both towers and writes closed-form weight edits without backpropagation. Free to edit any component, the agent improves COCO test retrieval on all 13 CLIP checkpoints and edits an output projection in 12 of the 13. We distill and refine its recurring correction into TowerBridge, a closed-form recipe motivated by a first-order analysis of the hard-negative margin. Computed in seconds from 25,000 labelled pairs, it requires no model-specific tuning and leaves the architecture and inference cost unchanged. TowerBridge improves COCO test retrieval by 16.8 RSUM and Flickr30k test retrieval by 6.6 on average across all 21 evaluated CLIP checkpoints, including 8 unseen by the agent, while limiting any drop in ImageNet zero-shot accuracy to 0.5 points. It also reduces hubness on all 21 checkpoints and improves retrieval on 4 of 5 dual encoders from other families.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31233) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31233)

## 6. Self-Supervised Dataset Distillation via Mutual Information Maximization

- **作者**：Mohammad Hossein Moslemi, Mahdi Beitollahi, Zhimin Mei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Dataset distillation (DD) aims to compress a large dataset into a small synthetic one that remains effective for training neural networks. Despite its success in supervised learning, DD remains underexplored in self-supervised learning (SSL), where distilled data must preserve the information needed to learn representations that remain consistent across augmentations. To bridge this gap, we introduce Mutual Information Prototype Alignment (MIPA), an efficient SSL dataset distillation approach that optimizes synthetic images to preserve augmentation-invariant information. Specifically, MIPA treats synthetic images as learnable prototypes and uses a frozen encoder to assign real samples to them based on feature similarity. A synthetic dataset that preserves such augmentation invariance should yield stable prototype assignments across multiple augmented views of the same real image. MIPA encourages this by maximizing mutual information between cross-view assignments while avoiding trivial solutions in which all real samples are assigned to only a few synthetic prototypes. Evaluations on image classification benchmarks show that synthetic data generated by MIPA yield higher-quality representations than existing distillation approaches while reducing time and storage demands during distillation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31225) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31225)

## 7. CTLink: A Text-Prompted Segmentation Model for Linking Radiology Report Findings to 3D Chest CT Scans

- **作者**：Mohammed Baharoon, Sung Eun Kim, Mahmoud Hussain Alabbad et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：23 pages, 6 figures, 8 tables

Radiology reports describe findings in text only, without visually marking them on the scan, so readers cannot directly see which abnormality each reported finding refers to. We introduce CTLink, a text-prompted 3D segmentation model that segments findings described in a chest CT report. CTLink was trained on 4123 noncontrast chest CT scans with 9547 segmented report findings, including ReXGroundingCTv2, a newly annotated dataset of 1134 scans and 1895 findings with every instance of each finding segmented. On the 200 ReXGroundingCT validation scans, CTLink achieved a hit rate of 0.816, an instance F1 score of 0.456, and a mean Dice score of 0.400, compared with 0.533, 0.175, and 0.234 for VoxTell, a publicly available text-prompted segmentation model (P < .001 for all). On the test set of the MICCAI 2026 ReXGrounding challenge, an ensemble of seven CTLink models achieved the highest hit rate (0.782) and instance F1 score (0.386) of all 48 participating teams. On 50 test scans segmented twice by independent radiologists, CTLink matched or exceeded inter-rater agreement. Diffuse non-focal findings remained the main limitation, partly because their extent is outlined inconsistently even between radiologists. The CTLink model weights are available at https://huggingface.co/rajpurkarlab/CTLink, and the dataset at https://huggingface.co/datasets/rajpurkarlab/ReXGroundingCT.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31222) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31222)

## 8. LeJEPA Features Are Not Isotropic, and Should Not Be

- **作者**：Richeek Das, Pratik Chaudhari
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

The SIGReg term in LeJEPA regularizes features to have an isotropic Gaussian distribution. Our first result is to show that this regularizer, in practice, cannot achieve its goal. We design a modified regularizer to enforce the objective better. This leads to slightly improved performance. For example, on ImageNet-100, our modified regularizer reaches the final accuracy of SIGReg in 1.3$\times$ fewer epochs. Our second result argues that self-supervised encoders should not have isotropic Gaussian features. We show that the alignment term in LeJEPA conflicts with SIGReg, because it couples the magnitude of the features with their directions. In contrast, isotropic Gaussian features would entail independent magnitude and directions. Features obtained with LeJEPA are, in fact, not isotropic. So LeJEPA has two issues. Its regularizer is weak, and the isotropic Gaussian target is too strict. Our third result is about reformulating the isotropic Gaussian regularizer to enforce only the marginal distributions of the magnitude and direction, without enforcing independence. This leads to a simple regularization term, PolarReg, that can be optimized reliably across many different datasets, batch sizes and embedding dimensions without any hyper-parameter changes. On ImageNet-1k, our technique matches the accuracy of LeJEPA and its variants in 4.7$\times$ fewer epochs. It improves the final accuracy by 1.8 points.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31216) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31216)

## 9. WorldLatent: A Unified 4D Latent Space for World Perception and Generation

- **作者**：Jingxi Chen, Yujia Chen, Cornelia Fermuller et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project Website: http://WorldLatent.github.io

Generative Pre-Training (GPT) represents a profound milestone in natural language processing, serving as the foundation for most of today's state-of-the-art large language models. By casting diverse understanding and generation problems into a shared token representation, GPT enables a single architecture and unified optimization loop to master heterogeneous tasks across massive corpora, giving rise to powerful foundation models for natural language. In contrast, 4D spatiotemporal vision lacks a common representational protocol: perception (such as depth and camera estimation) and generation are either handled by separate one-way pipelines or bolted together from unaligned single-modality spaces. In this work, we introduce WorldLatent, a framework that formulates a unified 4D latent space as a common protocol for both world perception and generation. Rather than relying on fragmented, task-specific models or stacked single-modality representations, our framework proposes a multimodal VAE architecture that compresses and fuses complex 4D visual appearance, geometry, and dynamics into a single structured latent representation. Within this formulation, perception and generation operate as complementary denoising processes over the same latent space, enabling one generative model to learn both capabilities simultaneously within a single training loop. Experiments across representative 4D benchmarks verify that our fused latent protocol achieves specialist-level zero-shot 4D perception, coherent 4D generation, and controllable editing from a single model. By demonstrating that diverse 4D tasks can be seamlessly unified under a shared representational protocol, our work provides a scalable foundation for general-purpose 4D spatial intelligence.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31209) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31209)

## 10. ToolSmith: An Automatic Tool-Generation Framework for Multimodal Search Agents

- **作者**：Rui Sun, Junhua Huang, Luca Zancato et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.CV

Recent multimodal search agents use reasoning, vision, and live web search across many turns to answer questions beyond parametric knowledge by orchestrating a small library of hand-crafted search and visual tools. However, the library is laborious to create and maintain, and frozen when new query types, evidence formats, and failure modes are encountered in the wild. Human practitioners, by contrast, cope with insufficient tools by fashioning new ones from familiar parts and reusing them across tasks. Inspired by this, we propose ToolSmith, an automatic tool-generation framework that grows and reuses modules on top of a lean base tool library. We evaluate ToolSmith on three in-domain multimodal search benchmarks and ten out-of-domain benchmarks under a VLM-as-a-Judge protocol. ToolSmith consistently improves over the base library across deployed agents of varying scales, with larger backbones reaping greater gains, demonstrating the scalability of the approach. Our code and data are available at https://threesr.github.io/toolsmith/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31198) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31198)

## 11. On-Policy Codec Distillation for Efficient All-in-One Video Generation

- **作者**：Boyang Wang, Kevin Blackburn-Matzen, Oindrila Saha et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://kiteretsu77.github.io/CodecOPSD_website/

Modern visual generative models operate on tokens, and every visual asset they receive, whether an identity, a scribble, a depth map, or a motion video, is tokenized and appended to the input token sequence. Consequently, conditioning quickly dominates the computation cost. Yet most of these tokens carry redundant spatiotemporal information. Our key insight is that identifying what is genuinely new is exactly the problem that classical video codecs have long solved. Thus, we first introduce a codec-based token pruning method that discards redundant tokens. Then, we propose an on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) strategy to train a DiT adapter capable of learning the codec pruning pattern well without quality degradation. Together, they form On-Policy Codec Distillation (CodecOPSD). We scale CodecOPSD to 16 condition modalities on our own video diffusion model, where it prunes 66% of the condition tokens on average with almost no quality drop. Therefore, denoising becomes 2x faster, and the cache memory shrinks by up to 73% in KV cache mode. CodecOPSD also extends to other state-of-the-art pretrained image and video generative models, where its benefit grows further with more conditions, longer frames, and higher resolution.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31184) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31184)

## 12. SPROUT: Predicting Objective-Grounded Prompt Utility for Efficient Text-to-Image Preference Tuning

- **作者**：Kangping Hu, Akshay Mehra, Stephen Mussmann
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Preference tuning of text-to-image diffusion models spends most of its compute generating candidate images, yet training prompts are typically sampled uniformly at random, wasting compute generating images that provide too little signal for model alignment. To best utilize the available compute, we study prompt selection, where at each iteration we identify the most useful prompts for preference tuning. We separate prompt selection into a utility score, an estimator of that utility score, and batch selection. Within this framework, we propose Selection by PRedicting Objective-grounded UTility (SPROUT). The utility score is predicted on every prompt in the pool, including unseen prompts, by a text-only model, refit after every iteration on all utilities observed so far. A randomized acceptance rule then turns these predictions into a batch, so no images are generated beyond those used for training. For direct group preference optimization (DGPO), the objective-grounded utility is the norm of the gradient that the objective produces from a prompt's group of images. Fine-tuning Stable Diffusion 1.5 with DGPO on GenEval, SPROUT matches the final score of uniform sampling in about 44% of the iterations and improves the area under the learning curve with respect to both uniform sampling and CGPO, a recent variance-based prompt sampler. The efficiency gains hold under four held-out reward models never used in training, and the ranking of methods carries over to DPG-Bench and T2I-CompBench++. Our code is available at https://github.com/ADDAPT-ML/SPROUT.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31163) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31163)

## 13. Analyzing and Improving FlowMap Distillation of Autoregressive Video Models

- **作者**：Yong-Hyun Park, Guande He, Joon Hyeok Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Preprint, Project Page: https://enkeejunior1.github.io/ar-video-flowmap/

FlowMap distillation offers a natural route to few-step generative modeling by directly learning a teacher's denoising trajectories. Yet for autoregressive (AR) video generation, existing few-step models still rely on distribution matching distillation (DMD) to remain competitive, sacrificing sample diversity and requiring a large bidirectional teacher. In this work, we analyze what limits standalone AR FlowMap distillation and introduce two complementary guidance mechanisms to close this gap: (1) anti-drifting guidance, which helps the AR teacher correct drifted student rollouts by training an auxiliary model to mimic student drift and using it as a negative guidance branch; and (2) motion-aware guidance, which makes motion easier for a FlowMap student to learn by locating motion patches and selectively applying skip-layer guidance. Empirically, our approach applies to $π$-Flow, MeanFlow, and consistency distillation with 1.3B AR teachers, improving generation quality and reducing drift over 30 s rollouts. Notably, our 4-step $π$-Flow students match DMD students distilled from 14B teachers on VBench while preserving the sample diversity of their AR teachers, demonstrating that pure FlowMap distillation is effective for few-step autoregressive video generation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31164) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31164)

## 14. Tokens as Locality-Preserving Anchors: Scalable Novel View Synthesis with Target-Adaptive Token Routing

- **作者**：Sagnik Ghosh, Aswin C. Sankaranarayanan
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：25 pages

Transformer-based view synthesis models render each target viewpoint from the source images, without an explicit 3D representation, which lets them reproduce fine detail and view-dependent effects. However, their decoder attends to every source token for every frame, so rendering slows with each source image. Rasterization avoids this by culling primitives that the viewpoint does not see, but source tokens cannot be culled this way, as they have no explicit position or extent. Our key insight is that source tokens are nonetheless spatial anchors: each encodes a local region of the scene, so the decoder's attention for any viewpoint concentrates on a few of them. A lightweight router predicts these tokens from the source tokens and the viewpoint, and the decoder attends only to a fixed budget of top-ranked tokens, so its cost no longer depends on the number of source tokens. We supervise the router with the decoder's attention, using non-maximum suppression to demote redundant tokens. With 24 source images and two views' worth of tokens, our model renders 6.4 times faster than LagerNVS, at a PSNR only 0.68 dB lower. One ranking serves every budget, so the budget can change at render time.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31156) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31156)

## 15. Head Anything: Correspondence-Guided Joint Generation of 3D Heads from Few Images

- **作者**：Umut Kocasari, Bartłomiej Baranowski, Richard Shaw et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project website: https://kocasariumut.github.io/HeadAnything/ , Video: https://youtu.be/EQm6y_Z0Frk

Large image-to-3D generators recover detailed heads from a single photograph but treat every image as a new object. For several images of one person, they hallucinate unseen regions independently without using information from the other images. We present Head Anything, which generates detailed head meshes from a few images with changing expressions and viewpoints. Instead of training a multi-image prior, we turn a pretrained single-image generator with a native 3D latent space into a joint generator that denoises all images together. Since global attention between images is hard to learn from scarce 3D head data, lightweight correspondence-attention adapters let every 3D token attend only to its corresponding points in the other images. We obtain correspondences by fitting a neural parametric head model to the generator's own single-image outputs, requiring no ground truth at test time. Training on both ground-truth and fitted correspondences makes the adapters robust to fitting errors. Each mesh stays pixel-aligned with its own image and borrows the unseen regions from all others. On head scans, Head Anything lowers the head Chamfer distance of the best image-to-3D generator by 32% and its cross-frame inconsistency by 47%, and its meshes improve face tracking. Code and models will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31143) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31143)

## 16. HoloSplat: Real-Time Token Streaming for Feed-Forward Human Reconstruction

- **作者**：Chaofan Li, Qihan Ding, Shihan Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR, cs.MM
- **备注**：43 pages including supplementary material, 20 figures. Project page: https://heaticy.github.io/HoloSplat/

Live free-viewpoint video streaming of humans enables immersive telepresence and interactive XR applications. However, this requires reconstructing each frame in real time, transmitting the data within strict bandwidth budgets, and decoding it on lightweight client GPUs. Existing approaches, whether relying on per-frame optimization or feed-forward inference, generate massive 3D Gaussian payloads that exceed practical streaming limits, while aggressive compression severely degrades rendering quality. In this work, we present HoloSplat, a real-time streaming framework for human-centric volumetric video. HoloSplat splits a feed-forward reconstructor before its Gaussian head, introducing a multi-rate streaming codec driven by server-side token compression and client-side token restoration. On the server side, we apply opacity-guided token pruning, PCA-based dimensionality reduction, and lossless geometry coding for token anchors. On the client side, a feature reconstruction hypernetwork is fused into the Gaussian head to recover compression-induced quality loss. Furthermore, we introduce Neural Session Calibration (NSC), which rapidly fine-tunes the Gaussian head online using incoming camera views during a warm-up phase. This resolves quality degradation from novel camera parameters and varying capture conditions with zero per-frame overhead. In our evaluations, HoloSplat averages 32.2 FPS on four RTX 5090 GPUs at 50 Mbit/s. It decodes each frame into 3D Gaussians in 22.1 ms on an RTX 3050 Ti laptop GPU, transmitting 22.8$\times$ less data than uncompressed float32 Gaussians, while calibrated decoding improves PSNR by 1.050 dB.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31140) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31140)

## 17. SRCWDNet: Semantic-Relational Cross-Window Dual-Graph Network for Multi-Label Remote Sensing Scene Classification

- **作者**：Fareeha Amir
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：14 pages 11 figures

Multi-label remote sensing scene classification remains a challenging task due to complex spatial-spectral patterns, extreme class imbalance, and inter-label dependencies that are often insufficiently modeled. To address these challenges, we introduce a Semantic-Relational Cross-Window Dual-Graph Network (SRCWDNet), which integrates three key modules: (1) a Cross-Window Attention module that captures local and global visual context through learnable global tokens; (2) a Dual Label-Graph module that adaptively combines statistical co-occurrence graphs and learned semantic-embedding graphs to facilitate label-relational reasoning; and (3) a Neural Classifier Chain that sequentially predicts labels to exploit inter-label dependencies. Evaluated using five-fold cross-validation and trained with an Asymmetric-Graph-Consistency loss, SRCWDNet achieves accuracies of 98.93% and 96.53%, with corresponding F1-scores of 0.9365 and 0.8723 on the MLRSNet and China-MAS50k datasets, respectively, outperforming CNN, transformer, and lightweight baselines. These findings demonstrate that jointly modeling visual attention and adaptive label correlations can substantially improve multi-label remote sensing scene classification, particularly under long-tailed class distributions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31135) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31135)

## 18. CrownFusion: 3D Dental Crown Generation Using Geometry Images and Latent Diffusion

- **作者**：Johan Ziruo Ye, Xingguang Yan, Søren Hauberg et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：Accepted at MICCAI 2026. 11 pages, 6 figures. Equal contribution: J. Z. Ye and X. Yan. Project page: https://crownfusion.github.io/

We introduce CrownFusion, a latent diffusion model to generate 3D dental crown designs from geometry images, a 2D-grid encoding of surface coordinates. Departing from traditional approaches that use point clouds, our "CrownImage" representation not only unlocks the full power of image-based generative architectures, but also preserves fine geometric detail by operating at far higher spatial resolutions than point clouds to alleviate smoothing artifacts in the 3D generation. Conditioned on jointly learned embeddings of the surrounding dentition, our probabilistic diffusion model produces anatomically plausible crowns with morphological variations tailored to individual patient cases. To accommodate multiple valid designs, we propose an occlusal fit metric based on proximity to the antagonist teeth. We evaluate our method through quantitative experiments and qualitative studies involving expert testimonials. We also release the FDI 16 Crown Dataset, comprising 11,513 annotated intra-oral scans, to support future research in dental CAD.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31128) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31128)

## 19. WeaveVLA: 3D Relational Grounding for Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Zien Wang, Yuhao Pan, Yunfeng Fan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages, 7 figures

Vision-language-action (VLA) models leverage pretrained vision-language representations for instruction-conditioned robot control, yet their understanding of task-relevant 3D relations and how these relations evolve during manipulation remains largely implicit. To address this challenge, we introduce \textbf{WeaveVLA}, a framework that enhances VLA action generation by explicitly modeling task-relevant relative 3D relations and maintaining relational context across observations. WeaveVLA employs a task-conditioned sparse graph neural network to encode relative 3D relations among objects, the gripper, and nearby references. Pair-specific recurrent edge states integrate geometric evidence across observations to maintain evolving spatial context. A learned-query readout summarizes the updated graph representations into compact relational tokens, which condition the action expert alongside the original vision-language context. An auxiliary geometry reconstruction objective further encourages spatial structure preservation. We instantiate WeaveVLA on StarVLA-GR00T and $π_{0.5}$, achieving over 98\% average success on LIBERO with both backbones. On LIBERO-Plus, WeaveVLA improves success from 74.9\% to 82.0\% and from 85.7\% to 87.8\%, respectively, with additional improvements on CALVIN D$\rightarrow$D. On three real-world tasks, the StarVLA-GR00T-based variant improves average success from 55.6\% to 70.0\% under standard conditions and from 33.3\% to 53.3\% under OOD perturbations, surpassing the $π_{0.5}$ baseline in both settings.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31126) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31126)

## 20. RUIBench: What Makes a Good Robot-Use Interface?

- **作者**：Yanzhe Chen, Zhijun Cao, Zechen Bai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.MM
- **备注**：Project website: https://showlab.github.io/RUIBench

Foundation models can reason about complex physical tasks, yet translating this intelligence into reliable robot execution remains challenging. We argue that a critical, often overlooked factor is the robot-use interface (RUI), which determines how agents express and execute physical actions. To systematically study its impact, we introduce RUIBench, comprising 30 manipulation tasks across five suites spanning world knowledge, in-context learning, discovery, dynamic interaction, and contact-rich manipulation. We further develop RUIKit, a unified framework that crosses six action abstractions with two interaction forms, yielding 13 interfaces under shared perception and control. In simulation, changing only the RUI shifts a frontier agent's success rate from 45% to 81%, and that of a fine-tuned 4B model from 13% to 47%. Our analysis reveals that effective interfaces reduce unnecessary decisions while preserving essential feedback, with their benefits shaped by agent behavior and physical uncertainty. We further uncover a verification gap, where agents frequently declare success despite observable failures, revealing the need for task-level verification beyond individual actions. Together, these findings establish RUI design as a critical factor in embodied agent reliability.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31115) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31115)

## 21. Leakage-Safe Auditing of Attribution-Driven Data Curation in Medical Imaging

- **作者**：Malay Phadke, Akshay Gokhale
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：14 pages, 4 figures. Accepted to the Findings Track of the Machine Learning for Health (ML4H) Symposium 2026

When can an attribution-derived anomaly justify changing a medical-imaging training set? We formulate attribution-driven curation as three separately testable claims: anomaly detection, harmfulness estimation, and intervention utility. We test the first and third using a patient-disjoint, identifier-preserving audit of a 3D convolutional classifier for computed tomography angiography aneurysm candidates. We identify 232 exactly zero-valued arrays among 1,201 positive patches, including 170 training positives, and invalidate a historical 321-patch attention-failure subgroup whose identifier-free records were joined to files by position. On the held-out test set, GradCAM++ spatial concentration is central for every candidate class, reversing the historical pattern. GradCAM++ and Integrated Gradients show negative rank-order agreement for positives, limiting method-independent anatomical interpretation. Across five paired training seeds, removing the 170 corrupted training positives yields AUC 0.9617 +/- 0.0044 versus 0.9652 +/- 0.0039 for full-data training (difference -0.0035; 95% CI -0.0082 to 0.0007). Excluding 34 raw-zero test positives widens rather than closes the gap, countering a blank-memorization explanation. Budget-matched random-removal controls suggest the result is not simply an effect of reducing training size. Input corruption does not necessarily imply that deletion improves generalization. Our framework makes leakage control, sample identity, and intervention testing explicit in attribution-driven medical-imaging data curation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31096) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31096)

## 22. Wrist-Camera Video Diffusion as Generalizable Robot Policies

- **作者**：Jingyuan Liu, Yongwei Chen, Yishan Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

A generalist manipulation policy should perform new tasks, operate in unfamiliar scenes, and transfer skills across robot embodiments. We present Wrist Video Policy (WVP), a closed-loop manipulation framework that learns how manipulation unfolds through video prediction and recovers executable motion through geometric estimation. Our key idea is to represent robot motion through wrist-camera trajectories, which determine the corresponding end-effector poses through a fixed hand-eye transformation. We pair a video generator that predicts future wrist views with a pose estimator adapted from a pretrained 3D model to recover the corresponding camera trajectories. Model stitching maps the generator's predicted latents directly into the estimator's intermediate feature space, bypassing RGB decoding. This decomposition allows task knowledge to expand through broad human and robot video data while reusing geometric motion estimation across tasks and embodiments. The resulting pose estimator transfers across Franka Panda, PiPER-X, and Galaxea R1 Pro without further fine-tuning. Across tabletop and laundry-room settings, WVP generalizes to tasks ranging from precise grasping and inserting to handling deformable objects, achieving a success rate of over 50% in zero-shot out-of-distribution evaluations. Our project page is at https://wrist-video-policy.github.io.

## 23. UMAMI: A Multimodal Benchmark Dataset for Evidence-Grounded Dietary Decisions

- **作者**：Safal Thapaliya, Jiatan Huang, Debapriya Chatterjee et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：40 pages, 15 figures, 16 tables. Dataset, code, and dashboard: https://umami-benchmark.github.io

Dietary decisions require identifying a food's nutrient content and considering individual dietary needs, but recognizing food from an image alone does not provide this information. Visually similar foods can have different nutrient profiles, so selecting the wrong food record can lead to incorrect decisions even when nutrient rules are applied correctly. Furthermore, satisfying nutrient constraints does not necessarily establish a food's relevance to a person's intake. Evaluating these challenges together requires connecting food images, nutrient records, and individual intake histories. To support this evaluation, we introduce UMAMI (Unified Multimodal Assessment of Meals and Intake), a multimodal dataset and benchmark linking independently collected food images with nutrient profiles and respondent health and intake records. Using this dataset, we construct three diagnostic tasks covering visual nutrient question answering, personalized food recommendation, and condition-aware food substitution. Paired instances and input controls examine how visual identity and respondent context affect decisions. Our experiments identify errors in both food-record selection and nutrient-rule application, while showing that higher rule compliance does not necessarily improve alignment with subsequent recorded intake. These findings highlight the need to evaluate how multimodal systems select and use structured evidence for dietary decisions. The dataset, code, and dashboard are available at https://umami-benchmark.github.io.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31068) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31068)

## 24. RoFE: Rotated Frozen Embeddings for Low-Data Multimodal Tabular Learning

- **作者**：Yihao Qiao, Jiajian Ma, Wushuang Rui et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV, stat.ML
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/yihao-qiao/RoFE. Project page: https://yihao-qiao.github.io/RoFE

Multimodal tabular learning combines tabular data with images and text, often in settings where labeled data are scarce. Tabular foundation models such as prior-data fitted networks (PFNs) offer a promising solution via in-context learning without task-specific training. However, modern image and text encoders produce embeddings with hundreds or thousands of dimensions, far exceeding the number of features encountered during PFN pretraining. Existing approaches bridge this gap using principal component analysis (PCA) to compress frozen embeddings. Surprisingly, we find that this PCA interface can underperform simple linear classifiers when labeled examples are scarce, even though PFNs are designed for small datasets. We identify the underlying failure mechanism: the PFN's per-column standardization removes the variances of the PCA scores. When the PCA keeps every direction of the labeled examples, the examples collapse into a regular simplex in which all pairwise distances are equal. To address this problem, we propose Rotated Frozen Embeddings (RoFE), a training-free method that rotates PCA scores before PFN inference. We theoretically show that rotation makes the column variances nearly equal, so that standardization approximately preserves the embedding geometry. Across image and text classification tasks, encoders, and tabular foundation models, RoFE improves low-data performance over the PCA interface and outperforms baselines that require task-specific training. The improvements also extend to regression.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31049) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31049)

## 25. Distill the Correction, Not the Rewrite: Hindsight Twins for Self-Improving Text-to-Image Models

- **作者**：Jiaze Li, Jingyang Chen, Wenhao Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Text-to-image models can learn from their own mistakes by distilling a self-teacher conditioned on a vision-language critic's revised prompt. However, such self-teachers barely improve alignment, and both they and their hindsight-contrast variants absorb the critic's unintended additions to the scene, such as lighting or scenery. We attribute this to two factors: (1) the prompt already specifies the desired fix, so a useful revision must go beyond the failed span, often rewriting the entire scene and adding details that overshadow the fix; (2) although the critic localizes the error to a single span, self-teachers condition on the rewrite, entangling the intended fix with unintended additions. Motivated by these insights, we propose Hindsight Twins (HinT), which fills the critic's revision template once with the intended fix and once with the observed error and distills only their velocity difference, anchored at the user's prompt. Because text encoders spread the edit of a single slot to all other tokens, HinT further aligns the twins' token states outside the slot. Across Qwen-Image-2512, SD3.5-M, and FLUX.2-klein, HinT keeps the rate of critic-induced bias at the base-model level while matching or exceeding the alignment gains of prior self-distillation, and it keeps improving over twelve rounds of self-training where contrastive baselines decay.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31048) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31048)

## 26. Déjà VAE: When Do Autoencoder Traces Reveal Generated Images?

- **作者**：Saugat Pandey
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：29 pages, 10 figures, 7 tables. Code and per-image scores: https://github.com/iamsaugatpandey/deja-vae

A latent diffusion model's output has already passed through its autoencoder, so a second round trip changes it very little. This "déjà vu" underlies reconstruction-based detectors of generated images; we analyse when it reveals a generated image and when it does not. Read by a frozen vision foundation model without training, the trace is strongest in intermediate layers, and, unlike pixel-level reconstruction error, it treats blur as a sign of a real image. It belongs to the generator's exact decoder: on Synthbuster, the exact decoders of Stable Diffusion 1.3, 1.4, 2 and XL are the strongest of eight autoencoders and catch 94-100% of their generators' images at a 5% false-alarm rate (SDXL's version identified post hoc), while near-identical versions catch 7-42%. A detector trained only on real photographs and their autoencoder copies matches one trained on 162k generated images on GenImage (+2.3 points, 95% CI [0.9, 3.7]) and complements it in fusion, but one resize with JPEG re-save, or a mild blur, costs it 19-27 points. Two lessons concern benchmarks. The in-the-wild benchmark Chameleon can be solved from image width alone. And on Synthbuster, how the real photographs are matched to the generated images' size decides whether our detector leads or trails (+4.4 or -9.7 points): the matching protocol alone can reverse a detector comparison. Code and per-image scores: https://github.com/iamsaugatpandey/deja-vae

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31036) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31036)

## 27. Token Pruning Needs a Protected Sink Token: Coherent Spatial Aggregation in Vision Transformers

- **作者**：Julian Wyatt, James Willoughby, Allison Clement et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/Julian-Wyatt/MCTP

Vision Transformers (ViTs) are a predominant architecture for computer vision, with token reduction emerging as a popular paradigm to improve their efficiency. Prior work compares reduction algorithms, but not how models respond to them, nor how their response changes with model depth. We propose Monte Carlo Token Pruning (MCTP), which estimates each token's impact on the prediction when tokens are explicitly removed during the forward pass. Applying MCTP to recent pretrained models, we observe that most, such as DINOv3, maintain a similar ranking of token importance at every depth (high Coherent Aggregation), while SigLIP 2 does not. This coherence requires predictions to survive pruning, which depends on keeping high-norm "sink" tokens: prefix tokens are kept by design, while prefix-free SigLIP 2 forms them on patch tokens, which pruning may remove. We show that in removing DINOv3's prefix tokens, its predictions and coherence collapse, while protecting only 5 tokens for SigLIP 2 cuts the token impact outside the object's bounding box by up to 62%. Beyond this, MCTP offers a post-hoc method to evaluate how a pretrained ViT responds to pruning, and suggests that token reduction methods should keep "sinks".

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31025) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31025)

## 28. WildAnySplat: Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting in the Wild

- **作者**：Richard Shaw, Arthur Moreau, Athanasios Papaioannou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to SIGGRAPH Asia 2026 (Conference Papers). Project page: https://wildanysplat.github.io/ ; Supplementary material: https://wildanysplat.github.io/wildanysplat_supp.pdf

We present WildAnySplat, a feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting framework that enables robust novel-view synthesis from in-the-wild multi-view images. Unlike controlled captures, real-world images often contain multi-view inconsistencies caused by transient occluders, illumination changes, and camera ISP, which degrade the quality of existing feed-forward reconstruction methods. To address this challenge, we introduce a student-teacher framework that learns transient-robust and appearance-consistent 3D representations. A teacher network operates on clean images, while a student processes the corresponding in-the-wild inputs. The teacher provides clean supervision to guide the student toward a consistent canonical representation. Since paired clean and in-the-wild data are scarce, we use a hybrid data strategy, combining real paired captures, synthetic augmentations, and pseudo-clean labels generated from optimization-based reconstructions, bridging the domain gap between synthetic and real-world data. We adopt a transformer architecture with dedicated DPT heads for camera pose, depth, and Gaussian prediction. We additionally predict per-view transient masks to reweight photometric supervision and suppress unreliable Gaussians. To account for cross-view appearance variation, we incorporate a bilateral grid module that models per-image color transformations and enables recovery of a canonical radiance representation. Experiments on challenging in-the-wild benchmarks show that WildAnySplat improves robustness to transient occlusions and appearance variation while retaining the fast inference of feed-forward Gaussian Splatting.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31023) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.31023)

## 29. OmniV-Bench: Can MLLMs Comprehend Immersive Spatio-temporal Scenes?

- **作者**：Liu Yang, Huiyu Duan, Yixuan Gao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Omnidirectional videos (ODVs) capture dynamic environments with a full $360^\circ$ field of view, providing rich spatial context and temporal dynamics for applications such as virtual reality (VR), augmented reality (AR), and embodied intelligence. Recent multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made great progress in spatial and temporal understanding, yet their ability to comprehend the immersive 360 spatio-temporal information conveyed by ODVs remains largely unexplored. To bridge this gap, we first introduce OmniV-Bench, a Benchmark designed to comprehensively evaluate the capabilities of MLLMs on spatio-temporal Omnidirectional Video understanding. OmniV-Bench contains 4,123 high-quality ODVs collected from diverse scenes, along with 8,923 human-annotated question-answer pairs spanning 11 fine-grained tasks across three categories, i.e., general, spatial, and spatio-temporal understanding. Extensive benchmark experiments reveal that the spatio-temporal complexity of ODVs poses significant challenges for current MLLMs. To address these limitations, we further propose OmniV-Harness, an agentic harness that enhances ODV understanding through viewpoint-guided evidence initialization and task-relevant evidence acquisition with adaptive tool use. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that OmniV-Harness consistently improves immersive spatio-temporal reasoning ability of MLLMs. Both OmniV-Bench and OmniV-Harness will be released upon publication.

## 30. Parsing Visual Latents for Multimodal Spatial Reasoning

- **作者**：Zhuoying Yang, Yichen Guo, Kathy Jia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL
- **备注**：18 pages, 8 figures, 12 tables. Code: https://github.com/Harvard-AI-and-Robotics-Lab/LatentParse

Spatial reasoning across multiple views requires inferring the 3D relations between objects and the camera from 2D observations, which depends on cross-view correspondence and scene geometry that vision-language models (VLMs) do not reliably extract. Pretrained vision encoders already capture this information in their latent features, with CLIP linking objects to image regions, DINOv3 matching semantics across views, and VGGT encoding multi-view geometry. However, VLMs do not benefit from these features when we present them directly in context as grayscale feature images. We therefore propose LatentParse, a training-free framework that parses latent features into explicit spatial evidence for VLMs. In LatentParse, an analyst VLM probes the features of all three encoders with predefined tools that locate objects, track them across views, and measure motion between views. It then distills the results into a concise text report, which is provided together with the images to the VLM to answer the question. We evaluate LatentParse on MMSI-Bench, MV-RoboBench and SPAR-Bench, where it improves all five VLMs over the photo-only baseline by 0.7-2.4 percentage points on average. On MMSI-Bench, it raises Qwen3.8-27B from 34.4% to 39.5%, with the largest improvements of 19.2 percentage points on the camera-related questions. These results suggest that latent features become useful for spatial reasoning once they are parsed into relation evidence, without training. Code is available at https://github.com/Harvard-AI-and-Robotics-Lab/LatentParse.

## 31. Demographic Encoding is Not Exposure: Fairness and Privacy in Chest X-ray Foundation Models

- **作者**：Sahar Askari, Amin Golnari
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV, cs.LG

Medical foundation models encode patient demographics and can reveal whether a patient was in the training set, and these properties are often treated as one problem. We tested whether they are aligned by measuring demographic encoding, diagnostic fairness gaps and membership-inference vulnerability on the same models. We trained 200 linear or multilayer-perceptron (MLP) heads on frozen BiomedCLIP, DINOv2 and RAD-DINO embeddings, with three seeds, and audited them on CheXpert and NIH ChestX-ray14. Demographic encoding explained almost no variance in patient vulnerability, and all 72 CheXpert associations were statistically equivalent to zero. Erasing linearly decodable demographics left leakage unchanged but reduced age-related underdiagnosis gaps. Leakage depended instead on head capacity and training length. On RAD-DINO, a linear head reached a membership-inference area under the curve (AUC) of 0.694, compared with 0.907 for a 30-epoch MLP, at similar test performance. Fairness and privacy should therefore be audited separately, and linear heads or shorter training reduce leakage with little or no loss of utility. The code is available on GitHub for public use.

## 32. Reinforcing Diffusion Models by Instantaneous Trajectory Distribution Matching

- **作者**：Yihong Luo
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV

Forward diffusion reinforcement learning (RL)---enabling deterministic ODE sampling without trajectory backpropagation---has grown increasingly popular for efficiently aligning diffusion models. However, we reveal that existing methods essentially rely on signed reward-weighted regression (RWR), regressing the \emph{trainable policy} velocity onto rollouts from a \emph{behavior distribution}. Unbiased baseline subtraction implicitly assumes that the policy velocity equals the behavior marginal velocity---an idealized assumption violated under classifier-free guidance (CFG) rollouts, offline/off-policy updates, and imperfect online self-consistency. This \emph{behavior--policy mismatch} breaks baseline invariance and injects \emph{baseline-induced gradient drift}, destabilizing training. To resolve this issue, we propose \textbf{I}nstantaneous \textbf{T}rajectory \textbf{D}istribution \textbf{M}atching (\textbf{iTDM}), taking the infinitesimal limit of Trajectory Distribution Matching toward a time-local reward-guided target via the posterior-score identity to yield a sample-level gradient anchored at the behavior velocity without learning an additional reward-tilted target score. Tracking this velocity with an online auxiliary denoiser guarantees exact baseline invariance at the auxiliary optimum, effectively suppressing baseline drift across both online and offline RL. Extensive experiments across compositional generation, text rendering, and preference alignment show that iTDM achieves state-of-the-art in-domain and out-of-domain performance. Notably, on the challenging GenEval2 benchmark, iTDM converges nearly $3\times$ faster than competing methods while scaling smoothly to $0.87$ without collapse. Code is available at https://github.com/Luo-Yihong/iTDM

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30986) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30986)

## 33. Graph2Scene: Relation-Aware Text-to-Image Generation with Graph-Guided Diffusion Model

- **作者**：Peizheng Li, Xin Ai, Guanghui Cheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Text-to-image diffusion models still struggle with complex prompts containing many elements and interactions. Existing attention-control methods mainly focus on controlling where elements appear, but often ignore how objects interact with each other. As a result, relation tokens describing interactions between elements may assign high attention to irrelevant regions, leading to missing or incorrect interactions. In this paper, we propose Graph2Scene, a training-free framework that treats scene graphs as a core control signal for diffusion generation. It represents elements as nodes and their relations as edges, and translates these element-relation dependencies into selective attention guidance. It grounds each element to its corresponding region and guides relational interactions among the involved elements. To achieve appropriate relation control without over-constraining the generation process, Graph2Scene further adapts the control duration to scene complexity and progressively relaxes graph guidance during denoising. Experiments on T2I-CompBench and ConceptMix show clear improvements in compositional generation, especially for object relations. On ConceptMix, Graph2Scene improves relationship accuracy from 0.59 to 0.84 over the strongest FLUX.1-based regional-control baseline. Results on LongBench-T2I further show its advantage on long and complex prompts.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30964) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30964)

## 34. Foresight Forcing: Learning Spatial Foresight for Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Mishuo Han, Zhixuan Liang, Zhaoyuan Wan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project website: https://anson2333.github.io/foresight-forcing

Robotic manipulation policies can improve action prediction by anticipating how objects move and how their spatial relationships change. World Action Models (WAMs) learn to predict future visual observations, but their training objectives typically leave the underlying 3D geometric transitions implicit. To alleviate this problem, we introduce Foresight Forcing ($\mathcal{F}^{2}$), a WAM that learns a spatial transition representation from changes in a pretrained 3D feature space. During training, a frozen 3D foundation model extracts patch-wise feature differences from aligned current and future observations that provide dense supervision for geometry-aware feature transitions. We distill these targets into spatial transition queries, which are progressively refined from the current observation, language instruction, and proprioceptive state, and use them alongside current visual features to generate actions. At deployment, the policy predicts the queries without future observations or the 3D teacher. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin2.0 demonstrate strong manipulation performance and improved robustness, including 95.01% average success on RoboTwin2.0 Full. On six real-world manipulation tasks, $\mathcal{F}^{2}$ achieves a mean task score of 85.8%.

## 35. Self-Evolving Evaluation Environments for Computer-Use Agents

- **作者**：Wenhao Wang, Panrong Tong, Quyu Kong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

Computer-use agents (CUAs) are rapidly improving, yet their evaluation remains largely static. Existing benchmarks provide a strong foundation, but fixed difficulty ceilings, limited feature coverage, and coarse aggregate scores progressively weaken their discriminative and diagnostic value. We introduce CUA-SEE, a closed-loop evaluation environment that co-evolves tasks and executable verifiers from rollout evidence. It disentangles three objectives: benchmark evolution expands evaluation scope through cohort-calibrated difficulty and coverage growth; diagnostic evolution increases resolution through agent-conditional capability-boundary probes; and verifier evolution improves precision by translating changing requirements into state-grounded checkpoints with adversarial validation. Across multiple agents, applications, and rounds, CUA-SEE generates challenging, measurable tasks while preserving process-level evidence. Our results position adaptive evaluation as a scalable counterpart to self-evolving agents, continuously tracking what agents can do, where their boundaries lie, and how reliably progress is measured.

## 36. Diffutoon-2: Toon Shading with Video Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Yuze Sun, Zhongjie Duan, Yingda Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Toon shading renders objects with a flat, stylized appearance. Diffusion models open a new route to this task by transforming photorealistic videos into an anime style. However, existing approaches often rely on curated source and style training pairs or additional visual references. Diffutoon avoids these requirements through structure guidance and colorization, but builds on an image diffusion backbone with short-window motion modules and repeated overlapping-window denoising. We present Diffutoon-2, a video diffusion framework that learns toon shading through animation reconstruction. Two complementary modules independently reconstruct animation clips from outlines and blurred colors, with the video backbone frozen. At inference, the modules jointly guide generation using the same types of conditions extracted from photorealistic footage, requiring neither cross-domain training pairs nor external style references. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Diffutoon-2 outperforms both general-purpose and domain-specific baselines, across quantitative metrics and human evaluation.

## 37. SIAMBA: A Pseudo-Siamese Mamba-based Deep Learning Network for Multimodal Flood Change Detection

- **作者**：Muhammad Fasih Ishfaq, Giulio Weikmann, Lorenzo Bruzzone
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages, 3 figures, presented at SPIE Environmental Remote Sensing, 2026

Flood change detection from bi-temporal multispectral and synthetic aperture radar (SAR) images is essential for rapid disaster response. In this context, Sentinel-2 multispectral imagery provides discriminative spectral observations, whereas Sentinel-1 SAR provides weather-independent structural information. Using these modalities jointly requires modelling temporal evidence while accounting for cross-modal heterogeneity. In particular, change detection is complicated by cloud contamination, illumination variability, acquisition geometry and speckle, and heterogeneous land-cover responses. To address these challenges, we propose SIAMBA, a pseudo-siamese Mamba-based network for bi-temporal flood change detection from paired Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 observations. SIAMBA uses dedicated modality-specific encoders with dual-pooling squeeze-and-excitation recalibration and selective state-space processing for long-range spatial context. A gated cross-modal change module derives absolute, signed, and relative log-ratio differences at multiple semantic levels, and attention-refined skip connections support progressive multi-scale decoding. Experiments are carried out on OMBRIA benchmark, which includes an independent test set and geographically diverse subsets in France, Albania, Guyana, and Timor. SIAMBA achieves 81.98% IoU, and 93.57% OA on the independent test set, reaching the highest values in the considered comparison. It also provides the highest unweighted average IoU and OA across four regional subsets spatially disjuncted. These results indicate that combining selective state-space contextual modelling with gated multiscale discrepancy learning effectively improves flood change mapping across the evaluated geographic settings.

## 38. FGSCalib: Targetless Camera-LiDAR Extrinsic Calibration from Foreground Segmentation

- **作者**：Marcell Kégl, Csaba Benedek
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 5 figures, and 4 pages of supplementary material. Code: https://github.com/zsungi/fgscalib

Camera-LiDAR pairs installed as fixed infrastructure need periodic recalibration, but a calibration target takes the installation out of service. We present FGSCalib, a targetless method that calibrates a stationary camera-LiDAR pair from the objects moving in its view. We only assume that arbitrary moving objects are present in the scene: each sensor separates the moving foreground from the static background, without relying on object classes. With the correct extrinsic, the LiDAR foreground points project onto the camera foreground. FGSCalib turns the camera mask into a boundary-proximity field and finds the extrinsic by two-stage simulated annealing over selected frame pairs. It needs no measured initial extrinsic (random starts give similar results) and works with both rotating and non-repetitive LiDARs. We evaluate it on 21 simulated scenarios with exact ground truth and on 72 real ones (nine camera-LiDAR pairs in eight scenes), with a reference averaged from 43 manual calibrations. In simulation the median error is 5.3 cm and 0.19°. On real data we use a tolerance of 18 cm and 1.8°, met by 93% of single manual calibrations. Keeping the lowest-cost of five runs, FGSCalib meets it in 51 of the 72 scenarios: in 20 of 24 with the Livox, where the strongest of five baselines reaches 11, and in 31 of 48 with the two rotating LiDARs, where only one baseline estimate does. Its median error is 9.5 cm and 0.9°, against 7.3 cm and 0.7° for a single manual calibration. The code and the dataset are available online.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30935) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30935)

## 39. CHICO: Bridging Sparse Metadata and Multimodal Evidence in Cultural Heritage Collections

- **作者**：Vagner Inácio de Oliveira, Paula Dornhofer Paro Costa
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.DL, cs.CV
- **备注**：6 pages, 3 figures

Cultural heritage aggregators provide access to millions of digitized items. Yet their catalog records often contain sparse, heterogeneous metadata, with associated media links that may not lead to accessible images. Structuring and validating these resources for multimodal AI research can support reproducible experimentation. To this end, we present CHICO (Cultural Heritage Images and Catalog Objects), a multimodal dataset derived from Europeana that brings together curated catalog fields, images, and contextual information to support evidence-grounded description generation while retaining the original Europeana records. It comprises more than 60,000 cultural heritage records with associated images. Although originally developed for that purpose, it can also support multimodal information retrieval, metadata enrichment, and evidence-based evaluation.

## 40. CoSel: Cooperation as Selection for End-to-End Driving

- **作者**：Yiping Li, Haohua Que, Zhipeng Bao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages, 11 figures, 9 tables

Cooperative driving can extend a vehicle's awareness beyond its own sensors, but existing cooperative end-to-end methods inject shared information into the planner and rely on cooperative driving demonstrations to learn how to use it. The moments in which shared information should change a driving decision are rare in such data, providing limited supervision for learning this behavior. We argue that cooperation need not be learned by the planner. Instead, reacting to shared information requires predicting how surrounding agents will move and selecting a response from the candidate trajectories of an ego planner. We introduce CoSel, a cooperation-as-selection framework that leaves the planner and its ego-only input unchanged. A separately trained cooperative predictor uses observations from the ego vehicle, roadside units, and connected automated vehicles to predict surrounding agents and their future motion. CoSel evaluates the planner's candidate trajectories against these predictions, preserves the planner's choice when it remains safe, and selects the closest safe alternative when shared information reveals additional risk. Each safety intervention is traceable to the shared evidence that triggers it. In closed-loop evaluation, CoSel achieves a driving score of 70.7 on V2Xverse, outperforming the best compared cooperative method by 7.5 points, and the highest average driving score of 85.8 on MDrive. Under missing ego detections, the collision count of CoSel increases only from 17 to 19 in 72 scenarios, compared with 19 to 32 for an existing cooperative end-to-end model.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30918) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30918)

## 41. Do Waste Classifiers Recognise Items They Have Never Seen? Object-Disjoint Evaluation on Household and Medical Waste

- **作者**：Nusrat Jahan, Mst. Eshrat Jahan Esha, Masuma Sadia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Automatic waste classification supports source separation, which enables recycling and keeps hazardous medical items out of household waste. Waste classification studies often report near-perfect accuracy on random image splits, which put photographs of one item in both training and test sets. To measure how well classifiers recognise items they have never seen, we built BanglaWaste-23, with 4,160 images of 172 objects in 23 household and medical waste classes from Bangladesh. Every image also records which physical item it shows. We evaluated 11 ImageNet-pretrained models and two models trained from scratch (a compact custom network and MobileNetV3-L) using object-disjoint five-fold cross-validation and a random split. On unseen objects, the best pretrained model reached a macro-averaged F1 score of 98.8%, and six models were statistically tied. The random split overstated from-scratch accuracy by 15 to 19 percentage points. The from-scratch models also missed 11 to 13% of medical items, against under 1% for pretrained models. On a second dataset from Dhaka, pretrained accuracy fell by 15 to 22 points. Classifiers should therefore be evaluated on unseen objects, with random-split results read as an upper bound. The data are on Zenodo and the code at https://github.com/saadbaust/BanglaWaste-23.
