## 42. Replay Before You Reply: Second-Look Prompting for Unified Multimodal Encoding

- **作者**：Yicheng Zhang, Xun Zhang, Jingyuan Ma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Solving multimodal tasks requires Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to form a unified understanding of the image and the question text. However, under causal attention in current VLMs, a token can attend only to what precedes it. Whichever modality comes first is therefore encoded under partial observation of the input, uninformed by the rest of the multimodal context. We introduce Second-Look Prompting (SLP), a training-free method that repeats the image-question pair in the input. In the repeated copy, every visual and textual token can attend to the complete input, yielding a unified multimodal encoding of the pair. Moreover, the added visual tokens contribute computation beyond re-exposing the image: even a blank image in their place can improve reasoning. Across 12 benchmarks, SLP improves the overall average score of every evaluated model, spanning nine open-source models (3B-72B) and three proprietary frontier models, with gains reaching 5.6 points for Gemini 3 Flash on MATH-Vision. Our analysis reveals two complementary mechanisms: the question guides where the second image attends, and the added visual tokens act as a prefill scratchpad whose hidden states retain answer-relevant information from the image and question. These findings highlight input presentation as a simple yet effective way to improve VLM performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30909) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30909)

## 43. DexLatentWorld: 3D Cross-Embodiment Latent Action World Models for Zero-Shot Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Vinay Udit Mohan, Xuchuan Huang, Rolandos Alexandros Potamias
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：15 pages, including supplementary material

World models facilitate planning and control in robotic manipulation by enabling agents to anticipate the consequences of their actions. However, they typically rely on action-labelled interaction data that is costly to acquire and difficult to scale. These models are also often tied to the specific embodiment they were trained on, limiting transferability to previously unseen robot morphologies. We introduce a 3D latent-action world model that learns continuous action representations directly from point-cloud observations of retargeted human manipulation demonstrations. A contrastive objective is used to encourage consistent representations of motion across embodiments. The resulting unified latent action space serves directly as the search space for model-predictive control, where sampled latent actions condition particle-based future predictions. Executable robot commands are then recovered from the predicted geometric motion without requiring an embodiment-specific action decoder. Through extensive evaluations, we demonstrate that the learned latent actions exhibit controllable and compositional motion, transfer to unseen robot embodiments, and support zero-shot manipulation with unseen gripper geometries. Following task-agnostic adaptation, our model achieves 90%, 92%, and 60% success on reaching, grasping, and placing, respectively, compared with DexWM's reported 72%, 58%, and 28%. Finally, we demonstrate zero-shot real-world manipulation with an unseen dexterous hand, achieving an 86% success rate across 50 trials on five diverse tasks involving pushing, grasping, and lifting everyday objects. These results highlight the potential of geometry-grounded latent actions as a unified, transferable interface for world-model-based robotic manipulation. Project materials, including demonstration videos and source code, are available at https://vinaymohan0409.github.io/DexLatentWorld/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30907) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30907)

## 44. GlanceNav: Efficient Vision-and-Language Navigation with Streaming Memory and Counterfactual Rewind

- **作者**：Anh Dao, Quan-Dung Pham, Danh Vinh Le et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have become strong policies for vision-and-language navigation (VLN). However, most of them encode a sample of past frames again at every decision and are trained on isolated decisions, so computation is redundant and the usable history is short. They also learn from their own mistakes through outcome rewards that give one scalar to a whole episode. We ask whether a navigator can encode each frame only once, at a constant cost per step, and still remember what matters and learn efficiently from its mistakes. We present GlanceNav, a streaming navigator built on a hybrid MLLM. Its Gated DeltaNet layers carry a recurrent state through the whole episode, while its attention layers keep a short window of recent observations and a small bank of keyframes, selected by how much each frame changes the recurrent state. This constant-size memory keeps the cost per step fixed and allows training on whole episodes with the same memory as at inference. Because the entire memory can also be copied in constant time, we further propose Counterfactual Rewind (CoRe), a reinforcement learning stage that rewinds a drifting rollout to its closest approach to the goal, lets an expert correct a few actions, and credits decisions by the resulting change in outcome. Trained only on the R2R-CE and RxR-CE training splits, with 1,000 instructions for reinforcement learning, a 4B GlanceNav reaches success rates of 71.9% and 73.6% on R2R-CE and RxR-CE val-unseen. On R2R-CE, it outperforms the strongest previous single-RGB method trained without external data by 9.1 points, while running at 104 ms per step.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30900) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30900)

## 45. World Models as Native Latent Action Models

- **作者**：Qinyue Li, Weijie Qiao, Qingyi Tao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：19 pages, 7 figures, 6 tables. Project page: https://pawteam-eai.github.io/native-lam/

Large and diverse video sources, such as egocentric human video and heterogeneous robot recordings, carry rich dynamics but come either without actions or without a shared action space. Latent action models (LAMs) can recover these dynamics, but they train their inverse- and forward-dynamics models (IDM, FDM) from scratch, trading off pixel reconstruction against latent capacity and accuracy. Pretrained video world models natively generate high-fidelity dynamics, yet represent them densely, entangled across all layers. Viewing the world model as a LAM, we show that these dynamics can be extracted with minimal changes to the model. Native LAM operates one pretrained video diffusion transformer as both IDM and FDM, with learnable dynamics registers as the only causal bridge between the two, and trains on counterfactual temporal pairs that differ in motion alone, so that the registers distill a video's dynamics into a compact native latent action. We further propose a pretraining paradigm for vision-language-action (VLA) policies, Pretraining Actions from World models (PAWlicy), in which Native LAM serves as a dynamics teacher on large-scale video; unlike world action models, it never generates video. Native latent actions outperform existing LAMs on the LARY benchmark in both action classification and control regression, and replay the same motion on new robots, in new scenes, and from human hands to robots, although no transfer objective is used in training. With a 3.8B model, PAWlicy reaches a 93.6% average success rate on RoboTwin 2.0 trained with domain-randomized demonstrations, the highest among VLAs. On RoboDojo, it is competitive on every dimension we evaluate, and its Precision, evaluated by us, is on par with the leading leaderboard entries. On real robots, it improves over the pi_0.5 baseline on both tasks. Both stages use only public data and a publicly released world model.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30898) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30898)

## 46. Speak When You See It: Proactive Task Assistants Should Wait for Evidence, Not the Clock

- **作者**：Bohe Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：31 pages, 10 figures, 27 tables

Proactive assistants that guide people through physical tasks must decide, every few seconds, whether a step is complete and whether a mistake has occurred. We find that two benchmarks for this task largely reward knowing when rather than seeing what: a timer that never looks at the video, raising an alarm at 25 s and confirming each step at 30 s, reaches a mistake F1 of 0.373 on Ego-MC-Bench, above every published result (at most 0.27). We formalize this as the video-blind frontier, the best trade-off between completion accuracy and mistake F1 that policies reach from the elapsed time, the instruction and training statistics alone: every published per-step result on Ego-MC-Bench lies inside it, and so do all twelve zero-shot models we run on QICD. To move beyond it, we introduce Evidence-Contrastive Training (ECT): fine-tune on the benchmark's own questions, and pair its positives with negatives from the same recording at the same elapsed time, so that elapsed time cannot separate the two sides of a pair. Trained on 209 QICD videos in about 15 GPU-hours, a 2B model with ECT crosses the frontier on both benchmarks in all four seeds; transferred to Ego-MC-Bench without adaptation, it confirms 59% of mistake-free steps in time, against 30% for a published model with the same backbone trained on about 25k synthetic counterfactuals and real recordings. Its decisions follow the video beyond what timing explains: at equal elapsed time it tells a finished step from an unfinished one in 90-92% of test pairs, where the instruction's typical duration reaches 65%, rescaling the time stamps between the frames leaves it essentially unchanged, and swapping in another recording, of the same recipe where one exists, cuts its completion accuracy by two thirds. The code and model weights will be publicly released.

## 47. A High-Resolution UAV Benchmark Dataset and Baseline Evaluation for Teak Crown Segmentation in Western Ghats Tropical Forests

- **作者**：Anand S Sahadevan, Sunita Arya, Anup Kumar Das et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：5 pages

Accurate teak-tree segmentation from high-resolution drone imagery is critical for sustainable plantation management, yet lacks dedicated benchmarks. We introduce TeakTreeNet, a comprehensive dataset and evaluation protocol for binary teak-tree segmentation at native 2cm resolution. Four state-of-the-art architectures: U-Net, PSPNet, DeepLabV3, and SegFormer were rigorously benchmarked across scratch and ImageNet-pretrained settings with three-seed reproducibility. SegFormer establishes new SOTA performance with F1 0.938 +/- 0.002 (Teak), IoU 0.968 +/- 0.001 (Teak), and Accuracy 0.986 +/- 0.001, outperforming all pretrained CNNs. Domain-adaptive training suffices, as the scratch SegFormer outperforms ImageNet pretrained baselines, avoiding large-scale pretraining overhead. Comprehensive analysis reveals Transformer's superior boundary delineation for dense plantation scenarios. TeakTreeNet provides researchers with 6391 expertly annotated drone patches (224x224) and establishes reproducible evaluation protocols, enabling direct comparison of future methods. Our benchmark demonstrates SegFormer's optimal balance of accuracy, efficiency, and deployability for operational forestry monitoring of teak plantations for India's western tropical forests.

## 48. Learning the Next Best Move with Visual Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Amir Razlighi, Ali Mahdavi Amiri, Yiorgos Chrysanthou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.GR, cs.CV
- **备注**：project page: https://amirhossein-razlighi.github.io/next_best_move

In this paper, we show that visual diffusion models can learn to predict the next best move (NBM) in tasks requiring sequential reasoning and lookahead, without explicitly exploring future steps or exhaustive searching at inference. Previous work demonstrated that these models can generate complete solutions to geometric and reasoning problems. Here, we show that simple fine-tuning on visual state--NBM pairs enables a pretrained model to select actions whose value depends on consequences several steps ahead. Each move is predicted directly in image space from the current state, without an explicit symbolic representation or search procedure. Repeated NBM predictions then construct a solution one decision at a time. We demonstrate this capability on chess puzzles and tile-based pathfinding, and show that it extends to Connect Four, Go and billiards. In chess, the model predicts legal moves and finds the best move far more often than by recalling similar training positions, including piece sacrifices whose benefit emerges only through later tactical continuations. In pathfinding, it constructs shortest paths on uniform-cost maps and cost-optimal routes across terrain tiles with different traversal costs, choosing longer routes when they are cheaper overall. These costs are learned implicitly from examples, without numerical cost supervision. These results show that direct visual move prediction can support globally informed decisions and successful sequential problem solving.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30879) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30879)

## 49. XFreq: Geometry-Aligned Cross-View Residual Learning for Sparse 3D Ultrasound Reconstruction

- **作者**：Yuhuan Lu, P. Ellen Grant, Davood Karimi
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Three-dimensional (3D) ultrasound provides valuable volumetric context for anatomical assessment, motivating reconstruction from widely available two-dimensional (2D) slices. Sparse slices leave much of the volume unobserved, even when their acquisition poses are known. Existing methods interpolate observed intensities or incorporate learned 2D image features into 3D reconstruction, but their complementary roles in predicting smooth structure and residual details remain less explored. We introduce XFreq, a geometry-aligned framework for sparse 3D ultrasound reconstruction. It uses known slice poses and finite footprints to query 2D image features at 3D locations, complementing splatted intensities and sampling geometry. A low-frequency decoder predicts a smooth 3D field from the splat representation, while a high-frequency decoder additionally incorporates the queried 2D features to predict residual details. A learned gating module regulates the residual contribution based on geometry and cross-slice feature variation. We evaluate XFreq on an in-house fetal brain dataset and the public CETUS cardiac dataset, using slices resampled from 3D ultrasound volumes and fivefold cross-validation. Among the compared methods, XFreq achieves the highest PSNR and SSIM in most settings across both datasets, reaching 26.82 dB PSNR on CETUS with 32 input slices. Ablation studies further show the contributions of local feature querying, geometric cues, and residual prediction, with gains generally increasing as more input slices are used. Our findings highlight the value of geometry-aligned cross-view feature integration for reconstructing 3D ultrasound volumes from sparse 2D slices.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30869) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30869)

## 50. CableMoE: Harm-Regularized Mean-Residual Expert Fusion for Transmission-Corridor Segmentation

- **作者**：Shima Salehi
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Submitted to IEEE TPEC 2027

Semantic mapping of transmission corridors benefits from heterogeneous LiDAR and image-guided predictors, but replacing a strong ensemble with a learned router can damage rare electrical assets. We introduce CableMoE, a post-hoc mixture of four frozen segmentation experts. Its deployable prediction is a calibrated residual around availability-aware probability averaging, while a harm penalty discourages extra loss on points that the mean already classifies correctly. A matched component study shows why each design choice is needed: an unconstrained learned mixture loses 1.032 mIoU points relative to probability mean, harm regularization restores 0.359 points at fixed capacity, and removing an unnecessary point-wise gate slightly improves accuracy and stability. With three zones for fitting, two disjoint zones for residual-strength selection, and router-untouched t5b5 for final reporting, CableMoE obtains 84.906% mIoU and 98.140 % overall accuracy. This is +10.000 points over SPT and +0.263 over probability mean; all three seeds improve the latter. Across pylon, conductor, structural-cable, and insulator classes, macro-IoU rises from 84.517% for SPT and 97.153% for probability mean to 98.144 %. The results support conservative residual composition, not a claim of a new segmentation backbone or universal state of the art

## 51. Age and Gender Estimation from Face and Ocular Smartphone Images via Identity Transfer and Ordinal Learning

- **作者**：Fernando Alonso-Fernandez, Kevin Hernandez-Diaz, Jose Maria Buades et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted for publication at 16th International Conference on Pattern Recognition Applications and Methods, ICPRAM 2027

We investigate the transferability of identity-recognition representations to gender and age estimation from face and ocular images. Three CNNs of varying complexity, pre-trained for face or ocular identity recognition, are evaluated using fixed features with SVMs and end-to-end adaptation. Experiments use 11,299 frontal smartphone images from Adience, following its predefined five-fold protocol and subject-disjoint validation. Results show that domain-matched identity pre-training outperforms direct ImageNet and scratch initialisation in end-to-end training, while combining both ocular regions narrows the gap with full-face estimation, especially for age. We additionally introduce an ordinal loss that exploits the ordering of age groups and consistently improves 1-off accuracy across architectures and input regions. Across the evaluated configurations, best gender accuracies reach 88.1% for the face and 82.9% for combined ocular regions, while age estimation reaches 59.0% exact and 93.1% 1-off accuracy for ocular images, versus 57.6% and 95.2% for the full face. To our knowledge, these are the highest ocular age accuracies reported on Adience.

## 52. DyLoMo-DiT: Dynamic Looped Mixture-of-Experts Diffusion Transformers

- **作者**：Lernie Li, Yuanliu Liu, Pengfei Xiong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：46 pages, 29 figures, 33 tables. Code, weights and project page: https://oneboxcream.github.io/DyLoMo-DiT/

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) scale model capacity by increasing both parameter count and computation. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) variants expand parameter capacity while keeping per-token computation roughly fixed, but still require substantial parameter storage. In this paper, we propose Dynamic Looped MoE DiT (DyLoMo-DiT), which reuses parameters rather than adding them: a weight-shared loop repeatedly applies the middle layers, while sparse experts allocate parameter capacity across tokens. This design deepens the network without adding parameters, freeing the parameter budget for other uses, such as more experts or more distinct layers. It also allocates compute dynamically, varying the number of passes across noise levels. Experiments show that DyLoMo-DiT makes more effective use of parameter and compute budgets, with further gains from assigning more passes to low-noise steps. On ImageNet $256\times256$, DyLoMo-DiT reduces FID by 11-24% relative to DiT and 36-56% relative to DiT-MoE at matched parameter and compute budgets. For text-to-image generation, a scaled DyLoMo-DiT achieves a higher GenEval score than FLUX.1 (0.714 vs. 0.673), with less than one tenth of its active parameters (1.05B vs. 12B).

## 53. Certified Defense against Adversarial Patches on Traffic Signs via Mask-Aware Training

- **作者**：Rudra Saxena, Vishal Dev Verma, Rohan Sharma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：4 pages, 4 figures

Adversarial patches are a practical threat to traffic-sign recognition: a small sticker on a sign can change a classifier's decision. PatchCleanser is a certified defense that applies two rounds of masking and, for every image it certifies, provably returns the correct label for any patch that fits inside one mask. We show that on traffic signs the defense certifies almost nothing when paired with a normally trained classifier (1.7% to 4.8% certified accuracy on GTSRB), because a single mask can hide the small central symbol that distinguishes sign classes. We propose mask-aware training, which trains the classifier on images occluded by random pairs drawn from the defense's own mask set, with a sampling bias toward masks over the sign's centre. The defense, mask set and certification procedure are unchanged, so the guarantee is preserved. For an ImageNet-pretrained ResNet-50, mask-aware training raises certified accuracy from 4.8% to 60.0%. Under real per-image adversarial patches, the defended ResNet-50 reaches 85.7% accuracy, versus 74.6% for the original PatchCleanser with a normally trained ResNet-50.

## 54. Can Multimodal Fusion MakeSense for Embodied Vision in Degraded Sensing?

- **作者**：Cheng-Ru Chou, Shih-Yu Lai, Pei-Lin Chiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.RO

Embodied vision systems rely on complementary sensors to perceive and act as conditions change. Even models that accept arbitrary sensor subsets can exhibit negative fusion: adding a sensor increases prediction error. We propose MakeSense, a multimodal fusion framework that preserves sensor-specific evidence in private streams and combines information through shared bottleneck tokens. Training pairs full and subset views of the same degraded observations, penalizing higher error with more sensors. Where geometry permits, the distance readout uses range measurements alone, while the rest of the prediction draws on all available inputs. Paired tests across five datasets reveal helpful and harmful sensor additions. MakeSense reduces negative fusion on MM-Fi. With lightweight adapters and per-dataset training, the same design improves UAV localization on MMAUD, cooperative 3D detection on V2X-Real, few-shot pose transfer to RELI11D, and command prediction on Humanoid-Everyday, where a world model trained on the frozen core's outputs forecasts future fused states more accurately than a linear baseline at longer tested horizons. MakeSense supports embodied sensing across human activity, aerial systems, robotics, and autonomous driving.

## 55. Unpacking the Evaluators: How Configuration Shapes the Evaluation of Alignment in Explainable AI

- **作者**：Gizem Karagoz, Tanir Ozcelebi, Nirvana Meratnia
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026, Evaluations & Datasets Track

Explainable AI (XAI) evaluation fundamentally requires human-centered evaluation, assessing whether explanations align with human reasoning and expert knowledge. However, such evaluations often require expert-driven manual validation and are difficult to scale, leading to the widespread use of alignment-based metrics, where attribution maps are compared against predefined ground-truth (GT) representations. Despite their popularity, alignment evaluations are implicitly bias towards multiple design choices made during the evaluation process, for example GT representations, metric families, metric formulations (soft vs. hard), and thresholding strategies. In this paper, we formalize alignment evaluation as a structured evaluation configuration space through which the influence of these design choices can be systematically analyzed. We further introduce a structured alignment evaluation framework that models interactions between XAI methods, GT representations, metric families, metric formulations, and thresholding strategies across 2D and 3D synthetic and real-world datasets. In addition, we propose a novel taxonomy of XAI output behavior based on local entropy and boundary mass ratio, enabling structured characterization of attribution patterns for alignment evaluation. Our experiments reveal systematic flows (triples of XAI-GT-metric configurations), showing that alignment outcomes provide distinct behavior patterns under certain configurations, such as soft/hard metric implementations. We further observe varying levels of threshold sensitivity across metric families and attribution structures. These findings demonstrate that alignment evaluation cannot be treated as a fixed or invariant measure of explanation quality, and provide practical guidance for designing more reliable and behavior-aware XAI evaluation protocols.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30816) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30816)

## 56. HIVE-Bench: Evaluating Patch-Level Visual Representations for Egocentric Robot Manipulation

- **作者**：Qiwei Liang, Zhengtu Liang, Minghao Lai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：39 pages, 12 figures

Pretrained vision is a common start for robot learning, but most comparisons still score one pooled vector from a third-person image. Bimanual policies instead read dense patch tokens from cameras on the robot. We introduce HIVE-Bench: one shared flow-matching policy reads 20 encoders from seven families on 24 egocentric bimanual tasks, across adaptation, task sharing, and sweeps of data, model size, and layer. Keeping the patches matters. Averaging each view to one token removes most of the success, adaptation helps every pair we test, and one shared policy keeps most of the success of separate policies at a fraction of the updates. The same tokens are then scored with statistics, state and object readouts, published vision scores, and an inverse-dynamics readout. That readout of the commanded motion is one of the strongest measurements available before a policy is trained. Recognition, depth, and correspondence do not transfer reliably. Fine-tuning can improve the action readout even when other measurements move the other way, and no statistic is stable enough to replace a rollout before any deployment.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30813) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30813)

## 57. Go Far, Look Less: Active Multi-Room Reconstruction with Persistent Goals and Selective Views

- **作者**：Jiawei Jiang, Haonan Zhou, Wendi Hu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Active 3D reconstruction aims to allocate a sparse view budget to recover complete geometry and high-fidelity renderings. However, existing methods degrade sharply in multi-room homes: myopic next-best-view scoring gives transit no credit for distant rooms and over-polishes irreducible nearby residuals, whereas dense mappers reach far rooms only by streaming thousands of frames. We propose ROAM-GS, a training-free active multi-room reconstruction framework that couples persistent goals with selective views: each decision selects a goal and a variable-horizon prefix toward it, capturing only at its endpoint. A Surface Reconstruction Memory scores poses by projecting post-update defects and viewing directions. Persistent goals credit transit with their remaining value and are released once arrivals stop measurably improving them. Without per-scene tuning, ROAM-GS achieves state-of-the-art multi-room reconstruction while preserving single-room Replica fidelity, raising mean coverage from 74% to 93% on HSSD and from 47% to 96% (+11.7 dB PSNR) on HM3D, and matching 90-minute dense mapping using $173\times$ fewer views.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30802) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30802)

## 58. PestWeave: Linking Semantic Modeling and Evidence-Guided Text Modulation through Visual Prototypes for Fine-Grained Pest Learning

- **作者**：Xueheng Li, Yu Wang, Yuxiang Shen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures

Agricultural pests threaten crop yield and quality, making accurate pest recognition essential for targeted management and sustainable agriculture. Their diverse appearances complicate recognition and motivate multimodal approaches incorporating complementary species knowledge from text. However, complex backgrounds can obscure discriminative visual cues, while textual descriptions may contain information unsupported by the current image, limiting effective cross-modal fusion. To address these challenges, we propose PestWeave, a multimodal framework connecting content-adaptive visual organization with text fusion through visual prototypes. Its visual pathway augments a Receptance Weighted Key Value (RWKV) architecture with semantic token grouping and three types of auxiliary tokens, enabling content-adaptive scanning and contextual modeling while preserving spatial structure. Feature-similar tokens are reordered into adjacent scanning positions, while cluster prototype, global, and register tokens provide group-level summaries, global context, and additional interaction capacity, respectively. These prototypes also guide text modulation by assessing visual support for semantic components and regulating their relative contributions and overall fusion strength, reducing interference from unsupported descriptions. Extensive experiments on multiple pest recognition datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of PestWeave and its potential for practical pest management.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30796) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30796)

## 59. BridgeMVS: Bridging Multi-View Stereo and Monodepth via Bidirectional Dynamic Fusion

- **作者**：Jianfei Jiang, Haochen Yu, Qiankun Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted by NeurIPS 2026

Multi-View Stereo (MVS) and Monocular Depth Estimation (MDE) provide complementary cues for dense 3D reconstruction. While MVS leverages explicit geometry for metric depth, it struggles in challenging regions where feature matching is unreliable. Conversely, MDE provides robust structural priors but typically yields scale-ambiguous results. Existing hybrid methods mainly exploit monocular cues in a unidirectional manner, leaving the mutual enhancement between MDE and MVS insufficiently explored. To address this limitation, we propose BridgeMVS, a unified cascaded multi-stage MVS framework that bridges monocular depth features and multi-view cost volume representations through Bidirectional Dynamic Fusion (BDF). At each stage, BDF performs dynamic mono-to-cost and cost-to-mono updates, where monocular structural features guide cost volume refinement for more robust MVS depth estimation, while multi-view geometric evidence recalibrates monocular representations to improve their metric-scale consistency. Furthermore, the fused monocular and multi-view representations are progressively propagated from coarse to fine stages and constrained by stage-wise relative-scale MDE supervision and metric-scale MVS supervision, enabling cross-branch interactions to benefit subsequent high-resolution depth estimation. In addition, BDF is designed as a plug-and-play module that can be conveniently integrated into existing cascaded MVS frameworks. Extensive experiments demonstrate that BridgeMVS achieves state-of-the-art performance on the DTU, Tanks-and-Temples, and ETH3D benchmarks, while simultaneously boosting the accuracy of monocular depth estimation. Notably, BridgeMVS ranks first on both the Tanks-and-Temples Intermediate and Advanced benchmarks. The source code is available at \href{https://github.com/JianfeiJ/BridgeMVS}{https://github.com/JianfeiJ/BridgeMVS}.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30787) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30787)

## 60. AvatarAnyone: Agentic Reconstruction of Mesh-Based Layered Avatars from YouTube Video

- **作者**：Aymen Mir, Peter Wonka, Stefanos Zafeiriou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://miraymen.github.io/avataranyone

We introduce AvatarAnyone, a method for reconstructing avatars from casual YouTube videos as layered meshes, with each garment and footwear as a separate mesh layered on top of the body mesh. In such videos, people differ in garment classes, clothing types, and layers, and each combination needs a different reconstruction. A single fixed layered reconstruction pipeline therefore fails. AvatarAnyone solves this problem with AI agents that plan a custom optimization for each person's reconstruction. The agents predict the garment classes, the clothing type, and the layering. They use these predictions to choose an initialization and stage the optimization. Because the avatars are layered meshes, they support applications such as virtual try-on and cloth simulation. We change the clothing layers of an avatar and render it in different garments and poses. We can also simulate a clothing layer as cloth over the animated body with a standard cloth simulator. Most existing avatars are one fused surface or a set of Gaussians and cannot do this. We load the avatars directly into Blender, Unreal Engine, and robotics simulators such as MuJoCo. As the avatars are meshes, they support contact modeling with other objects. We show results from around 150 YouTube videos. On four datasets, AvatarAnyone outperforms existing mesh-based methods for animatable monocular reconstruction. Our project page is available at https://miraymen.github.io/avataranyone.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30777) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30777)

## 61. Reconstructing 3D+t Cardiovascular Flow From Sparse Planar Velocity Fields With Neural Fields

- **作者**：Viraj Patel, Deblina Bhattacharjee, Katharine Fraser
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 figures

Reconstructing time-resolved three-dimensional (3D+t) cardiovascular flow from sparse planar measurements is challenging because each plane observes only the two in-plane velocity components, on a small part of the volume. We propose a plane-aware neural-field framework that learns a continuous 3D velocity field from space and time coordinates through a per-plane projection, and then investigate the potential benefits of physics constraints. We evaluate the framework on computational aortic and aneurysm datasets, and on planes resampled from a real cardiac 4D Flow MRI scan. A plain multi-layer perceptron (MLP) was more accurate than classical radial basis function (RBF) interpolation on both computational datasets. For the aorta, a physics-constrained SIREN (PC-SIREN) reduced the rRMSE further to $0.3756$, compared with $0.4273$ for the plain MLP and $0.7256$ for RBF, and it was the most accurate model in every anatomical segment of the MRI data. For the aneurysms the plain MLP remained the most accurate model, with an rRMSE of $0.07$ to $0.16$ against $0.55$ to $0.71$ for PC-SIREN. Further experiments investigated scanning configuration, number of scans, and spatial resolution. These findings show that continuous neural fields can reconstruct volumetric cardiovascular flow from sparse planar measurements, and that physics constraints have to be matched to the flow.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30775) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30775)

## 62. GRAIN: Shared-Budget Complementary Re-observation for Object Detection

- **作者**：Chengxuan Zhou, Shaoyang Zhang, Rui Qian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Re-running a frozen detector on enlarged image crops recovers small and crowded objects without retraining, yet every crop costs an extra detector call. Existing slicing and adaptive-cropping pipelines plan crops per image in isolation, even though inspection demand varies widely across an image collection. We present GRAIN, which unifies cross-image call allocation and bounded-output admission around cross-image comparable resolution-closure gains over boundary-safe crop interiors. Coarse hypotheses carry resolution deficits, crops close a geometric share of those deficits for hypotheses enclosed in their interior positive region, and multiplicative residuals discount covered evidence within a monotone submodular surrogate. Prefix sums and a global max-heap execute this collection-wide plan on CPU before crop inference, and Positive-Region Fusion aligns post-inference spatial admission with the planned interior support before class-balanced top- selection. Across 17 frozen detectors from five families on COCO, CrowdHuman, and VisDrone-DET, GRAIN beats call-matched tiling in 100 of 102 AP@100 settings and transfers to AI-TOD-v2. With RF-DETR-S on CrowdHuman, it nearly matches two-crop tiling using one eighth of the crop calls. At fixed budgets, the surrogate orders candidate plans with Spearman correlations of 0.95 to 0.99 while the sweep attains at least 98% of the exact surrogate optimum. Code will be publicly released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30770) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30770)

## 63. Screening for nonrigidity in structure from motion using projected reprojection residuals

- **作者**：Íñigo Cirauqui-Viloria
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：34 pages including 19 pages of supplementary material. 5 main figures and 5 supplementary figures

Sparse structure from motion (SfM) assumes a static scene, yet deformation may still yield plausible reconstructions. Reprojection-error summaries discard how errors are arranged across points and views. We propose a diagnostic that first removes residual components explainable by small point and camera adjustments and simple spatial trends that change over time. It then measures alignment with fixed spatial and temporal patterns. The diagnostic uses one estimated SfM reconstruction and provides screening evidence rather than a physical deformation estimate. Across four synthetic tubular families, the diagnostic detected all eight acquisitions with prescribed motion, compared with five or six for summaries of reprojection-error magnitude. Each family's cutoff was the highest static score among the other three families, so the static acquisition with the highest score also screened positive. With the same cutoffs, the diagnostic retained positive detections in 19 of 20 reconstruction variants spanning changes in appearance and reconstruction pipeline, with one numerical abstention. These findings show that the arrangement of reprojection errors across points and views can reveal inconsistencies that their magnitudes alone miss. The four families were known during development, and the transfer variants reused two of them. Within this setting, the diagnostic offers a practical way to flag a plausible reconstruction for closer inspection using only its estimated cameras, 3D points and image observations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30769) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30769)

## 64. Learning Dense Head-Object Contact Estimation with Synthetic Data

- **作者**：Daniel Sungho Jung, Kyoung Mu Lee
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://heco-release.github.io/

Head serves a critical role in human cognitive and perceptual functions while remaining vulnerable to external impacts, making the understanding of head contact essential for human safety. Despite its importance, dense head-object contact estimation remains largely unexplored, primarily due to the absence of head-object contact data. Recently, it has been shown that synthetic data can serve as powerful training data in limited data scenario, implying the potential of synthetic data in head-object contact estimation. There are two core challenges for dense head-object contact estimation. First, head-object interactions are highly diverse, resulting in substantial variations in contact regions and patterns across different objects and interaction types. Second, head contact regions are often heavily occluded by hair, making direct visual observation of the actual contact regions difficult. To address these issues, we introduce HEad COntact estimation (HECO) with synthetic data. To handle diverse head-object interactions, we leverage semantic context from text features that encode semantic relationships between head regions and interacting objects. To resolve hair occlusion issue of actual head contact regions, we introduce occlusion-aware contact learning that learns to infer head contact beyond visible information from images via amodal inpainting. As a result, we enable head-object contact estimation with HECO trained with our synthetic dataset, overcoming the issue of absence of contact data. The codes will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30751) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30751)

## 65. Object-Centric Differentiable Logic Networks for Abstract Visual Reasoning

- **作者**：Hanne Say Eren, Ana Ezquerro, Ozan Özdenizci
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible

Human visual intelligence enables efficient reasoning capabilities on abstract visual problems with bounded cognitive resources and minimal experience, and yields remarkable generalization to novel problems with its ability to draw relational analogies and modular abstractions. In contrast, deep learning models require exhaustive task-specific training on large datasets, making them prone to exploiting visual shortcuts and resulting in poor generalization. While neuro-symbolic approaches attempt to bridge this gap by decoupling perception from reasoning with dedicated computation units, existing solutions rely either on explicit rule supervision or parameter-heavy transformer architectures that reintroduce the computational inefficiencies they aim to solve. We introduce Slot-Dwn to address these limitations, a novel neuro-symbolic architecture that unifies unsupervised object-centric perception with logic-based symbolic reasoning. Our model utilizes a Multi-Head Differentiable Weightless Network (MH-DWN) that performs efficient reasoning entirely in Boolean space using look-up tables (LUTs). Our framework reduces visual perception complexity and introduces a LUT-based relational bottleneck with sparse input routing to minimize computational overhead. We evaluate Slot-Dwn across various abstract visual reasoning benchmark tasks targeting systematic rule generalization, sample-efficient anomaly detection, and multi-relational reasoning. We demonstrate that Slot-Dwn yields competitive performance with respect to the state-of-the-art, under extreme out-of-distribution regimes, while requiring up to 52% fewer trainable parameters, with significantly faster convergence.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30736) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30736)

## 66. EgoDepth-Bench: Benchmarking Metric Depth for Egocentric Manipulation

- **作者**：Yuan Gao, Dongchen Wei, Xinyang Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：8 pages, 5 figures, 6 tables. Project page: https://egodepth-bench.github.io/

We introduce EgoDepth-Bench, comprising 941,233 synchronized samples collected across 37 distinct physical environments. Each sample combines six calibrated RGB views with metric depth in the native fisheye view, reconstructed from five active-infrared stereo modules. A calibrated ball-bar confirms millimeter-level geometric accuracy, with a median error of 1.27 mm on a known sphere spacing. Environment-held-out splits enable evaluation of generalization to unseen environments. Across monocular, stereo, and multi-view models, the benchmark measures both depth accuracy and prediction coverage, including on hands and objects. Fine-tuning MapAnything on EgoDepth-Bench reduces metric AbsRel from 0.708 to 0.091 and scale-aligned AbsRel from 0.327 to 0.072 on unseen environments. These results demonstrate that EgoDepth-Bench provides effective supervision and enables rigorous evaluation of metric and relative depth estimation in egocentric manipulation. The project page provides the evaluation code and interactive RGB-depth sequences.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30734) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30734)

## 67. Same Seed, Different Picture? Deciding Early at Low Resolution for Seed-Faithful Few-Step Flow Sampling

- **作者**：Huiran Duan, Weilun Feng, Yuqi Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：43 pages, 27 figures, 38 tables. Project page: https://duanhuiran.github.io/DtR

Few-step samplers make large text-to-image flow models fast, but from the same noise they no longer generate the image of the standard 50-step sampler. Subjects, attributes and layout change, not just fine detail. We trace this loss of seed fidelity to the start of sampling. The coarse part of the initial noise decides coarse structure first, while the noise level is still above 0.9. Few-step samplers cross this range in their first steps, which account for 25-52% of their distance to the 50-step reference. Because this decision is coarse, a half-resolution run from the same coarse noise can make nearly the same decision at a third of the cost. We therefore propose Decide-then-Render (DtR), a training-free sampler that decides the composition in a few low-resolution steps, rebuilds the full-resolution state with the seed's own noise and renders the details. On 4 large models, DtR runs 11-25$\times$ faster than the reference sampler. At each speed-up, it is closer to the reference and rated higher by ImageReward than every compared sampler, including few-step schedules calibrated per budget and published multi-resolution samplers. On FLUX.1-dev, it remains the most faithful sampler down to about $8\times$, ahead of caching accelerators. A user study on this model found DtR's samples closer to the reference in 86% of pairwise trials. DtR transfers to a held-out model and to video, and distilled models can serve as its renderers. Project link: https://duanhuiran.github.io/DtR

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30728) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30728)

## 68. Co-speech Gesture Attribute Annotation with 3D Human Mesh Recovery

- **作者**：Frances Yung, Muhammad Naufilr
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Co-speech gestures convey meaning alongside speech, but annotating their physical attributes in video is labour-intensive. We propose two approaches to automate annotation of ten gesture attributes using human mesh recovery (HMR), which estimates a full 3D body skeleton from a single video frame: AI-assisted geometrical rules and a graph convolutional network (SL-GCN). Both use pose estimates from SAM 3D Body, evaluated on 2,020 gestures from the GESRes dataset. For physically defined attributes such as height, arm swivel and movement speed, both methods achieve agreement with expert annotation comparable to that between human annotators. For attributes whose annotation requires higher-level multimodal contextual inference, performance remains lower. The rule-based approach requires substantially less annotated data, is fully transparent, and can be adapted to different annotation schemes, offering a practical and interpretable complement to data-driven methods

## 69. LiveAudio: From Bidirectional to Autoregressive Diffusion for Real-Time Streaming Video-to-Audio Generation

- **作者**：Yuxuan Jiang, Zehua Chen, Yusheng Dai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.SD, cs.CV, cs.MM, eess.AS
- **备注**：23 pages, 12 tables, 14 figures

Video-to-audio generation synthesizes sound that matches a video in content and timing, with applications ranging from film post-production to live streaming, games and interactive world models. Interactive applications receive video continuously and need the sound to follow it in real time, yet most existing video-to-audio models are bidirectional and must observe an entire clip before producing any sound. We propose LiveAudio, a streaming video-to-audio framework that generates high-quality and synchronized audio in real time while the video is playing. In contrast to prior streaming models trained from scratch, LiveAudio converts a bidirectional model into a streaming generator so as to build on its audio quality and synchronization. LiveAudio generates audio block by block, and each block depends only on the video that has already been played. The streaming generator is obtained from the bidirectional model through progressive causal fine-tuning and distillation, so that each block needs only a few sampling steps. Experiments on VGGSound show that LiveAudio outperforms existing streaming models in audio quality and synchronization, and surpasses the bidirectional model it starts from in synchronization. On a single A100 GPU, it runs in real time with a real-time factor of 0.79 and a time to first frame of 143 ms. Audio samples are available at https://streamaudio.github.io.

## 70. Intra-Class Diversity Loss for Person Re-Identification

- **作者**：Wei Zhou, BaXian Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures, 2 tables

Person re-identification (ReID) aims to retrieve images of the same pedestrian across different cameras. However, the non-overlapping identities between training and testing sets, together with large intra-class variation, make it challenging to learn generalizable pedestrian representations. Most existing ReID loss functions mainly focus on intra-class compactness and inter-class separation, which may overlook useful intra-class differences. To address these issues, we propose a novel Intra-Class Diversity (ICD) loss that aims to maximize intra-class differences in classification-irrelevant directions. To the best of our knowledge, we are the first to introduce this class-weight-orthogonal diversity learning objective for person ReID. Specifically, based on the geometric relationship between the learned representation and its corresponding class-weight vector, we decompose each representation into the component along the class-weight direction and its orthogonal component. This decomposition provides a clear and interpretable geometric basis for modeling intra-class diversity. ICD then encour- ages larger differences among the orthogonal components of samples belonging to the same identity, while the classification loss maintains identity discrimination. The proposed ICD loss can be incorporated into existing ReID frameworks as an additional regularization term without modifying the feature extraction network. Extensive experiments on three widely used ReID datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of ICD and its complementarity with existing metric learning losses.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30707) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30707)

## 71. Speculate in the Right Order: Efficient Self-Speculative Decoding for Autoregressive Image Generation

- **作者**：Yihan Chen, Jintao Zhang, Jun Zhu
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Self-speculative decoding accelerates autoregressive image generation by leveraging the same model for parallel drafting and sequential verification, without requiring an additional draft model. While existing methods primarily focus on improving drafting and verification strategies, recent advances in random-order autoregressive modeling introduce an additional degree of freedom: the generation order. In this work, we theoretically analyze how generation order affects self-speculative decoding efficiency, identifying two key factors: draft-target mismatch, which determines token rejection probabilities, and early-rejection cost, which reflects the higher cost of rejecting tokens earlier in a speculative round. Motivated by these insights, we propose Speculation-Aware Ordering (SAO), a training-free framework for offline optimization of generation orders. Instead of evaluating candidate orders through costly speculative decoding, SAO uses simple pairwise conditional-TV statistics to formulate a rejection-cost-aware objective and efficiently construct speculation-friendly generation orders offline. Extensive experiments across different backbones and speculative widths demonstrate that SAO improves decoding efficiency, with the additional benefit of improved generation quality. Furthermore, SAO provides complementary efficiency gains when combined with existing self-speculative decoding methods. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first work to identify generation order as a new optimization dimension for efficient self-speculative decoding in autoregressive image generation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30704) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30704)

## 72. Optimising VLM Harnesses to Localise Errors in Generated Videos

- **作者**：Ankit Yadav, Nikhil Cherian Kurian, Arpit Garg et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 tables, and 2 figures

Identifying subtle errors in generated videos is an important step toward automated video generation, where errors must be detected to assess and refine generated clips. Recent work shows that pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) can localise such errors, but their effectiveness depends strongly on the workflow used: choices about which frames to inspect, which tools and model calls to make, and how to combine predictions can lead to substantially different results. We study VLM harnesses for video error localisation. Instead of relying on manually designed harnesses, we propose Harness Search and Merge (HSM), an algorithm that automatically constructs them by searching over executable evaluation programs. An LLM orchestrator refines candidate harnesses independently on random training subsets, records findings in a memory, and constructively merges complementary candidates. We evaluate HSM on Spotlight, a recently proposed benchmark for localising errors in generated videos. HSM achieves 0.234 +- 0.012 F1, exceeding the strongest benchmark strategy (0.173 +- 0.016), and retains its lead on an unseen video generator (0.213 +- 0.008 versus 0.140 +- 0.003). On IPV-Bench, it achieves strong localisation performance with lower false-alarm rates than existing optimisation baselines. Together, these results show that searching over complete VLM harnesses can outperform existing evaluation strategies, transfer to an unseen video generator, and reduce false alarms on real videos.

## 73. DriveDreamer-RSI: Recursive Self-Improvement of Driving Perception and Agentic Data Generation

- **作者**：Dingyi Yao, Tingdong Yu, Guosheng Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Controllable generation can expand training data for driving perception, but visual realism alone does not ensure learning gains. Effective generation must adapt both what data to generate and how to construct them as the perception model evolves. In this paper, we present DriveDreamer-RSI, a framework for recursive self-improvement of driving perception and agentic data generation through feedback between perception learning and data construction. Its outer loop implements Gap-Guided Target Adaptation, generating labeled videos from current perception gaps and updating targets after training. Its inner loop uses a vision-language agent to iteratively edit scenes through observation and execution feedback. Across rounds, Learning-Feedback-Driven Strategy Refinement derives reusable construction advice from execution experience and downstream learning outcomes. On the nuScenes validation set, DriveDreamer-RSI surpasses the dataset-replay baseline by 4.09 mAP and 4.13 NDS points using approximately half the synthetic frames.

## 74. Zhengrong: World Models with Pole-Based Action-Response Factorization for Direct Visual Planning

- **作者**：Yu Zhang, Jingrui Li, Congyu Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 4 figures, 11 tables. Technical report, Beijing Freedo Technology Co., Ltd

Direct visual world models predict the effects of candidate action sequences without recursive latent rollout, yet they leave the temporal structure of action effects unspecified. We introduce Zhengrong (Zhengrong-PoleWM), a world model that decomposes a latent trajectory into an initial-condition anchor, a free response, and delayed responses to action innovations. Nonlinear networks map visual history and causal action prefixes to response coefficients. A shared bank of stable conjugate poles determines how each response evolves with elapsed time. This factorization preserves nonlinear interactions across actions, anchors predictions exactly at the initial state, prevents future actions from affecting earlier predictions, and yields bounded responses to finite control plans. We derive an exact re-anchoring identity that identifies the additional basis direction $Ψ_κ(s)$ needed to represent shifted trajectory tails. Across four offline image-goal control benchmarks (Cube, TwoRoom, PushT, and Reacher), Zhengrong-PoleWM achieves $90.83\% \pm 0.47\%$ macro-average success over two training seeds at epoch 20, exceeding the strongest baseline (QQWorld) by 6.08 percentage points. Selecting checkpoints by validation loss yields $91.21\% \pm 1.00\%$. In a controlled first-epoch Cube study without restart regularization, neural and pole response heads outperform a generic direct predictor by 20.67 and 23.34 percentage points, respectively. On an NVIDIA A100 GPU, median latency for a full 30-iteration cross-entropy method search ranges from 74 to 87 ms across the four tasks. The mean of these medians is 77.5 ms, a 15.8% latency reduction relative to Fast-LeWM and a $7.1\times$ speedup over autoregressive LeWM.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30666) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30666)

## 75. WeMM-Ecom: Advancing Multimodal Content Understanding for WeChat E-Commerce

- **作者**：Binhui Xie, Jinmin Li, Haomiao Tang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

We present WeMM-Ecom, a family of multimodal large language models that deliver frontier e-commerce capabilities while achieving state-of-the-art general-purpose performance at their respective scales. The family spans five sizes, from a 0.8B dense model to a 35B mixture-of-experts model with 3B active parameters. Our training pipeline begins with two-stage continued pre-training that progressively raises the proportion of e-commerce data within a diverse multimodal data mixture, followed by hybrid-thinking post-training that combines on-policy distillation and reinforcement learning to support both non-thinking and thinking modes. For evaluation, we construct a comprehensive suite of 28 e-commerce benchmarks (23 in-house and five open-source) covering eight business scenarios and complement it with 64 public general-purpose benchmarks. On the e-commerce suite, WeMM-Ecom-35B-A3B surpasses substantially larger open-source flagship models, including Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, HY4, and DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash. On the general-purpose suite, every WeMM-Ecom model surpasses its similarly sized Qwen3.5 counterpart, with the lightweight variants (e.g., WeMM-Ecom-2B) showing clear advantages over leading lightweight models such as Qwen3.5-2B, MiniCPM-V-4.7-1B, and LFM3-VL-2B. Beyond benchmarks, WeMM-Ecom now powers hundreds of production use cases in WeChat, including catalog management, multimodal product search, and livestream commerce, serving billion-scale requests per day. We publicly release all model weights and detail our data curation, training recipe, and benchmark construction to facilitate future work on domain-specialized multimodal models. We have released the model weights and code to facilitate future research at https://github.com/Tencent/WeMM-Ecom.

## 76. Active Observation for Robust Visual Servoing in Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Zhilun Hu, Yuqi Cheng, Yuxiang Tan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：6 pages, 4 figures, 3 tables. Published in 2026 IEEE 22nd International Conference on Automation Science and Engineering (CASE), pp. 3026-3031

Robust visual servoing during robotic grasping remains challenging, as approach-phase occlusion frequently disrupts closed-loop control. To address this, we present an active observation framework in which one arm of an existing dual-arm platform carries an RGB-D camera as an active observer while the other executes grasping. The observer repositions to maintain or recover a shared target-gripper view within the feasible shared-view workspace. Occlusion during the final approach is particularly acute: fixed eye-to-hand lacks an active recovery mechanism once the target is hidden, while eye-in-hand can suffer from self-occlusion and sensitivity to hand-eye calibration. The observer first uses an active two-phase viewpoint strategy to align high-precision RGB-D axes with task-relevant error components. If visibility later degrades, an occlusion-triggered exploration module pauses the worker arm and scores candidate viewpoints by observation quality to recover a shared target-gripper view. Because both objects are detected in a single RGB-D image, the resulting Relative Servo Error avoids differencing two independently transformed target and gripper estimates. We evaluate the system on a dual 6-DoF Piper platform using an open-vocabulary detector with text prompts, without task-specific fine-tuning or demonstrations. Across 50 randomized trials per object, the system achieves 94% grasp success versus 76% for a reactive viewpoint baseline and 64% for a fixed camera. On triggered severe-occlusion trials, recovery takes $1.8 \pm 0.6$ s on average.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30658) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30658)

## 77. Part-wise Certified State Estimation of Articulated Objects from Passive Stereo

- **作者**：Orion Desbonnet, Kei Higuchi, Alex Sykes et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 4 figures

Robots in manufacturing often handle thin, hinged objects known from CAD models that change shape during the task, like folding cartons. Monitoring such tasks requires estimating the object's articulation state, its pose and the shape of any semi-deformable part. Learning-based estimators generalise across objects but lose precision while handling thin parts. Model-based fitting is precise but often needs accurate depth, a close initial estimate and a clean object mask. We present part-wise certification of an articulated object's state, estimated from passive stereo. Our estimator rivals the precision of the best model-based fitting method without using depth, a close initial estimate or a mask. We detect the corners that the CAD model defines in both stereo images and fit the articulated model to them. Geometric certificates, which we extend from rigid to articulated objects, then tell whether to trust each part, because the fit returns a state even for hidden parts. Certified estimates on unlabelled video become labels that improve sim-to-real transfer. On five of seven simulated objects, we locate the parts we certify three to five times more precisely than the strongest learned baseline on the same readings, given depth and true masks. On a real folding carton, our median corner error on the end panels is 2.1% of the carton's size, at least 40% lower than every learned baseline and also lower than a depth-based fit initialised near the true state.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30646) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30646)

## 78. Beyond Naming Accuracy: Template Composition and Species-Name Decisions

- **作者**：Junle li, Bochang zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：29 pages, 10 figures

Vision-language classifiers identify species by matching images to text prompts containing species names. BioCLIP2 changes its predicted species less often than BioCLIP 2.5 Huge across four photographic templates, but the ranking reverses across a bank of 80 templates. This comparison leaves open whether the reversal depends on the number of templates or their wording. We hold the count at four and compare the classifiers on every four-template subset of the 80-template bank and of a second pool of 24 reviewed photographic templates, keeping images and candidate names fixed. Huge is more consistent across the reviewed pool as a whole, yet 19.09% of its four-template subsets favor Bio-CLIP2 on iWildCam and 5.10% do so on Rare Species. We then test whether both rankings hold on 5,487 frames from previously unused iWildCam sequences. Among 4,356 subsets containing two photo and two photograph templates, simultaneous 95% intervals support lower disagreement for Huge in 1,526 subsets and for BioCLIP2 in 43. Even with the same prompt count and noun mixture, the choice of sentences can determine which classifier appears more consistent.

## 79. Motion on Demand: Adaptive Optical Excitation for Event Cameras

- **作者**：Yingtao Li, Chaoran Feng, Haoran Jiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project Page: https://passwerob.github.io/EvGaze/

Event cameras respond only to brightness changes, so whether an edge is visible depends on image motion perpendicular to it---a motion that constantly shifts with camera and scene dynamics. Rotating a wedge prism in front of the lens introduces controlled image motion to reveal static or slow edges. However, a fixed rotation speed cannot adapt to changing motion conditions: when natural motion is already sufficient, additional optical excitation generates redundant events and introduces unnecessary alignment errors. Inspired by the division of labor between fast haltere feedforward and slower visual feedback in fly gaze control, we present EvGaze, an event camera system that adaptively regulates prism rotation speed. EvGaze employs a learned multimodal controller that integrates luminance, event, and inertial observations, with a fast inertial pathway that refines speed decisions between visual updates. To preserve geometric consistency during speed changes, we compensate for prism-induced image motion at individual event timestamps using measured encoder angles. On EvMotion, EvGaze recovers more edges than full-speed prism rotation with 39% fewer events. On DSEC with simulated optical modulation, it reaches 0.73 feature-tracking success (compared with 0.55 on native events and 0.67 at 180 RPM) while generating 26% fewer events than 360 RPM. Real-device experiments further demonstrate online closed-loop adaptation, reducing average event rates by 17% relative to full-speed rotation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30641) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30641)

## 80. SWEEP-Bench: Do Embodied Policies Act on the Speed They See?

- **作者**：Hiroaki Honda, Koya Sakamoto, Shu Morikuni et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：28 pages, 24 figures. Project page: https://damin3927.github.io/SWEEP-Bench

Robot policies must adapt to the speed of moving objects, yet existing benchmarks rarely measure this ability across diverse tasks and speeds. We introduce SWEEP-Bench, a simulation benchmark with 80 conveyor-belt manipulation tasks spanning 16 skill categories, each evaluated at 13 belt speeds (0.10-0.40 m/s). Objects are transported through physical contact with a visibly moving belt. Each task has a static counterpart, and each evaluation episode uses the same randomized scene at all 13 speeds. We fine-tune eight vision-language-action and world-action models on demonstrations spanning the full speed range, comparing observations from the current frame alone with those including two past frames. For six models, the success rate peaks at intermediate speeds, and their failures indicate that they tend to produce actions suited to the average demonstration speed rather than to the actual one. Past frames raise the success rate by 10-40 percentage points for three models, and linear probes show that the belt speed is also most linearly decodable from these three. Adding ground-truth speed input to four of the models improves all of them, and a speed estimated from the images keeps most of this gain. These results show that SWEEP-Bench exposes speed-dependent failures and suggest that past frames help only policies from which the speed is most decodable, such as those pretrained on multiple frames. Project page: https://damin3927.github.io/SWEEP-Bench

## 81. Auxiliary Clues Aware's Geometry Problem Solving

- **作者**：Qigong Lei, Qingsong Wang, Wang Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：Accepted by NeurIPS2026

Geometry Problem Solving (GPS) serves as a rigorous test of the complex reasoning capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), particularly when solutions require auxiliary constructions absent from the input diagram. However, explicit diagramming methods suffer from unstable image generation quality and cascading errors, while existing implicit attention mechanisms primarily focus on visible elements, limiting their ability to support reasoning that requires additional geometric constructions. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{AuxCA} (Auxiliary-Clues-Aware GRPO), a reinforcement learning framework that helps models learn transferable auxiliary-construction strategies that can be applied beyond the training domain without explicit diagram generation at inference time. Specifically, we introduce Auxiliary Clues Dependency (\textbf{ACD}) to quantify the influence of auxiliary visual clues on token generation and integrate it into GRPO through fine-grained advantage weighting. Furthermore, we incorporate an auxiliary incentive reward to encourage the effective use of auxiliary geometric clues in correct solutions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AuxCA-8B outperforms the explicit-plotting baseline Bagel-Canvas and achieves competitive performance with leading proprietary models on auxiliary-dependent plane geometry problems. Improvements on analytic geometry, solid geometry, and external benchmarks further suggest that the learned strategies can transfer beyond the training domain. Code is available at https://github.com/fukua23/AuxCA

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30617) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30617)

## 82. CineAct: Interaction-State Agents for Playable, Branching Film Generation

- **作者**：Junyan Lin, Jianqi Ma, Shujie Duan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Open video generators such as MiniMax-H3 produce short clips of only several to a dozen seconds per run. To synthesize longer sequences, existing methods either rely on agentic systems following preset scripts, or roll out autoregressively from the previous clip's last frame, losing off-screen states and accumulating hallucinations across turns. Consequently, users still cannot take an explicit goal, choose how to accomplish it, and see that choice persist across subsequent clips. We study goal-grounded interactive video generation, where one goal admits multiple valid approaches, the user's chosen approach must be executed on screen, and its consequences must persist across clips. To this end, we propose CineAct, a state-first agentic framework in which language-model agents handle reasoning while frozen image and video generators serve purely as renderers. Before interaction, the Architect initializes the world as a spatial layout with cameras, an asset library, and a scene graph; at each turn, the system deduces the planned state change, generates a target end frame with an image generator and rigorously verifies it, and synthesizes intermediate motion with an FL2V model. We further introduce a multi-space benchmark covering two suites: long-horizon step execution and open-intent branching admissibility. Experiments show that explicit world-state modeling boosts multi-step rollout consistency over the pixel-only baseline (+11.4 automated, +23.1 human points) and raises paired branching success toward a shared goal by +30.8 points.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30613) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30613)

## 83. Tri-DNO: Trilateral Diffusion Noise Optimization for 3D Human Object Interaction

- **作者**：Paul Hinzer, Niklas Gard, Anna Hilsmann et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR

Generating realistic 3D human object interactions (HOI) is essential for applications, such as augmented or virtual reality (AR/VR) or character animation of interactive entertainment. A particular challenge lies in the faithful generation of hand grasps in human object interaction scenes, where even minor geometric inconsistencies, such as insufficient contact or interpenetration, can significantly compromise realism. While diffusion-based generative models have shown promising results in HOI synthesis, ensuring physically plausible hand-object interactions remains challenging. We present Tri-DNO, a diffusion noise optimization strategy to sample from a pretrained multimodal HOI diffusion model, improving hand-object contact. Rather than modifying or retraining the generative model, our approach iteratively optimizes the initial noise of the diffusion generator, thereby generating firm hand object contact, while mitigating penetration and keeping the hand position realistic. Experiments on the GRAB dataset demonstrate improvements over the baseline in grasp quality without additional model training or increased computational cost compared to existing methods. We further demonstrate the applicability of our approach to practical media production scenarios.

## 84. Towards an Armenian Epigraphic Corpus

- **作者**：Chahan Vidal-Gor{è}ne, Arpine Avetisyan, Edita Matevosyan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

The Divan hay vimagrut'yan (1960-2017), the printed corpus of Armenian inscriptions, comprises nearly 7,600 records; a growing number of the inscriptions they describe, damaged or destroyed in recent decades, survive only in its pages. Armenian epigraphy nevertheless lacks a digital resource of the kind available for Greek, Latin or Georgian. This paper presents the Corpus Inscriptionum Armenicarum Electronicum, which turns the ten volumes of the Divan into an open digital corpus. Layout detection, OCR and a vision-language model produce a first structured reading of each record, which specialists then check on a proofreading platform. First corrections show that letters are read reliably and that residual errors concentrate in the editorial signs. The corpus is designed as a lasting platform for documenting Armenian inscriptions and as a source of silver data in Classical and Middle Armenian for language models and computational linguistics.

## 85. GSDet: Gaussian Splatting for Oriented Object Detection

- **作者**：Zeyu Ding, Jiaqi Zhao, Yong Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：IJCAI 2025

Oriented object detection has advanced with the development of convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and transformers. However, modern detectors still rely on predefined object candidates, such as anchors in CNN-based methods or queries in transformer-based methods, which struggle to capture spatial information effectively. To address the limitations, we propose GSDet, a novel framework that formulates oriented object detection as Gaussian splatting. Specifically, our approach performs detection within a 3D feature space constructed from image features, where 3D Gaussians are employed to represent oriented objects. These 3D Gaussians are projected onto the image plane to form 2D Gaussians, which are then transformed into oriented boxes. Furthermore, we optimize the mean, anisotropic covariance, and confidence scores of these randomly initialized 3D Gaussians, using a decoder that incorporates 3D Gaussian sampling. Moreover, our method exhibits flexibility, enabling adaptive control and a dynamic number of Gaussians during inference. Experiments on 3 datasets indicate that GSDet achieves AP$_{50}$ gains of 0.7\% on DIOR-R, 0.3\% on DOTA-v1.0, and 0.55\% on DOTA-v1.5 when evaluated with adaptive control and outperforms mainstream detectors.

## 86. ViDiHand2: Unifying Video Diffusion Priors and Hand Geometry for 3D Hand Motion Reconstruction in the Wild

- **作者**：Yuxi Wang, Yufei Liu, Chengkai Jin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Main text 8 pages with supplementary material, 54 pages in total

Learning robot manipulation from egocentric human videos requires accurate metric 3D hand motion. Recent methods leverage pretrained video diffusion model (VDM) priors to recover occluded hands directly from full-frame videos, but their appearance-based depth regression generalizes poorly to unseen cameras. In this work, we propose ViDiHand2, a geometry-driven framework that reformulates VDM-based hand depth estimation from direct regression into fine-grained geometric constraint solving. Specifically, we introduce Fine-Grained Geometric Lifting and Clip-Level Ray Fitting, which recover metric depth by enforcing geometric consistency between pixel-aligned 2D joints and the metric structure of MANO hands, while leveraging temporal observations across the clip. Since the lifting relies on these 2D joints, to improve robustness under occlusion and image truncation, we further develop Confidence-Gated Fusion to adaptively combine geometrically lifted joints with MANO predictions according to localization confidence. Finally, we enhance the VDM backbone to better capture spatial-temporal representations and produce the per-frame 2D joint and MANO predictions that both steps rely on. Extensive experiments on seven public datasets demonstrate that ViDiHand2 consistently outperforms 18 baselines, reducing in-the-wild absolute joint error by 42% and detection failures by 8.82x compared with the best baseline on each metric, while running at 57 FPS. The code is available at https://github.com/NTUYWANG103/ViDiHand.

## 87. Advancements in Diabetic Retinopathy Classification Using Novel Multi-Stacked U-Net Architectures

- **作者**：Ameya Uppina, S Navaneetha Krishnan, Talluri Krishna Sai Teja et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Diabetes leads to several health complications throughout the body, with the complication in the eyes being Diabetic Retinopathy. It leads to damaging or abnormal changes in the blood vessels' structure, causing vision loss. The need for massive screening of a large population of diabetic patients has created a necessity for computer-aided, fully automatic diagnosis of DR. CNNs have shown great promise in the classification of DR based on the Fundus images. Building a reliable model faces considerable challenges due to the limited availability of high-quality annotated datasets and issues with varying image quality and class imbalances. This paper aims to demonstrate the UNET, and its Stacked Architecture variant and their performance across several datasets. This paper has compared and studied the UNET architecture's performance across multiple stacks and has come to a soft conclusion that the architecture performs its best at three stacks, and any more leads to deterioration of its performance. The model aims at classifying the images into five categories, 0 to 4, where 0 is no DR, and 4 is proliferative DR.
