## 88. HOSE: Hierarchical Orthogonal Subspace Erasure for Concept Unlearning in Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Xiaoqi Wei, Li Li, Kejiang Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Text-to-image diffusion models can generate harmful or sensitive content, such as pornographic or violent imagery, raising serious security concerns. Model editing has recently emerged as an efficient approach to mitigate this issue. It modifies pretrained models with minimal parameter updates to alter specific concepts while preserving overall model capabilities. However, due to the entangled representations of different concepts in diffusion models, direct modifications inevitably disrupt non-target concepts. Meanwhile, U-Net's hierarchical heterogeneity hinders unified strategies from balancing erasure and quality. To address these issues, we propose Hierarchical Orthogonal Subspace Erasure (HOSE). This method projects parameter updates into the subspace orthogonal to general knowledge, thereby eliminating interference with non-target concepts. Considering the hierarchical heterogeneity of the U-Net architecture, we further propose a hierarchical adaptive mechanism, constructing differentiated subspace projections for different layers to precisely adapt to the model's hierarchical structure, thereby resolving the inherent trade-off between erasure and image quality in unified constraints. Experimental results demonstrate the proposed method reduces harmful content generation by 80% compared with the unedited model. While it inevitably incurs some image quality degradation, it still outperforms other editing-based baselines in terms of quality preservation, and achieves a significantly better trade-off between harmful content suppression and quality loss than these baselines. Notably, our approach demonstrates higher adversarial robustness than existing SOTA method, indicating that the target concepts have been more thoroughly erased. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/sevenqiiu/HOSE.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30592) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30592)

## 89. DARE: Dual-Level Adversarial Learning with Domain-Aware Regularization for Whole Slide Image Classification

- **作者**：Yuxuan Jiang, Haichuan Dong, Manning Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. 22 pages, 7 figures, including the appendix

Pathological whole slide images (WSIs) exhibit substantial domain shifts across hospitals due to variations in scanning devices, staining protocols, and tissue preparation procedures, leading to significant performance degradation when models are applied to data from different sources. Although unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA) has shown strong performance in transferring models from a labeled source domain to an unlabeled target domain in natural images, it remains relatively underexplored in computational pathology. The ultra-high resolution of WSIs and their pronounced inter-slide and intra-slide heterogeneity pose unique challenges for domain adaptation, often rendering existing UDA methods unstable and susceptible to negative transfer. To address these challenges, we propose Dual-level Adversarial learning with domain-aware REgularization (DARE), a plug-and-play UDA framework for WSI classification. DARE incorporates adaptive pseudo-labeling within a teacher-student architecture to provide stable supervision for the target domain, alongside adversarial alignment of both patch-level and slide-level embeddings. Together, these components enable the joint optimization of semantic representations at both levels for cross-domain alignment. We further introduce domain-aware attention masking to regularize attention and improve cross-domain generalization. Extensive experiments on four public WSI datasets demonstrate that DARE consistently outperforms state-of-the-art approaches across various domain shifts.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30589) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30589)

## 90. Foundation Model and Radiomics Distance Scores for Post-Hoc Segmentation Failure Detection

- **作者**：Simon Johannes Joham, Gianluca Guglielmo, Mateusz Kozinski et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at the UNSURE MICCAI 2026 Workshop

Medical image segmentation models can fail silently when clinical data differs from training data. Deployed models rarely ship with quality control, and are often black boxes. Prior methods that work in this post-hoc failure detection setting still need a separate regression or segmentation model, registration, or access to model internals. We instead propose two failure scores based on the Mahalanobis distance to a labeled reference set. The first uses radiomic shape features of the predicted mask. The second uses frozen foundation model (FM) features, made mask-aware by overlaying the mask on the image, encoding it with a frozen DINOv3, and pooling patch features with more weight on the mask region. We test on multi-site prostate and hippocampus MRI under imaging-protocol and population shift. Ranking cases by predicted failure risk within each site, both scores are competitive with the registration-based (Atlas-RCA) and ensemble-based (pairwise Dice) baselines, without registration that can fail silently or access to individual predictions. Our results show that pooling all test cases into one ranking, as prior work does, rewards ordering sites by difficulty, so a detector can rank sites well, miss failures within them, and still look strong. Within-site evaluation measures true per-case failure. The two evaluation schemes reward different behavior, so we recommend reporting both. The code is available at https://github.com/imigraz/fm-radiomics-failure-detection.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30582) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30582)

## 91. A Search Worth a Billion Pixels: Explaining Gigapixel Classifiers

- **作者**：Danilo Danese, Angela Lombardi, Tommaso Di Noia
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Perturbation-based attribution methods explain model predictions without accessing their internal representations, but scaling them to high-resolution images requires balancing perturbation granularity and computational cost. Fine-grained perturbations require many model evaluations, whereas coarse masks may overlook small but relevant evidence. We introduce Wavelet Find-and-Refine (WaFR), a model-agnostic attribution method combining adaptive hierarchical search with sparse multiscale regression. By selectively refining regions that influence the prediction, WaFR maintains fine-grained localisation with a computational cost that depends on the spatial distribution of the evidence. As image resolution increases to 8,192 pixels per side, WaFR maintains a Pointing Game score of at least .995 in controlled localisation experiments, including at matched forward-pass budgets. On gigapixel whole-slide images, it requires a median of 176 forward passes and achieves the highest mean patch-level AUROC among the evaluated perturbation-based methods (.949).

## 92. Write, Evolve, Read: Streaming Cameras into a Persistent World State for 4D Occupancy Forecasting

- **作者**：ZiAn Wang, Tianyi Wang, Zhihao Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

4D occupancy forecasting predicts the 3D semantic occupancy of a driving scene seconds ahead. Over a few seconds, the scene changes little while the vehicle moves, so most of the space ahead has already been seen. Sorting the future into unchanged, changed, and unseen space, we find that current camera-based world models fall short on the unchanged part: they fail to hold on to what they have already seen, rather than to forecast what will change. We therefore make what the model has seen its state. PersistWorld streams camera frames into a persistent 3D world state anchored in the scene, writing each frame once instead of rebuilding the state from past frames. The state is learned end to end with the forecast, and an evolver predicts it at any queried time along the ego trajectory. On Occ3D-nuScenes, PersistWorld raises the IoU of the best camera-based world model by 19 points at 1-3 s and by 18 to 24 points up to 8 s, while running more than twice as fast. Code and models: https://github.com/wangzian828/PersistWorld. Project page: https://wangzian828.github.io/PersistWorld

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30573) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30573)

## 93. Reliable Recursive Memory for Causal Multi-scale Video Polyp Segmentation

- **作者**：Tong Wang, Wenbo Peng, Yaolei Qi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages, 9 figures, 9 tables. Extended version of CMSA-Net (MICCAI 2026). Submitted to Medical Image Analysis

Video polyp segmentation (VPS) is essential for computer-aided colonoscopy, as it localizes and tracks polyps frame by frame. However, VPS remains challenging because polyps resemble the mucosa and change quickly in scale, which calls for history beyond a short clip, and because blur, specular highlights, and occlusion often degrade only part of a frame. Existing methods keep history as whole frames, so their cost grows with the history length, and a partly degraded frame carries its degraded content to later predictions. To address these issues, we propose STREAM, a streaming framework for VPS that extends the causal multi-scale aggregation (CMA) of our conference work from a fixed clip to an online stream. STREAM introduces a Recursive Slot Memory (RSM) that summarizes multi-scale features and soft foreground and background cues into a constant number of slots, which the current features read and pass to CMA, supplying long-range history at a constant cost. Furthermore, we design Reliability-Aware Slot Writing (RASW), which estimates a pixel-level quality map from the current frame, supervised by its local error, and uses it to control which locations are written into RSM and how much each slot is updated. Experiments on SUN-SEG and CVC-ClinicDB demonstrate that STREAM achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving the Dice of the conference model from 81.3% to 85.7% on the hardest subset while running at 48 FPS with a constant state of 0.32 MB. Code will be released at https://github.com/wangtong627/STREAM.

## 94. Seeing Is Locating: Spatial Awareness and Geographic Bias in Decision Language Models

- **作者**：Wilson Wongso, Hada Melino Muhammad, Xiachong Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL

Decision models, which output probability distributions over candidate labels instead of generating text, are increasingly used as classifiers and evaluators for inputs associated with real-world locations. However, it remains unclear whether they can leverage spatial context and whether their predictions are geographically biased. We introduce GeoDecisions, a multimodal benchmark for evaluating the spatial awareness and geographic bias of decision models. GeoDecisions comprises two tasks: (1) geo-aware image classification, and (2) location rating on objective and subjective topics. Evaluating six decision models against supervised task-specific models, we find that spatial awareness requires vision: textual coordinates alone provide insufficient context for classifying everyday photos and satellite images. We also find substantial geographic bias. On everyday photos, despite achieving higher accuracy, open-weight decision models exhibit more spatially clustered errors than supervised models. Moreover, across all five subjective topics, every decision model gives lower ratings to locations with lower socioeconomic conditions. Finally, we propose a training-free method that combines decision models with a location prior to improve their spatial awareness without substantially increasing performance bias. Our code is available at https://github.com/cruiseresearchgroup/GeoDecisions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30564) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30564)

## 95. JONES-19: A Cultural Image Dataset Based on The Grammar of Ornament

- **作者**：Linh Pham, Stuart Shieber, David Alvarez-Melis et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：13 pages. Published in the Proceedings of the 22nd International Conference on Culture and Computer Science (KUI '25), Berlin, Germany, September 2025. Dataset available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/harvardseas-cultural-ornaments/JONES-19

We introduce JONES-19, a high-quality image dataset documenting 1,901 ornament designs belonging to nineteen human cultures. The images and their annotations are based on an open access archive of The Grammar of Ornament (London, 1856), by Owen Jones. The dataset poses numerous challenges as a benchmark for computer vision classification tasks and for research at the intersection of machine learning and art and design: a small sample size, image samples of human-designed artifacts rather than common objects in-context or natural scenes, imbalanced class distribution, and image distinctions based on fine details involving line patterns, reliefs, and colors. As a design-inspired dataset, JONES-19 can serve as a benchmark for various research fields, such as visual recognition, data-efficient learning, art-historical research, architectural style analysis, and cultural heritage. This paper describes the curation of the JONES-19 dataset and reports a baseline classification benchmark that evaluates the suitability of the dataset for training classifiers and exposes insights into inter-class relationships--particularly cultural similarities--by examining patterns in misclassification errors. The dataset and its accompanying documentation are available at: https://huggingface.co/datasets/harvardseas-cultural-ornaments/JONES-19.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30558) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30558)

## 96. RAPID: Codec-Aware Video Decoding for Efficient Long-Video Understanding

- **作者**：Yuna Lee, Yulhwa Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.MM
- **备注**：16 pages, 16 figures

Recent vision-language models (VLMs) can process hour-long videos, but the large number of input frames incurs substantial inference overhead. Existing approaches to efficient long-video understanding largely overlook video decoding, which can take longer than LLM prefill. We propose RAPID (Reference- and Access-Point-Informed Decoding), a codec-aware framework that exploits information embedded in compressed bitstreams to reduce both frame decoding and selection costs. RAPID consists of two components. RAPID-Dec exploits reference-frame information and random access points to skip unnecessary frames and decode the retained frames in parallel while preserving exact reconstruction of the target frames. It achieves up to a 3.7x decoding speedup over the fastest baseline on H.264/AVC videos. RAPID-Sel uses keyframes as coarse representatives of video segments, avoiding the dense candidate decoding and scoring required by existing frame selection methods. It achieves comparable accuracy with up to a 3.0x preprocessing speedup. Together, RAPID achieves a 4.9x time to first token (TTFT) speedup on hour-long videos over a state-of-the-art frame selection method using the fastest baseline decoder.

## 97. SVUBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Proactive Streaming Video Understanding

- **作者**：Yuxuan Liang, Bohan Zhang, Haiyang Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Proactive streaming video understanding requires models to interpret unfolding events, respond timely, and retrieve relevant historical evidence. To systematically evaluate these capabilities, we introduce SVUBench, a comprehensive proactive streaming video benchmark comprising 12,131 questions and 62,881 answer points across ten proactive tasks spanning Perception, Understanding, and Reasoning. Constructed through model-assisted annotation and human verification, SVUBench introduces two complementary interaction modes. Basic evaluates sustained proactive responses from video onset, while Trajectory requires historical recovery and continuous response updates following task activation during streaming. A Duration-Scaled Challenge further evaluates these capabilities across four video duration tiers. We develop the Proactive Response Score (PRS) with task-adaptive answer windows to jointly assess answer correctness, response timeliness, historical consistency, and content quality. Extensive experiments and analysis reveal the strengths and limitations of existing models, highlighting directions for advancing proactive streaming video understanding.

## 98. Where Compositionality Hides: Shared-Content Interference in Vision-Language Retrieval

- **作者**：Chimata Anudeep, Harsh Vardhan Poddar
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 7 figures, 10 tables. Code in ancillary files

Paired compositional benchmarks such as Winoground ask each image to prefer its matching caption and each caption to prefer its matching image. A failed Group decision can come from a wrong caption preference or from the cross-image comparison alone, and Group Score merges the two. Choosing between retraining the encoder and changing the scoring rule therefore requires separating them. We study mean-erasure, which projects each frozen image embedding away from the mean of the two normalized candidate captions. We prove that the projection preserves every within-image caption ranking and makes Group Score equal baseline Text Score. On Winoground, mean-erasure raises CLIP ViT-B/32 Group Score from 9% to 31%, and five CLIP and SigLIP checkpoints gain 21 to 27 percentage points. ColorSwap shows a similar gain for color-object binding. A score decomposition shows that about 60% of Image failures already score the correct joint assignment above the swapped one and fail only on an offset between the two images. Fixed corrections and a learned bilinear scorer gain under one point. A direction estimated from retrieved captions gains about five points with two captions and falls below baseline with sixteen. Before retraining an encoder for paired retrieval, measure the Group loss that mean-erasure recovers: gains beyond baseline Text Score require changing the caption decisions themselves.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30538) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30538)

## 99. DelayOPSD: On-Policy Self-Distillation from a Synchronous Teacher for Real-time Asynchronous Policies

- **作者**：Hyeonjae Kim, Jeongsol Kim, Sunwoo Park et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：24 pages. Code and videos: https://github.com/present091/DelayOPSD

Flow-based vision-language-action (VLA) policies incur substantial inference latency due to their large vision-language backbones and iterative denoising. Asynchronous execution reduces this latency by generating the next action chunk while executing the current one, but the new chunk is conditioned on a stale observation, degrading performance as the delay increases. We investigate this mismatch by comparing actions predicted from observations at planning and execution times. We find that these predictions differ little on expert demonstrations but diverge substantially on the policy's own delayed rollouts, particularly in failed episodes. Motivated by this observation, we propose DelayOPSD, which trains an asynchronous policy to imitate its synchronous counterpart on the policy's own rollouts. Specifically, a synchronous teacher, obtained from the same policy using the observation at execution time, supervises a delayed student that observes only the earlier frame and the actions still being executed. The student learns to match the teacher throughout its own denoising process. Under a 10-step delay, training SmolVLA on demonstrations achieves only 70.2% success, comparable to naive asynchronous execution (70.4%), whereas training on its own rollouts improves success to 78.7%. Across LIBERO and Kinetix, DelayOPSD achieves the highest average success among the compared methods, without introducing additional modules at deployment. On a real robot, it further improves success from 46% to 62%.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30534) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30534)

## 100. BEND: Spectral Bregman Losses for Multi-Image Unlearning in Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Mohamed-Hicham Leghettas, Markus Püschel
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：25 pages, 8 figures, 22 tables

Diffusion models memorize training images and reproduce them almost verbatim when prompted, creating copyright and privacy liabilities. Data unlearning erases the influence of specific training samples from a trained model without retraining, and is two-sided: it must forget the targets while preserving generation quality on everything else. Prior methods use the squared-L2 diffusion loss, at most with a hand-set frequency mask; however, it is known that every Bregman divergence is a mathematically valid choice and the squared-L2 is only the simplest example. BEND is the first to search this family: it fits a spectral Bregman divergence to the unlearning task through a bilevel loop, so the loss itself is shaped to forget thoroughly while protecting quality. It also redirects a memorized image to the mean of its retain images, where ReTrack, the state of the art, redirects it to a single near-copy. On Stable Diffusion, a single BEND model erases all 48 verifiably memorized benchmark images at once. It forgets at least as much as per-target, sequential, and jointly trained ReTrack (SSCD 0.084 versus 0.094-0.120), at the quality of the model before unlearning (CLIP-IQA 0.81 versus 0.64-0.69). BEND also outperforms the state of the art on CelebA-HQ in all three measures of the benchmark, and matches or exceeds it on MNIST and CIFAR-10.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30532) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30532)

## 101. ReDiffDet: Rotation-equivariant Diffusion Model for Oriented Object Detection

- **作者**：Zeyu Ding, Yong Zhou, Jiaqi Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：CVPR 2025

The diffusion model has been successfully applied to various detection tasks. However, it still faces several challenges when used for oriented object detection: objects that are arbitrarily rotated require the diffusion model to encode their orientation information; uncontrollable random boxes inaccurately locate objects with dense arrangements and extreme aspect ratios; oriented boxes result in the misalignment between them and image features. To overcome these limitations, we propose ReDiffDet, a framework that formulates oriented object detection as a rotation-equivariant denoising diffusion process. First, we represent an oriented box as a 2D Gaussian distribution, forming the basis of the denoising paradigm. The reverse process can be proven to be rotation-equivariant within this representation and model framework. Second, we design a conditional encoder with conditional boxes to prevent boxes from being randomly placed across the entire image. Third, we propose an aligned decoder for alignment between oriented boxes and image features. The extensive experiments demonstrate ReDiffDet achieves promising performance and significantly outperforms the diffusion model baseline.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30531) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30531)

## 102. Assembly-JEPA: Using World Models for Fine-Grained Industrial Image Verification

- **作者**：Michael Hollmer, Andreas Fischer
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Automated verification of industrial assembly processes remains a challenging problem due to substantial visual variability, limited availability of labeled defect data, and the fine-grained nature of many assembly errors. Existing approaches often rely on object detection, CAD models, or supervised classification pipelines, which can struggle to generalize across changing products and assembly configurations. In this work, we propose a novel self-supervised assembly verification framework based on Joint Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs). Starting from the JEPA-based world model LeWorldModel, we investigate how the architecture must be adapted to verify the correctness of fine-grained assembly operations. To this end, we formulate assembly verification as a predictive world modeling task operating on before-and-after observations of assembly processes. Experimental results on industrial assembly tasks demonstrate that the proposed approach reliably detects both fine-grained incorrect assembly states and action-state mismatches, achieving an AUROC of 86.9% on a newly introduced assembly verification dataset. These results indicate that JEPA-based predictive representations constitute a promising foundation for robust and data-efficient industrial assembly verification.

## 103. VeriOrigin: A Proactive Image Protection Framework for Provenance, Tamper Localization, and Lossless Recovery

- **作者**：Jiale Chen, Wei William Wang, Junnan Yin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Versatile watermarking, which seamlessly integrates provenance and tamper detection, has recently attracted widespread attention. In this paper, we propose \textbf{VeriOrigin}, a comprehensive and unified image protection framework that not only robustly achieves provenance and highly precise tamper localization under various complex distortions, but also guarantees perfect recovery of the original cover in lossless channels. To realize this framework, we depart from conventional flow based architectures. Instead, we design \textbf{XOnet}, a CNN based invertible encoder. XOnet comprises two standard CNN based watermarking sub networks, Xnet and Onet, which achieve reversibility through cross residual prediction. Based on XOnet, we train the proactive protection scheme via a two stage strategy: first optimizing the watermarking branch, followed by the tamper localization branch. During inference, the cover image undergoes sequential watermarking and tamper residual embedding. Extensive experimental results on four datasets demonstrate that VeriOrigin achieves precise tamper localization and strong robustness, while guaranteeing lossless cover recovery for integrity authentication. Project page is available at https://veriorigin.github.io/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30523) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30523)

## 104. GeoRead3D: Grounding Part Descriptions for 3D Pointing and Reorientation

- **作者**：Zhangcheng Hou, Kevin Tonkich, Tomoaki Ohtsuki
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 3 figures, 5 tables

Language often refers to an object by its parts, as in asking to point to a part or to turn an arbitrarily posed object so that two named parts lie in given directions. Both tasks need the direction from the object's center to the part's center, not the part's own orientation. A natural approach segments the part and takes the centroid of its mask. However, high mask overlap predicts the pointing direction poorly when a part is spread out relative to its distance from the object center. Language-guided part segmentation is trained and evaluated by mask overlap, which leaves a gap between the masks it produces and the part geometry that these tasks use. We close this gap by learning point evidence, weights over the observed points whose weighted centroid gives the part's location. Geometric Readout Supervision (GRS) trains these weights through the centroid's position and adds direction supervision on the parts used in reorientation commands. GRS improves both tasks even with a single frozen encoder and a single input pose. We implement this supervision in GeoRead3D, a system for pointing and reorientation from part descriptions. A language model maps each description to a known part name. Dual-Route Part Evidence combines refined segmentation evidence with directly predicted point evidence to locate each part. On PartNeXt and Artiverse, GeoRead3D points within $22.5^\circ$ for 74.1% and 60.0% of queries, 5.8 and 7.3 percentage points above the strongest baselines. Its reorientation success exceeds the strongest baselines by 10.4 and 7.8 points.

## 105. Field-Level Crop Classification via Similarity-Induced Supervision from Imperfect Labels

- **作者**：Raul Lomonte Figueiredo, João Rebouças Dorea, Dário Augusto Borges Oliveira
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：38 pages, 21 figures, 8 tables

Regional-scale crop mapping is critical for food production monitoring, yet remains challenging in tropical regions, where persistent cloud cover limits optical imagery. Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) enables continuous observation throughout the agricultural cycle, but a second obstacle is label quality: national-scale land cover products exhibit systematic biases that underestimate less prevalent crops, and training directly on them inherits and amplifies their errors. This work proposes a methodology to build large-scale crop classification models from imperfect public land cover products, requiring no new field campaign, ground survey, or manual label correction. The approach combines two mechanisms. The first reduces the semantic inconsistency of the labels by shifting supervision from the sample level to the group level: a similarity-based clustering model organizes SAR time series into coherent groups, and noisy labels are used only to assign each group its most representative class. The resulting clusters are highly pure with respect to the reference classes (89.0% and 91.2% in the two study regions). The second reduces the spatial inconsistency of the predictions: a segmentation model delineates crop field boundaries from optical imagery, and patch-level predictions are consolidated by majority voting within each field, yielding spatially coherent maps. Evaluated in two Brazilian municipalities, Campo Verde (Mato Grosso) and Luís Eduardo Magalhães (Bahia), the approach consistently outperforms the noisy baseline in mean class accuracy, with gains of up to 10.14 percentage points, especially for minority crops. A state-scale analysis across Mato Grosso and Bahia shows stable behavior without further model adjustment, and the derived municipal crop areas are consistent with official IBGE/PAM statistics, an independent estimate the model never observes.

## 106. When Do Spatial Plans Help Text-to-Image Generation? Causal Control, System Utility, and Text Responsiveness

- **作者**：Lu Han, Jingyao Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A generator can follow a spatial plan, but the benefit of supplying that plan depends on what the model can already do from text. In a controlled study with 18 paired training seeds, correcting predicted positions and sizes improves spatial intersection-over-union by 0.030, yet direct text is non-inferior at matched training compute. In a pretrained generator, a fixed rule layout improves two-object task success over the same text-only adapter by 12.50 percentage points, mostly by separating objects that already appear in the correct order. We then ask whether a more precise instruction can achieve the same separation across conditioning paths. On 288 new task families, adding a minimum-gap instruction improves success by 37.15 points with the text-only adapter, by at most 0.69 points with widely spaced regions, and by 6.94 points with tightly spaced regions. Under tight regions, 230 of 288 baseline images have the correct objects and direction yet miss the required gap, ruling out a high baseline success rate as the sole explanation for the weak text response.

## 107. EgoStudy: How Does Scaling Egocentric Data Affect VLA and WAM Pretraining?

- **作者**：Puzhen Yuan, Yanjiang Guo, Yichen Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project Page: https://puzhenyuan.github.io/EgoStudy-website

Egocentric human manipulation data offer a scalable resource for training dexterous robot policies. However, how effectively robots can learn and benefit from these data remains unclear due to the embodiment gap between humans and robots. We present EgoStudy, a systematic, controlled study of egocentric data for robot learning. Holding the robot dataset fixed, we vary the scale and diversity of egocentric data, data processing strategies, and co-training configurations. We investigate three questions: (1) Which data processing and pretraining choices most effectively exploit egocentric data? (2) Which components of robot action prediction benefit most from scaling these data? (3) How do scaling effects differ between vision-language-action models (VLAs) and world-action models (WAMs)? Our experiments yield three main findings. First, data diversity, alignment between trajectories, and detailed language annotations are key factors in effective transfer. Second, scaling egocentric data yields larger gains in predicting end-effector translation than in predicting fine-grained finger movements. Third, WAMs perform better when egocentric data are limited, whereas VLAs exhibit greater gains as the amount of egocentric data increases. Together, these findings provide practical guidance on processing and using egocentric data to train VLAs and WAMs, while clarifying the benefits and limitations of scaling these data.

## 108. Do learned image captioning metrics generalize? Auditing duplicated samples in human-judgment benchmarks

- **作者**：Xiaochen Zhang, Jin Li, Chunlong Xia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Learned image captioning metrics are trained and tested on human judgments, so their reported correlation is a claim about generalization. We audit this claim for the three most recent collections of such judgments, Polaris, Nebula, and Spica, matching images at the pixel level. First, test samples reappear in training sets: the Spica training set contains about two-thirds of the Polaris test set, with identical human ratings. Second, controlled retraining shows that duplicates can inflate a metric: for Pearl, the gain is confined to the duplicated samples and grows with every epoch; for DENEB, it stays below one point. The gain depends on the learner and on how often a training set repeats a sample. Third, Kendall's $τ$ over all samples is the sum of two terms, detecting captions with the lowest human score and ranking the others; the first supplies most of the reported correlation and is nearly saturated. The recent test sets cannot order Polos, DENEB and Pearl consistently, and a ridge regression on frozen features matches or exceeds all three. Fourth, we propose a protocol that ranks a learned metric only with a model trained without test samples, reports both terms, and compares metrics by paired tests. Under it, zero-shot vision--language judges rank first on the three recent test sets, and a fine-tuned judge ranks first on the three earlier benchmarks. The same analysis applies to any evaluator learned from human judgments; filter lists and code are released at https://github.com/morninghut/ic-dataset-audit .

## 109. Test-Time Latent Computation to Improve Visual Perception in Multimodal Large Language Models

- **作者**：Chen Yang, Zheng Fang, Wenqi Fan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Recent work on Large Language Models (LLMs) suggests that reasoning capabilities can be enhanced at test time not only through longer token-level reasoning traces, but also through additional computation in latent space. This raises a natural question for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs): can additional computation in latent space improve visual perception at test time? In this work, we first verify that MLLMs contain stable reusable layers that can be re-forwarded at inference time without disrupting normal generation. Based on this observation, we propose \textbf{Lo}ok \textbf{Ba}ck in the \textbf{L}atent \textbf{S}pace (\textbf{Lobals}), a training-free decoding method that identifies the contiguous layer window with the strongest visual-token attention and re-forwards through it, allowing the model to revisit visual evidence in latent space. We demonstrate that this latent-space revisit strategy can improve MLLMs' performance across diverse multimodal settings, including general vision-language tasks, visual hallucination tasks, and vision-centric tasks.

## 110. Target-Part Preference Optimization for Part-Aware 3D Generation

- **作者**：Zishan Qin, Rui Li, Zeyu Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Part-aware 3D generation requires not only globally plausible objects but also complete, well-separated semantic parts, yet high-quality part decompositions remain too scarce to support large-scale supervised fine-tuning. We study whether preference fine-tuning based on Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) can adapt a pretrained part-aware generator from limited part-annotated data without architectural changes. We rank candidates generated with different random seeds against ground-truth (GT) geometric metrics, and select the (object, target-part) examples with the largest across-seed quality gap. Each selected example stores a seed pair whose two flow trajectories share one clean GT compositional latent and differ only in Gaussian noise. We derive a reference-relative target-block preference loss from FlowDPO that compares the two trajectories against a frozen reference model. GT flow-matching losses on the target block and complete part set provide absolute supervision, while only the diffusion transformer (DiT) is updated. Human-ranked controls test an alternative ordering source, and synthetic cropping provides controlled loser targets that are incomplete by construction. Among the three training-set scales, the medium-scale setting gives the strongest result on held-out Objaverse and outperforms both the pretrained generator and the reported direct supervised fine-tuning baseline on the multi-part aggregate. Further analysis shows sensitivity to pair ordering, target-part coverage, and the balance between pairwise and GT supervision. Cross-dataset experiments show less consistent gains under distribution shift.

## 111. Inclusion of obstacles in ground point classification in ALS data for terrain models used for analysis of vehicle traversability

- **作者**：Mikael Lundbäck, Erik Wallin, Sima Mohtasami et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：28 pages, 17 figures

Classification of ALS point clouds has been studied for decades, but it remains challenging in forest contexts due to the great variety of terrain types, sensor equipment, flight parameters, and resulting point densities. Point cloud density has increased significantly in recent years, and existing algorithms, developed for lower density point clouds, may no longer be effective. It is beyond the scope of most existing algorithms to classify obstacles, such as boulders, while also distinguishing non-obstacles, such as bushes and undergrowth. We evaluate the performance of different algorithms for ground point classification in ALS data in a context where obstacles are included in the ground class, resulting in terrain models useful for offroad machinery route planning and navigation. The evaluation is performed using metrics directly on the point clouds (Precision, Recall, $F_1$-score) and on resulting terrain models (RMSE and maps of differences). The results show that there are several algorithms that perform well, but performance is highly affected by the parameters used. The best results in terms of terrain model RMSE are obtained with the CSF-algorithm, with an average RMSE of 0.08 meters. The results indicate that existing algorithms can be adapted to include obstacles in the ground class under the tested conditions.

## 112. Struct2Det: Grounding Category Structure in Visual Evidence for Few-Shot Object Detection

- **作者**：Kaixuan Chang, Huabing Zhou, Yanduo Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 14 figures

Few-shot detectors struggle to distinguish categories that look alike but differ in local components, because a few examples rarely reveal which differences determine category identity. Category descriptions specify components and relations but do not establish their presence in an image. We propose Struct2Det, which uses category structure to guide detection and verify candidates in three stages. Relational Structure Encoding maps category graphs to component embeddings and a global condition. Component-Grounded Detection uses component queries to retrieve multi-scale image evidence that, with the global condition, modulates category text embeddings for initial detection. Verification-Guided Refinement matches candidate regions with components expected by their top-scoring categories; when class scores are close and matches are reliable, matched pairs correct region features before the shared detector predicts again. Category structure informs both classification inputs: text embeddings integrate image-wide component evidence, while region features incorporate candidate-specific matches. We also construct InduSym, with 88,912 symbol instances across 275 categories from 674 engineering drawings and a structural graph per category. Struct2Det improves few-shot-category AP over the strongest baseline by 6.02 points on InduSym and 9.68 on Digitize-PID, with the highest base-category AP on both. InduSym ablations show few-shot gains from correct category structure, image-level retrieval, and candidate verification. Further tests show gains in few-shot natural-object detection and zero-shot Chinese character recognition.

## 113. LiveMirror: Real-Time Diffusion Virtual Try-On on a Consumer GPU and Its Self-Supervised Adaptation to Indian Ethnic Wear

- **作者**：Jigyansha Jeevan Rout, Sukanta Kumar Sabut
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：12 pages, 10 figures, 6 tables

Diffusion virtual try-on (VTO) models produce convincing garment transfers from a single product photograph, but at hundreds of milliseconds to seconds per image every deployment today is offline or falls back to AR mirrors that need a rigged 3D asset per product. We present LiveMirror, a live-camera try-on mirror that renders a flat garment photograph onto a moving person with a diffusion model on one 16 GB consumer GPU. Its core is asynchronous keyframe try-on: generation never blocks the display, every displayed frame is an optical-flow warp of the latest generated keyframe, and keyframes are replaced only through a cross-fade. The display runs at camera rate (25 FPS at 720p) while a new try-on arrives every 370-490 ms; we measure the residual flicker, give a stage-by-stage latency budget, and report which accelerations (step count, consistency LoRA, tiny decoder, compilation) keep garment prints intact. We then show that the same GPU fixes the model's biggest cultural blind spot: try-on models trained on VITON-HD and DressCode render a knee-length kurta or sherwani as a torso-length top or a dress over bare legs. A thigh-covering mask, self-supervised pairs built automatically from 24.9k on-model e-commerce photographs, and 3.2 GPU-hours of attention-only fine-tuning of CatVTON halve the perceptual error on ethnic wear (LPIPS 0.137 to 0.077 on IndoFashion, 0.135 to 0.073 on Atlas; 200 held-out pairs per source) with no regression on Western tops. A two-pass hem-aware mask then removes the remaining failure, recoloured trousers below the hem, on all but 3-4% of pairs at no training cost; in the mirror the hem comes from the previous keyframe for free. The adapted model beats FASHN VTON 1.5 on every aggregate ethnic-wear metric at about $20\times$ lower inference cost, and its weights drop straight into the mirror.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30461) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30461)

## 114. Rethinking the Evaluation of Contrastive Decoding for Hallucination Mitigation in Multimodal Large Language Models

- **作者**：Ting Chen, Geng Li, Guohao Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.CV

Contrastive decoding (CD) mitigates object hallucination in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by contrasting token distributions under different conditions to adjust token scores. However, existing generative and discriminative benchmarks primarily measure final task scores, which can be confounded by candidate filtering, changes in generation trajectories, and response biases, making it difficult to isolate the effects of the underlying contrastive adjustments. To address these limitations while complementing existing evaluations, we introduce CONCOR (Contrastive Correction Evaluation), a controlled benchmark that directly evaluates the competition between ground-truth and different types of hallucinated candidates using the Contrastive Grounding Margin (CGM). CGM also enables analysis of directional consistency, composition, and reliability of contrastive corrections. Our evaluation reveals that existing CD methods exhibit distinct correction behaviors: some consistently favor ground-truth candidates, whereas others produce strong corrections but also introduce more errors. Crucially, these differences help explain their contrasting responses to increased contrast strength, which improves some methods while degrading others. These findings highlight the importance of evaluating the contrastive correction itself, rather than relying solely on final-output performance, and CONCOR provides a controlled and interpretable framework for understanding and improving CD methods for hallucination mitigation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30460) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30460)

## 115. Decompose and Describe: Narrowing the Diagnosis-to-Report Gap in 3D CT Report Generation

- **作者**：Zhisong Wang, Wanxing Chang, Weiwei Cao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：30 pages, 9 figures, 14 tables, including appendices

Automated 3D CT report generation could support radiology workflows, yet medical vision-language models (MedVLMs) often omit diagnostic findings from generated reports. We ask whether the diagnostic ability of these models carries over to free-form reporting. Across five MedVLMs, diagnostic VQA achieves higher macro-F1 than reports generated from the same CT studies and evaluated on the same disease categories. We call this task-dependent discrepancy the diagnosis-to-report gap. Conventional Report Adaptation improves reporting for most models but does not close the gap. To investigate and narrow it, we introduce DeCAD (Decompose CT Analysis and Describe), which progressively decomposes CT report generation at diagnostic, regional textual, and regional visual levels. The model identifies positive findings before writing a report, organizes diagnosis and description into regional tasks, and restricts each task to visual tokens from the corresponding region. Diagnostic and regional textual decomposition improve report-level diagnostic performance across the five MedVLMs, whereas regional visual decomposition provides limited additional gains. In a standard VLM without medical VQA pretraining, the first two levels remain effective; regional visual decomposition also provides further gains with a region-level contrastively pretrained encoder. Full DeCAD achieves state-of-the-art performance on CT-RATE and Merlin. Analyses show greater CT-token attention during diagnosis and higher recall of reference-positive findings in both the diagnostic output and final report than with Report Adaptation. These results show that explicitly organizing diagnosis improves diagnostic reporting and helps narrow the diagnosis-to-report gap.

## 116. Spatial-Zero: Self-Evolving Spatial Reasoning in Vision-Language Models from Zero Data

- **作者**：Yutong Li, Haoyu Zhao, Daoguo Dong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：19 pages, including appendices. 4 figures and 3 tables in the main text

Spatial reasoning requires a vision-language model~(VLM) to understand metric distances, three-dimensional relationships, and viewpoint-dependent geometry. Learning these capabilities typically requires externally curated training data. This paper introduces Spatial-Zero, a VLMs' training framework that can self-evolve through autonomous spatial exploration with zero data. Spatial-Zero addresses two key challenges in autonomous spatial exploration: reliably generating valid spatial interaction tasks and providing accurate supervision for learning from these tasks. Specifically, a proposer specifies object configurations and questions, and a Coder turns them into executable scene programs that produce multi-view images and explicit scene geometry. A Geometric Oracle checks spatial constraints, visibility, and answer uniqueness and derives ground-truth labels from executed geometry; these labels supervise the Solver and ground difficulty feedback, while validation outcomes guide subsequent task generation. Across six spatial reasoning benchmarks and three VLM backbones, Spatial-Zero yields consistent gains through iterative self-evolution. After three iterations, Qwen3.5-9B improves average accuracy by 3.23 percentage points; with Qwen3.5-4B, Spatial-Zero also outperforms existing VLM self-evolution methods. Our code is available at https://github.com/YutongLi2024/Spatial-Zero

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30424) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30424)

## 117. GeLiFiSH: Recursive LiDAR Keypoint Estimation with Ground and Moving Object Segmentation

- **作者**：Nikolaos Stathoulopoulos, George Nikolakopoulos
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：8 pages, 3 figures, 3 tables, submitted

LiDAR registration and loop closure often rely on sparse keypoints, which must remain repeatable across sessions, viewpoints, and sensors. To handle differing scan patterns, detectors typically operate on accumulated submaps, which introduces latency and processes each submap independently, so repeated observations are never fused. Height-based detectors also reference structure to an estimated ground, so its inconsistency across sessions propagates into the keypoints. Moreover, dynamic objects leave ghost trails that can be mistaken for static structure. We present GeLiFiSH (Geometric LiDAR Filtering with Sequential Hypothesis testing), a learning-free filter that recursively estimates persistent keypoints at scan rate. A height-structure detector runs on a rolling voxel map, and detections initialize tracks with a position, covariance, and existence probability, accepted or rejected by a sequential probability ratio test. Ground segmentation provides a sensor-agnostic height reference, and moving object segmentation removes dynamic points before map insertion. On SemanticKITTI, HeLiMOS, and HeLiPR, spanning five LiDARs and multiple sessions, GeLiFiSH attains the highest average keypoint repeatability among the compared detectors and the second-highest cross-session registration recall from keypoints alone, behind ISS which extracts four times as many keypoints. With one parameter set and no sensor-height prior, both ground and moving object segmentation modules outperform their baselines on average.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30402) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30402)

## 118. LiquidGazeNet: Towards Event-Based Near-Eye Gaze Estimation under Low-Light Conditions

- **作者**：Himanshu Kumar, Sanjay Singh
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Near-eye gaze estimation under low-light nighttime conditions is challenging because the quality and effective temporal resolution of conventional intensity frames can be reduced. This paper presents LiquidGazeNet, an event-only gaze-estimation framework that combines polarity-aware spike-latency encoding with a custom LTC-based recurrent module. Positive- and negative-polarity events are represented in separate channels, while exponentially decayed time surfaces retain per-pixel temporal recency across successive event windows. A shared convolutional encoder extracts spatial features from each window, and a two-layer recurrent module composed of LTC-inspired cells integrates them causally over short temporal sequences. The network is trained using a hybrid objective that combines screen-space coordinate regression with centre-referenced directional consistency. The framework is evaluated on Gaze-FELL under cross-subject and cross-scene protocols and is further assessed through event-representation, temporal-module, window-duration, and computational ablations. LiquidGazeNet achieves mean errors of 3.75 degrees and 2.41 degrees under cross-subject and cross-scene evaluation, respectively. The results indicate that polarity-aware temporal-recency encoding, together with recurrent integration, is effective for event-only gaze estimation under the evaluated low-light nighttime conditions.

## 119. BASE: A Backend-Agnostic Streaming Embodiment Layer for Full-Duplex Conversational Avatars

- **作者**：Chenye Yang, Jin Jiang, Shengcai Liao
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages, 7 figures, 10 tables

Many end-to-end spoken dialogue models can now converse in real time and full duplex, and turning them into 3D avatars requires full-body gestures, listening feedback, and turn-taking. Existing methods couple the motion generator tightly to one dialogue model by reading its internal states or training speech and motion jointly. The motion module must then be retrained whenever the dialogue model changes, cannot be attached to cloud APIs that expose only audio, and entangles role, voice, and motion style. We propose BASE, a backend-agnostic streaming embodiment layer whose only interface to the dialogue model is its emitted speech. First, a conversation state manager driven solely by audio handles speaking, listening, idling, barge-in, and proactive turns on one timeline, while role, voice, motion style, and 3D appearance are set independently. Second, to run an offline 2 s flow-matching gesture model on live audio, we reformulate inference as sliding-window continuation with overlapped look-ahead ensembling, which removes seam jitter. Third, we show that the remaining streaming gap comes from errors accumulating in the self-generated motion history rather than missing future speech; our Drift-Aware History Perturbation (DAHP) reduces this gap in about 50 min on one GPU by injecting inference-like drift into ground-truth training histories, and transfers to a second generator. On BEAT2, streaming BASE reaches an FGD of 0.342, on par with the tightly coupled MIBURI (0.333), and viewers prefer its body motion over MIBURI's in 82% of forced-choice judgments. Without retraining, the same layer drives a cascaded pipeline, three open full-duplex models, and a cloud API in real time.

## 120. ColonoWorld: Camera-Controlled Full-Procedure Colonoscopy Video Generation

- **作者**：Jihwan Hong, Sunghwan Steve Cho, Ping Shu Ho et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages, 8 figures, 11 tables. Project page: https://aidaslab.github.io/ColonoWorld

Navigation systems in colonoscopy are advancing from guiding the endoscopist to steering the scope autonomously. Since an incorrect motion can injure the patient, developing and evaluating these systems calls for a world model of colonoscopy. Such a model must follow a commanded scope motion, keep the colon anatomy consistent, and show lesions and procedural events. Yet camera-controlled generators trained on general scenes lose the colon's structure under motion, and endoscopic generators offer no camera control. We present ColonoWorld, a colonoscopy world model conditioned on a camera trajectory and a coarse 3D tube of the colon, trained on in vivo videos. From a whole-colon tube, it generates a full cecum-to-rectum procedure, even for unseen colon shapes. It also places polyps, instruments, and irrigation at specified locations and times. ColonoWorld outperforms state-of-the-art video generators, reducing camera trajectory error by 32% and FVD by 29%. Our code and annotations will be released at https://aidaslab.github.io/ColonoWorld.

## 121. Mind the Gaps: State-Guided Supervision Allocation for Object Detection Dataset Distillation

- **作者**：Jiehui Xie, Zhengxun Jin, Jiahao Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Jiehui Xie and Zhengxun Jin contributed equally. Corresponding authors: Sung-Ho Bae and Chaoning Zhang

Recent advances in dataset distillation have enabled large-scale training datasets to be compressed into compact synthetic sets. However, dataset distillation for object detection remains challenging, as it requires preserving diverse classification and localization signals under limited image budgets. Existing approaches often emphasize individual-image informativeness without explicitly accounting for supervision accumulated across the distilled dataset, potentially leading to redundant supervision and uneven class-scale coverage. To address this limitation, we propose State-Guided Supervision Allocation (SGSA), a framework that formulates object detection dataset distillation as a dataset-level progressive supervision allocation problem. SGSA establishes class-scale supervision quotas and maintains a global state that tracks detector predictions accumulated from previously selected synthetic images. The remaining gaps between the target quotas and accumulated supervision guide adaptive object sampling, detector-guided canvas refinement, and marginal-gain-based candidate selection, encouraging complementary supervision across images. On MS COCO, SGSA outperforms a matched OD3 baseline by 3.73-4.06 mAP points across three image budgets (0.25%-1.0%). On PASCAL VOC, SGSA outperforms the strongest evaluated baseline by 4.53-6.90 mAP50 points across three image budgets. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of dataset-level supervision allocation guided by accumulated detector feedback under limited image budgets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30369) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30369)

## 122. REACT: Response-based Expert Assembly from Contextual Trajectories

- **作者**：Hesen Yang, Yuxue Liu, Yanzhe Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Dense feed-forward networks are unaffected by how their neurons are grouped: every neuron is evaluated. Sparse execution makes this grouping consequential, because each input can access only a few experts. An arbitrary partition can therefore retain pretrained weights while separating computations that an input needs together. We argue that upcycling should organize neurons according to their responses to similar inputs. To establish this correspondence, we introduce a context-guided upcycling approach REACT, Response-based Expert Assembly from Contextual Trajectories, that uses frozen denoising trajectories to connect input contexts with pretrained neuron responses. We first identify contexts after timestep-wise centering to account for distribution shifts across noise levels, then use their associated responses to allocate intact neurons to routed experts. The same contexts initialize the corresponding router entries, aligning expert contents with their intended inputs, while global activation statistics select an always-active shared expert. Layer-wise reconstruction and end-to-end adaptation refine this initialization for sparse execution. On ImageNet-1K, our approach achieves better generation quality while activating only 37.5% of the original FFN parameters and reducing total per-token activated parameters by 27.6%.

## 123. Boosting zero-shot learning through neuro-symbolic integration

- **作者**：Francesco Manigrasso, Fabrizio Lamberti, Lia Morra
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, stat.ML
- **备注**：15 pages, 2 figures

Zero-shot learning (ZSL) aims to train deep neural networks to recognize objects from unseen classes, starting from a semantic description of the concepts. Neuro-symbolic (NeSy) integration refers to a class of techniques that incorporate symbolic knowledge representation and reasoning with the learning capabilities of deep neural networks. However, to date, few studies have explored how to leverage NeSy techniques to inject prior knowledge during the training process to boost ZSL capabilities. Here, we present Fuzzy Logic Prototypical Network (FLPN) that formulates the classification task as prototype matching in a visual-semantic embedding space, which is trained by optimizing a NeSy loss. Specifically, FLPN exploits the Logic Tensor Network (LTN) framework to incorporate background knowledge in the form of logical axioms by grounding a first-order logic language as differentiable operations between real tensors. This prior knowledge includes class hierarchies (classes and macroclasses) along with robust high-level inductive biases. The latter allow, for instance, to handle exceptions in class-level attributes and to enforce similarity between images of the same class, preventing premature overfitting to seen classes and improving overall performance. % CHECK: original sentence had no verb ("Both class-level and attribute-level prototypes through an attention mechanism ...") FLPN learns both class-level and attribute-level prototypes, the latter through an attention mechanism specialized for either convolutional- or transformer-based backbones. FLPN achieves state-of-the-art performance on the GZSL benchmarks AWA2 and SUN, matching or exceeding the performance of competing algorithms with minimal computational overhead. The code is available at https://github.com/FrancescoManigrass/FLPN

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30358) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30358)

## 124. STAR: Structure-Aware Temporal Action Recovery for Point Supervised Temporal Action Localization

- **作者**：Yawar Rehman, Min Young Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Point supervised temporal action localization is becoming the need of time in this era of ever growing and non-stop video data. Existing method mainly rely on snippet level action response to generate pseudo labels. However, these labels often suffer from boundary ambiguity, over extended proposals, and weak temporal structure. To address these issues, we propose STAR, a structure-aware Temporal Action Recovery framework for point supervised temporal action localization. STAR works by enhancing the base S model with temporal difference interaction with attention module to capture forward and backward snippet changes, and introduces the learning of start, core, and end roles to model the internal temporal structure of actions. In addition, STAR further improves the pseudo label generation through All point proposal ranking and Rolebank based structural re-ranking, which suppress neighbor crossing and structurally inconsistent proposals. Finally, temporal action detector to STAR feedback supervision transfers high quality detector prediction back to the STAR model through soft start, core, end, action, and background targets. This helps to further refine the pseudo labels. Extensive experiments on four standard benchmarks shows the effectiveness of STAR. By using I3D features, STAR achieves 67.2% at AVG@0.1:0.7 on THUMOS'14, 58.1% on GTEA, 74.6% on BEOID, and 31.7% at AVG@0.5:0.95 on ActivityNet-1.3, outperforming previous point supervised methods across multiple benchmarks.

## 125. NNS-GS: Non-Local Neural Scattering for Relightable Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Shiyu Liang, Yang Gao, Lili Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Creating relightable 3D assets of translucent objects is challenging because changing the illumination at one surface location can affect the appearance elsewhere through subsurface scattering. Existing Gaussian-based scattering models typically predict scattering from local features, making this dependence difficult to capture. We present Non-Local Neural Scattering for Gaussian Splatting (NNS-GS), which learns non-local scattering together with geometry-guided local reflection. The scattering module learns how illumination from light-visible surface regions in light space contributes to the shading point in view space, conditioned on regional diffuse responses and spatial relationships. The reflection module supplies these diffuse responses and uses additional geometric guidance to better capture specular highlights and sharp shadow boundaries. To reduce component mixing and support controllable scattering appearance, anchor-based progressive training establishes the diffuse-scattering base before introducing specular reflection and supervises the components separately. Under calibrated point lighting, NNS-GS improves mean PSNR over existing methods by 2.47 dB and 0.81 dB on the synthetic and real translucent scenes, respectively. Additional synthetic comparisons further show that NNS-GS better matches the reference when adjusting scattering attenuation.

## 126. SceneCast: Learning Indoor Scene Synthesis from Curated Synthetic Videos with LLM Reward

- **作者**：Ziyang Zong, Canming Xia, Haoran Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

3D indoor scene synthesis requires generating objects to form a complete and coherent scene. Existing approaches rely heavily on manually annotated datasets, leading to high costs and limited generalization. We present SceneCast, an annotation-free framework that trains on large-scale, unlabeled videos, addressing two key challenges: (1) Videos provide only partial views of a scene, requiring the model to extrapolate unseen 3D regions, which is inherently error-prone; (2) Raw videos available online are uncurated, suffering from uncontrolled conditions and variable data quality. First, we design a Transformer-based model that predicts objects sequentially and is fine-tuned with reinforcement learning (RL), where an LLM acts as a spatial common-sense evaluator. The LLM provides reward signals through chain-of-thought reasoning and code-executable geometric checks to refine object configurations. Second, we introduce \trim, a data curation approach that generates training videos with controllable video generation models. Crucially, it retains only high-quality prefix clips via multi-criteria quality assessment with an entropy-based weighting strategy. Evaluated on curated synthetic video sets, our annotation-free approach outperforms state-of-the-art supervised methods in scene quality.

## 127. Shift by Analogy for Compositional Zero-Shot Learning

- **作者**：Zanwu Liu, Bo Li, Guanglin Niu
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Compositional zero-shot learning recognizes attribute-object pairs absent from training. An unseen pair has a semantic description but lacks an observed visual reference. We propose Shift by Analogy (SBA) to make unseen compositions visually seen through a shift-then-match process. Its core add-subtract-add rule transfers an attribute change between two seen image-text residuals on a source object to a reference residual on the target object. This shifts the target's own text feature toward its visual center; learned refinement produces a proxy for matching query images. During training, each target is excluded from its supports, and recognition is learned using references constructed from other pairs. An analogy library and same-object attention extend this construction across candidates, while joint learning integrates proxy matching with semantic and primitive evidence. Visual-center analysis confirms substantially improved correspondence for unseen compositions. Experiments across three standard benchmarks show that SBA consistently outperforms strong CZSL baselines under both closed- and open-world evaluation.

## 128. AirVT-15k: A Query-Diverse UAV Video-Text Dataset and Query-Adaptive Evidence Alignment Network for Retrieval

- **作者**：Xi Chen, Xu Chen, Xiangyang Jia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 6 figures, 3 tables

Unmanned aerial vehicle video-text retrieval (UAV-TVR) requires matching language to both scene context and subtle object changes. A harbor-layout query relies on global scene context, whereas a departing-vessel query requires local functional cues and motion states. Existing benchmarks provide diverse aerial descriptions but give limited attention to distinct evidence needs within the same video. We introduce AirVT-15k, a benchmark of 15,504 UAV videos and 155,040 captions, with ten complementary bilingual captions per video describing scenes, object states, and temporal changes. We also propose AirFocus-CST, which selects query-relevant frames and patches, encodes temporal feature differences, and combines coarse-to-fine interaction (CFI) with optimal-transport alignment (OTA). Average mean recall reaches 63.67% on AirVT-15k and 24.41% on CapERA, improving over the strongest baselines by 2.96 and 1.91 percentage points, respectively. The dataset and code are available at https://chenxi000233.github.io/AirVT-15k-site/.

## 129. Draft Forcing: Target-guided Speculative Drafting and Learned Verifier for Autoregressive Video Generation

- **作者**：Chenkai Xu, Yuji Ren, Zihui Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 8 figures, 5 tables, including appendix

Autoregressive video diffusion models enable streaming synthesis chunk by chunk, yet invoking the large model for every chunk remains costly. While chunk-level speculative decoding can offload chunks to a lightweight draft model, existing approaches suffer from two limitations: (1) an off-the-shelf draft diverges from the target's trajectory, compounding errors in history; and (2) they either omit verification or rely on costly post-decode aesthetic scores rather than verifying consistency against the target. We address both with Draft Forcing, a framework coupling target-guided drafting with an on-policy trained verifier. Specifically, to align the draft for high acceptance, we opt for feature-level drafting, conditioning it on multi-layer target hidden states persisted from committed chunks, whose outputs are pushed to match target trajectories with multi-aspect losses. For verification, we propose an on-policy trained feature-space verifier predicting CDF-calibrated frame-wise relative RMSE against the target. It is trained on speculative rollouts as contexts anneal from random to inference-aligned mixtures, and gates proposals without VAE decoding. On the 14B Self-Forcing target, Draft Forcing achieves $1.63\times$ DiT and $1.57\times$ end-to-end speedups on VBench with minimal quality loss, and up to $1.93\times$ DiT ($1.82\times$ end-to-end) when combined with orthogonal KV compression. It generalizes to accelerate a streaming variant of MiniMax-H3, generating over $50\%$ draft frames with competitive quality.

## 130. TAED: Trajectory-Aligned Encoder--Decoder Distillation for Detection Transformers

- **作者**：Zilong He, Zhangchi Hu, Hebei Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Distilling localization knowledge into compact detection transformers (DETRs) requires consistent teacher-student query correspondence across decoder layers. However, in DETRs with encoder-based query selection, the teacher and student select top-K proposals independently. Same-rank queries can originate from different spatial locations and correspond to different objects, making rank-based layer-wise supervision unreliable. To address this problem, we propose Trajectory-Aligned Encoder-Decoder Distillation (TAED), a framework for knowledge transfer between DETRs of different capacities. Encoder feature distillation matches normalized multi-scale features to strengthen student representations and increase the number of teacher-student query pairs from the same encoder locations. Source-indexed query alignment pairs native queries from the same encoder locations and retains pairs associated with the same object for which the teacher provides reliable and more accurate final localization than the student. Trajectory-aligned localization distillation maintains these correspondences and uses teacher proposals and corresponding decoder-layer box predictions to guide student refinement, without introducing additional queries. On COCO, TAED improves DINO-R18 and two-stage Deformable DETR-R18 by 2.5 and 1.7 AP, respectively. Further gains on D-FINE and ECDet demonstrate applicability to recent real-time DETRs at no additional inference cost.

## 131. Accuracy Is Not Evidence: Measuring and Training Evidence Dependence in Video Temporal Grounding

- **作者**：Zijie Jin, Shuai Zhao, Haoran Tao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：Preprint, 47 pages. Code, models and data: https://jinzijie-bupt.github.io/Accuracy-Is-Not-Evidence/

In video temporal grounding, a correct interval can come from finding the event, answering blind or fitting one annotation style, and Recall at IoU scores them alike. We measure evidence dependence, how much the answer rests on the event's frames. Our fixed-answer audit edits either the frames inside the annotated interval or, as a placebo, as many frames elsewhere in the same video, and compares the two drops in the annotated answer's likelihood. Supervised fine-tuning and three public checkpoints trained with IoU-reward reinforcement learning raise the accuracy of Qwen2.5-VL-7B by 9 to 20 points, partly by answering blind and by fitting one annotation. Yet their answers rest on the event no more than the base model's, and the same holds for IoU-reward training on our data at the audit's frame rate. This comparison is a difference of the model's own likelihoods, so we can train on it. A placebo contrast and a bound on the placebo drop rule out learning to react to any edit. With them, evidence dependence rises in all five base models we train, and the weight on clean supervision controls how much. Added to IoU-reward training, the objective raises evidence dependence while keeping the accuracy gain. Masking the event's frames makes the trained models decline or miss the event more often than their base; masking as many other frames does not. The dependence moves to another event when the query names it, and holds on a second dataset and under patch scrambling, an edit never used in training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30320) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30320)

## 132. Video Generalized Category Discovery

- **作者**：Bardia Safaei, Vishal M. Patel
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to WACV 2027

Generalized Category Discovery (GCD) aims to categorize unlabeled data from both known and unknown classes using labeled data from only the known classes. While recent GCD methods have shown promise on static images, their performance degrades in videos due to temporal dynamics, larger intra-class variation, and the lack of temporal consistency in existing approaches. We introduce VideoGCD, a framework tailored for video action recognition under the GCD setting. Our method integrates (i) a partitioned Mixture-of-Experts module that encourages specialization for known and novel categories, (ii) a novel-class balancing loss to reduce bias toward known classes, and (iii) a temporal optimal transport loss that promotes consistent routing across frames. Experiments on three video benchmarks demonstrate that VideoGCD significantly improves performance over existing GCD methods, particularly on novel categories. Our findings highlight the importance of combining temporal modeling and expert specialization for video GCD.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30316) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30316)

## 133. SafeRank-ReID: Candidate-Conditioned Re-Ranking under Identity Conflicts for Multimodal Person Re-Identification

- **作者**：Yizhi Wang, Zixuan Li, Zhuwen Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at ACM Multimedia Asia 2026 (MMAsia 2026). 8 pages, 5 figures

Multimodal Person Re-Identification (ReID) exploits complementary identity cues from different sensing modalities to mitigate the performance degradation caused by challenging imaging conditions. Existing methods typically estimate Modality Reliability at the query level, making it difficult to identify conflicting inputs that retain normal visual quality but correspond to an incorrect identity. Such inputs may receive high confidence, promote irrelevant gallery candidates, and lead to negative fusion. To address this issue, we propose SafeRank-ReID, a candidate-conditioned safe re-ranking framework for Multimodal Person Re-Identification (ReID). To capture the varying support of the same modality for different candidates, we introduce a candidate-conditioned evidence arbitration mechanism that integrates unimodal retrieval evidence, input quality, and cross-modal consistency to estimate trust and conflict logits for each modality-candidate pair. Together, these logits contribute to the normalized modality weights, allowing support strength to vary across modalities and candidates while reducing the contribution of potentially unreliable evidence. A candidate-set refinement module subsequently applies self-attention to jointly model candidate representations and predicts residual scores from their competitive relations, thereby refining the initial fused ranking. For fusion results that remain uncertain after refinement, a query-level fallback gate estimates an interpolation coefficient from the overall conflict level and cross-modal uncertainty, adaptively combining the fused scores with those of the most reliable unimodal branch. Experiments on RGBNT201 and MSVR310 show that SafeRank-ReID maintains competitive clean-input retrieval while improving robustness to Identity Conflicts across person and vehicle benchmarks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30314) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30314)

## 134. Seeing Together, Acting Apart: Shared Environmental Understanding for Multi-Robot Navigation

- **作者**：Haihong Hao, Lei Chen, Mingfei Han et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026

Multi-robot embodied systems are typically designed to collaborate by coordinating actions, sharing trajectories, or learning joint policies. We ask a complementary question: can collaboration emerge before decision-making, by first building a shared understanding of the environment? We introduce Shared Environmental Understanding, a policy-agnostic representation that aggregates partial egocentric observations from multiple robots into a shared latent world model. This shifts collaboration from the policy space to the representation space, enabling decentralized decision-making with shared environmental context. To this end, we propose Shared Environment Encoding and Retrieval (SEER), a framework that organizes multi-robot observations into a shared memory, retrieves relevant context via spatio-temporal similarity, and encodes it using a predictive latent world model. The resulting representations are injected into downstream navigation agents as tokens. Experimental results demonstrate that SEER captures action-relevant environmental structure and consistently improves navigation performance across both specialized VLN agents and general vision-language model agents, while preserving single-agent capabilities.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30310) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30310)

## 135. PinGround: Pinning Queries to Points for Parallel Grounding in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Shuxuan Li, Jialiang Kang, Yanghao Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-language models (VLMs) can localize through their text interface, grounding open-vocabulary objects, complex referring expressions, text lines and layout regions with strong semantic understanding. They write the boxes one after another as a single sequence, although the instances of a query, unlike the words of a sentence, form an unordered set with no sequential dependence.
