The number of decoding steps therefore grows with the number of instances, which makes dense scenes the most expensive. Decoding the instances in parallel avoids this growth, but streams that cannot see each other duplicate some instances and miss others. We propose \method, which writes a single point into each query as a spatial anchor and asks for the matching instance nearest to that point within a local radius, or \none. Given the image, the anchored queries are independent, so they are decoded in parallel as leaves of a prefix tree that shares the image and the query, and the model is trained on the same tree. Coarse-to-fine anchors refine only the regions where instances are found, duplicate suppression keeps one box per instance, and set-level reinforcement learning optimizes the set F1. Only the prompt and the attention mask change, not the architecture. On three different VLMs and 12 benchmarks covering detection, OCR, layout and referring, \method is at most 5.5 F1 below sequential decoding trained on the same data, while its speedup grows with instance density and reaches 21.9$\times$ on dense scenes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30304) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30304)

## 136. Uncovering Separable and Composable Skill Mechanisms in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Miaoyu Li, Boyang Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Recent work has sought to localize the internal mechanisms underlying visual reasoning skills in vision-language models. These methods largely overlook separability and composability in both mechanism identification and evaluation. We propose separability and composability as two important desiderata for skill localization. Separability requires identified mechanisms to contribute primarily to their target skills while having limited effects on other skills, enabling precise diagnosis and targeted intervention. Composability requires skill mechanisms to be combinable to support complex tasks, allowing a small set of mechanisms to account for broader capabilities. In this work, we propose a multi-skill interpretability framework DISCO, built around these two properties. For identification, we propose Separable Head Selection, which selects skill-specific attention heads based on their contributions across skills. For evaluation, we are the first to design metrics for measuring separability and composability of identified heads. Extensive experiments on three VLMs and four fundamental visual reasoning skills demonstrate the effectiveness of DISCO. It achieves the highest separability, outperforming the strongest baseline by 24.80, 18.38, and 13.75 points across the three models, respectively, while also achieving the highest composability on all three models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30290) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30290)

## 137. Face Is Not All You Need: MIME Benchmark for Incomplete Multimodal Emotion Recognition

- **作者**：Yuxin Jia, Xing Lan, Wensong Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.MM, cs.CV
- **备注**：Published in the Proceedings of the 34th ACM International Conference on Multimedia (ACM MM '26), November 10--14, 2026, Rio de Janeiro, Brazil

Multimodal Emotion Recognition (MER) is a fundamental task in multimedia understanding, where state-of-the-art methods have achieved prominent success relying on the assumption of complete, well-aligned multimodal inputs. However, real-world unconstrained scenarios often suffer from unpredictable modality degradation and information loss, leading to catastrophic performance collapse of existing MER models that heavily depend on facial cues as the core inference anchor. However, few systematic works evaluate model robustness under diverse, fine-grained incomplete settings, as prior works are limited to coarse-grained full-modality removal and neglect interpretable analysis of emotional reasoning. To bridge this gap, we formally define the Incomplete Multimodal Emotion Recognition (IMER) task, which benchmarks model generalization and robustness under multi-grained modality and emotional information loss. We further construct MIME, a dedicated IMER benchmark with 2,000 video segments covering realistic fine-grained facial-detail degradation, severe facial degradation, and extreme full-modality loss, surpassing the limited coarse-grained settings of prior works. We also propose the Chain of Emotion (CoE) analysis paradigm with tailored metrics to dissect the fine-grained emotional cue perception and reasoning process of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). Extensive experiments on seven mainstream MLLMs reveal that body language and scene context act as critical compensatory mechanisms under facial degradation, while a pronounced gap exists between models' basic visual perception ability and high-level cognitive reasoning. Our work provides a focused evaluation platform and actionable insights for advancing robust multimodal emotion recognition. Our dataset and code are public at https://yuxinokk.github.io/MIME.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30288) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30288)

## 138. AdvMTO: Multimodal and Temporal Adversarial Object Attacks via 2D Gaussian Splatting for BEV-Based 3D Object Detection

- **作者**：Zhiyang Cui, Hao Tan, Ruonan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Adversarial object attacks pose a threat to BEV-based 3D object detectors in autonomous driving. Existing attacks typically target a single modality, coordinate camera and LiDAR observations at the current timestamp, or model temporal persistence mainly in camera streams. Without a unified construction of current and historical observations, attacks can leave part of the detector's accumulated evidence unsuppressed, limiting their effectiveness. In this paper, we propose AdvMTO, a 2D Gaussian Splatting (2DGS) framework for suppressing detection of persistent adversarial objects. Its oriented surfels encode appearance, geometry, and opacity in one object state, from which camera images and scanline LiDAR returns are generated. AdvMTO then constructs current and historical inputs through detector-native processing, regenerating LiDAR returns in each native source sweep for fusion detectors. We optimize bounded appearance perturbations for each detector, propagating gradients through historical BEV memory for temporal camera detectors. For fusion detectors, bounded geometry refinement along local normals further perturbs both sensing modalities. Experiments on nuScenes demonstrate effective suppression of traffic-cone detection across three BEV detectors. Temporal ablations further highlight the importance of detector-native history modeling for both attack construction and optimization.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30283) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30283)

## 139. RigidAnchor: Cross-View Geo-Localization for UAV via Iterative Planar Rigid Alignment

- **作者**：Bo Wang, Dekang Zhu, Jian Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A UAV that flies without GNSS has to estimate its absolute position and heading without any external signal. Most existing methods for UAV-to-satellite cross-view geo-localization (U-S CVGL) treat the task as image retrieval: they compress the UAV image and each satellite tile into separate descriptors, rank the tiles by their similarity, and return the center of the best tile. The output can therefore only lie on a pre-defined grid, its accuracy is limited by the tile size, and no heading is produced. Regressing the pose in a single pass removes the grid, but it leaves no chance to correct the estimate afterwards. We observe that satellite tiles are not independent samples. They are laid on the map at a fixed spacing, scale and orientation, so neighboring tiles join into one continuous satellite block with a consistent metric, and a nadir UAV view of that block differs from it by a planar rigid transform only, i.e. a 2D translation plus an in-plane rotation. We therefore reformulate U-S CVGL as solving for this transform between two images of different sizes. Position and heading then become parts of one geometric quantity, and the accuracy is no longer limited by the tile grid. We propose RigidAnchor, which solves the transform iteratively. The network encodes the two images and builds a multi-level correlation pyramid between them. At each iteration, it indexes the pyramid with every aerial cell carried through the current estimate, pools the descriptors of all cells into one hidden state, and lets two decoupled heads predict a translation increment and a rotation increment, the latter being composed rather than added. On Bearing-UAV-90K, the cross-view mean errors are 78.6% lower in position and 69.7% lower in heading than Bearing-UAV, and HSR@15 increases by 19.06 points. Code is available at https://github.com/wwsource/RigidAnchor.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30278) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30278)

## 140. OCCA-Seg: Object and Channel Context Aggregation for Semantic Segmentation of Remote Sensing Images

- **作者**：Gang Yang, Si Chen, Quan Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Semantic segmentation, which assigns a class label to each pixel, is a key task in computer vision. However, semantic segmentation of remote sensing images (RSIs) remains challenging because of complex scenes and objects at multiple scales, which limits segmentation accuracy. To address these challenges, we propose a context aggregation scheme called object and channel context aggregation (OCCA), consisting of three components: object contextual attention module (OCAM), channel contextual attention module (CCAM), and aggregation module (AM). Firstly, motivated by the observation that each pixel shares the class label of the object to which it belongs, object-level context represents each pixel through aggregating context from its corresponding object class. Secondly, because feature channels are correlated with object categories, channel-wise context models channel-wise contextual dependencies conditioned on object categories. Finally, the context information from OCAM and CCAM are fed into AM and used to weight and modulate the object context, enhancing the discriminative ability of the segmentation model. The experiments on several public remote sensing datasets including LoveDA, UAVid, ISPRS Potsdam, and Vaihingen datasets demonstrate competitive improvements over existing methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30267) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30267)

## 141. TriSegAgent: Context-Efficient 3D Annotation with MLLMs via Tri-Planes

- **作者**：Qingqiao Hu, Jialin Li, Meilong Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Interactive segmentation models turn a few clicks into a segmentation prediction across domains, so annotators only decide where the prediction still needs correction. Annotation thus becomes a loop of inspecting the prediction and clicking again. Each step of this loop is a visual judgment, which suits general-purpose multimodal large language model (MLLM) agents. In this work, we focus on letting these agents run this loop on 3D volumes. However, the MLLM in such an agent takes only 2D images as input, so the agent must observe the 3D volume in 2D. Since the loop calls the MLLM many times, this observation must be token-compact enough to keep every call cheap, yet informative enough to show error regions in the 3D prediction. We meet both needs with tri-planes, the three orthogonal planes that cross at one point in the volume. We show, both theoretically and empirically, that tri-planes are context-efficient yet keep sufficient detail to show error regions in the 3D prediction. Building on tri-planes, we introduce TriSegAgent, an MLLM agent that refines the 3D prediction iteratively. Within the agent, the MLLM only judges the prediction on tri-planes, while a visual harness turns each judgment into a 3D action in code. To make the loop reliable, the harness places each click at an exact voxel in the volume and checks whether to accept the 3D prediction. To evaluate the agent, we build a diverse benchmark of 690 regions of interest (ROIs) from 35 public medical datasets across three imaging modalities. With frontier MLLMs, TriSegAgent reaches an overall Dice about 10 points above the strongest baseline, and its lead is even larger on targets below 3 cm. With flash-level MLLMs, it outperforms a recent 3D segmentation agent built on a frontier MLLM.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30240) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30240)

## 142. AIGI-26: A Benchmark for AI-Generated Image Detection Across Diverse Modern Generators

- **作者**：Qishuai Ding, Yifeng Wang, Shichuang Xie et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Existing AI-generated image (AIGI) detection benchmarks predominantly rely on earlier synthesis systems with limited content diversity and conspicuous artifacts, failing to fully reflect the detection challenges posed by high-fidelity images from contemporary models. We introduce AIGI-26, a benchmark of 253,200 images covering 26 recent open- and closed-source generators. Its novel, diverse, and heterogeneous sources provide a basis for systematically evaluating detection methods on modern generated imagery. Additionally, to capture subtle statistical discrepancies across complementary forensic views, we propose the multi-aspect forensic detection network (MAFDNet). Its fixed forensic operators expose complementary texture, local prediction, resampling, and chromatic responses, summarized by a hierarchical encoder using multiscale means and standard deviations. In parallel, a spectral branch captures Fourier log-magnitude and neighboring-frequency phase variation across radial bands and directional axes. Fusing these descriptors combines local inconsistencies with global spectral organization for authenticity detection. Trained on 12,660 AIGI-26 images, MAFDNet achieves 88.5% accuracy and 94.9% average precision on the 240,540-image test set, with only 1.04M parameters and 1.39 GFLOPs per $256\times256$ input. MAFDNet also demonstrates effective cross-dataset generalization on DailyBench and Treasure-37.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30236) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30236)

## 143. PixelOne: From Options to Pixels in a Single Forward for GUI Grounding

- **作者**：Kanghui Tian, Shuai Dong, Yuhan Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

GUI grounding requires precise clicks at low latency. Recent models such as GUI-Decisions and GUI-Swift predict coordinates in one forward pass using fixed global bins with local refinement. However, screenshot downsampling removes detail needed to locate small controls, while fixed bins cover larger pixel ranges at higher resolutions. We introduce PixelOne, which transfers spatial knowledge from a search teacher to a student that predicts clicks directly from screenshots at native resolution. The teacher uses VideoJEV's text-option scorer to select rows and columns and progressively zoom into candidate regions. The student inherits the teacher's parameters and matches its distributions over explored regions through spatial distillation alongside box supervision. At inference, the same decision head selects a visual token, and a lightweight pixel head predicts the click within it, all in one forward pass. A click-aligned loss encourages pixels inside the target box to score above those outside. Across four benchmarks, PixelOne-Pro-35B-A3B achieves 83.8% mean accuracy, the highest among the evaluated single-forward grounders, with a mean latency of 0.43 s per target.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30235) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30235)

## 144. EditGRN: Inversion-Free Image Editing by Source-Target Generative Synchronization

- **作者**：Jiahuan Wang, Jianan Chen, Jian Han et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages,10 figures

Text-guided image editing faces a persistent trade-off between preserving source content and realizing coherent target semantics. We explore this challenge through training- and inversion-free image editing based on discrete generative models. Existing inversion-free discrete editors primarily rely on post-hoc probability correction, creating a disconnect between local token-level adjustments and globally coherent semantic generation. To bridge this gap, we introduce EditGRN, a training- and inversion-free framework with trajectory-wide source-target generative synchronization in discrete space. EditGRN adopts a dual-branch design---the source branch constructs source references and the target branch generates the target image-enabling trajectory-wide synchronization between the two. Exploiting GRN's ability to recover clean tokens from arbitrary binary corruptions, the source branch directly constructs corrupted source states at each refinement step to extract useful editing features, without recovering a specific source-generation trajectory. The target branch synchronizes with the source branch through the same corruption schedule, fusing the corresponding source context with editable representations intra-model. By synchronizing source context with target generation along the entire trajectory, our approach faithfully preserves the background while producing high-quality edits that blend seamlessly with the surrounding scene through intra-model fusion. PIE-Bench show state-of-the-art performance on background preservation and text alignment across diverse editing tasks against continuous and discrete baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30224) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30224)

## 145. RoomTokens: Masked Diffusion over Structured Tokens for Indoor Scene Synthesis

- **作者**：Xiaohao Sun, Angel X. Chang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：Project page: https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/RoomToken/

Generating 3D indoor scenes requires a representation that captures object semantics, instance identity, height structure, and orientation while respecting room geometry. Methods that model scenes as sequences of object vectors learn spatial constraints implicitly, leaving collisions and boundary violations to losses or post-processing; semantic-map methods make occupancy explicit, but collapse instance and directional structure. We introduce RoomTokens, a sliced structured-token representation in which each token stores semantic class, instance-color identity, and facing field. This representation allows a scene to be generated as a discrete grid while preserving cues for instance grouping, orientation estimation, and 3D extent prediction. To learn the RoomToken distribution, we propose MDIRT (Masked Diffusion over RoomTokens), a masked discrete diffusion model that denoises semantic/instance-color/facing channels jointly with a shared cross-slice backbone. Architectural tokens are clamped as explicit floor- plan constraints, so the model samples furniture layouts that respect room geometry while retaining instance and object-attribute structure. Experiments on 3D-FRONT show that RoomTokens with MDIRT improve layout fidelity and physical consistency over sequence/vector- and map-based methods, suggesting structured spatial tokens as a practical choice for discrete 3D scene synthesis.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30219) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30219)

## 146. Benchmarking Geospatial Foundation Models under Calibrated Label Errors

- **作者**：Mollah Md Saif, Showrin Rahman, Anindya Majumder et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：100 pages (15 pages main text and references, 85 pages appendix), 78 figures, 68 tables. Extended version including supplementary material

Earth-observation labels from maps, older products or hurried annotation arrive misaligned, coarse, loosely outlined or incomplete. Geospatial foundation models (GFMs) are nevertheless benchmarked with labels taken as correct. A practitioner therefore cannot tell which encoder to choose when the labels are imperfect. We present a benchmark protocol for fine-tuning under such labels. Four spatially structured error types are calibrated to equal label mIoU and applied identically to every model and seed. Every contrast is paired and read against an equivalence margin of $\pm 3$ points and a minimum detectable effect. We apply it to nine encoders, six decoder heads and ten datasets. In an exploratory reading at a matched learning rate the satellite-pretrained and web-pretrained DINOv3 encoders are equivalent within the margin on 8 of 9 datasets. Multispectral GFMs and an ImageNet ResNet-50 are equivalent within the margin on about half of them. Labels with random offsets cost the least at equal label quality. Under other decoder heads the verdicts mostly hold on FLAIR-2 and are decoder dependent on HLS Burn Scars. On four of five real label pairs the real label costs more than its matched simulated type. No choice of pretraining data makes an encoder consistently more robust, so the encoder is better chosen by its accuracy under imperfect labels.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30215) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30215)

## 147. ToWo-VTON: Take-Off Wear-On Virtual Try-On via Topology-aware Decoupling

- **作者**：Zhenchen Wan, Tianxi Chen, Runqi Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Virtual Try-On (VTON) aims to synthesize garment transfer without physical trials. Although recent methods perform well on upper-body garments, they still struggle in lower-body and full-body scenarios with complex occlusions and large silhouette changes. To address this limitation, we introduce ToWo (Take-Off Wear-On), a two-stage paradigm that decouples body structure estimation from garment synthesis. Take-Off first generates a tight-fitting body proxy that provides estimated structural guidance, and Wear-On then synthesizes the target garment conditioned on this prior. This decoupling reduces source-garment interference, supporting garment placement under substantial silhouette variations. Built on this paradigm, we propose ToWo-VTON, a unified topology-aware framework with structure-aware supervision. We construct the Body-Fitting DressCode (BFDC) dataset via body-aware diffusion and VLM-based filtering to provide body-proxy supervision across upper-, lower-, and full-body cases. We instantiate ToWo-VTON on both diffusion-based and MLLM-guided backbones. For diffusion-based models, we fine-tune on BFDC using the body proxy as the person input. For the MLLM-guided model, we use native backbone conditioning for the body proxy and target garment, and add Garment Boosting Attention (GBA) to preserve garment details. Experiments on VITON-HD and DressCode demonstrate improvements over the evaluated baselines, with KID reductions of 26.1% and 58.6% for diffusion-based and MLLM-guided implementations, respectively. The combined BFDC-training and Take-Off/Wear-On strategy also produces more coherent limb layouts and garment boundaries across the evaluated garment categories.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30203) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30203)

## 148. Quantization for Action, Execution for Deployment: Joint Precision and Execution Selection for World Action Models

- **作者**：Tianqi Li, Xin He, Xue Geng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

World action models guide robots by predicting the effects of actions or generating commands from observations. A model may compare hundreds of candidate futures before acting, repeatedly reading weights that must fit in device memory. Post-training quantization reduces the number of bits used to store pretrained weights, but a smaller model is not necessarily a deployable one: temporary tensors can exceed graphics processing unit (GPU) memory limits, while even small prediction errors can change the selected action. This creates two coupled challenges: identifying bit assignments that preserve task performance and determining which of them meet real execution constraints, without exhausting costly task trials. We present PTQ4WAM, a framework for choosing layer-wise bit assignments and execution routes under memory, latency and task-evaluation budgets. Calibration measurements guide candidate proposals, optionally with a language model agent. We profile full-model routes on the GPU before spending task trials on feasible assignments. Fusing group rescaling and reading visual history in place can make an otherwise infeasible assignment deployable. Across 15 workloads from four model families, the median task score is 99.2% of the corresponding 32-bit floating-point (FP32) score. On fixed GPU replays, the median speedup over FP32 is 2.36 times and the median reduction in peak allocated memory is 71.5%. The code will be released soon.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30195) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30195)

## 149. Visual-RFT++: Extending Visual Reinforcement Fine-Tuning to Multimodal Tool Use

- **作者**：Ziyu Liu, Zeyi Sun, Xiaoyi Dong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Our code is available at https://github.com/Liuziyu77/Visual-RFT

Reinforcement Fine-Tuning (RFT) has recently become a central paradigm for advancing Large Reasoning Models (LRMs), as demonstrated by systems such as OpenAI's o3 and the R1 series. Despite significant progress in LLMs, how to leverage RFT to unify visual perception, step-by-step reasoning, and agentic tool use into one coherent training paradigm remains an open challenge. In this work, we present Visual Reinforcement Fine-Tuning++ (Visual-RFT++), a unified framework that extends RFT to both visual perception tasks and multimodal agentic workflows, enabling LVLMs to reason with images, act upon images, and evaluate their own outputs using verifiable reward. With Visual-RFT++, open-source LVLMs not only show significant gains on diverse visual perception tasks, but also acquire agentic abilities such as web searching for real-time information and code-based image manipulation (e.g., cropping, rotation, and analysis). Across visual perception tasks, including fine-grained classification, few-shot detection, reasoning grounding, and open-vocabulary detection, Visual-RFT++ delivers strong gains over SFT, including a +24.3 percentage points improvement in one-shot fine-grained classification and +21.0 percentage points on COCO's 4-shot setting and +15.4 percentage points on LVIS with around several hundred samples. To evaluate agentic behaviors, we introduce the Multimodal Agentic Tool Bench (MAT) with MAT-Search and MAT-Coding. Visual-RFT++ surpasses its baseline by +18.6 F1 / +13.0 EM percentage points on MAT-Coding and +10.3 F1 / +8.7 EM percentage points on MAT-Search. It also shows gains of 29.3 F1 and 25.9 EM percentage points on multi-hop QA benchmarks such as 2Wiki and HotpotQA, demonstrating strong cross-modal generalization. Visual-RFT++ introduces a data-efficient and reward-driven framework that strengthens both visual reasoning and agentic adaptability.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30193) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30193)

## 150. OmniSenMo: A Unified Multisensory Language Model for Generalizable Human Motion Estimation, Understanding, and Prediction

- **作者**：Cheng Guo, Qiming Cao, Pu Perry Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project website: https://ch3ngguo.github.io/omnisenmo/

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) estimate, understand, and predict human motion, but mainly from RGB cameras. Depth, LiDAR, mmWave, and WiFi complement RGB under poor lighting, through occlusions, and in privacy-sensitive applications, but existing multisensory models cover only some of these sensing tasks. Covering all of them from any sensor subset is challenging, since the tokens of heterogeneous sensors are semantically inconsistent and spatially misaligned, paired multisensory data are scarce, and the tasks need different temporal granularities. We present OmniSenMo, a multisensory language model that takes the human skeleton as a common reference for all sensors. Its skeleton-grounded motion representation turns each sensor into joint tokens, one per body joint, so that any sensor subset is fused joint by joint and expressed in the motion vocabulary that the language model learned from motion capture and text. We pretrain it on all five sensors generated by our multisensory simulator, and Multi-Granularity Observation gives each task the frames it needs. On a cross-action split of MM-Fi, the same model with all five sensors outperforms the models compared on each task, including LEO-Fuse in pose estimation (84.1 vs. 89.0 mm MPJPE) and HoloLLM in unseen-action recognition (69.2% vs. 42.8%). Even without RGB, it outperforms both methods that use RGB, and in a cross-dataset validation on XRF55, it recognizes unseen actions far better than HoloLLM.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30176) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30176)

## 151. Audio-Visual Rearrangement

- **作者**：Ruohao Guo, Jiatong Ye, Chenwei Dong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM, cs.SD

We introduce Audio-Visual Rearrangement (AVR), a novel task in which an agent hears a spoken request, retrieves a suitable object, and delivers it to the requesting user or an instruction-specified destination. AVR requires the agent to understand the spoken request and acoustically localize the speaker for delivery. To support this task, we construct a dataset covering three query types: explicit target specification, speaker-relative spatial references, and implicit user intent, spanning eight subtypes with diverse object selection and delivery requirements. Moreover, we propose a framework that integrates a motion-aware spatial audio module into an audio-visual large language model to encode spatial cues from the spoken request and update them as the agent moves. We further incorporate auxiliary supervision for speech understanding, spatial audio perception, and visual grounding through multitask instruction tuning to improve action prediction. Experiments establish baseline performance in both seen and unseen environments, laying the groundwork for future research on embodied intelligence and human-robot interaction. The code will be publicly available soon.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30171) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30171)

## 152. GUI-Q: Task-Sensitive Granularity and Relation-Preserving Quantization for GUI Agents

- **作者**：Tianqi Li, Yang Wang, Xin He et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Graphical user interface (GUI) agents enable automated interactions with mobile, web, and desktop interfaces. However, their large memory footprint and repeated model calls hinder deployment on resource-constrained devices, motivating post-training quantization (PTQ) without full retraining. Quantization can degrade GUI response accuracy and distort the instruction-vision affinities and local spatial relations needed for GUI target selection. Small coordinate errors can cause missed clicks, while early action errors can alter subsequent interface states and disrupt task execution. Local reconstruction error alone does not directly assess whether these responses and relations are preserved. We propose GUI-Q, a PTQ framework that targets response and relation preservation with 4-bit weights and 16-bit activations (W4A16). Task-Sensitive Granularity Allocation (TSGA) allocates finer groups under a scale-byte budget by combining response sensitivity with the measured reduction in activation-weighted distortion. Relation-Preserving Symmetry Selection (RPSS) then selects symmetric or asymmetric grids to preserve instruction-vision affinities and local spatial relations in GUIs. Across three GUI-capable models, GUI-Q improves mean grounding TargetHit by 8.04 to 12.55 points over the strongest evaluated 4-bit configurations, while reducing static peak graphics processing unit (GPU) memory by 49.9% to 56.2% relative to bfloat16 (BF16). Fixed-output replay with packed 4-bit execution provides 1.7 to 2.8 times model-call speedups. The code will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30170) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30170)

## 153. PathStream: Towards Streaming Diagnostic Copilots for Whole-Slide Pathology

- **作者**：Dankai Liao, YuFeng Wu, Ziyue Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Pathologists reach a diagnosis by integrating morphological evidence across regions and magnifications along viewing trajectories. Existing pathology AI rarely reflects this process: models predict from selected image regions, answer questions within fixed visual contexts, or navigate slides autonomously. We formalize pathologist-led streaming diagnosis, in which the pathologist controls navigation while a copilot selectively describes incoming views and synthesizes accumulated evidence into a final diagnosis. We record five pathologists' viewing trajectories on 1,791 TCGA whole-slide images (WSIs) spanning ten organs, with response labels, morphological descriptions, and session-level findings and diagnoses. We construct PathStreamBench to assess response timing, morphological description, and session-level diagnosis, alongside auxiliary video understanding. We introduce PathStream, which jointly learns streaming responses and diagnosis through two-stage training, with a diagnosis-attention loss guiding attention during diagnosis to relevant visual and textual evidence accumulated as the examination unfolds. PathStream outperforms 14 vision-language models and three domain-fine-tuned baselines on the core tasks, achieving a trajectory WSI Relevance score of 77.87, surpassing the strongest domain-fine-tuned baseline's 62.76. Without additional training, its diagnostic capabilities generalize to new educational pathology videos, three external centers, and an unseen organ.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30167) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30167)

## 154. Multimodal View Training: Learning More Through Different Modalities

- **作者**：Devang Kulshreshtha, Priyanka Kaswan, Minh Pham et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Can a language model learn more from the same information simply by encountering it in different forms? We answer this in the affirmative by introducing Multimodal View Training (MVT), a simple training approach that presents identical information through text, speech, and images, without requiring new facts or supervision. Starting from a text sample, MVT generates corresponding audio and rendered-text image views, and trains a multimodal language model to produce the same target output across all three views. To further bring these benefits to text-only large language models (LLMs), we train lightweight connector modules to pretrained audio and vision encoders, allowing the LLM to learn from multimodal inputs while retaining its text-only architecture at inference. Across question answering, machine unlearning, and knowledge editing, adaptive MVT improves QA recall by +15.2 percentage points, forget quality by +40.7 points, and editing generality by +25.2 points over text-only training. Combining MVT with standard augmentation further improves QA and editing generality. Our theoretical analysis illustrates how training across modalities can shift weight updates toward shared factual content and reduce their reliance on features specific to each view.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30165) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30165)

## 155. WaDiS: Boundary-Preserving Diffusion Segmentation via Multi-Scale Wavelet Conditioning and Frequency-Aware Gating

- **作者**：Qiang Gao, Yi Wang, Yong Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Diffusion models have shown strong potential in medical image segmentation due to their generative capabilities and robustness. However, existing methods often struggle to preserve fine anatomical boundaries and to retain multi-scale structural information due to the loss of spatial and frequency details during feature encoding, particularly in complex clinical imaging conditions. This work presents WaDiS, a wavelet-powered conditional latent diffusion framework for medical image segmentation that addresses these limitations through multi-scale wavelet conditioning and frequency-aware information filtering for boundary preservation. The model adopts a multi-scale wavelet conditioning encoder to retain rich structural features, leveraging its lossless nature to better preserve semantic priors critical to the diffusion process. In addition, a Frequency-Aware Gating Block (FAGB) is introduced into the skip connections of the diffusion U-Net, enabling adaptive spectral filtering and residual fusion to better emphasize boundary structures while reducing irrelevant content. To improve generalization across clinical domains, we adopt a structure-preserving color augmentation strategy during training. Specifically, global color statistics are transferred between samples in the LAB color space, allowing the model to decouple appearance from structure and focus more on anatomical shape rather than color bias. Experiments on five benchmark datasets demonstrate that WaDiS consistently outperforms existing diffusion-based and conventional segmentation methods, with notable improvements in boundary accuracy, robustness to noise, and generalization under color variation. The code for this work will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30164) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30164)

## 156. OpenViVE: New Benchmark and Dual-Stream Co-Attention Framework for Vietnamese Visual Entailment

- **作者**：Bao Quoc Trinh, Tin Van Huynh, Kiet Van Nguyen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL

Visual Entailment (VE) determines whether a textual hypothesis is entailed, contradicted, or unverifiable from an image. However, existing VE benchmarks are largely English-centric and do not capture Vietnamese-specific linguistic distinctions or culturally grounded visual contexts. To address this gap, we introduce OpenViVE, the first large-scale native Vietnamese VE benchmark, containing 62,268 balanced image-hypothesis pairs from 10,378 culturally grounded images. OpenViVE is constructed using an Artifact-Aware Hybrid LLM-Human Pipeline that combines constrained generation, human validation, and PMI-guided refinement. The pipeline substantially reduces hypothesis-only shortcuts, with Contradiction accuracy dropping from 96% to 66% after artifact refinement. We further evaluate a dual-stream PhoBERT-ConvNeXt framework with bidirectional co-attention to align visual evidence with Vietnamese hypotheses. The framework achieves 84.84% accuracy and 84.90% macro-F1 on the test set, while PhoBERT consistently outperforms multilingual XLM-R variants. These results highlight the importance of language-specific encoding and explicit cross-modal interaction for Vietnamese VE.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30161) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30161)

## 157. UUBench: Understanding the Unspoken toward Full-Duplex Multimodal Interaction

- **作者**：Ye Pan, Chenfei Liao, Bin Ren et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：49 pages, 37 figures, 9 tables, including appendices. Preview version; work in progress

Effective real-time, full-duplex multimodal interaction requires systems to understand not only what users say, but also the communicative needs conveyed through prosody, facial expressions, gestures, and context, and to generate audiovisual responses that appropriately address those needs. Existing evaluations offer limited insight into whether systems translate an understanding of such unspoken needs into effective responses. We introduce UUBench, a benchmark for assessing how well multimodal interactive systems Understand the Unspoken intent and address users' implicit communicative needs. The inherent subjectivity of implicit intent inference poses a key challenge for evaluation. To address this, we introduce Process-Oriented Scoring, which grounds the assessment of intent fulfillment in observable auditory and visual cues, such as prosody and gestures. Rather than relying solely on a single overall score, it evaluates how well a response addresses the communicative needs conveyed by each applicable primitive dimension. We aggregate the dimension-level scores according to each dimension's relevance to the annotated evidence. Beyond intent fulfillment, Outcome-Oriented Scoring evaluates the naturalness, appropriateness, and human-likeness of the reply itself under audio-only, visual-only, and audiovisual conditions. To make these judgments traceable to observable evidence, we propose a Fact-Grounded Scoring JEV module, which uses structured, timestamped audiovisual observations of the user and the digital human's reply to assess one dimension or criterion at a time. UUBench provides a foundation for assessing whether multimodal interactive systems respond effectively to meaning beyond literal wording, supporting progress toward context-aware, real-time, full-duplex interaction.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30157) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30157)

## 158. Preserve to Compress: Beyond Low-Dimensional Latents in One-Step Diffusion Codec

- **作者**：Chuqin Zhou, Xiaoyue Ling, Yuhang Wei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Pretrained latent diffusion models bring powerful generative priors to ultra-low-bitrate image compression, but their latent spaces reflect a reconstruction--generation tradeoff. Low-dimensional latents reduce modeling cost but can discard image details, while higher-dimensional latents improve reconstruction yet make diffusion optimization harder under a fixed computational budget. Generative codecs that adopt these pretrained spaces also inherit this compromise, even though compression requires fidelity to a specific source image. In particular, low-dimensional latent interfaces can discard source details before coding and restrict how much decoded information reaches the image decoder. We propose Preserving Information for Compression and Synthesis (PICS), a one-step generative image compression framework that adapts these interfaces to preserve source information. Our Information-Preserving Adaptors (IPAs) expose richer features for coding and retain decoded information throughout reconstruction, while deferred decoder adaptation stabilizes training and helps the image decoder exploit the expanded representations. Experiments on Kodak, DIV2K, and CLIC 2020 show average BD-rate savings of 52.64% and 39.61% over StableCodec at matched LPIPS and DISTS, respectively.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30154) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30154)

## 159. LeadVLA: Learning Follower-Aware Route Execution for Robot Leading

- **作者**：Tianle Zeng, Hanjing Ye, Bingyi Xia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://louiszengCN.github.io/LeadVLA_PAGE/

Robot leading requires executing a route while continuously adapting to a designated follower. Existing robot-leading systems often mediate follower understanding and physical route execution through explicit states, planners, or modular interfaces; directly learning this coupling through end-to-end trajectory prediction can instead entangle route execution with follower response. We introduce LeadVLA, a structured multimodal policy that preserves the route as an explicit Path reference while learning a Pace for route progress and a Residual for local follower- and scene-conditioned adaptation. To make this problem trainable and measurable, we develop LeadInfra, a configurable Unreal Engine-based closed-loop data-generation infrastructure from which we instantiate an approximately 4M-sample training corpus, and LeadBench, a standardized benchmark with 108 fully held-out closed-loop episodes. LeadVLA achieves the highest task success (39.6%) among evaluated robot policies on LeadBench while maintaining strong route fidelity and follower responsiveness, and transfers to unseen real-world environments without real-world policy fine-tuning. Project Page: https://louiszengCN.github.io/LeadVLA_PAGE/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30149) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30149)

## 160. RMR-VLA: Recurrent Memory Reasoning for Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Yiheng Du, Jingqi Tian, Xu Jiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Long-horizon, history-dependent robotic manipulation often requires acting on visual cues that have disappeared, such as an object indicated earlier or a target hidden under a moving cup. The key challenge is to maintain what past evidence means for the next action as the scene changes. We introduce Recurrent Memory Reasoning (RMR), an explicit reasoning step that connects memory to action inside a vision-language-action model (VLA), with no external reasoning model. At each update, the VLA first infers an event from visual evidence, then revises the task state and grounds a subgoal in the current scene to guide action generation. Carrying the state and subgoal across updates lets remembered identities and task progress determine the current target, doubling RoboMME success over an otherwise identical model without recurrence. Joint training lets one VLA both reason and act; because its memory decisions are text tokens with exact likelihoods, GRPO refines them from rollout rewards without making action generation stochastic. Among the compared methods, RMR-VLA achieves the best results on RoboMME (66.46% overall, 79.83% on the Reference category) and LIBERO-Mem (90.08%).

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30145) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30145)

## 161. Separating Protocol Effects from Spatio-Temporal Evidence in Person Re-Identification Re-Ranking

- **作者**：Mochamad Rizal Fauzan, Doan Pham Thao Nhu, Jeslin Konthoujam et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：11 pages, 7 figures, 9 tables. Code: https://github.com/rizalfanex/spatiotemporal-bayesian-reid

Spatio-temporal priors re-rank person re-identification matches using the camera pair and the time gap, and are reported to add large gains on Market-1501. We ask how much of such a gain encodes the evaluation protocol rather than learned spatio-temporal evidence. Starting from a course-project re-ranker that adds log camera-transition and log time-gap terms to cosine similarity, we train a strong ResNet-50 baseline (Rank-1 94.3 %, mAP 86.2 % over three seeds), choose the fusion weights on an identity-disjoint validation split and evaluate on the official test set with control priors. The repository form adds 2.30 mAP points, but a one-parameter penalty on same-camera pairs alone adds 2.19: the protocol never places a true match in the query's camera, so the penalty removes distractors for free. The time term of that form is almost unused; a pair-level diagnostic traces this to its modelling the time gap given a match without dividing by the gap distribution of all candidate pairs. A likelihood-ratio prior adds 4.04 points without the penalty and 5.62 with it; shuffling the time stamps removes the extra gain, so about 3.43 points are tied to identity. The prior fixes the first-ranked match of 4.2 % of the queries and breaks it for 0.1 %. The gain shrinks as the visual baseline improves (11.9 to 5.62 points between a 70.5 % and a 86.2 % mAP model) and concentrates on queries whose true matches share the query's video sequence. The study covers one dataset, one backbone and three seeds.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30142) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30142)

## 162. AxE: Benchmarking Agent-Environment Generalization of Video World Models via Grounded Events

- **作者**：Haiyu Xu, Yiyang Chen, Jiayi Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://AxEBench.github.io

Edge cases are not the long tail; they are the norm. An embodied system deployed across changing environments, bodies, objects, and tasks will routinely encounter combinations that were absent from its training data. General-purpose physical intelligence therefore requires world models that reuse prior knowledge, predict consequences, and adapt with little or no additional training. Yet current evaluations of conditional video generation emphasize visual quality, prompt alignment, or isolated physical plausibility, leaving systematic generalization difficult to measure. We introduce AxE Bench, a benchmark that organizes condition changes along eight factors of the acting agent and of its environment, and pairs every original condition with controlled variants that change one side or both. WBench-plus applies the same structure to an existing multi-turn benchmark, pairing each WBench scenario with variants on the environment side, through its world setting or camera behavior, or on the agent side, through its interaction instructions. Additionally, to score generated futures, we develop an evaluation harness that combines vision-language reasoning with targeted computer-vision evidence, converting each video into temporally localized events and comparing generated events with reference events in both time and semantics. We summarize each model with the AxE Score, event Fidelity weighted by its Stability across original-variant pairs. Across twenty models, generated events match reference events far more often in timing than in content: no model exceeds a Fidelity of 13.01, the highest Stability is 0.55, and the highest AxE Score is 7.10. Together, these components turn world-model generalization from an informal aspiration into a falsifiable evaluation program for real and simulated interactions. Our project website can be found at: AxEBench.github.io

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30120) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30120)

## 163. What Remains After Concept Erasure? Sparse Autoencoder Probes for Auditing Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Zhuan Shi, Lu Wei, Yuta Nakashima et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Concept erasure aims to remove selected visual concepts from text-to-image diffusion models while preserving the generation of non-target concepts. Evaluation typically measures target suppression and non-target preservation in generated images, but a concept's absence from these outputs does not establish its removal from the model. We introduce SAE-Audit, a white-box audit using sparse autoencoder (SAE) probes to test whether activation interventions can restore target-concept generation without changing model weights or test prompts. After verifying target suppression, we compare the original and edited models using a shared SAE representation learned from original-model activations, identifying features whose target-related responses weaken after erasure. We inject sparse directions derived from the original-model responses into the edited model, keeping these directions fixed across test prompts, and evaluate recovery on held-out scene families. Experiments span six erasure methods and four domains: objects, artistic styles, copyrighted content, and nudity. Interventions using a few dozen selected SAE features yield higher average recovery rates than matched random-feature interventions, with recovery concentrated in objects and nudity following cross-attention edits. Most styles and copyrighted concepts show limited recovery under the tested intervention settings. These findings show that output suppression can coexist with responsiveness to sparse concept directions supplied from the original model.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30117) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30117)

## 164. Thermal-Guided Low-Light UAV Image Enhancement: A Reliability-Aware Guidance Network and a Unified Benchmark

- **作者**：Zhicheng Zhao, Yu Wang, Jiangong Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 12 figures, 9 tables

Thermal-guided low-light image enhancement (LLIE) is promising for nighttime unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) perception because thermal imagery remains informative under severe visible degradation. However, the degraded visible image serves as both the alignment reference and the enhancement target, making thermal guidance uncertain in its spatial correspondence and local utility under low illumination, sensor differences, and UAV motion. Misaligned or unsuitable thermal cues may introduce artifacts, while spatially varying degradation requires region-specific restoration rather than uniform enhancement. To address these challenges, we propose a Reliability-Aware Multi-Strategy Guidance Network (RMGNet) for thermal-guided UAV LLIE under weak optical-thermal alignment. Its Reliability-Aware Guidance Calibration Module (RAGCM) performs coarse-to-fine displacement estimation and jointly evaluates the reliability of aligned thermal guidance and the usability of degraded visible evidence. Based on calibrated guidance, degradation, and reliability cues, the Multi-Strategy Enhancement Routing Module (MSERM) generates candidates for detail preservation, highlight-protected relighting, and thermal compensation, then spatially routes their contributions. We further construct the UAV weakly aligned Optical-Thermal dataset (UWOT), the largest UAV-oriented benchmark to date for weakly aligned optical-thermal low-light enhancement, containing 5,000 triplets. Extensive experiments show that RMGNet outperforms representative single-modal and multimodal state-of-the-art methods in enhancement quality while retaining competitive computational efficiency and greater robustness to practical cross-modal misalignment. The dataset and code will be publicly available at https://github.com/mmic-lcl/Datasets-and-benchmark-code.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30114) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30114)

## 165. LMnOP: Pruning 3D Gaussian Splatting via Leave-Many-Out Importance

- **作者**：Sunjae Jeong, Jaeseung Kim, Hyoseok Hwang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：9 pages, 5 figures, 5 tables

3D Gaussian Splatting renders photorealistic views in real time. However, a single scene often needs millions of Gaussians, so these models are costly to store and render. Pruning reduces this cost by keeping a subset of the Gaussians. A common score is the leave-one-out importance, which is the loss increase when a Gaussian alone is removed. At high pruning ratios, many Gaussians are removed together, so Gaussians that are redundant alone but essential together are lost. We present LMnOP (Leave-Many-Out Pruning), which estimates the importance of each Gaussian under the removal of the other Gaussians. This leave-many-out importance is the average loss increase when a Gaussian is removed while the others are also removed at random. We compute the exact mean and variance of each pixel's color under random removal in closed form, and approximate the importance from them without sampling removal patterns. We refine each Gaussian's probability of remaining with its importance and prune once at the end. Without fine-tuning, LMnOP achieves the best results in 16 of 18 metrics on three datasets at 90% and 99% removal. It improves SSIM and LPIPS over GaussianPOP in all 13 scenes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30102) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30102)

## 166. Agent on Guard: Self-Improving Runtime Intervention for Robust Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Gengming Cui, Mingfei Han, Jiawei Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Robot foundation policies provide learned execution capabilities, while programming agents can reason about failures and construct recovery behaviors. Combining them effectively requires learning when intervention helps and when the policy can reliably resume. This challenge is inherently temporal: the physical world continues to evolve during reasoning, and a useful recovery may become infeasible before a decision takes effect. We introduce Agent on Guard (AoG), a hierarchical framework that improves collaboration between a frozen base policy and a programming agent through physical experience. An evolving outer planner allocates control as the physical state evolves, aligning decisions with the physical clock with attention to observation freshness, remaining recovery windows, and the time required for reasoning, recovery, and subsequent execution. Coupled inner loops jointly refine intervention timing, recovery skills, and handoff conditions. Rather than judging recovery by local correction alone, AoG uses downstream task progress, failure recurrence, final outcomes, and resource consumption to improve both recovery behaviors and scheduling. This turns temporary agent solutions into reusable collaboration capabilities that complement the frozen policy. On RoboDojo-Sim, AoG achieves a success rate of 50.57% and a task score of 57.26, exceeding the strongest evaluated baseline by 11.22 percentage points and 10.68 points, respectively. It also reduces end-to-end episode latency by 80.4% and online token consumption by 85.1% relative to the GPT-6 Astra agent.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30101) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30101)

## 167. Policy-intrinsic Demonstration Curation

- **作者**：Sunjae Jeong, Geunhyeok Yu, Dowoon Lee et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：15 pages (including 1 page of references and 6 pages of appendices), 5 figures, 7 tables

Fine-tuning a robot policy for a new target task requires target demonstrations, but only a few are available. Demonstrations of other tasks can fill this gap, and demonstration curation selects the useful ones among these candidates. Retrieval-based curation selects the candidates that are most similar to the target demonstrations. Since it does not use the policy, any candidate that looks similar receives a high score. Performance-aware curation therefore estimates the effect of each candidate on the policy. However, this requires training on the candidates and takes a long time. We propose a third branch, policy-intrinsic curation, which uses the policy and still takes little time. It estimates this effect with forward passes of the pretrained policy and needs no training. We instantiate this branch with PICKER (Policy-Intrinsic Curation from Known Errors). It selects the candidates on which the policy makes errors that agree with those on the target demonstrations. PICKER reaches success rates comparable to those of performance-aware curation and takes about as long as retrieval-based curation. Its selection is also stable across seeds.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30099) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30099)

## 168. When "A or B" Is Less Likely than A: Coherent Probabilities for Vision Decision Models

- **作者**：Seongrae Noh, SeongEun Hong, HyeongYeop Kang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Project page: https://seongraenoh.github.io/core/

Vision decision models answer a multiple-choice question about an image with one probability per option, and software uses them directly. We show that the probabilities depend on how the options are grouped: a merged option "A or B" receives on average 11.5 points less probability than the sum of A and B. This under-additivity appears at every model size from 0.85B to 26B and remains after fine-tuning on labels. We propose CORE, which asks each training question with its options regrouped and trains the answers to all these questions toward one target distribution over the original options, summed into each question's groups. On 1,050 items from seven benchmarks, CORE reduces the total variation distance between the answer to a regrouped question and the original answer summed into the same groups from 14.8 to 5.8 points on the 0.85B model, against 9.4 for the best baseline trained on the same questions. At all five model sizes, CORE reduces this distance without lowering accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30096) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30096)

## 169. RadarOSV: Unified 3D Scene and Motion Perception from mmWave Radars

- **作者**：Yuan Fang, Long Zhuang, Xijia Wei et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Millimeter-wave radar offers weather-resilient sensing for 3D occupancy prediction. Existing radar occupancy methods mainly model geometry, with limited joint modeling of semantics and motion. Cascaded imaging radar provides fine spatial detail but has a limited unambiguous velocity range, whereas single-chip radar offers a wider range but coarser spatial resolution. We propose RadarOSV, to our knowledge the first radar-only framework that jointly predicts occupancy, dynamic object semantics, and ground-relative velocity from raw radar data in unified voxels, by exploiting complementary measurements from both radars. Its Polar-to-Cartesian Geometry (PCG) backbone maps polar imaging-radar features into Cartesian voxels through fixed and deformable sampling, guiding cross-radar fusion for geometry and attribute prediction. Single-chip detection outputs provide class evidence, while raw Doppler spectra supply motion features and radial-velocity candidates. The velocity readout selects from these candidates using fused features to resolve aliasing, then combines the selected radial estimate with learned motion. Ego-motion compensation uses radar-estimated velocity, so inference requires no external odometry or other sensors. On K-Radar, single-frame PCG achieves 40.7% mIoU within 12.8 m, compared with RadarOcc's reported 34.3%. On the BRIDGE-Liv dataset, compared with a cascaded-radar baseline predicting the same outputs with external ego velocity, RadarOSV improves macro-averaged accuracy across four dynamic object classes within annotated object regions from 63.1% to 75.5% and reduces object radial-velocity MAE against manual Doppler annotations from 1.03 to 0.51 m/s, while maintaining geometric occupancy mIoU.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30078) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30078)

## 170. SF-Flow: Spectral Flow Matching with High Structural Fidelity for Hallucination-Suppressed Scientific Image Generation

- **作者**：Zehua Wang, Patxi Fernandez-Zelaia, Mohamed Wahib et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Scientific image generation focuses on reproducing experimental appearance while following a prescribed spatial layout. A key challenge is acute data sparsity, which requires operating with unpaired collections of materials (real images and prescribed layouts), where no direct supervision is feasible. Work on unpaired translation has shown that it is possible to reproduce the texture and intensity variations of real images, but it is extremely hard to retain the intended phase boundaries in the prescribed locations. This paper tackles this challenge and introduces SF-Flow, a flow-matching model trained only on real material images that imposes spectral constraints during generation, producing images that are high-fidelity and consistent with the prescribed layouts. Because layouts enter only at generation time, the approach extends to layout maps outside the distribution of any available training layouts. We evaluate the method on synchrotron tomography of concrete, lens-coupled laboratory microCT of powders, and industrial XCT of additively manufactured metal. Without instrument-specific modeling, we show that SF-Flow achieves substantially higher template agreement and joint fidelity--structure score than previous methods, including contrastive unpaired translation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30073) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30073)

## 171. SalivaryHSI-Seg: A New Challenging Benchmark for Ex Vivo Salivary Gland Hyperspectral Tissue Segmentation

- **作者**：Xiaoqi Guo, Xianran Wang, Xinquan Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Salivary gland tumors are common lesions in oral and maxillofacial practice, and accurate identification of tumor regions and surrounding salivary gland tissues is important for tissue assessment, pathological sampling, and clinical decision support. Hyperspectral imaging (HSI) records continuous spectral responses together with spatial imaging information, providing a promising way to capture fine-grained tissue-discriminative information. Nevertheless, the development of salivary gland HSI tissue segmentation remains limited due to the lack of standardized benchmarks with pixel-level annotations and unified evaluation protocols. To address this issue, we introduce SalivaryHSI-Seg, a hyperspectral tissue segmentation benchmark for ex vivo salivary gland specimens, containing 119 HSI samples from 31 patients with multi-class pixel-level annotations. We establish a patient-level five-fold cross-validation protocol and evaluate different input representations, model architectures, and challenging subsets with small tumor and complex boundary. Based on this benchmark, we further propose HyBSD, a hyperspectral boundary-guided spectral-difference network for full-HSI salivary gland tissue segmentation. HyBSD exploits boundary-guided spectral-difference cues to improve tissue delineation in ambiguous transition regions. Extensive experiments show that full-HSI input provides more discriminative information than sampled spectrums, and that HyBSD achieves 74.97% mDice and 87.76% Tumor Dice, with further advantages on the challenging subsets. Dataset and code will be released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30071) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30071)

## 172. Pixel as the Common Language: Unified Multimodal Embedding via Visual Rendering

- **作者**：Xu Yuan, Pangjing Wu, Wenqi Fan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.IR

Multimodal embedding models map heterogeneous inputs into a shared representation space, yet text-only, image-only, and composed inputs are still presented in distinct formats and follow different routes through the model. This fragmentation prevents heterogeneous tasks from jointly optimizing the same end-to-end encoding process, limiting supervision transfer across input configurations. To address this issue, we propose unification before encoding and instantiate this paradigm in RenderEmbed. The framework uses Visual Substrate Rendering to convert heterogeneous inputs into a common visual substrate for encoding with a VLM. From the resulting visual tokens, Progressive Hierarchical Anchor Encoding produces compact retrieval representations by interleaving learnable anchors and fusing their states across layers, retaining broad context and fine-grained cues. Interventional Rendering Learning trains these representations to remain sensitive to content changes and stable across rendering variations through complementary semantic and rendering interventions. Extensive experiments on MMEB and ViDoRe v2, spanning diverse retrieval tasks, show that RenderEmbed outperforms prior state-of-the-art models at the same scale and exhibits strong out-of-domain generalization. Compared with MetaEmbed, RenderEmbed improves MMEB Overall Precision@1 by 1.7 points, with a larger 2.1-point gain on out-of-domain tasks. Transfer and scaling analyses further show that a shared visual pathway enables more effective learning from heterogeneous supervision.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30069) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30069)

## 173. FolioWeaver: A LayerGraph-Grounded Agent for Layered Image Generation and Editing

- **作者**：Wentao Lu, Shengzhe Cui, Xihao Wu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures, 6 tables

Layered image generation separates images into compositable layers, allowing users to manipulate individual elements independently. However, such asset-level editability does not enable the generation system to interpret successive feedback, perform targeted edits, and preserve unaffected content. We introduce FolioWeaver, a LayerGraph-grounded agent that unifies layered-image generation and iterative editing through a persistent Executable Scene State. FolioWeaver constructs a LayerGraph and generates independent visual assets conditioned on a shared scene reference. An Editing Agent performs feedback-driven modifications and self-repair through scoped actions, selective layer execution, and visual verification, enabling targeted updates while reusing unchanged assets. We also publicly release LayerFolio, a dataset of 100,377 human-reviewed layered scenes spanning six visual domains, together with two evaluation protocols: AtomicQA for measuring requirement satisfaction and CBLP for assessing layer organization. Experiments demonstrate that FolioWeaver achieves superior layer organization and composite fidelity compared with direct layered-generation methods. Analyses validate the contributions of component adaptation, execution-grounded repair, and cross-domain training. FolioWeaver advances layered-image generation toward persistent, controllable, and iterative visual creation. The dataset is available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/FolioWeaver/LayerFolio.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30067) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30067)

## 174. Regularizing Hard Examples Improves Adversarial Robustness

- **作者**：Hyungyu Lee, Saehyung Lee, Ho Bae et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：48 pages, 16 figures, 22 tables. Published in the Journal of Machine Learning Research (JMLR)

Recent studies have validated that pruning hard-to-learn examples from training improves the generalization performance of neural networks (NNs). In this study, we investigate this intriguing phenomenon -- the negative effect of hard examples on generalization -- in adversarial training. Particularly, we theoretically demonstrate that the increase in the difficulty of hard examples in adversarial training is significantly greater than the increase in the difficulty of easy examples. Furthermore, we verify that hard examples are only fitted through memorization of the label in adversarial training. We conduct both theoretical and empirical analyses of this memorization phenomenon, showing that pruning hard examples in adversarial training can enhance the model's robustness. However, the challenge remains in finding the optimal threshold for removing hard examples that degrade robustness performance. Based upon these observations, we propose a new approach, difficulty proportional label smoothing (DPLS), to adaptively mitigate the negative effect of hard examples, thereby improving the adversarial robustness of NNs. Notably, our experimental result indicates that our method can successfully leverage hard examples while circumventing the negative effect.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30065) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30065)

## 175. Beyond Binary Correctness: A Surrogate for Confidence Learning in Large Language and Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Chen Li, Xiaoling Hu, Chao Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Confidence estimation for large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs) aims to assess how likely a particular generated response is to be correct. We investigate how learning this assessment can benefit from the model's predictive reliability on the underlying query. Binary correctness supervision, however, assigns identical labels to occasional errors and frequent failures, as well as isolated successes and consistent success. These outcomes can arise from substantially different predictive behavior under stochastic decoding. We propose a confidence-learning framework that supplements realized response correctness with query-level predictive reliability. We estimate reliability through repeated generations under a fixed model and decoding configuration, then combine the empirical success rate with individual correctness to construct a graded confidence surrogate. The surrogate differentiates responses sharing the same correctness label while preserving the average predictive reliability of the sampled response pool. To learn this target, we condition an independent confidence estimator on the query and its full generated response, and optimize backbone LoRA adapters together with a projection and scalar confidence head using mean squared error. Repeated sampling is required only for supervision construction; inference scores an individual response without additional generations. We establish ordering and mean preservation properties of the surrogate and derive a selective-risk bound connecting population surrogate prediction error to the error rate among accepted responses. We evaluate the framework across language and vision-language models, examining confidence calibration and selective prediction.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30059) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30059)

## 176. FireDesmoke: Fire Smoke Removal via Real Smoke Transfer and Semantic Rectification

- **作者**：Chih-Chung Hsu, Rong-Lin Jian
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages

Fire smoke removal must restore dense and medium smoke regions while preserving the clear (smoke-free) content. Existing methods either leave the smoke or alter the visible content, and generative models may hallucinate people or remove flames. Real fire smoke is spatially local: the degradation and its corruption of semantic features are both concentrated in the smoke regions. We propose FireDesmoke, built on this locality: Real Smoke Transfer calibrates smoke fields on real-fire recordings and transfers their spatial layout onto clean images to synthesize training pairs, and Semantic Rectification uses pixel features to rectify the tokens of a frozen foundation encoder where smoke corrupts them. With matched training budgets, replacing the transferred pairs with SmokeBench pairs lowers dense-smoke PSNR by 0.74 dB, and removing Semantic Rectification leaves clear regions 1.31 dB below the input. On what is, to the best of our knowledge, the first paired dataset of real fire smoke for image restoration, with 1,500 test frames from a held-out viewpoint, FireDesmoke improves dense- and medium-smoke PSNR by 3.73 and 2.63 dB while keeping clear regions at the input level (+0.41 dB); no other compared method gains at least 1 dB in dense smoke without lowering clear-region PSNR, and no hallucinated person is found in its outputs. It achieves state-of-the-art overall results on this benchmark and, trained on SmokeBench, the highest PSNR with the second-best SSIM and LPIPS.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30057) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30057)

## 177. Bi-Refine: Semantic Verification-Guided Progressive Refinement for Moment Retrieval

- **作者**：Mengnan Liu, Sanping Zhou, Kun Xia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Moment Retrieval (MR) aims to identify the temporal segment in an untrimmed video that corresponds to a natural language query. Existing approaches commonly refine temporal predictions within the localization pathway, without explicitly verifying candidate-level semantic consistency. Moreover, directly reusing semantic scores as feedback can cause over-correction when the verification signal is unreliable. To address these issues, we propose Bi-Refine, a semantic verification-guided progressive refinement framework that formulates MR as a closed-loop verification-and-refinement process. A Heuristic Localization Branch first generates candidate moments, while a Semantic Verification Branch evaluates their query consistency in a semantically enriched latent space. Query supervision further trains the verifier with the same candidate moment but different queries, encouraging it to distinguish the matched query from semantically similar mismatched queries. The resulting verification features are incorporated into a Progressive Refinement Loop that updates both query representations and multimodal features. To suppress potentially harmful updates, a lightweight reliability estimator gates query updates and reweights candidate contributions in feature feedback. A convergence-based adaptive stopping strategy additionally terminates unnecessary refinement during inference. Experiments on three standard MR benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and state-of-the-art performance of our method.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30055) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30055)

## 178. PsyDrift: Healthy-Reference Latent Diffusion for Anomaly Scoring in Autism Spectrum Disorder

- **作者**：Yasaman Mohammadpour, Pouya Shaeri, Ariane Middel
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV, eess.SP, stat.ML
- **备注**：Extended version of a paper accepted at the CVPath-Vision4Neuro Workshop at WACV 2027. Includes expanded related-work and future-work discussion

Individual heterogeneity and acquisition-site variability complicate neuroimaging-based characterization of autism spectrum disorder (ASD). We propose PsyDrift, combining ASD/healthy-control-supervised functional-connectivity representations with healthy-reference latent diffusion. An encoder produces 128-dimensional representations, and a denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM) learns exclusively from healthy training latents. Distance from a diffusion-denoised reconstruction provides a continuous deviation score. We evaluate 1,102 subjects from all 20 ABIDE-I sites using five-seed leave-one-site-out cross-validation. The classifier and DDPM achieve mean-site AUROCs of $0.798 \pm 0.003$ and $0.718 \pm 0.013$, respectively; uncertainties denote seed standard deviations. Matched healthy-reference scorers on the same representation achieve 0.68-0.71; the statistical significance of diffusion's modest numerical advantage remains unestablished. Within ASD, scores show weak associations with ADOS Total (Spearman $ρ= 0.108$, $p = 0.038$) and full-scale IQ ($ρ= -0.138$, $p = 0.002$), without adjustment for age, sex, site, or head motion. Frozen evaluation without retraining or hyperparameter adaptation yields pooled AUROCs of 0.69 on ABIDE-II and 0.61 on ADHD-200, providing preliminary evidence of cross-cohort and cross-disorder transfer. These results demonstrate healthy-reference deviation scoring within an ASD-supervised representation; incremental predictive and clinical value remains unestablished.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30050) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30050)

## 179. SWAM: State-aware World Action Model for Multi-Agent 3D Environments

- **作者**：Jenna Kang, Bingxuan Li, Colin Groth et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

Interactive world models generate virtual environments autoregressively from player actions and visual history. However, visual history alone cannot reliably capture the evolving states of independently acting non-player characters (NPCs) in real games, particularly when they are outside the camera view. Recent multi-agent world models address this challenge through joint multi-view generation or persistent shared state, but often rely on external observations, like maps or scene geometry, or predefined state transitions. We present SWAM, a State-aware World Action Model that separates agent-state evolution from autoregressive visual generation in interactive 3D environments. A lightweight state model propagates character dynamics from actions without consuming generated frames, combining motion priors with learned scene-dependent corrections. The predicted states then guide an autoregressive diffusion transformer alongside visual history through a novel conditioning scheme. Rather than requiring an explicit scene geometry map or visibility tests, the conditioning indicates where characters may appear, allowing the diffusion model to learn their appearance and occlusion. This design enables NPCs to evolve independently of visual generation while maintaining consistent observations across visibility changes, interactions, and respawns.
