Evaluations in a custom Godot-based first-person shooter game demonstrate improvements in visual fidelity, NPC consistency, and long-horizon stability over existing approaches.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30042) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30042)

## 180. Teaching How to Reason, Beyond What to Answer: Evidence-Faithful On-Policy Distillation for streaming video understanding

- **作者**：Mengnan Liu, Sanping Zhou, Kun Xia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Moment Retrieval (MR) aims to localize the temporal segment in a video that corresponds to a given textual description. However, a textual query often provides only a sparse semantic description of the target event, whereas the video contains rich and fine-grained visual variations. This mismatch creates an inherent gap between textual semantics and visual content, making a single textual input insufficient for precise cross-modal alignment. As a result, when multiple temporally adjacent or semantically similar segments exist, the model may struggle to distinguish the target moment from confusing candidates. To address this challenge, we propose a Multi-Perspective Auxiliary Representation (MPAR) framework, which aims to bridge this alignment gap by enriching a single textual input with complementary visual cues from multiple perspectives. Specifically, MPAR consists of three key components: (1) Auxiliary Representation Initialization (ARI) constructs multiple learnable representation slots from the original query, preserving the query intent while preparing flexible carriers for subsequent visual cue injection; (2) Visual-Induced Semantic Structuring (VISS) injects target-specific visual attributes into each representation while preserving semantic coherence with the original query; and (3) Multi-Perspective Semantic Aligner (MPSA) aligns each auxiliary representation with corresponding video regions to enhance discriminative localization. By constructing multi-perspective auxiliary representations and guiding the model to attend to relevant visual regions across these representations, our model achieves more accurate moment localization. Extensive experiments on MR benchmarks demonstrate that our MPAR outperforms state-of-the-art methods, validating the effectiveness of MPAR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30040) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30040)

## 181. QSRadar: Query-Conditioned Sparse Radar-Camera Fusion for Efficient 3D Object Detection

- **作者**：Chenhao Yan, Hongyu Liu, Xiang Cai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Radar-camera fusion, 3D object detection, autonomous driving

Reliable 3D object detection for autonomous trucks requires both the semantic detail provided by cameras and the geometric and motion cues measured by 4D radar. Existing radar-camera detectors commonly construct global radar bird's-eye-view (BEV) representations, whose computational and memory costs grow with the perception range. Sparse query-based detectors offer greater efficiency, but global feature construction can obscure point-level radar geometry and motion information. We present QSRadar, a query-conditioned sparse radar fusion framework for efficient long-range 3D detection. Built on a Sparse4Dv3-style visual-temporal detector, QSRadar retrieves a compact set of raw radar points around each evolving object hypothesis. The selected points are represented in an anchor-aligned coordinate frame, augmented with motion residuals, radar cross section, and sweep timing, and aggregated by a query-conditioned set encoder. Radar neighborhoods are retrieved again after each anchor refinement, coupling evidence selection with object estimation. Under the official MAN TruckScenes validation protocol, QSRadar achieves 38.09 mAP and 52.20 NDS, compared with 37.08 mAP and 50.78 NDS for our reproduced Sparse4D-Radar global Radar-BEV baseline. In matched tests on an RTX 4090 D, QSRadar reduces latency by 45%, peak memory usage by 49%, and GFLOPs by 43%.

## 182. MOVER: Planning Beyond Motion

- **作者**：Xiangyu Li, Yu Jiang, Tianyi Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Residual world models forecast a driving scene through its temporal change, so feature components that stay constant over time vanish from the residual pathway. We ask whether evidence with weak temporal change, such as a waiting pedestrian or a stopped vehicle, can be used more effectively for planning, and introduce MOVER, a complementary event-routing mechanism. A training-time teacher measures how each spatially grounded event query responds when a past frame is removed from shared history and contrasts that sensitivity with the event's pooled temporal residual, prioritizing events that depend on history but receive weak residual evidence. A student predicts the resulting priorities from unperturbed history, so the teacher adds no inference cost. Under fixed total routing mass, one distribution scales a low-rank future bird's-eye-view update and biases trajectory-conditioned event reading. On nuScenes, MOVER reduces ResWorld's UniAD-style average L2 from 0.65 to 0.55 m and its collision rate from 0.23% to 0.12% without direct planner ego status (0.50 m and 0.09% with it), adding 4.1 ms per frame. Matched controls show that the complementary teacher outperforms either signal alone and that the allocation acts mainly during training. Adapted to BridgeDrive-LEAD, MOVER reaches a 96.52 +/- 0.65 driving score over three seeds on the 220-route Bench2Drive closed-loop benchmark, exceeding matched controls in each paired seed by a margin within seed variation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30022) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30022)

## 183. ReplaySAM: Zero-Shot 3D Particle-Pack Segmentation with SAM 2

- **作者**：Bogong Wang, Yulai Zhang, Philipp D. Lösel et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages, 5 figures. Source code: https://github.com/bogongwang/ReplaySAM

X-ray computed tomography (XCT) provides non-destructive three-dimensional (3D) images of particle packs, making their internal structure accessible for quantitative analysis. Extracting particle-level measurements from these volumes first requires identifying and separating individual particles, a task that remains challenging in dense, irregular packs. Existing workflows often require manual correction or task-specific training, while promptable models need a strategy for automatically locating individual particles and recovering their instance masks. We present ReplaySAM, which adapts the pretrained Segment Anything Model 2 (SAM2) to three-dimensional particle-pack segmentation without additional training. It generates point prompts from foreground geometry and fuses SAM2 predictions across orthogonal slice sequences to recover particle instances. On XCT volumes of real quartz and iron-ore particle packs, ReplaySAM achieves higher mean instance Dice scores than expert-assisted watershed and nnInteractive under the evaluated protocols. ReplaySAM thus provides a training-free framework for 3D particle-pack instance segmentation, with geometry-based prompting that eliminates manual prompt placement.

## 184. 3AGameFactory-Motion: Agentic Rigging and Reliable Complex Motion Generation for Diverse 3D Assets

- **作者**：Bohan Zeng, Kaixin Zhu, Yifan Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：19pages, 10 figures

Animating arbitrary 3D assets from instructions requires both structural inference and motion control, yet existing motion benchmarks mostly cover conventional human or animal skeletons with simple instructions. We introduce DivDynBench, which evaluates rigging and motion generation across six morphology categories, with instructions requiring multi-phase part coordination and explicit timing, angle, repetition, and trajectory constraints. With large language models, we systematically compare three common paradigms: specialized motion generation, video generation followed by motion recovery, and direct agent-driven generation. We analyze their control capabilities, cross-morphology adaptability, and generation costs. Guided by this comparison, we propose an Agentic Motion Framework in which an agent writes compact parametric skeleton and motion programs, and a deterministic executor performs rigging, kinematic solving, and geometric correction. Compact parameter control reduces generation overhead and improves rigging reliability and motion-control accuracy, providing practical guidance for deploying complex 3D motion generation.

## 185. FlexGaze: Weakly Supervised Gaze Estimation at Scale

- **作者**：Zongjian Li, Mohammad Soleymani
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：23 pages, including 12 pages of supplementary material

Most gaze estimation models are trained on data collected using specialized equipment. The limited diversity of subjects, scenes, and head poses in these datasets hinders generalization to new conditions. We present FlexGaze, a family of 3D gaze estimators pretrained with weak supervision from gaze labels (pseudo-labels) automatically generated from in-the-wild images. We use off-the-shelf models to predict 2D gaze targets and scene depth, then combine these predictions to generate 3D gaze labels for approximately 3.24 million head instances. At inference, FlexGaze predicts gaze from a head crop alone and can handle profile and rear views without requiring face detection or head pose estimation. We use data augmentation during training to improve robustness to variations in crop position and scale. Pretrained using only these generated gaze labels, FlexGaze generalizes to unseen benchmarks. After supervised fine-tuning on public gaze datasets, we evaluate FlexGaze on eight benchmarks under a unified evaluation protocol. Our models match or outperform publicly available gaze estimators with several times as many parameters on most benchmarks. We release our dataset of generated gaze labels, all trained models, and code via https://github.com/ihp-lab/FlexGaze.

## 186. MORSE: Optimizing Streaming Memory Design via Open-World Auto-Research

- **作者**：Ce Zhang, Jianshu Zhang, Jinxi He et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://zhangce01.github.io/morse/

A streaming video system must decide in a single pass what evidence to retain under bounded frame storage, before it knows which details a future question will require. Recent recursive self-improvement methods automate the design of the harness that stores and retrieves this evidence. They revise the harness in response to failures, but the editor must still devise each repair from its prior knowledge and a predefined set of tools. A failure that recurs across videos, however, may point to a problem that prior work has already addressed. We introduce MORSE (Memory Optimization with Recursive Self-Evolvement), an open-world auto-research framework that uses recurring failures to guide an online search for solutions. Its editor groups failures in training traces by cause, then searches papers, model cards, and code for methods it can adapt or pretrained perception models it can use. When the editor introduces a new perceptual capability, it can also revise how the harness stores and retrieves the resulting evidence for a frozen video-language model. On held-out validation streams, MORSE first selects revisions that improve answer quality, then seeks faster revisions that preserve those gains within a specified tolerance. Across three streaming video benchmarks, MORSE consistently achieves the highest answer accuracy within each frozen-backbone comparison. On OVO-Bench, our Qwen3-VL-8B harness answers queries in 0.30 seconds on average while achieving 75.1% accuracy, 3.1 percentage points ahead of the next-best evaluated harness using the same frozen backbone.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30011) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.30011)

## 187. Antiphony: A Dual-Process Agent for Deepfake Video Detection with Real-Video Calibration

- **作者**：Xinyu Yan, Yurong Hao, Zherui Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CR

Reliable video deepfake detection requires generalization to unseen generators while limiting false alarms on authentic footage. Although existing detectors capture diverse visual cues, they may produce overconfident errors under generator shift, limiting the reliability of confidence-based fusion. Threshold calibration is further complicated by shifts in authentic capture domains and the lack of future-generator examples. To address these challenges, we present Antiphony, a framework that combines calibrated fusion of four visual experts with selective investigation. Authentic videos serve as a shared reference for score fusion and threshold calibration, enabling threshold updates without target deepfakes. For this expert-fusion stage, we establish finite-sample false-alarm control when the scoring procedure is fixed independently of calibration and authentic samples are exchangeable. To refine decisions near the calibrated threshold, a tool-using multimodal investigator examines these videos and contributes an additional fusion score. We train the investigator on generator-held-out predictions and counterfactual expert outputs, to improve robustness to unreliable expert evidence. Across six benchmark protocols, Antiphony achieves a mean benchmark score of 88.3%, exceeding the strongest evaluated MLLM and conventional detector baselines by 14.7 and 11.5 points, respectively. It also outperforms ARGUS by 20.2 points on average and by 3.6 balanced-accuracy points on FaceVid's 20 unseen generators.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29999) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29999)

## 188. One Size Doesn't Fit All: Input Adaptive Class Activation Mapping for Improved Visual Explanations

- **作者**：Ayesha Sahar, Tahir Mustafa Madni, Tehseen Zia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 5 figures, 5 Tables

Despite their widespread use in tasks such as image classification, object detection and semantic segmentation, the limited interpretability of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and Vision Transformers (ViTs) can undermine trust and hinder adoption in safety-critical applications. Explainable artificial intelligence (XAI) methods seek to address this by providing human-understandable explanations for model predictions. Class Activation Maps (CAM) constitute one such widely used XAI technique for visualizing discriminative regions. However, existing CAM methods may exhibit limitations in channel weighting, localization, and adaptability across network architectures potentially resulting in noisy or imprecise explanations. To address these issues, we propose ADAPT-CAM, a gradient-based, parameter-adaptive CAM framework. The method combines first-order gradient energy with activation-weighted second-order curvature to derive channel contributions. It further incorporates adaptive local contrast normalization and knee-guided soft channel selection to refine the resulting explanations. Importantly, key parameters are estimated independently for each input, allowing the framework to adapt to variations in feature representations across network architectures. We validate ADAPT-CAM on ImageNet and Oxford 102 Flowers across CNN and ViT architectures. Quantitative results demonstrate competitive or superior performance against established CAM methods across multiple evaluation metrics. In parallel, qualitative comparisons illustrate its ability to highlight discriminative image regions. Further parameter analysis and ablation experiments examine the behaviour and contributions of the individual adaptive components. These findings demonstrate the applicability of ADAPT-CAM across different architectures and datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29993) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29993)

## 189. FASCIA: Seeing Beneath the Surface with Queryable, Personalized Anatomy in Any Pose

- **作者**：R. James Cotton, Tim Unger, Lucinda Williamson
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present FASCIA (*Functional Anatomical Structures and Composition via Implicit Atlas*), a Momentum Human Rig (MHR)-anchored framework that predicts and poses a person's internal anatomy from their external body shape and skeletal proportions. FASCIA maps a single 290-structure population template onto each individual through a smooth, invertible warp. As people with the same shape can differ internally, a body-dependent Gaussian prior predicts both average warp coordinates and their inter-subject variability. The warp deforms a learned base template into bones, muscles, organs, and fat depots. Extending MHR skinning into the interior enables direct supervision of this model across four automatically labeled CT cohorts and one whole-body MRI cohort. On 82 held-out CT scans, FASCIA reaches a mean volumetric Dice (volume agreement) of 0.48 on named bones and 0.63 on muscles, keeps the majority of bones and muscles in one piece, and yields calibrated voxel probabilities. On 33 held-out whole-body MRI subjects, FASCIA's pointwise field improves tissue overlap over HIT across all compartments, predicts between-person differences in whole-body adipose percentage ($R^2 = 0.88$ vs. $0.38$ in women and $0.54$ vs. $-0.01$ in men), and recovers segment centers of mass within 1.1 mm. Because it needs only an MHR fit, FASCIA can be driven from images, video, or any system that outputs MHR. This opens the door to fitting individualized calibrated musculoskeletal digital twins to video motion data.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29970) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29970)

## 190. CARE-3D: Reinforcement Learning for Evidence Grounded Agentic Reasoning in Situated 3D Scenes

- **作者**：Xiaoqian Hu, Ruihong Qiu, Yanran Tang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

Situated 3D reasoning requires embodied agents to interpret objects, scene geometry, and viewpoint dependent spatial relations. Existing methods learn grounded interactions from supervised traces, but largely imitate prescribed evidence acquisition patterns rather than optimizing the support that the returned evidence provides for the correct answer. Moreover, outcome rewards provide limited credit for intermediate tool choices and may leave infrequent tools underexplored. An effective agent should instead adaptively decide what to ground, which tool to invoke, and how to assess the utility of returned evidence. To this end, this paper presents CARE-3D, a reinforcement post-training framework for adaptive grounded reasoning. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on grounding instructions first establishes valid interactions, after which Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) optimizes the agent while keeping the 3D grounding module frozen. CARE-3D combines Counterfactual Attribution of Returned Evidence for evidence sensitive credit assignment, and Group Coverage Sampling to preserve tool coverage. Experiments on MSQA and Beacon3D show consistent gains over SFT and GRPO using only answer and format rewards, especially in spatial reasoning and grounding answer coherence. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/xiaoqian-UQ/CARE-3D}{CARE-3D}.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29969) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29969)

## 191. When Should We Trust Medical Image Models? Evaluating Confidence Reliability under Cross-Dataset Shift

- **作者**：Haoran Zhang, Sarah Lam, Yuting Lin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：7 pages, 1 figure, 2 tables. Course research project, CS585 Image and Video Computing, Boston University, Spring 2026

Deep learning models for chest X-ray analysis often achieve strong performance on benchmark datasets, but their reliability under cross-dataset deployment remains unclear. In clinical practice, models trained in one hospital system may be applied to data from another, where distributional and labeling differences can affect both prediction accuracy and model confidence. We conduct a controlled empirical study of model confidence under cross-dataset transfer. We train ResNet-50 and DenseNet-121 on CheXpert and evaluate them on MIMIC-CXR across five experimental settings, focusing on Pneumothorax and Pleural Effusion. We measure predictive performance using AUC and confidence reliability using Expected Calibration Error (ECE), Brier score, and average confidence on incorrect predictions, all reported with 95% bootstrap confidence intervals. We further evaluate post-hoc temperature scaling fit on the source validation set and use Grad-CAM to examine the mechanism behind cross-domain performance differences. Cross-domain AUC drops 7-13 percentage points, and the best in-domain model is not the best cross-domain model. Source-domain temperature scaling produces marginal ECE changes but systematically increases confidence on incorrect predictions across all four model-pathology combinations. Grad-CAM reveals that DenseNet relies on a concentrated spatial prior that fails to transfer, while ResNet's more distributed attention generalizes better. These findings argue for cross-site validation and safety-specific calibration metrics in medical AI evaluation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29955) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29955)

## 192. KGLP-Net: Kalman-Reliable Global--Local Learning With Group Fisher Pruning for Hyperspectral Image Classification

- **作者**：Wenshuai Deng, Wen Xie, Abdel Hamid Soliman et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Accurate hyperspectral image (HSI) classification requires both the effective integration of broad spatial context and fine-grained spectral--spatial information surrounding target pixels. However, the reliability of these complementary representations may vary considerably across heterogeneous scenes, and uncertainty estimates obtained during feature fusion are rarely exploited to guide subsequent model compression. To address these challenges, we propose Kalman-Reliable Global--Local Learning with Group Fisher Pruning (KGLP-Net), a unified framework that couples uncertainty-aware feature fusion with reliability-guided structured pruning. Specifically, an asymmetric dual-view architecture employs a Global Mamba Encoder to capture long-range spatial dependencies and a Local CNN Encoder to extract fine-grained spectral--spatial features from target neighborhoods. To adaptively integrate these complementary representations, Kalman-Reliable Fusion (KRF) estimates their relative uncertainties in feature space and performs a Kalman-inspired posterior update, treating global features as a contextual prior and local features as an observation. Building upon the resulting posterior uncertainty, Kalman-Reliable Group Fisher Pruning (KRGF) quantifies group-level reliability through normalized posterior precision and integrates it with Group Fisher importance to identify and prune Kalman-coupled channel groups, enabling structurally consistent model compression. Experiments on four public HSI datasets demonstrate that the resulting Compact48 model, with a hidden width of 48, achieves the highest overall accuracy among ten representative competing classifiers. Compared with the unpruned Dense64 model, Compact48 reduces the number of parameters, FLOPs, and inference time by 38.5%, 34.8% and 37.6% on average, respectively, while incurring only a 0.03-percentage-point decrease in mean overall accuracy.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29944) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29944)

## 193. Benchmarking VLM Medical Hallucination across 5 Modalities

- **作者**：Zhixing Cao, Yusen Peng
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：5 pages, 6 figures, preprint

Hallucinations in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) within the medical domain remain a difficult and not well-understood problem, and it is still unclear where these models break down. Although several benchmarks targeting medical hallucination have been introduced, they often lack broad coverage across different medical imaging modalities. We introduce Multi-Med, a medical hallucination benchmark containing 1,000 image-question pairs spanning five distinct modalities. Using this benchmark, we perform an in-depth experimental study of 10 widely used VLMs across five modalities and four question categories. Our findings indicate that model performance differs substantially depending on the imaging modality and question type, and that the differences among the best-performing models are not statistically significant.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29934) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29934)

## 194. Dynamic Gaussian Scene Reconstruction by Integrating Events and Structural Priors

- **作者**：Zihao Yu, Guozhen Liu, Jie Yan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Existing approaches to event-based dynamic Gaussian reconstruction rely on two assumptions for support augmentation and reliability estimation. First, existing Gaussians can absorb reconstruction errors. Second, photometric optimization has converged sufficiently to estimate reliability using analytical variance. Both assumptions can fail under fast motion. When the first fails, regions without Gaussian support cannot generate densification candidates. When the second fails, Gaussians in moving regions where optimization has not converged may be excluded as motion references. We propose Event-Structure Collaborative Dynamic Gaussian Reconstruction (ESC-DGR), using event observations independent of both conditions. Events first identify changes that require additional support, placed only where rendered depth is valid. The augmented representation still needs reliable motion references. Online rendering statistics and historical event evidence therefore propagate displacement estimates from reliable Gaussians to weakly observed neighborhoods. These references describe dominant motion at the frame-interval timescale. Target-time events therefore further condition bounded position residuals for active Gaussians. Support placement, reference selection, and residual prediction thus operate on the same evolving representation within one optimization run. For event-driven monocular dynamic scene reconstruction, our method achieves the best reconstruction quality among the evaluated methods on the E-D3DGS benchmark. Relative to E-D3DGS, dynamic-region PSNR (dPSNR) improves by 1.02 and 1.45 dB on synthetic and real scenes, respectively. The total dynamic Gaussian count across eight synthetic scenes decreases by 8.5%. Although mean frame rate decreases by about 21%-25% relative to E-D3DGS, rendering remains above 80 FPS in all twelve scenes. Code: https://github.com/zh-yu1/ESC-DGR.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29920) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29920)

## 195. SpectralRoute: Adaptive Spectral Routing for Training-Free Open-Vocabulary Hyperspectral Segmentation

- **作者**：Qingmei Li, Xiaoya Fan, Juepeng Zheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Open-vocabulary semantic segmentation enables the recognition of arbitrary categories through natural language descriptions and has recently been extended from natural images to remote sensing. However, existing approaches primarily focus on three-channel optical imagery or limited-band multispectral data, while pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) struggle to directly exploit the rich spectral information of hyperspectral imagery (HSI) without additional adaptation. More importantly, different land-cover categories rely on distinct spectral cues, which may be weakened by uniform fusion. To address these challenges, we propose SpectralRoute, a training-free framework for open-vocabulary hyperspectral segmentation. Specifically, we construct complementary spectral views from HSI and enrich category descriptions with external spectral knowledge, enabling frozen vision-language models to extract diverse spectral-semantic responses. We then introduce a class-adaptive spectral routing mechanism that selectively amplifies discriminative spectral responses for each category, overcoming the limitations of uniform fusion. Furthermore, physical spectral priors are incorporated to refine predictions for visually similar land-cover categories. Experiments on three hyperspectral benchmarks demonstrate that SpectralRoute consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods without additional training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29919) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29919)

## 196. Generalizable Variable-Rate Adaptation for Image Coding for Machines

- **作者**：Yui Tatsumi, Hiroshi Watanabe
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to 2026 IEEE International Conference on Visual Communications and Image Processing (IEEE VCIP 2026)

Image Coding for Machines (ICM) aims to preserve task-relevant information for machine vision under limited bitrates. However, most ICM models are trained for a fixed operating point, and extending them to variable-rate compression is challenging because their training objectives differ across tasks and model designs. In this paper, we propose a loss-agnostic distillation framework for variable-rate ICM. A pretrained fixed-rate ICM model is used as a frozen teacher, while a student model learns variable-rate behavior through importance-adaptive quantization and distillation-based optimization. This enables continuous bitrate control with a single model while preserving task-relevant latent components, without requiring the original task-specific loss or downstream task labels. Experiments based on existing ICM models demonstrate that the proposed method provides continuous bitrate control and achieves competitive recognition performance compared with the fixed-rate models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29914) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29914)

## 197. Bridging the Gap between Continual Learning and Test-Time Adaptation with Domain Preview

- **作者**：Frédéric Li Combeau, Nicolas Michel, Toshihiko Yamasaki
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：37 pages, 4 figures

Domain shift is a major problem for training machine learning models. While Continual Learning (CL) assumes distribution shift during training and Continual Test-Time Adaptation (CTTA) considers new domains at test time, setups that require explicit online adaptation to domain shift during both phases remain under-explored. We propose continual learning with domain preview (CL-P), a new combination of CL and TTA where both train and test data suffer from distribution shift. The training phase of CL-P is analogous to domain-incremental learning while the testing phase consists of a TTA phase on a future domain (which we call preview), and an evaluation phase on all seen domains. The model must then adapt to new domains at train and test time. In this paper, we evaluate mainstream CL and TTA methods that adapt only at train or test time, as well as their naive combination. Experimentally, TTA heavily damages the domain on which the CL method has just trained and increases forgetting. Notably, this damage mostly comes from the BatchNorm statistics shared by all domains. In that sense, we propose DOSE (Dosing by Online Statistics of Each domain), which allows a seamless combination of any CL and TTA methods by adaptive domain inference at test time, where the adaptation is applied to each image in proportion to the probability that it comes from the preview domain. Through extensive experiments with ResNet and ViT backbones, various combinations of CL and TTA baseline methods and datasets considering diverse domains, we show that DOSE matches or improves on CL methods alone in all 24 settings and on the naive combination in all but one. The code can be found in appendix.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29905) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29905)

## 198. Motion Mamba 2.0: Autoregressive Diffusion Mamba for Long-Horizon Humanoid Control

- **作者**：Yue Huang, Tianshan Zhang, Zeyu Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Code: https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/MotionMamba2.0. Website: https://aigeeksgroup.github.io/MotionMamba2.0

Long-horizon, real-time controllable motion generation is essential for interactive animation and humanoid robotics, where an agent must respond to changing instructions and spatial goals while moving. Existing autoregressive diffusion methods generate motion incrementally, but bounded attention caches limit access to distant history, while repeated denoising introduces substantial inference overhead. We present Motion Mamba 2.0, a Mamba-based root-body diffusion architecture that integrates local attention, resumable recurrent states, and persistent content memory (PCM) for long-horizon controllable motion generation. PCM transforms compressed motion history from Mamba recurrent states, together with state changes and text-conditioned features, into dynamically updated content slots accessible to local attention. To enable efficient inference, we further develop an execution scheme tailored to this architecture, combining CUDA Graph replay, fused kernels, and execution-flow simplification while preserving the canonical history update. Evaluations on retargeted HiPHI demonstrate accurate terminal control over rollouts of up to 39.52 s, while short-horizon comparisons on BONES Seed and retargeted HumanML3D show that the model also retains strong spatial control and text-motion matching. On an RTX 4090, the optimized execution reduces median complete-window latency from 1685.35 to 276.14 ms, achieving a median paired speedup of 6.138$\times$ while preserving identical outputs and committed states in the tested rollouts. Moreover, we conduct extensive real-world evaluation on the Unitree G1, demonstrating sequential text-guided action composition, expressive motion, and interactive locomotion through keyboard and mouse control.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29897) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29897)

## 199. Segment Anything In-Context via Visual Demonstrations

- **作者**：Yuzhu Wang, Manni Duan, Hongliang Ye et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present the Segment Anything Model via Visual Demonstrations (SAYD), a foundation model for in-context segmentation. Given visual demos comprising prompt images and their segmentation masks, SAYD segments coherent regions (e.g., objects or their parts) in a query image by establishing visual correspondences between the prompts and the query. SAYD generalizes to previously-unseen segmentation tasks, including those requiring tacit visual knowledge or involving concepts that are difficult to articulate unambiguously in language. We train SAYD on 12 public datasets originally curated for panoptic segmentation using our proposed techniques, including a lightweight mask decoder for prompt-agnostic mask generation and a learning mechanism for recognizing novel query classes absent in the prompts. These innovations enhance both computational efficiency and out-of-domain generalization. Consequently, SAYD significantly outperforms existing methods of in-context segmentation and open-vocabulary segmentation across 24 out-of-domain benchmarks, spanning natural, biological, ecological, cosmological, medical, pathological, and satellite imagery.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29896) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29896)

## 200. Reparameterizing Your Object Detectors for Weight Space Programming

- **作者**：Chien-Yao Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：29 pages, 12 figures

Weight-Space Programming (WSP) edits network weights without backpropagation, yet directly editing converged object detectors stalls or collapses because set prediction penalizes duplicate boxes as false positives while compact parameterizations trap weights in an isotropic Euclidean minimum. We propose Inverse-Reparameterized Object-Detection WSP (IR-ODWSP), lifting detector layers via exact function-preserving operators (E, C) into decoupled moment, 2D-DCT, PCA, and Stiefel-Procrustes subspaces. Solving winner-exclusive updates and folding back (Delta Params=0%, Delta FLOPs=0%) induces an anisotropic Riemannian metric, improving all 26/26 MS COCO settings across 17 YOLO26, EdgeCrafter, and RF-DETR detectors (up to +0.31 APs), +1.42 mAP cross-basin COCO-O, zeroimage repair (+2.95 mAP), and label-free weather adaptation (+2.79 AP). Without retraining, IR-ODWSP supports -100% to +100% latency budgets across bridged contraction, 1.00x folding, dynamic (IR-ODWSP-DYN), and static (IR-ODWSP-SG) graphs: -10% retains 51.62 AP / 33.48 mAP, while positive budgets scale monotonically (+0.30 AP at +10%, +1.12 on RF-DETR-N; 54.83 AP at +100%; 55.91 AP / 35.44 mAP in IR-ODWSP+). Source code is released at https://github.com/WongKinYiu/ODWSP.

## 201. Towards Agentic Video Corpus Moment Retrieval

- **作者**：Chenwei Xu, Shang Wu, Zhenyu Pan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video corpus moment retrieval (VCMR) requires finding the video and time interval that match a text query in a corpus of videos. We present RELAY, a training-free framework for VCMR that shares memory across queries. RELAY processes a batch of queries in three stages: search with a query bank, ranking with video notes, and localization with video prompt caching. In the search stage, an embedding model scores each video by its best-matching frame. The query bank penalizes videos that score highly for many other queries in the batch. In the ranking stage, a vision-language model (VLM) writes notes on each candidate video's scenes, people, objects, and actions once. Then, a large language model (LLM) reranks the candidates from these notes. In the localization stage, a VLM predicts the time interval of the moment in the top-ranked video. When multiple queries select the same video, prompt caching lets the VLM reuse its frames. On the three VERIFIED benchmarks, RELAY outperforms the trained baselines in video retrieval R@1 without training. Without any labeled moment, its predicted intervals are shorter than the annotated ones. In VCMR, it achieves similar performance to VideoSearch-R1 on one benchmark and trails it at IoU 0.5 on the other two. Human annotators also mark shorter moments than the original annotations of the benchmarks. A single margin, calibrated on a few labeled queries, widens the predicted interval to the annotation convention of the benchmarks. With it, RELAY outperforms the trained baselines in VCMR at IoU 0.5.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29877) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29877)

## 202. Escriba-OCR: A 1B Vision-Language Model for Error-Preserving Transcription of Handwritten Portuguese Essays

- **作者**：Tardelli Ronan Coelho Stekel
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.CV, cs.CY
- **备注**：21 pages, 6 figures, 13 tables. Model: https://huggingface.co/tardellirs/Escriba-OCR-1B ; code and benchmark: https://github.com/tardellirs/escriba-ocr

Feedback tools and research on learner writing need transcriptions of handwritten essays that keep exactly what the student wrote: misspellings, line breaks, struck-through words and insertions. Vision-language models (VLMs) read handwriting well, but they also tend to rewrite it into correct language. We measure this on BRESSAY, 1,000 handwritten Brazilian Portuguese essays, with a benchmark that adds error preservation, invented errors, edit-markup precision and recall and line structure to the character error rate (CER) and uses paired bootstrap confidence intervals. We restrict the study to open-weight models that schools can run on their own hardware. Of 22 open baselines, the best, Qwen3-VL 235B-A22B, reaches 5.6% CER on the 199 test pages; none reproduces the edit markup reliably, and every VLM replaces 39-78% of the words absent from a Portuguese dictionary with dictionary words. We fine-tune LightOnOCR-3-1B on the 647 training pages in 85 minutes on one GPU. The resulting Escriba-OCR-1B reaches 2.06% CER, 3.5 points below the best baseline (95% CI [-4.2, -2.9]); under the ICDAR 2024 competition metric it reaches 2.1% CER, below the best published page-level results (2.2-2.4%). It recovers 67% of insertions and 77% of illegible deletions, and reproduces the line structure of 97% of the pages, but still normalises 60% of likely misspellings. Three seeds agree within 0.04 CER points, and quantised to 4 bits the model runs on an ordinary CPU at about one minute per page. On 40 letters of another corpus it matches its base model (without that model's appended comments) but misreads unseen words more often; weight interpolation recovers half the gap. A teacher's review finds that 45% of the judgeable apparent corrections are errors in the BRESSAY ground truth, so dictionary-based scores likely understate preservation. We release the model, the benchmark code and the review data.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29874) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29874)

## 203. LexiDet-OVD: Benchmarking Lexical Sensitivity in Open-Vocabulary Object Detection

- **作者**：Amit Thakur, Mukesh Singhal
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted at WACV 2027

Open-vocabulary object detectors localize objects from free-form text prompts, but their reliability depends on how users describe the target. Standard evaluations usually test a fixed class name, leaving prompt-induced failure modes underexplored. We introduce LexiDet-OVD, a benchmark and metric suite for studying lexical sensitivity in open-vocabulary object detection. We organize prompt sets into equivalent, conditional, and broad semantic scopes, and evaluate four detector families on fixed COCO and LVIS samples, each covering 30 classes (800 held-out COCO images and 1,000 LVIS images). Beyond mean and worst-prompt AP50, we report P10, CVaR at 20%, leave-one-prompt-out influence, detection agreement, box consistency, and confidence variance using one-to-one multi-instance matching. Equivalent-prompt best-to-worst gaps range from 5.3 to 23.4 points on COCO1000 and from 5.1 to 29.9 points on LVIS1000-30c, showing that strong average accuracy and lexical stability are distinct properties. We compare maximum-score selection, weighted-box fusion, Prompt Consensus, and an exploratory fusion rule. Aggregation recovers performance in several settings, but no method dominates all model-dataset pairs. A controlled Detic/SHiNe comparison further shows that semantic hierarchy enrichment does not by itself eliminate lexical sensitivity. These results support reporting prompt-distribution robustness alongside conventional open-vocabulary detection accuracy.

## 204. Ego2Wrist: Bootstrapping Wrist-View Synthesis from Egocentric Video

- **作者**：Ziyao Zeng, Xiatao Sun, Hao Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-23 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：27 pages (13 main + 14 appendix), 9 figures, 20 tables. Project page: https://adonis-galaxy.github.io/Ego2Wrist/

Wrist cameras provide an action-centric view that is directly relevant to robot manipulation. However, collecting human wrist-camera videos at scale is challenging due to cumbersome setups that can interfere with natural hand movements. In contrast, head-mounted human egocentric videos are much easier to scale. We introduce Ego2Wrist, which synthesizes synchronized wrist-camera views from human egocentric videos and task descriptions. Trained on approximately 940 hours of paired head- and wrist-camera recordings, Ego2Wrist jointly generates three views. To evaluate generated wrist views without ground truth, we propose hand-centric cross-view consistency (HCC), which measures consistency between generated wrist views and corresponding hand regions in the input head view. HCC ranks model checkpoints comparably to ground-truth-based LPIPS, and human evaluators prefer HCC-selected generations in 77% of responses. Evaluated on four datasets, Ego2Wrist outperforms fine-tuned WorldWander, reducing LPIPS by 28% and receiving human preference in 76% of responses. More importantly, HCC facilitates recursive self-improvement on in-the-wild egocentric videos by selecting generated wrist views for further training. Generation quality progressively improves over three rounds. These results demonstrate a scalable approach to generating real-world wrist-view human manipulation videos for robot policy training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29850) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29850)

## 205. Dynamics Forcing: Mitigating Error Accumulation in Causal Video World Models

- **作者**：Xin Zhou, Cong Miao
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO
- **备注**：9 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables

Causal video world models generate block by block from their own previous outputs. Small errors in early blocks are fed back as history and grow until the scene and the robot fall apart. Teacher forcing trains on clean ground-truth history, so the model never sees the history it will actually condition on, and it also finds a shortcut: copying the previous frame instead of predicting how the scene changes. We present Dynamics Forcing, a training scheme that keeps real video as the only supervision and reshapes how history is presented. History is perturbed in three ways: random noising, self-resampling by the model itself, and joint denoising, in which the most recent blocks are still partly noisy when the next block is predicted. On top of this we move history guidance and first-frame anchor guidance from inference into training, so that the model is pushed to use its history and its reference frame rather than ignore them. Dynamics Forcing needs no teacher model, no distillation, and no additional model at inference. Applied to a 5B video model with LoRA on 77 hours of manipulation video on a single GPU, it better matches unseen real continuations and reduces error accumulation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29839) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29839)

## 206. Continuous-time 4D Reconstruction via Event-guided Latent Feature Interpolation

- **作者**：Gwangtak Bae, Seunggu Kang, Junho Kim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：BMVC 2026

Recent geometric foundation models can successfully convert a video sequence into frames of registered point clouds, but cannot predict moments beyond the observed temporal resolution. We propose a continuous-time 4D reconstruction method that extracts 3D structure and camera poses at any time instant, with additional guidance from event-camera measurements. While event cameras provide evidence at microsecond resolution to continuously fill the temporal gap, integrating them into existing RGB-based foundation models remains challenging. We formulate the problem as interpolation in the latent feature space and augment event-based losses in the image space as an auxiliary task to boost accuracy. Operating in the latent space leverages the powerful geometric priors of foundation models, while the event loss provides physically grounded guidance. To effectively address the lack of paired training data, we further propose a self-supervised training framework that uses pseudo-ground-truth labels of estimated geometry and simulated events generated from high-frame-rate RGB videos. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that the proposed pipeline outperforms baselines that temporally interpolate either the input RGB videos or the estimated geometry, and that it generalizes to unseen real-world scenarios. Controlled experiments with synthetic corruptions show that it remains robust under motion-blur and low-light conditions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29838) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29838)

## 207. Data-Quality-Aware Fundus Classification with Anatomy-Guided Glaucoma Assessment and Cross-Dataset Evaluation

- **作者**：Imdadul Haque Sourav, Marjana Jannat Miti, Md. Jehadul Islam Mony
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：28 pages including supplementary material

Background: Deep learning fundus classifiers often perform well internally, but duplicated images, inconsistent labels, acquisition differences and poor threshold transfer can undermine their reliability. We assess nine-class classifiers beyond internal accuracy, using a data audit, frozen-model external evaluation and an anatomy-guided specialist. Methods: Nine-class experts (ConvNeXt-Small, EfficientNetV2-Small, DenseNet169) were trained with three seeds. Auditing 5,318 images for exact duplicates and cross-label conflicts removed 464 conflict groups (942 images) and six same-label copies, leaving 4,370 images. An anatomy-guided glaucoma specialist read disc-centred crops with vessel and disc/cup segmentation maps, plus cup-to-disc features. Frozen models were evaluated on RFMiD and JSIEC, and a prespecified comparison on REFUGE was replicated on ORIGA, G1020 and PAPILA. Results: ConvNeXt-Small reached an internal accuracy of 0.910, macro-F1 of 0.917 and AUC of 0.982; disc and cup Dice scores were 0.966 and 0.915. Across seeds, the fixed anatomy-guided mix left internal accuracy unchanged relative to equal soft voting (-0.001) but raised mean external closed-set accuracy by 4.9 percentage points on RFMiD (positive in two of three seeds) and 2.3 on JSIEC. On REFUGE (800 photographs) it improved balanced accuracy by 7.4 percentage points (95% CI 5.1 to 9.5), and by 2.1 on ORIGA, with positive gains on G1020 and PAPILA. Thresholds transferred poorly (REFUGE sensitivity 0.950, specificity 0.411). Conclusions: Retinal classifiers should be judged by data-quality auditing, anatomy-informed modelling and multi-cohort evaluation. Anatomy-guided fusion improved transfer at no internal cost, but gains varied by cohort and thresholds did not transfer, so internal metrics alone do not establish reliable performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29834) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29834)

## 208. Model Immunization Cannot Reliably Protect Models from Harmful Adaptation

- **作者**：Tong Li, Chenru Wang, Chris Thomas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV

Efficient fine-tuning techniques have substantially reduced the cost of adapting open-weight models, enabling malicious actors to readily fine-tune them toward harmful capabilities. In response, model immunization modifies a pretrained checkpoint to resist subsequent adaptation toward designated harmful capabilities while preserving its utility and adaptability for benign tasks. Furthermore, a few immunization methods have sought to keep their protection robust to some changes in the attacker's adaptation. However, fine-tuning offers attackers many more low-cost options, and we observe that existing methods still substantially overfit to the attacker configuration they are optimized against. Such overfitting stays hidden when immunization is evaluated under, or near, this configuration, so the reported protection may give a false sense of security. To rigorously evaluate model immunization, we conduct experiments, varying one component of this configuration at a time: the harmful data, the adaptation strategy, or the model initialization. Across four immunization methods on text-to-image generation and image classification, with additional results on language models, these variations substantially degrade the protection, and in many cases the immunized model relearns the harmful capability nearly as well as the un-immunized model. Existing immunization therefore cannot reliably protect models, leaving them vulnerable to harmful adaptation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29815) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29815)

## 209. Hierarchical Reasoning Action Head for Tiny Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Aditya Makineni, Amitesh Sahoo, Qing Tian
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 figures, 4 tables

Real-time onboard robot control calls for a lightweight vision-language-action (VLA) model, yet shrinking the backbone leaves less capacity for deliberation at inference time. Chain-of-thought decoding, dual-system architectures, and larger backbones can restore deliberation but reintroduce the latency and memory costs that motivated going small. Recurrent action heads offer a lighter alternative by iterating in latent space before committing to an action, but existing designs refine a single undifferentiated state that must simultaneously encode slowly evolving task context and rapidly changing motor commands. We propose the Hierarchical Reasoning Action Head (HR-AH), which maintains two latent states updated at different frequencies: a low-level state that updates fast, grounded in action tokens and proprioception, and a high-level state that updates slowly, grounded in visual understanding and the instruction-informed low-level state. The two states exchange information through nested reasoning cycles. Because all iteration occurs in the compact head after a single backbone pass, HR-AH adds hierarchical reasoning without increasing the backbone size. With a 22.5M-parameter head, 3-4.3x smaller than those of RD-VLA and VLA-Adapter on the same backbone, HR-AH reaches 97.0% success rate on LIBERO and a 4.40 average chain length on CALVIN ABC->D, at the lowest latency among compared methods. We show that this hierarchy improves performance over a parameter-matched single-state head, and that routing distinct modalities to each level yields further gains in both accuracy and inference cost.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29813) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29813)

## 210. Revisiting RadioGalaxyNET: modern detectors for radio galaxy detection and host identification

- **作者**：Argo Saakyan
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, astro-ph.IM
- **备注**：8 pages, 1 figure, 2 tables. Code: https://github.com/ArgoHA/radiogalaxynet-dfine-vs-galdino ; weights: https://huggingface.co/ArgoSA/dfine-seg-radio-galaxy-net

RadioGalaxyNET is the public benchmark for finding extended radio galaxies in EMU pilot-survey images, classifying them and locating their infrared hosts. Its best published results date from 2023: 54.5 box AP (Gal-YOLOv8) and 48.1 host keypoint AP (Gal-DINO). We train three current detectors, D-FINE-seg, YOLO26-pose and RF-DETR, on the same data and score them with the benchmark's own evaluation code, which reproduces the published Gal-DINO numbers exactly. All exceed the published results. D-FINE-seg M reaches 67.1 box AP and 72.4 host AP, 12.6 and 24.3 points above the best published results, and the highest host AP of the detectors we tested; on boxes it is level with YOLO26. Its host lead over YOLO26 comes from host-centered images, which make up most of the benchmark. End to end, D-FINE-seg S is the fastest model at 1.9 ms per image; engine against engine, YOLO26 is faster at the same size. We also report what matters for anyone using the benchmark: its scoring protocol adds 5 to 10 AP, its host labels sit at the image center, its box labels are offset from the image by 1.5 pixels, and its images can be rebuilt from the public survey data, which recovers sky positions and doubles as extra training data. We suggest that future work report host identification F1, as a measure closer to catalog use: D-FINE-seg M 71.8, YOLO26-pose M 70.5, RF-DETR 63.8, Gal-DINO 59.2.

## 211. A Cascaded Framework with a Cross-Attention-Guided Mamba-CNN Model for Pre- and Post-Treatment Glioma Segmentation

- **作者**：Siavash Taremi, Mahmoud Heidari, Seyed Mohammad Hossein Hashemi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Accurate glioma segmentation is vital for diagnosis and follow-up, but manual segmentation is time-consuming, subjective, and variable across interpreters. Gliomas are heterogeneous in size, shape, and location. Deep learning has therefore emerged as a potential solution. CNNs excel at local features but may struggle with long-range dependencies, whereas Vision Transformers capture global context efficiently but incur higher computational complexity. Most previous studies focused on pre-treatment gliomas; post-treatment cases with resection cavities (RCs) remain less explored. This study proposes a Cross-Attention-Guided Mamba-CNN (CMC-Seg) model within a cascaded framework for pre- and post-treatment diffuse glioma segmentation, aiming to balance accuracy and computational cost. Experiments used BraTS 2025 mpMRI of diffuse glioma patients. After preprocessing, a coarse segmentation model extracted tumor-centered patches, which were fed to CMC-Seg for fine-grained segmentation. CMC-Seg was compared with 3D U-Net, SegResNet, SwinUNETR, UNETR, nnFormer, and SegMamba. On the test set, CMC-Seg achieved the highest mean Dice (81.20%) and non-enhancing tumor core (NETC) Dice (77.09%). It also achieved an RC Dice of 78.63% and the lowest 95% Hausdorff distance (HD95; 4.39 mm). Results were comparable to CNN and Mamba baselines and significantly better than Transformer-based baselines (P < 0.05). CMC-Seg required the fewest inference GFLOPs (381.49). Ablation experiments indicated that multi-direction scanning and cross-attention improved performance, particularly for RC (P < 0.05). Grad-CAM explainability experiments showed that CMC-Seg focuses on diagnostically relevant tumor regions.

## 212. Beyond Language Optimization: Enhancing Visual Representations for Radiology Report Generation

- **作者**：Rui Zuo, Qinyue Tong, Ziqian Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Accurate chest X-ray radiology report generation (RRG) requires recognizing clinically relevant abnormalities and accurately describing them. Although Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) improves the clinical accuracy of generated reports, our analyses suggest limited improvements in visual representations for abnormality recognition. To address this limitation, we propose a selective regional visual distillation framework that enhances visual representations of challenging abnormalities while preserving existing visual capabilities. Specifically, we train a region-aware teacher with targeted supervision on challenging anatomical regions to acquire complementary visual knowledge. Rather than transferring all teacher features, we verify their utility through regional feature replacement and select only those that improve the original student's performance. We further introduce a Spatially Gated Residual Adapter (SGRA) to distill these verified features while minimizing interference with the student's existing visual representations. Experiments on MIMIC-CXR and IU-Xray demonstrate improved clinical report accuracy and region-level abnormality recognition across different multimodal large language model backbones, while largely preserving performance on non-target findings.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29783) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29783)

## 213. S1Arena-MM: Which Multimodal Decisions Can Be Left to System 1?

- **作者**：Lang Lin, Tianyu Chen, Yi Gu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：26 pages. Lang Lin, Tianyu Chen, Yi Gu, Yang Zhao, Xinran Song and Xinyang Liu contributed equally. Code and data: https://github.com/zhaoyang02/S1Arena-MM ; media: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Swindl/S1Arena-MM

Perception systems often make discrete decisions: flag a hateful meme, predict whether a pedestrian will cross, detect a spoofed voice, or select the next move in a game. System-1 models, such as OneJev and Jev-Omni, read the media and return probabilities over the options in a single forward pass, while multimodal large language models (MLLMs) generate text. Which multimodal decisions can System-1 models handle, and where do MLLMs perform better? We introduce S1Arena-MM, a benchmark that gives every model the same decision requests and scores the probabilities it returns. It contains 51,060 questions across 94 tasks with image, video, and audio input, drawn from 94 public datasets and three program-generated ones. It also includes 102 closed-loop game environments that a model plays from screenshots; in real-time mode, the game continues while the model decides. We evaluate 33 open models: 12 System-1 models and 21 MLLMs read at the answer token. On static decisions, the best System-1 model leads the best MLLM by 4.9 percentage points on images and is within one point of it on video and audio. Post-training for decisions mainly improves the probabilities: on video, a System-1 model has a lower Brier score than the MLLM that it is post-trained from in 10 of 12 pairs. With up to 2,048 thought tokens per question, the most accurate MLLMs pass the best System-1 models only slightly, and thinking raises the Brier score of most MLLMs. Closed loops separate the models: three System-1 models built on a 27B MLLM reach 22.0-33.3% progression in BALROG, and no other model exceeds 9.7% with thinking off. In real-time GameWorld, latencies of up to 1.1 s per decision cause no measurable loss of progress for System-1 models. Code and data: https://github.com/zhaoyang02/S1Arena-MM.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29773) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29773)

## 214. CivicBench: Robust Detection of AI-Generated Political Video

- **作者**：Mert Onur Cakiroglu, Elham Buxton, Mehmet Dalkilic et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

AI-generated video of politicians can pass for real footage, and once such fakes are common, real footage can be dismissed as fake. Platforms address both problems with detectors, but whoever uploads a clip controls its pixels. Existing datasets of political video are small, show real politicians, and test no attacks. We introduce CivicBench, with 10,800 generated clips of invented politicians from eight open generators and 2,632 public-domain or openly licensed real clips. Each clip can be attacked, to hide a fake or to frame real footage. Published detectors trained on a general benchmark reach at most .781 AUC on CivicBench. Adversarial changes of 1 gray level per pixel hide nearly every generated clip from the detectors not built to resist attacks. An attack's reach is how far it can move a clip's score. A clip keeps its label when its margin from the threshold exceeds this reach, and we find that the reach grows almost linearly with the budget. On this basis, we propose Tau-Anchor, which fine-tunes an encoder pretrained on attacked images. It attacks real and generated clips during training and adds a margin term at its deployed threshold. It reaches .941 AUC on CivicBench. It also keeps .668 of generated clips flagged at 2 gray levels, against at most .289 for published detectors. When real clips are framed at the same 2 gray levels, Tau-Anchor's false alarms rise only from 8% to 14%, whereas four published detectors flag nearly every framed real clip at just 1 gray level. External political footage and two general benchmarks show the same pattern under attack. Code and data are available at https://github.com/KurbanIntelligenceLab/CivicBench.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29766) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29766)

## 215. DictaRashi: A State-of-the-Art OCR Model For Rashi Script

- **作者**：Ometz Shmidman, Yonah Taurog, Aryeh Sanders et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We release DictaRashi, a Tesseract OCR model for scanned Hebrew books printed in the so-called "Rashi" font. We trained DictaRashi on over one million manually corrected lines of text from over 100 different books of Rabbinic and post-Rabbinic literature. On a held-out set of 1,120 manually transcribed lines from scanned Rashi-script pages, DictaRashi reaches a character error rate (CER) of 0.86% and a word error rate (WER) of 3.38%, setting a new state-of-the-art for publicly-available Tesseract models. We provide a full comparison of our results to those of the 31 other publicly available Hebrew Tesseract models; the best of those models achieves a 2.98% CER and 11.86% WER. We further demonstrate that DictaRashi outperforms the current frontier LLMs on this OCR task. We release the test set of Rashi-script documents so that it may be used as a benchmark for future work in this area. We are pleased to release the model and test set to the community under a CC BY NC 4.0 license.

## 216. DREAM: Dual-Memory Reasoning for Anticipatory 3D Perception in Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Sriram Mandalika, Athira Nambiar
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：CVPR 2026 Workshop

Autonomous driving systems must not only interpret the current scene but also anticipate perceptual challenges such as occlusions, sparse observations, and recurring failure patterns. However, most existing LiDAR semantic segmentation methods operate in a frame-centric manner, processing each scan independently and discarding past contextual experience, which limits robustness under dynamic scene changes. We propose DREAM, a Dual-memory REAsoning framework for continual LiDAR seMantic segmentation that reframes perception as an experience-driven and anticipatory process. DREAM maintains two complementary memory banks in a strictly online setting: a latent memory that stores compact semantic abstractions of previously observed scenes, and an error memory that records representations associated with uncertain predictions. At each timestep, relevant memory entries are retrieved via cosine similarity and integrated into the feature space through a lightweight modulation mechanism, enabling the model to reinforce consistent semantic patterns while suppressing recurring failure modes. The backbone remains frozen and no past scans are replayed, ensuring computational efficiency and bounded memory growth. Extensive experiments on multiple large-scale LiDAR benchmarks demonstrate that DREAM achieves state-of-the-art performance, with consistent improvements on dynamic and small-scale objects, highlighting the effectiveness of persistent and error-aware memory for robust long-horizon perception.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29753) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29753)

## 217. MISeg-Agent: A Human-in-the-Loop Agentic Framework for Error Diagnosis and Executable Prompt Review in 3D Medical Image Segmentation

- **作者**：Delin An, Mingqi Xu, Jian-Xun Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Despite the strong performance of modern medical image segmentation models, their predictions can still contain localized errors that compromise segmentation quality. Identifying and correcting these errors is therefore essential for improving the reliability of existing segmentation results. While interactive segmentation models enable targeted corrections, identifying potential errors, determining where to intervene, and evaluating corrective actions remain challenging. Moreover, each correction changes the current mask, requiring subsequent error diagnosis and recommendations to adapt accordingly. To address these challenges, we propose MISeg-Agent, a human-in-the-loop agentic framework that diagnoses segmentation errors and provides evidence-based correction recommendations. MISeg-Agent first selects a suitable pretrained segmentation model based on benchmark performance and computational requirements. Given its predicted mask, the framework identifies potential missing and excess foreground regions and recommends suspicious slices and corrective points for review. The framework then evaluates candidate point prompts using an interactive segmentation model and presents the resulting corrections for human verification before applying them. By operating directly on images and predicted masks, the error diagnosis process remains independent of the underlying segmentation model architecture. Evaluations on CT datasets demonstrate that MISeg-Agent improves initial segmentation results and produces final masks closely aligned with reference annotations. By integrating adaptive error diagnosis with validated correction recommendations, MISeg-Agent provides an interpretable approach to identifying and reducing residual errors in existing medical image segmentations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29741) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29741)

## 218. Seeing Beyond Names: Image-Grounded Concept Linking for Robust Vision-Language Recognition

- **作者**：Behraj Khan, Shabir Ahmad, Tahir Qasim Syed et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A zero-shot vision-language classifier is highly sensitive to the class names it receives. Even when images remain unchanged, replacing familiar labels with synonyms, abbreviations, or ontology terms can significantly alter predictions. We investigate this \emph{vocabulary shift} using a leakage-free evaluation protocol across Tiny ImageNet, ImageNet-R, ImageNet-A, and ImageNetV2. With CLIP ViT-B/16, alternative class names reduce top-1 accuracy by $14.8$--$17.6$ percentage points. To address this issue, we propose \emph{image-grounded concept linking}, a training-free method that maps class names to visual concepts and uses a reliability gate to improve predictions. The method outperforms a template ensemble across all four datasets, achieving a pooled gain of $1.8$ points. We further evaluate five vision-language backbones across four datasets. Our method improves performance in $16$ of $20$ backbone--dataset combinations, with no significantly negative results. It also reduces pooled negative log-likelihood across all five backbones. Stronger text linking and transductive refinement provide no consistent improvements, suggesting that accurate visual class-name linking remains the main challenge. A second CLIP ViT-B/16 seed confirms the improvement on ImageNetV2, but not on ImageNet-A.
