## 219. Lightweight Spectral Subspace Filtering for Trojan-Resilient AI Inference Engines

- **作者**：Soumita Chatterjee, Sudip Ghosh, Tamal Ghosh et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：13 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables, 1 algorithm. Accepted at ICISS 2027

Backdoor attacks pose a significant security threat to deep neural networks (DNNs) deployed on AI inference engines and hardware accelerators. By embedding hidden triggers during training, these attacks induce malicious behavior while maintaining high accuracy on benign inputs. Multi-trigger backdoor attacks are particularly challenging to detect and mitigate because of the distributed and stealthy nature of their effects. To address this challenge, this paper proposes a training-free Spectral Subspace Filtering framework that detects and suppresses backdoor-induced activations by analyzing latent feature representations. Exploiting the geometric separation between benign and malicious feature distributions, the framework employs Singular Value Decomposition (SVD) to identify the dominant malicious subspace and performs inference-time spectral scrubbing through activation projection. This approach suppresses backdoor effects without model retraining, requiring only a small set of triggered samples collected offline. Experimental evaluation across CNN and transformer architectures, including AlexNet, ResNet18, VGG16, MobileNetV2, and Vision Transformer (ViT), demonstrates that the proposed method reduces the attack success rate (ASR) from 94.2-99.8% to an average of 2.07%, while preserving clean accuracy of up to 98.54% with low inference overhead. The framework also demonstrates robustness across varying poisoning rates and multiple concurrent triggers, highlighting its potential as a lightweight runtime defense for enhancing the security and reliability of AI inference engines against backdoor attacks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29734) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29734)

## 220. Reasoning Beyond Imitation: Observation Conditioned Distillation for Video LLMs

- **作者**：Junzhou Chen, Jindong Wang, Gang Zhou
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Reasoning distillation offers a promising approach to transfer temporal reasoning capabilities of powerful Video-LLMs to open-source student models. However, teacher and student Video-LLMs may construct different temporal observations from the same video due to the adoption of distinct video sampling pipelines. A reasoning trajectory valid under the teacher's observations may rely on evidence inaccessible to the student and thus cannot be directly executed based on the student's observations. We formalize this as a conditional target mismatch, thereby revealing the Reasoning Acquisition-Execution Gap. To bridge this gap, we propose an acquisition-to-execution framework that first distills teacher trajectories into a student reasoning prior and then further optimizes the execution capability of this prior across multiple observations. We further introduce Execution-Aware Reference Regularization, which retains the existing prior when it is executable under current observations, while allowing a greater degree of adaptive adjustment when it is not. Experiments across multiple Video-LLM backbones and six video reasoning benchmarks show that explicitly optimizing observation-conditioned execution consistently outperforms supervised distillation and standard RL-based optimization, with gains extending to held-out out-of-domain benchmarks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29728) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29728)

## 221. ApexID: Hierarchical Learning for Multi-Species Animal Re-Identification

- **作者**：Seifeldin Elkerdany, Mohamed ElBassat, Gamal Abouelhamd et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Animal re-identification (Re-ID) must distinguish individuals across species and capture conditions. We introduce ApexID, a hierarchical SphereFace2-based loss that integrates global identity discrimination with balanced animal-category supervision. Using 132,533 images curated from 19 public datasets, we evaluate three model families and four objectives on four external datasets with observation-aware retrieval protocols. ApexID achieves a dataset-averaged Rank-1 accuracy of 64.89% with ConvNeXt-Small and an mAP of 32.54% with MIEW-ID, outperforming their pretrained reference models. On elephants, a category absent from animal Re-ID training, our ConvNeXt-Small variant achieves the highest retrieval performance among the fourteen evaluated configurations. Scores vary by backbone and dataset, and open-set evaluation reveals remaining discovery challenges. These findings support hierarchical category-identity learning to improve transfer to unseen datasets and species.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29723) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29723)

## 222. UniDR: Learning To Generate Anatomical Deformations From Images via Unified Diffusion Modeling and Registration

- **作者**：Swakshar Deb, Tonmoy Hossain, Tom Fletcher et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at WACV, 2027

This paper presents a novel framework, Unified Diffusion modeling and Registration (UniDR), which learns to generate arbitrary anatomical deformations from images with preserved topology. Existing approaches often model the generative process separately from registration-based deformation estimation; hence making them prone to propagated errors from the registration stage. To address this limitation, we propose a unified paradigm based on a conditional diffusion model over deformation fields that are jointly optimized from the registration process. A principled registration-guided score-matching objective function is then introduced to align the diffusion process with the deformation distribution induced by registration. As a result, our UniDR establishes an explicit connection between generation and registration: the diffusion model enables exploration of the space of plausible anatomical deformations, whereas registration provides structured supervision for learning transformations consistent with observed images. Once trained, UniDR is able to sample diverse, anatomically plausible transformations conditioned on any single input image. We evaluate UniDR across multiple real-world applications and demonstrate its effectiveness in generating high-fidelity image samples compared to the state-of-the-art. Additionally, we demonstrate that these generated samples improve performance in downstream medical image analysis and vision tasks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29712) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29712)

## 223. HandiVid: A High-Quality Dataset for Hand-Driven Interactive Video Generation

- **作者**：Chaoyang Zhang, Shaohao Rui, Xiaofeng Mao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Hand-driven egocentric interactive video generation aims to generate dynamic scenes with realistic interaction responses conditioned on hand movements and camera motion. However, existing egocentric video datasets struggle to provide scene diversity, meaningful hand-object interactions, and reliable motion annotations simultaneously. To address these limitations, we introduce HandiVid, a high-quality dataset comprising 200K curated and annotated egocentric interaction video clips for hand-driven interactive video generation. We develop a unified data processing pipeline that integrates public datasets, online videos and synthetic videos. Through video quality assessment, hand detection filtering, and multi-stage interaction validation using vision-language models (VLMs), we select high-quality video clips with meaningful hand-object interactions. Since 3D hand reconstruction relies on initial hand detections, we integrate SAM 3 into the hand detection pipeline to reduce missed detections and improve the completeness of 3D hand annotations. Each video clip is provided with high-quality hand renderings, camera trajectories, and interaction descriptions. Comprehensive dataset analysis and downstream video generation experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of HandiVid in terms of data diversity, annotation quality, and controllable video generation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29693) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29693)

## 224. Ref2Light: From One Image to Interactive Glasses-Free 3D Characters

- **作者**：Hanzhong Zhang, Haoyang Wang, Siyang Song
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：25 pages, 9 figures

Animating character illustrations on light-field displays demands coherent binocular parallax, real-time pose articulation, and fine-grained expression control. However, existing 2D animation and 3D reconstruction pipelines cannot combine these with rapid character switching. We present Ref2Light, a feed-forward framework that maps a single A-pose illustration and continuous pose and expression controls to display-native light-field animation, without per-character 3D reconstruction, rigging, or test-time optimization. Our key insight is to decompose light-field synthesis into static identity, view-dependent parallax, and control-driven articulation. A shared encoder caches multi-scale identity features once per character for rapid switching. A conditional decoder then synthesizes ordered angular anchors from these features via backward texture transport and direct color synthesis. A global-local control scheme deforms the anchors with head-body controls and uses the updated correspondence fields to track facial regions across views for masked local facial refinement. Finally, calibrated differentiable subpixel interlacing converts the anchors into the native panel raster, enabling end-to-end native supervision without rendering all views at full resolution. On held-out VRoid identities, Ref2Light reduces head-body LPIPS by 20.1%-55.6% over 2D and 3D baselines and lowers non-target change MAE in mouth closure by 95.6%-96.5% versus monolithic decoding, while running at 34.0-40.1 FPS for 1440x2560 native rasters on a consumer GPU. Code and demo are in the Supplementary Material.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29678) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29678)

## 225. DeclareViT: Decoupling Early Classification from Feature Refinement with Exit Query for Efficient ViTs

- **作者**：Xinrui Gong, Yao Rong, Chuangtao Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision Transformers achieve remarkable recognition performance but incur substantial computational cost during inference, limiting their use in latency- and compute-constrained deployment scenarios. Early-exit inference offers a promising solution by allowing easy samples to terminate at intermediate layers. However, existing approaches typically couple early classification with the shared backbone, requiring intermediate representations to support both early classification and subsequent feature refinement. Under joint training, the corresponding optimization objectives jointly update shared backbone parameters, which can lead to conflicting gradient directions. Motivated by this observation, we introduce DeclareViT, a framework that decouples early classification from feature refinement. Each exit uses a dedicated query to read intermediate features through one-way cross-attention without modifying the backbone token stream. The query branches are optimized for early classification while the backbone and final classifier remain frozen. Across ViT, DeiT, and Swin on CIFAR-100, ImageNet-1K, and Food-101, DeclareViT consistently establishes a state-of-the-art accuracy-efficiency trade-off: It achieves 1.58 times to 2.57 times multiply-accumulate (MAC)-based speed-up with only a 0.39-1.91 percentage-point accuracy drop. Wall-clock measurements on CPU and GPU further demonstrate practical inference acceleration. Code is available at https://github.com/TUDa-HWAI/DeclareViT.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29674) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29674)

## 226. PROPS: Can Visual Generators Keep the Current Look of a Prop?

- **作者**：Xiangzhao Hao, Dianyi Wang, Keyan Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Many generation tasks draw the next image from a visual history, such as storyboards, illustrated stories and multi-turn editing. A new request often states what happens next without restating how earlier objects look. Instead of recovering an object's current look from the history, generators often redraw it from the request alone or bring back an outdated look, and the error can carry into later steps. We call this failure prop drift. Existing evaluations rarely isolate it, because similarity to the history rewards copying and a judge that sees a single history cannot tell a look recovered from that history from one the generator happens to prefer. We introduce PROPS, a benchmark of 2,257 pairs of twin histories that end on the same request but show one prop in two different looks. A pair passes only if the generator draws the matching look on each side and carries out the request, so a look preferred by the generator or the judge passes at most one side. All 34 open and closed systems we test show prop drift, and even systems that carry out the requested action on most sides often lose the current look of the prop. Swapping the planners and editors of agents further shows that both history understanding and image generation limit agent performance. We also build PROPS-Train, 80,250 training chains in four tracks whose targets recover an earlier state or carry scripted changes forward, and fine-tuning on it clearly improves all four tested models. Both datasets will be publicly released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29665) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29665)

## 227. How Reliable is AI-Generated Audio Description? A Multilingual Expert Evaluation

- **作者**：Annette Rios, Lukas Fischer, Sarah Ebling
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.CV

Audio description (AD) makes visual media, such as video, accessible to blind or low-vision (BLV) audiences, but the high cost of professional production limits coverage. Due to recent advances in AI, numerous approaches to automatically generate intermediate AD scripts and/or final AD audios have been proposed. We present an expert evaluation of AI-generated AD scripts for movies in Italian and German that compares two AI systems, a GPT-4o- and a Qwen-based pipeline, against human-authored descriptions. Professional audio describers rated description quality and annotated errors using a detailed error taxonomy that includes content, character, fluency and coherence errors. The results show that while GPT-4o outperforms the Qwen models, both AI systems still fall short of human professional quality: the generated descriptions show substantially higher error rates and lower quality ratings. Based on the error annotations, we identify two key areas that require improvement: 1) character references, which includes both issues with identification and coherent naming, and 2) content errors and hallucinations, where the AD misrepresents or invents events. These error types are particularly critical, as they directly affect the viewer's ability to follow the narrative.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29658) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29658)

## 228. SuperFine3D: Efficient Global-Local Recursive Refinement for Extremely Detailed 3D Geometry Generation

- **作者**：Lihan Li, Junwei Zhou, Yu-wing Tai
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

High-resolution images contain rich visual details that require fine geometric structures for faithful 3D reconstruction. However, generating extremely detailed 3D geometry from images requires substantial computational resources and training data, making high-resolution geometry generation challenging for existing state-of-the-art models. In this paper, we present SuperFine3D, an efficient framework for generating extremely detailed 3D geometry through global-local recursive refinement. SuperFine3D learns a shared refinement operator from paired coarse-to-fine representations and recursively reuses it to progressively synthesize finer geometry beyond the training resolutions, without requiring additional paired supervision at target resolutions. To make recursive refinement computationally efficient, we introduce spatial-cell attention, which restricts high-resolution interactions to local spatial neighborhoods while maintaining bounded communication across neighboring cells. We further propose spatially anchored cross-scale guidance, which leverages globally contextualized coarse geometry to guide local detail synthesis and preserve structural consistency. In addition, geometry-aware training objectives encourage the refinement process to preserve stable geometric structures while introducing fine-grained surface details. Experiments demonstrate that SuperFine3D generates geometry at a $4096^3$ decoder-grid resolution, corresponding to a sparse $256^3$ latent lattice, using a shared refinement operator trained only on $512^3$--$1024^3$ representations. Compared with a full-attention recursive baseline, SuperFine3D achieves a $9.4\times$ speedup in end-to-end inference and reduces peak GPU memory usage by $23.0\%$, while improving cross-scale structural consistency and producing cleaner surfaces with finer geometric details.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29630) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29630)

## 229. Contamination at the Operating Point: Train-Test Identity Overlap Inflates Low-FMR Face Recognition Accuracy by an Order of Magnitude

- **作者**：Fu Dai, Kamal Taha, Naoufel Werghi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CR
- **备注**：14 pages, 10 figures, 9 tables

Face verification systems are deployed by selecting a decision threshold that achieves a target false match rate (FMR); performance is then reported as the corresponding false non-match rate (FNMR). In practice, deployed systems operate at FMR $\le 10^{-6}$, but widely used benchmarks can share identities with the models' training corpora. Prior work (Wu et al., 2024) measures the resulting optimistic bias as a small additive change in 10-fold accuracy (up to 1.01 percentage points). We show that the bias is neither small nor constant: it is a multiplicative error that grows as FMR falls. First, we audit train-test overlap with a detector calibrated against a null distribution (rather than a hand-tuned threshold). Relative to WebFace42M, we flag 88.6% of MS1MV3 identities and 14.3% of IJB-C subjects as present, despite WebFace260M's published de-overlap explicitly naming IJB-C. Manual review of 100 flagged IJB-C subjects confirms 69.2%, implying a residual overlap of roughly 9.9%. Second, holding the model fixed, removing only the contaminated identities from the test set increases FNMR by 3.6$\times$ at FMR=$10^{-4}$, 6.3$\times$ at FMR=$10^{-5}$, and 13.9$\times$ at FMR=$10^{-6}$, while a same-size random removal does not. The effect is traced to a shorter impostor tail that lowers the threshold. The magnitude of the shift depends on the training corpus and does not cancel betweent margin between our model and a publicbaseline by half. Third, we give the first direct measurement of the effective number of independent impostor comparisons in IJB-C: only 2.7 million effectively independent comparisons at FMR=$10^{-6}$, which is below all commonly accepted reporting rules. Finallyth a known training corpus, we build areal-face protocol with audited zero overlap that resolves FMR=$10^{-8}$.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29629) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29629)

## 230. Mass Forcing: On-Policy Mass-Aware Distillation for Autoregressive Video Generation

- **作者**：Yiyu Li, Fei Wei, Yong Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) has emerged as a promising paradigm for real-time video synthesis, distilling pretrained diffusion models into few-step autoregressive generators. However, its reverse-KL objective is mode-seeking and can collapse the student's conditional video distribution onto a narrow subset of modes, leading to reduced sample diversity, diminished temporal dynamics, and color oversaturation. Existing methods mitigate this issue mainly via coverage-oriented student initialization, but we observe that such coverage is transient: across three initializations, the fraction of dynamic videos drops sharply during subsequent DMD training, indicating that the mode-seeking objective, not the initialization, drives this degradation. We propose Mass Forcing, a probability-mass-aware framework that moves on-policy DMD supervision from whole videos to individual states. Each update branches several continuations from a shared student-generated prefix, making the student's probability mass at that state measurable against the teacher. First, Mass Forcing estimates relative teacher-to-student density ratios among these continuations through latent-space score integration and uses them to rebalance conditional DMD updates, amplifying rollouts underrepresented relative to the teacher. Second, since mismatched mode probabilities do not necessarily induce strong local score corrections, it aligns generated rollout sets with a reweighted reference distribution defined by the same mass estimates, providing cross-rollout supervision. Experiments on MovieGen and VBench show that Mass Forcing improves video fidelity and sustains temporal dynamics throughout training, achieving the highest Total scores among compared methods and a Dynamic Degree of 83% after 1,000 generator updates, versus 12-32% for the baselines, with no additional inference-time overhead.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29618) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29618)

## 231. GravHMR: Coupling SLAM and Human Mesh Recovery in a Shared Gravity Frame

- **作者**：Hanglei Jin, Chenglu Sun, Junchen Ge et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://jhl25-pixel.github.io/gravhmr/

Recovering globally consistent 3D human motion from monocular videos remains challenging under dynamic camera motion. Although SLAM and human mesh recovery (HMR) provide complementary camera and body estimates, directly combining them often leads to inconsistent orientations and accumulated trajectory drift. We present GravHMR, a training-free, plug-and-play framework that couples SLAM and HMR through a shared gravity-aligned reference. By allowing gravity to be estimated independently of the HMR backbone, GravHMR accommodates world-grounded HMR models with or without explicit gravity-view representations. Specifically, Yaw-Aware Orientation Optimization (YAOO) resolves heading inconsistencies by correcting body and velocity orientations about the shared gravity axis. Conditioned on these corrected orientations, Gravity-Aware Trajectory Optimization (GATO) anchors root trajectories in the metric SLAM world by jointly leveraging 2D reprojection, the metric scale implicit in the human body, and the body's own motion. On EMDB-2, GravHMR achieves state-of-the-art performance with 154.0 mm W-MPJPE100 using ViPE and 189.8 mm using masked DROID-SLAM, outperforming TRAM (222.4 mm) under the same masked DROID-SLAM camera estimates. It further reduces W-MPJPE100 by 44.3% over GVHMR (276.5 mm) and by 20.0% over GVHMR + HTD-Refine (192.4 mm) with the same HMR backbone.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29614) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29614)

## 232. Action Windows Are Inherently Temporal: A Benchmark for Time-Sensitive Robotic Manipulation

- **作者**：Haoxiang Zhan, Haoqin Hong, Congcong Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Vision-language model (VLM) agents have surpassed learned-policy baselines on existing robotic manipulation benchmarks. However, these agents still struggle with time-sensitive everyday tasks that existing benchmarks for VLM agents cover only partially. In these tasks, evolving physical processes create finite windows for successful intervention. These action windows are inherently temporal and impose a shared time budget on decision making and physical execution. Coordinating intervention within this budget requires agents to determine when the window opens. We introduce AWAIT, a simulation benchmark that evaluates task completion within action windows while accounting for decision and execution time. Our evaluation reveals difficulties in recognizing window onset and shows that successive decisions and movements can exhaust an action window even when each decision takes less than the window duration. Project page: https://eyja42.github.io/await-site/.

## 233. Inspect Before You Decide: Evidence-Guided Visual Search for Industrial Anomaly Detection

- **作者**：Peng Chen, Chao Huang, Yangyang Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recent advances in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated remarkable potential for industrial anomaly detection and understanding. However, reliably identifying subtle anomalies remains challenging, as existing approaches often struggle to acquire and verify fine-grained visual evidence beyond global observations. To address this challenge, we propose EVAD, an evidence-guided visual search framework that enables MLLMs to actively seek and examine diagnostically informative visual evidence for reliable anomaly reasoning. Specifically, EVAD begins with a global visual assessment to identify potentially anomalous regions for further inspection. Building upon this observation, we introduce a Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS)-guided exploration strategy that progressively acquires visual evidence through two complementary operations: semantic refinement for fine-grained inspection and spatial expansion for contextual verification. The acquired evidence is subsequently evaluated and integrated to support more informed and reliable anomaly decisions. Extensive experiments across multiple industrial anomaly detection tasks demonstrate that EVAD achieves state-of-the-art performance in most evaluated settings, highlighting the effectiveness of active evidence exploration for fine-grained anomaly understanding and reasoning. The code will be publicly available upon publication.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29551) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29551)

## 234. Align the Pair, Not the Sample: Representation Alignment for Faster and Better One-Step Generato

- **作者**：Mingxiao Li, Xuantuo Huang, Encheng Su et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages

Representation alignment markedly accelerates diffusion training, but one-step generators raise a question that diffusion does not: \emph{which training path should receive the loss?} A one-step generator sees two kinds of inputs. Corrupted real images come with their clean counterpart, which provides an instance-paired target. Samples generated from pure noise have no corresponding real image, yet this is where the final model operates. We study this question on Priming, which trains one-step generators from scratch by reconstructing corrupted images and then matching feature distributions with optimal transport. Aligning the generative path, the natural choice, collapses recall ($0.45\!\to\!0.15$--$0.25$) in all 17 configurations we test, including five target constructions and loss weights down to $0.05$. Aligning the paired reconstruction path with a frozen DINOv2 encoder instead helps in every setting we test. A short analysis explains this asymmetry: when the target carries no instance information about the input, the optimal aligned feature is constant within each class, so the loss pulls samples toward class prototypes. A control confirms this: aligning the paired path to a random same-class image instead of the input's own image removes most of the gain. Inside the generator, paired-path alignment creates an interface block through which post-training routes noise, and the image semantics carried through it predict sample quality across 16 models. The resulting recipe, \method, accelerates \emph{both} training stages on ImageNet $256\!\times\!256$: it matches the 160-epoch baseline with $2\times$ fewer priming epochs, surpasses its final quality with $4\times$ fewer post-training updates, and lowers FID to 1.30 (B) and 1.155 (L) at the standard budget. A joint single-stage variant trains native one-step generators and reaches FID 1.22 with 115M parameters.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29547) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29547)

## 235. SCRA-CLIP: Spherical-Contextual Representation Adaptation for Zero-Shot Anomaly Detection

- **作者**：Peng Chen, Chao Huang, Wenqiang Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Recent advances in vision-language models have facilitated zero-shot anomaly detection (ZSAD) through transferable visual-semantic representations. However, detecting fine-grained anomalies remains challenging due to inadequate modeling of diverse anomaly patterns and their spatial context. To address these challenges, we propose SCRA-CLIP, a Spherical-Contextual Representation Adaptation framework that jointly enhances anomaly-aware textual semantics and context-sensitive visual representations. Specifically, we introduce Spherical Prototype-Conditioned Prompting (SPCP), which employs von Mises--Fisher mixtures to model the directional distributions of normal and abnormal patch features. These state-specific prototypes are incorporated into textual contexts to guide the learning of more discriminative textual representations. Furthermore, we develop Context-Deviation Guided Visual Adaptation (CGVA), which captures local feature deviations relative to multi-scale neighborhood contexts and refines patch representations through adaptive gating. By integrating distribution-aware semantic modeling with context-sensitive visual adaptation, SCRA-CLIP facilitates more discriminative cross-modal matching for fine-grained anomaly detection and localization. Extensive experiments on 11 industrial and medical benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and generalization capability of SCRA-CLIP across diverse unseen categories and domains. The code will be publicly available upon publication.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29545) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29545)

## 236. Seeing vs. Knowing: Counterfactual Supervision and Correction for 3D-Aware Object Editing

- **作者**：Jian Zahng, Zhijun Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Rotating, translating, or rescaling an object in a photograph requires revealing unseen surfaces, preserving visible appearance, and updating shadows and reflections. Reconstructed 3D assets, rendered at the target pose as a 3D state, provide explicit geometric guidance, but their imperfect textures may conflict with the photograph, while standard paired training does not explicitly teach the editor which source to trust. To address this, we propose counterfactual state supervision, which constructs controlled photo--state conflicts through hidden-surface repainting, hidden geometry with re-rendered shadows and reflections, and visible-surface texture replacement. This teaches the editor to follow the 3D state for unseen content while preserving photograph-visible appearance. However, learning this distinction does not ensure that it is maintained during sampling. We therefore introduce symmetric counterfactual correction, which uses state-side and photo-side twins, which share the photograph and the state respectively, to construct directed latent perturbations toward condition-inconsistent targets and train recovery toward the target specified by the current condition. Both strategies operate only during training, requiring no architectural changes or additional inference steps. Experiments on OBJect and SpatialEdit-Bench demonstrate strong spatial editing performance and content preservation, while controlled ablations show improved source selection and sustained responsiveness to conditioning during sampling.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29542) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29542)

## 237. STAGE: Structured Test-Time Adaptation with Global Entropic Soft-coupling for Cross-Subject Brain-to-Image Retrieval

- **作者**：Yihang Zou, Ruixi Liang, Chunmei Qing et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：38 pages, including the main paper and appendix

Cross-subject brain-to-image retrieval aims to identify viewed images from unseen subjects' brain signals. However, inter-subject variability hinders alignment of brain-image representations, while independent query ranking tends to concentrate predictions on a few candidates, making accurate retrieval difficult without target labels. We propose STAGE (Structured Test-Time Adaptation with Global Entropic Soft-coupling), a structured test-time adaptation framework for recovering cross-subject alignment from uncertain brain-image correspondences. Moment calibration reduces statistical discrepancies between features, while coupling-weighted initialization establishes initial alignment. Coupling-guided adaptation further refines residual feature mismatches, and soft-coupling ranking coordinates candidate competition across queries to prevent overly concentrated matching assignments. Using only unlabeled target batches, STAGE maintains consistent matching from feature calibration to image retrieval while keeping both encoders frozen. Experiments on three public datasets validate STAGE's retrieval performance. On THINGS-EEG2, STAGE achieves 68.25% Top-1 and 90.47% Top-5, improving Top-1 by 18.52 percentage points over SCORE recovery and CSLS on identical frozen NICE features. Calibrated on 200 queries and a 200-image gallery covering their identities, STAGE retains 42.05% Top-1 when ranking 1,854 images with the transform frozen.

## 238. A ROS2 based Human-In-The-Loop Framework for UAV Search and Rescue Operations

- **作者**：Anubhav Pandey, Harshit Nagpal, Aditya Agarwal et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：30 pages, 10 figures, Submitted to and under review in "International Journal of Intelligent Systems" of Wiley

Existing Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) autonomy frameworks lack human contextual awareness, limiting real-time adaptability in unstructured Search and Rescue (SAR) environments. To address data availability, we propose a Robot Operating System (ROS2)-based human-in-the-loop (HITL) framework that translates human directional gestures (left/right) into UAV navigation commands using RGB-Depth (RGB-D) sensing and YOLOv11 (You Only Look Once). The deprecation of ROS1 necessitates migrating to ROS2, prioritising real-time communication, scalability, and long-term maintainability. Unlike approaches optimised for peak accuracy under ideal conditions, the proposed system prioritises robustness and real-time deployment in degraded SAR environments. Our system achieves 89% confidence in depth estimation under occlusion and varying environmental conditions in Gazebo-PX4 simulations. In foggy conditions, RGB-D fusion consistently provides more informative cues than RGB alone, with an average improvement of 46.7% across all scenarios and distances. The framework demonstrates superior occlusion handling (+15%), adaptive RGB-D fusion, temporal consistency modelling, and real-time deployment via a ROS2 data distribution service (DDS), with end-to-end latency below 50 ms. We evaluate the modified YOLOv11 and the depth-weighted pose estimation on a held-out simulated test set and on real people from the public UAV-GESTURE dataset, in clear air and in Koschmieder fog. Without retraining, the framework reads the gestures of real people with 80% accuracy, depth keeps 77% at a visibility of 10 m where RGB drops to 24%, and the modified YOLOv11 reaches 91% in simulation and 99% on held-out real people when trained on real data. The open-source ROS2 implementation eases the transition of SAR-UAV platforms from ROS1, enabling UAVs to act directly on operator gestures without complex path planning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29507) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29507)

## 239. CoHABIT: Complementary Evaluation of Furniture Layouts through Spatial Rules and Household Routines

- **作者**：Ewsey Obzherin, Samira Atlangirieva, Anastasia Antsiferova et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：Includes supplementary material

We introduce CoHABIT, a framework for evaluating furniture layouts through complementary spatial rules and household routines. Static assessment captures geometric validity, ergonomic access, functional organization, and configurable design preferences, including Feng Shui. Dynamic assessment uses learned resident policies to simulate everyday activities, resource sharing, coordination, and individual burdens in multi-agent households. Controlled comparisons show how spatial criteria and household behavior reveal distinct aspects of layout quality, while resident profiles and scheduling influence furnishing rankings. Independent verification of household needs grounds dynamic comparisons in observed outcomes, enabling interpretable assessments of how furnished environments serve different households.

## 240. CAR: Reducing Decoder Output Shift in Visual Token Pruning via Coverage Selection and Attention Reallocation

- **作者**：Xixi Jiang, Pingcheng Dong, Chen Yang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Visual token pruning is an effective way to accelerate multimodal large language models (MLLMs). However, existing methods degrade substantially under aggressive pruning, especially on visual grounding (VG). We analyze the sources of this degradation from the perspective of the output shift that pruning causes in decoder layers, and decompose this shift in closed form into two terms, an image summary error and an attention allocation error. The summary error stems from the loss of visual information, and the allocation error from the drop in the share of attention assigned to visual tokens. To reduce both errors, we propose CAR (Coverage selection and Attention Reallocation), a token pruning framework that restores attention share while maintaining information. Specifically, to minimize the summary error, whose upper bound tightens as the retained tokens better cover all visual tokens, our coverage selection retains tokens that are both salient and non-redundant, with saliency from encoder heads chosen by a label-free probe. To minimize the allocation error, our attention reallocation reassigns the attention of the discarded tokens to the retained ones through a closed-form logit bias. On Qwen2.5-VL-7B with $90\%$ of the visual tokens pruned, CAR retains $93.0\%$ of the unpruned accuracy on visual question answering (VQA) and $77.9\%$ on VG, against $26.5\%$ for the best existing method on VG; the logit bias alone raises the VG accuracy of existing methods by up to $24$ points. Experiments on multiple MLLMs across VQA, VG and video understanding show that CAR generalizes well and consistently outperforms existing methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29489) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29489)

## 241. Neutron Imaging Enhancement for Lithium Batteries Based on Swin-Transformer and Image Fusion

- **作者**：Hansheng Ding, Fanxu Meng, Tian Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Neutron imaging is highly sensitive to light elements such as lithium and is therefore valuable for non-destructive testing (NDT) and analyzing internal structural evolution in lithium batteries. However, limited neutron-source flux and detector performance often result in images with mixed noise, low contrast, and blurred edges, hindering accurate assessment of battery internal states. To address these issues, this paper proposes a lithium-battery neutron image enhancement method combining an improved Swin-Transformer with spatial-domain image fusion. First, an improved Swin-Transformer incorporating Multi-Dconv Head Transposed Attention (MDTA) and Global Response Normalization (GRN) extracts and reconstructs image features to suppress high-density white spots and Gaussian-Poisson mixed noise. Second, guided filtering and a gradient-adaptive mask are used for selective edge enhancement, while Contrast Limited Adaptive Histogram Equalization (CLAHE) improves local contrast. Finally, a spatial-frequency (SF)-based local block-weighted fusion mechanism adaptively combines the edge-enhanced and contrast-equalized images to preserve microscopic details while suppressing noise. Experiments show that, compared with BM3D, DnCNN, and GAN, the proposed method achieves strong performance in full-reference and no-reference evaluations. Its best PSNR and SSIM reach 33.0517 dB and 0.9637, respectively, while the NR-IQA and Natural Image Quality Evaluator (NIQE) scores are as low as 0.1794 and 4.0974. The method reduces artifacts and over smoothing, improving the visual fidelity and structural identifiability of lithium-battery neutron images.

## 242. Separating Action Allocation from Image Matching in Visual Token Routing

- **作者**：Chenxi Wang, Bin Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：13 pages, 5 figures, 4 tables

Adaptive visual token routers aim to save computation while preserving the image detail needed to answer each question. A higher average score could reflect better choices for individual images or more frequent use of high resolution on tasks that need it. We distinguish the two by keeping action counts fixed within each task and comparing the router's assignments with random pairings of the same actions and images. On equally weighted ChartQA and TextVQA, both Qwen2.5-VL-3B and -7B gain from matching but lose more from their action frequencies relative to a task table, with a 1.71-point gain and a 3.95-point loss on 7B. Exchanging the continuous resolutions chosen by the external selector CARES also lowers quality on 7B. When requests arrive with task labels, the table outscores the discrete router by 1.92 points and saves 31.76 ms per complete 7B request on average.

## 243. RSWP: Robust SO(3)-Equivariant Watermarking for Panoramic Images

- **作者**：Pengzhen Chen, Yanwei Liu, Xiaoyan Gu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CR

Robust watermarking for panoramic imagery is impeded by the topological mismatch between spherical signals and planar representations. Conventional methods fail under $SO(3)$ viewing rotations due to non-linear geometric warping and struggle with viewport extraction, which typically necessitates computationally expensive inverse remapping. To address these challenges, we propose RSWP, a watermarking framework grounded in icosahedral group theory. Leveraging the property that the icosahedral group ($\mathcal{I}_{60}$) serves as the largest discrete subgroup of $SO(3)$, we decompose spherical rotations into tractable local planar rotations and combinatorial facet permutations. Accordingly, we introduce a decoupling architecture that embeds watermarks into discrete facets and a theoretically guaranteed Equivariance-to-Invariance decoding paradigm. By employing an equivariant feature extractor followed by a permutation-invariant sorting module, this paradigm rigorously synchronizes rotation-distorted features. Moreover, the dual-extractor design inherently supports direct, blind watermark retrieval from partial 2D renderings without geometric calibration. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RSWP achieves state-of-the-art robustness against continuous $SO(3)$ rotations and viewport transformations while preserving high visual fidelity.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29468) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29468)

## 244. Fill in the Blanks: Non-Autoregressive Schema-Parallel Key Information Extraction with CTC Decoding

- **作者**：Shaked Perek, Avihu Dekel, Ben Wiesel et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-language models (VLMs) extract structured data from documents by generating the requested JSON one token at a time, so their sequential steps grow with the length of the answer. Yet in key information extraction (KIE) the schema already fixes the answer's structure: keys, nesting and lists are known before decoding, and only the values are unknown. We exploit this with a non-autoregressive extractor that writes the structure from the schema, sizes every list with a count pass, and predicts all values in parallel, in a fixed number of passes that does not grow with the document. Each value is decoded with connectionist temporal classification (CTC) in its own block of slots, so its length never has to be predicted, and a value that is still open after its block gets a larger token budget. Zero-shot on VAREX, CORD and FUNSD, our 4B extractor is 3.5$\times$ faster than the diffusion-VLM competition on average (up to 5.6$\times$) and more accurate, with 4--6 forward passes per document where block diffusion needs 12--74. The same extractor also drafts for speculative decoding with its own autoregressive base: it runs 8.3$\times$ faster than autoregressive decoding (up to 10.4$\times$) within 0.4 points of its accuracy, and 3.3$\times$ faster than speculative diffusion decoding while 13.0 points more accurate. We release the model and code.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29460) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29460)

## 245. Exploring Attention-Driven Deep Learning for Accurate Lung Nodule Segmentation

- **作者**：Nima Shafiei Rezvani Nezhad, Meysam Mansouri, Saeid Ghayour et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：6 pages, 2 figures. Accepted manuscript of a paper published at IEEE EMBC 2025

This study explores the application of various deep learning models for the segmentation of lung nodules using LIDC-IDRI. Unlike traditional approaches that utilize the full dataset, our work emphasizes the efficacy of training models on a filtered subset of 356 samples. Novel configurations, including attention mechanisms and advanced preprocessing strategies, were employed to optimize segmentation accuracy. Among the models evaluated, the DPL nkNet50 with a Channel Attention Bridge and ResNet backbone demonstrated the highest performance with a Dice score of 0.86, significantly outperforming conventional architectures. This work underscores the potential of leveraging data efficiency and tailored architectures in achieving robust segmentation performance, paving the way for improved computer-aided diagnosis in clinical settings.

## 246. Seeing Through Preferences: Recovering Hidden Local Preference Neighborhoods for Text-to-Image Alignment

- **作者**：Pulkit Bansal, Vivek Srivastava, Shirish Karande
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Aligning text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models with human preferences typically relies on large-scale pairwise annotations, which are expensive to obtain and provide only sparse supervision. Moreover, not all preference comparisons contribute equally to learning: comparisons between semantically distant images often reveal little about the fine-grained perceptual factors underlying human judgments. We argue that informative supervision can arise from local preference neighborhoods surrounding human-preferred images, where semantically aligned alternatives expose meaningful perceptual and structural differences. Motivated by this observation, we propose PrefLens, a framework that recovers local preference neighborhoods from sparse human feedback. Specifically, PrefLens generates controlled perceptual variations around preferred images that largely retain semantic identity and selects compact sets of informative neighbors in a reward-defined preference space induced by pretrained reward models. By transforming each human preference annotation into a structured neighborhood of informative comparisons, PrefLens enriches supervision without requiring additional human labeling. Our experiments and diagnostic evaluations show that the recovered neighborhoods capture human-relevant preference characteristics and provide effective supervision for preference alignment across diverse settings.

## 247. Ride on Anything: Contact-Coupled Monocular 4D Reconstruction of a Rider and Horse in Camera and World Frames

- **作者**：Zehang Weng, Ci Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages, 7 figures, 6 tables. Code and interactive results: https://wengzehang.github.io/rideonanything/

We address the reconstruction of a horse and its rider from a single video. When a human estimator and a horse estimator are applied independently, both meshes can fit their silhouettes well while the rider hovers beside or passes through the horse, slides along its back and jitters relative to it. These errors lie along the camera axis and are not visible in the input view. We present Ride on Anything (RoA), a general, training-free methodology for reconstructing a person riding an animal, which couples frozen estimators of the rider and the mount at test time. We instantiate it for horseback riding with SAM 3D Body and 4DEquine. RoA aligns both estimators on a common frame index, projects both subjects through one pixel-isotropic camera initialized from the horse estimator, and jointly optimizes bounded corrections under interaction terms defined in the horse's body frame, including a collision term based on generalized winding numbers that remains valid on the non-watertight horse mesh. The result is lifted to a levelled world frame using the camera rotation from the background and the field of view from MoGe-2. On our riding clips, the raw outputs of the two estimators leave the rider's pelvis 15 cm from the horse's back on average. RoA reduces this seat gap to 1 cm, the drift of the seat along and across the spine by 64% and 89%, the 90th-percentile penetration depth by 57%, and the share of rider surface inside the horse from 12.1% to 7.9%. The motion is markedly smoother (94% lower acceleration of the rider relative to the horse, 80% lower horse jerk), while the rider's silhouette IoU decreases from 0.59 to 0.52. Qualitatively, the rider stays seated on the horse and moves together with it. A quantitative evaluation of 3D accuracy awaits calibrated recordings of ridden animals. We also test a clip outside the task, a horse led on foot, and analyse the failure modes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29435) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29435)

## 248. HorizonFlow: Few-Step Video Distillation in 600 Iterations

- **作者**：Xianglong Guo, Haobo Xu, Zanyi Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：21 pages, 10 figures, 14 tables, including supplementary material. Code: https://github.com/XIANGLONGGUO/HorizonFlow-repo

Distribution matching distillation (DMD) enables fast video generation by distilling a multi-step teacher into a few-step student. However, DMD-based methods often suffer from unnatural motion and costly training. We trace these limitations to the DMD gradient, which jointly optimizes appearance, layout, and motion without distinguishing their learning dynamics. Its static component, which carries appearance and layout, has a much higher signal-to-noise ratio than its motion component. As a result, per-frame quality converges within a few hundred iterations, whereas motion lags far behind. Beyond being noisy, the gradient also lacks two signals. (1) Video VAEs encode several frames into one latent frame (four in Wan2.1), and the gradient provides little guidance for spreading motion over these frames, causing periodic stutter. (2) DMD does not constrain the final sampling step to preserve the layout set by earlier steps, so objects merge and drift once the video moves. We propose HorizonFlow, which decouples the correction of motion from that of layout. It keeps the DMD loss and only shifts its regression target, with one training-only correction in the motion component and one in the orthogonal layout component. Chunk Evenness addresses (1) by spreading the motion of each latent chunk evenly over its frames, and Semantic Commitment addresses (2) by anchoring the coarse layout to the teacher's completion of the student's last step. In only 600 iterations, HorizonFlow distills Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B into a 4-step model using 8.4k training samples, 4.6-304$\times$ fewer than prior work. On VBench, HorizonFlow outperforms all released few-step Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B models. The same recipe transfers to Cosmos-Predict2.5-2B, where our student outperforms NVIDIA's distilled model. Code: https://github.com/XIANGLONGGUO/HorizonFlow-repo

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29431) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29431)

## 249. A colour-marker tracker for pose estimation of slender surgical instruments: vision-language-model-guided adaptive colour calibration

- **作者**：Jaesung Hong, Dong Won Lee
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：19 pages, 7 figures, 6 tables. Extended version of a manuscript submitted to a journal

Slender surgical instruments need small, narrow markers for camera-based pose estimation. A colour-stripe marker fits this requirement, but its colours in the image drift from the design values with lighting, printing and camera settings. Because the colours can only be corrected after the marker has been found, methods that rely on the design colours face a circular problem. We propose vision-language-model (VLM)-based adaptive colour calibration and compare it with two rule-based methods, which re-measure the colours from detections made with the design colours: Initial colour update does so once after tracking starts, and Colour adaptation continuously during tracking. VLM guidance uses a fine-tuned VLM to find the markers without relying on the design colours and builds the colour profile from their pixels. Recall/F1 (%) for Initial colour update, Colour adaptation and VLM guidance were 92.0/95.7, 87.8/93.3 and 98.2/99.1 on 672 synthetic image pairs; 51.3/67.2, 48.8/65.0 and 83.8/87.0 on eight degraded laboratory videos; and 38.9/55.2, 19.7/29.7 and 100/100 on a surgical-robot (da Vinci) video with strongly shifted colours. In that video the rule-based methods never found the purple/cyan marker, whereas VLM guidance found all 234 markers with no false positives. In a simulation, the hue boundary between the two stripes moved by less than 0.1 pixel for exposure gains of 0.5-2.0, compared with 2.9-4.0 pixels for a boundary found with a fixed brightness threshold. When the degradation is mild, the rule-based methods, which need no training and no GPU, are sufficient. When the colours change greatly, the gain comes from a VLM-guided candidate search that does not rely on the design colours. Combining colour-stripe markers with adaptive colour calibration enables tracking of slender surgical instruments with small markers.

## 250. MSBench: Benchmarking Script-Controlled Multi-Shot Text-to-Video Generation

- **作者**：Tao Huang, ZiHao Liang, Junhao Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Text-to-video (T2V) models can now generate visually plausible short clips, but multi-shot generation requires a different set of skills: composing multiple shots with intended cut timing, transition style, camera language, and cross-shot continuity. How far this multi-shot output can be controlled from text remains unmeasured: existing benchmarks either evaluate single shots or, when they provide shot-level scripts, typically check whether the requested shots appear but not when they change or how they are joined. We introduce MSBench, a benchmark for controllable text-driven multi-shot video generation built around structured Scene-Entity-Shot mini-scripts that specify each shot's time span, camera state, content, and outgoing transition. MSBench defines a four-dimensional hybrid evaluation framework spanning Visual Quality, Dynamic Quality, Semantic Alignment, and Cross-Shot Consistency with 11 sub-metrics. Experiments on 14 commercial and open-source systems show that visual quality does little to separate the systems, whereas multi-shot control does: many models cut mainly on scene changes and render requested gradual transitions as hard cuts. Human evaluations further validate MSBench, with its system ranking reaching a Spearman correlation of 0.87 with human preferences. The project page is available at https://totalhuang.github.io/MSBench-page/.

## 251. Who Plays What: Same-Instrument Music Separation from Video

- **作者**：Abhinaba Roy, Ambuj Mehrish, Sebastiano Vascon et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.SD, cs.CV

When two musicians in an ensemble play the same instrument, they sound alike and their instruments look alike. Audio-only separators, and audio-visual separators that rely on appearance, cannot tell them apart. What still differs is how each player moves, and when. We study same-instrument per-player separation: a user clicks one musician in a video and receives that musician's sound alone, even when other players in the video play the same instrument. Our model follows the clicked player and describes them with high level video features and the velocities of body and hand keypoints. A pretrained music separator attends to these tokens through cross-attention. Earlier motion-based separators were scored only on synthetic mixtures of solo videos. We give the first per-player evaluation of same-instrument separation on real ensemble recordings. On 58 players in URMP, each sharing an instrument with at least one other player in the piece, our model reaches a median SDR of 5.3 dB, while four released separators, audio-only and audio-visual, stay at or below 0.3 dB. The gap is wider on same-instrument duets, and our model also beats the audio-visual baselines on their own MUSIC benchmark. Tests show that the gain comes from movement: replacing the video with a still frame removes it, and so does shifting the video by a fraction of a second against the audio.

## 252. AgroVG+: Generalized Visual Grounding in Agriculture with Region-Supervised Action Generation

- **作者**：Haocheng Li, Juepeng Zheng, Guilong Xiao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 9 figures. Includes supplementary material

Generalized visual grounding in agriculture requires localizing all objects that match a natural-language description and recognizing when no matching target is present. We introduce AgroVG+, a dataset whose referring expressions are all written and reviewed by agricultural experts based on image content and corresponding target sets. The dataset comprises 7,404 images and 10,081 query records across six agricultural scene families, covering single-target, multi-target, and target-absent cases. We also propose ReSAG (Region-Supervised Action Generation), a framework that couples regional evidence supervision with explicit box-generation and termination decisions. ReSAG assigns each target an equally weighted supervision term on a shared spatial evidence distribution computed before answer generation. The BOX/END protocol exposes a choice between generating another box and terminating the response at box boundaries, while coordinates are generated using the original language vocabulary. Regional and sequence objectives jointly train a shared language decoder. On AgroVG+, ReSAG outperforms coordinate-only fine-tuning on single- and multi-target exact-set accuracy, and achieves higher aggregate accuracy than either component alone in ablations.

## 253. PINE: Point, Instance and Normal Estimation in One-Step Diffusion Model

- **作者**：Zeyu Ren, Jie Ni, Rui Qian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：28 pages. Project page: https://aigeeksgroup.github.io/PINE

Understanding a scene requires both geometry and semantics: geometry recovers its spatial structure, and semantics separates it into objects. Current models obtain the two from separate networks or separate passes. We present PINE, a one-step Omni Diffusion Transformer that predicts the point map, the surface normals, and the instance map of an image in one forward pass. PINE treats each map as a token stream, and a stream mask decides which streams interact. A camera-aware ray encoding positions every token by its camera ray rather than its pixel. Grounding attention adds the affinity of pretrained DINOv3 features to the attention logits. Trained on only 139K synthetic images for geometry, PINE achieves state-of-the-art point maps whether or not intrinsics are given and the best average rank on surface normals. It also exceeds SAM on large objects with less than 1 percent of its training images, and it predicts all three maps 5.7x faster than MoGe-3 with SAM.

## 254. SpectralGS: Spectral Gaussian Splatting for Unsupervised Spectral Super-Resolution

- **作者**：Qiaoying Qu, Bin Pan, Chen Jia et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Unsupervised spectral super-resolution aims to recover spectral information from RGB images without paired hyperspectral data. However, the limited spectral information in RGB observations makes learning an effective spatial-spectral representation challenging, requiring the incorporation of structured spectral modeling with sensor imaging. In this paper, we propose SpectralGS, a spectral Gaussian splatting framework comprising two components: Shared Spectral Gaussian Representation and Spectral Response Projection Rendering. The representation module constructs a continuous spatial-spectral representation via shared spectral and local spatial Gaussian primitives, exploiting the low-rank spectral structure of hyperspectral images. The rendering module utilizes the spectral response function (SRF) to apply integral projection to the continuous representation, blending it to generate an RGB image. Consistency between the rendered and observed RGB images provides training supervision. Our main contribution is a Gaussian splatting framework that integrates structured continuous spatial-spectral representation with imaging response-based rendering, enabling unsupervised spectral reconstruction. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms current state-of-the-art methods.

## 255. Contact Harness: Enabling VLM Agents to Perform Contact-Rich Manipulation

- **作者**：Shilin Shan, Yuxuan Hu, Chuhao Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：45 pages, 21 figures

Agents built on pretrained VLMs have demonstrated zero-shot control of real robots, but vision alone is often insufficient for reliable contact-state assessment in contact-rich manipulation. Force and tactile feedback provide complementary information, but their semantic ambiguity makes them difficult for agents to use directly. These signals must be interpreted according to their physical meaning, together with task phase and visual evidence. We propose Contact Harness, which uses reusable, optimized skills to guide a VLM agent in assessing contact states from multimodal feedback and selecting subsequent actions. A compliant execution mechanism regulates in-contact motions using real-time force feedback. The framework requires no model fine-tuning, task demonstrations, or simulation training. We conduct zero-shot real-robot evaluations using GPT-6 Astra on Rokae and Franka robots. With a single prompt skill, Contact Harness achieves a full-insertion rate of 90.7% across 15 connector types, with further evaluations on a real PC under variations in lighting, background, and position. Across four additional contact-rich tasks, including whiteboard wiping, writing, peeling, and pivoting, Contact Harness achieves an average success rate of 100%. On six simulated tasks, Contact Harness reaches 96.7% success, compared with at most 68.3% for pretrained VLA baselines fine-tuned on task demonstrations, showing the potential of agent-based manipulation for contact-rich real-world deployment.

## 256. ClothCast: Generative Cloth World Modeling under Partial Observability

- **作者**：Yushen Hu, Yilong Song, Zhen Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

World models support robotic manipulation by predicting how the scene evolves under a planned action. For garments this prediction is notoriously difficult: deformations are complex, and self-occlusion leaves much of the cloth unobserved. Planned gripper trajectories provide a useful motion prior because cloth held by a gripper moves with it, while the remaining deformation must be predicted. We introduce ClothCast, a world model that applies this prior to the cloth's points and learns the residual motion. ClothCast takes one RGB-D observation, the camera pose, the robot's own model and the planned gripper trajectories, with no cloth mesh or material parameters. It lifts the observation to a layered point set that includes hypothesized occluded layers, carries the points with a blended rigid prior derived from the gripper poses, and predicts the residual motion with a video-diffusion point-flow model. Over entire 4- to 11-second episodes at 10 Hz, the moved points are rendered into depth, from which a geometry-conditioned completion model synthesizes RGB; after release, a hand-designed settling step replaces the learned motion. We further release ClothCastBench, 10,000 simulated dual-arm cloth-manipulation episodes with the ground-truth cloth mesh, including occluded layers, at every frame. On 492 held-out episodes, ClothCast achieves 2.5x lower sequence-averaged visible-surface Chamfer distance than Flex-π, the strongest baseline in geometry (14.0 mm vs. 35.6 mm), and 3.7 dB higher cloth-region PSNR than Cosmos-Predict2.5, the strongest baseline in cloth PSNR. On a 50-episode ablation subset, the rigid prior alone, applied to ClothCast's learned initial geometry with the same post-processing, already outperforms both geometry-capable baselines, and the learned residual lowers its error by about a third.

## 257. Scenario Compliant Indoor Pedestrian Trajectory Reconstruction from Partial Observation

- **作者**：Qi Dang, Luis Quintero, Shibo Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Pedestrian trajectory reconstruction is vital for applications ranging from security surveillance to indoor navigation. While existing probabilistic and generative frameworks have shown success in outdoor GPS-based trajectory imputation, they often fail in indoor settings where movement is strictly governed by dense geometric constraints such as walls and corridors. Standard models typically treat trajectories as unconstrained coordinate sequences, leading to physically infeasible paths with high collision rates. To address this, we propose a map-conditioned generative framework based on conditional diffusion models. Our approach explicitly integrates dense scenario maps as structured spatial inputs via a CNN-based encoder, allowing environmental geometry to guide the iterative denoising process. Furthermore, we employ an endpoint-aware mechanism by embedding observations (start and end points) directly into the scenario map, providing critical spatial anchors through image-level features. Experiments on simulated indoor trajectories and the real-world Shopper trajectory dataset, with three training seeds, show that our method achieves the lowest errors among the compared generative methods. Scene conditioning reduces the collision and invalid rates by 19\% and 46\% on the simulated dataset, whereas on real shopper data the gains are consistent but small, mainly because the model rarely learns to route around obstacles between the endpoints. Compared with a shortest-path planner, which never collides, our method better captures the variability of real motion (31\% lower CRPS), but the timing of stops is reconstructed only partially. These results show that explicit environmental context improves physical consistency, while reconstructing realistic human motion in real indoor spaces remains challenging. Our code is available at https://github.com/dang-qi/trajectory_reconstruction

## 258. Beyond Attention Maps: Understanding and Improving Medical MLLMs via Visual Values

- **作者**：Yichen Zhao, Fenghe Tang, Wenxin Ma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Current MLLM methods largely focus on whether models attend to the right visual regions, implicitly treating spatial attention map as the primary key of effective visual perception. To examine this assumption, we introduce a medical VQA dataset with paired non-contrast (NC) and contrast-enhanced (CE) images that share corresponding anatomy while providing different cues. We show that this view is incomplete: across paired NC and CE medical images, MLLMs often exhibit similar attention patterns despite producing contrasting answers, while controlled bidirectional interventions induce substantially larger prediction changes on visual values than attention weights. These results identify the visual content retrieved through values as a critical factor in improving perception beyond spatial attention alone. Guided by this finding, we propose VIVID (VIsual Value Intervention Distillation), which uses enhanced visual values to identify and refine teacher corrections aligned with useful visual evidence to improve on-policy distillation from CE to NC images. Across six internal and external test sets, VIVID consistently outperforms existing on-policy distillation methods across different model scales and architectures, requires only NC images at inference, and maintains strong zero-shot performance on general-domain VQA benchmarks. The project page is available at https://qiuzyc.github.io/VIVID.

## 259. Polytropos: What Do Vision-Language Model Descriptions Make of a Cultural Object?

- **作者**：Yuechen Jiang, Enze Zhang, Yuyan Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-language models have become a new kind of describer, writing about almost anything people see, and their descriptions now shape how cultures are known by people and by other models. Descriptions of cultures tend to turn particular things into types, and what a description loses shows what its describer lacks. We introduce Polytropos, a benchmark that measures what model descriptions of cultural objects keep of the particular object by turning each description back into an image and comparing it with the original photograph. On 374 objects from the Metropolitan Museum of Art and the Getty, with 1,870 descriptions from five models and 230 written by curators, reconstructions are recognised as the recorded culture more often than the photographs, at 54.55% to 59.09% against 52.14%, yet 16% to 21% of objects change outcome and only 0.49% of reconstructions are identified as the specific object. On the same objects, models lose recorded cultures at a rate similar to curators but gain more. Model descriptions give each object many faces, each typical of its culture and almost none the object itself.

## 260. A Multi-Criteria Evaluation of Color Normalization Methods in Histopathology: Nuclei Preservation, Artifact Sensitivity, Background Fidelity, and Downstream Performanc

- **作者**：Falah Jabar, Aria Torkpour, Pasquale Lombardi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Submitted to Medical Image Analysis

Deep learning models for nuclei detection and classification may generalize poorly across medical centers because of variations in staining and scanning. Although color normalization aims to reduce these variations, its effects on nuclear structure, background preservation, artifact sensitivity, and nuclei detection and classification performance have not been systematically evaluated. We present a procedure to benchmark 11 classical and deep learning normalization methods using 7 public and private nuclei datasets. Additionally, we investigate the effect of 7 input color spaces. The normalization methods differed in their effects on nuclear structure, background, and sensitivity to artifacts. However, detection and classification performance varied more across datasets than across normalization methods, and the input color space had limited influence. No single method performed best on all criteria, and image-level metrics did not reliably predict downstream performance, highlighting the need for nuclei- and structure-aware normalization methods. To support this work, we introduce WSI Quality Control and Normalization (WSI-QCN), a publicly available pipeline for robust whole-slide image quality assessment and color normalization; the source code is available on GitHub.

## 261. MIMORPHEUS: Rethinking Cross-Modal Supervision for Human Pose Estimation from Radar Signals

- **作者**：Shizhao Zhang, Yanyan Zhang, Deshui Yu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures

Deep learning has shown promise for estimating human pose with multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) radar through walls. Building paired radar and pose datasets demands considerable time and manual effort, while large visual pose datasets provide no corresponding radar echoes. Physical simulation can supply synthetic signals, but requires expertise in scattering models and can leave a gap between simulated and real data. To address this challenge, we introduce MIMORPHEUS, a framework that generates radar echoes from 3D human poses through conditional diffusion. To prepare echo and pose representations, MIMO-VAE compresses the echoes according to their channel and fast time sample structure, while SkeleCoder learns pose features through contrastive alignment with echo features. These pose features then guide diffusion in the MIMO-VAE latent space, and the VAE decoder reconstructs echoes from the generated latents. Once trained, the generator augments radar data by producing different echoes for a recorded pose and supplies the radar modality for pose annotations in existing visual datasets. In data augmentation experiments, our method reduces MPJPE by 6.8%-11.2% across four radar pose estimators. Transfer experiments with three public pose datasets also show that visual pose annotations can supply useful radar training data. Code will be made publicly available on GitHub.

## 262. More Than Picturing the Future: Motion-Oriented Latent Reasoning for Generalizable Robot Manipulation

- **作者**：Xin Zhang, Yushuo Hou, Anqi Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV

Latent reasoning lets Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models deliberate at a fraction of the cost of explicit chain-of-thought, increasingly through visual foresight, which we show to be indispensable for predicting actions. Yet this paradigm is inherited from Vision-Language Models, where it serves understanding rather than action: each latent is aligned with an isolated future frame, often enriched with further modalities of that frame. We find that this binds the policy to the appearance of its training scenes, so that it degrades sharply once the scene changes. We argue that visual latents for action should capture not what a future frame looks like, but how the scene evolves toward the goal. We therefore propose Motion-Oriented Latent Reasoning, which redirects visual latents from the whole frame to the interaction that the action must bring about. Semantic latents, following the stages of embodied chain-of-thought, first establish what to accomplish and how. Conditioned on them, motion-oriented latents anticipate each future step from coarse to fine: from the global scene, to the interaction region spanned by the manipulated object and its destination, to the robot state. Relative to the state-of-the-art latent VLA LaST$_0$, our method raises the mean success rate on SimplerEnv-WidowX from 37.5% to 55.2% in distribution and from 22.4% to 30.2% under out-of-distribution shifts, and on an AgileX Piper dual-arm platform from 60.8% to 90.8% in training scenes and from 6.7% to 37.5% under environmental changes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29339) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29339)

## 263. Probe Before You Learn: Allocating Prompts from Initial Gradients under Label Noise

- **作者**：Jiazhe Zhang, Zibin Geng, Qiang Ma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Prompt learning provides a parameter-efficient way to adapt pretrained vision--language models through text and visual prompts, but noisy labels prevalent in real-world data inevitably bias prompt updates and thereby undermine adaptation effectiveness. Prior work on robust prompt learning focuses on suppressing unreliable updates under noisy supervision. We uncover a pivotal yet overlooked aspect--prompt allocation: shifting the parameter budget between text and visual prompts can substantially enhance robustness while keeping the training objective and total budget fixed. However, suitable prompt allocations vary across tasks and are unknown a priori, rendering brute-force candidate training computationally prohibitive. We propose GPrompt, a training-free method that derives allocation between text and visual prompts using only loss gradients at initialization before prompt optimization. Using a small sample of images and generated pseudo-labels as shared supervision, GPrompt performs an initialization probe to extract paired text and visual loss gradients, and characterizes the geometry of their trace-normalized Gram matrices through both concentration within each modality and overlap between modalities. GPrompt then derives a closed-form allocation, and projects it onto feasible prompt counts under the exact parameter budget, enabling task-specific selection without training candidate configurations. Experiments across eleven datasets show that GPrompt outperforms the strongest baseline by up to 7.75% while selecting configurations close to the best allocation identified through exhaustive training. The average runtime of gradient probe across datasets is 99.9% lower than that of exhaustive candidate training.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29336) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29336)

## 264. SKIA: Spatial-Knowledge Informed Attention for Robust Remote Photoplethysmography under Lower-Face Motion Artifacts

- **作者**：Van-Truong Le, Viet-Tham Huynh, Minh-Triet Tran
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 4 figures, 7 tables

Remote photoplethysmography (rPPG) enables contactless physiological monitoring from facial videos, but facial motion, particularly mouth and jaw movements during cognitive and communicative tasks, can severely degrade signal reconstruction. We present SKIA, a lightweight, mask-guided framework that suppresses motion-prone lower-face regions while preserving physiological cues from the forehead and cheeks. Using the UBFC-rPPG benchmark, we first investigate static lower-face suppression in the classical CHROM pipeline. Static masking improves waveform Pearson correlation from 0.3507 to 0.4166 (p < 0.001) and reduces heart-rate mean absolute error (MAE) from 33.50 to 23.44 bpm. Building on this finding, we introduce SKIA-Net, a compact spatiotemporal architecture that combines raw facial frames, statically masked representations, and temporal motion differences to reconstruct blood volume pulse (BVP) waveforms. The full model achieves a Pearson correlation of 0.7522 and a heart-rate MAE of 3.73 bpm on UBFC-rPPG, outperforming single-stream variants. Ablation studies show that the static mask provides complementary information to raw appearance and that static anatomical priors outperform the tested dynamic optical-flow-based masking strategy. These results suggest that spatially informed, temporally stable masking can improve motion-robust rPPG estimation. Code: https://github.com/lee-vtruong/SKIA-rPPG

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29330) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29330)

## 265. FP-Prune: Rethinking Visual Token Pruning from an Information Gain Perspective

- **作者**：Haozhe Zhao, Junxiang Zhang, Junxin Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
