Visual token pruning can reduce the inference cost of vision language models, but its effectiveness depends on preserving complementary visual information under a limited token budget. A central challenge is to quantify the additional information a candidate token contributes beyond the retained set, a contribution that conventional scores based on attention or similarity struggle to capture directly. In this work, we revisit visual token pruning from the perspective of information gain and use mutual information to characterize the incremental value of candidate tokens. We further establish theoretically that frame potential serves as an effective surrogate for mutual information. Building on this observation, we propose FP-Prune, a training-free visual token pruning method that iteratively removes tokens with low information gain to preserve as much complementary visual information as possible. Experiments on three widely used VLMs and eight datasets demonstrate that FP-Prune outperforms baseline methods in both accuracy and inference efficiency. Further analysis shows that the surrogate objective closely aligns with model performance. Our work provides theoretical grounding and an effective method for visual token pruning.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29327) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29327)

## 266. Prompt-Consistent Cross-View Object Geo-Localization

- **作者**：Lingyun Xing, Wenning Hao, Kai Jiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 9 figures

Cross-view object geo-localization (CVOGL) aims to locate an object indicated by a click point in a ground or drone query image within a satellite reference image. Through a quantitative prompt-sensitivity evaluation, we find that existing CVOGL methods suffer from a prompt-disobeying overfitting problem: when the click point is moved or removed, their predictions barely respond, indicating that the models rely on spurious feature correlations rather than genuinely following the prompt semantics. We trace this behavior to the limited scale of the benchmark dataset, which contains only 4,343 prompt-object pairs per task, so that the models simply memorize the object associated with each scene instead of learning the true prompt-to-object correspondence. To address this, we propose two plug-and-play components. First, we construct the CVOGL-MO dataset by expanding the existing training and test sets with diverse multi-object annotations, mitigating the memorization that induces shortcut learning. Second, we propose the Cross-View Co-Scene Multi-Object Contrastive Loss (CVMCL), which enforces distinct feature representations for different click points within the same scene, compelling the model to follow prompt instructions. We further design a prompt-sensitivity evaluation protocol with prediction-shift metrics, providing the first quantitative measurement of prompt-following capability for CVOGL. Both components are architecture-agnostic and can be integrated into existing methods without any architectural modification. Experiments on three representative backbones show that our components substantially improve prompt-following accuracy on the new multi-object test sets and markedly increase prompt sensitivity. Our dataset and code will be publicly released at https://github.com/Sunny-Fresher/CVOGL-MO.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29312) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29312)

## 267. PrimiTok: Learning to Diversify and Allocate Primitives for Visual Tokenization

- **作者**：Yizhu Chen, Chen Ju, Donghao Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Unifying visual understanding and generation requires representations that preserve both semantic information and fine-grained image details. Continuous tokenizers trained primarily for semantic alignment may omit fine-grained reconstruction details, whereas discrete tokenizers face information loss from codebook quantization. Conventional multi-codebook designs assign every token the same number of codewords regardless of local content. We instead view codewords as reusable visual primitives whose allocation and composition adapt to each token. We introduce PrimiTok, a compositional visual tokenizer that represents each visual token through adaptive allocation and weighted composition of learned codebook primitives. PrimiTok integrates two complementary components: Diverse Quantitative Primitives (DQP), which encourages diversity among sub-codebook centroids to reduce redundancy, and a Dynamic Primitive Allocator (DPA), which selects primitives according to token complexity. Embedding quantization error supervises how many primitives are selected from each sub-codebook, and DPA aggregates them with input-dependent weights. Joint reconstruction and image--text contrastive learning encourage the resulting representations to retain visual fidelity and semantic alignment. Experiments demonstrate improved reconstruction and semantic alignment over SOTA baselines, with further evaluations on multimodal understanding and visual generation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29311) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29311)

## 268. Counting Symbols on Technical Drawings: A Benchmark and Verified Rewards for MLLMs

- **作者**：Junxian Li, Ruixuan Yang, Zhenhua Xu
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：15 pages, 8 figures

Today's MLLMs can count objects in natural images. However, when asking them how many bolt holes appear in a mechanical drawing or how many nitrogen atoms are in a chemical structure, they often struggle. Counting in technical drawings remains challenging, as objects can be small, densely packed, or even implied by drawing conventions. To study this problem, we introduce CountDrawBench, a benchmark including floor plans, electrical schematics, mechanical drawings, and molecular structures, with 1,574 counting questions and instance-level annotations where available. Besides evaluation, we introduce Code-Verified RL, a reinforcement learning framework that teaches MLLMs to count with executable feedback. Our method combines three complementary rewards: a count reward that checks the final answer, an instance-matching reward that verifies object locations, and a zoom-based reward that checks counting consistency across local image regions. All three are integrated into a unified GRPO objective. Extensive experiments reveal that even leading MLLMs struggle on CountDrawBench, with the best evaluated model achieving only 59.3% accuracy. Our method improves Qwen3.5-9B from 49.3% to 54.3% accuracy, outperforming all evaluated open-source baselines. These results establish CountDrawBench as a challenging testbed for technical drawing understanding and demonstrate the value of code-verified rewards in these tasks. Code and data will be released upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29309) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29309)

## 269. Where Does Personalization Reside in Photo Editing? Evidence from One Photographer's Lightroom History

- **作者**：Dongwu Qian
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR
- **备注**：10 pages, 4 figures, 10 tables. Code and aggregate results are provided as ancillary files

Personalized photo-editing systems must infer both a photographer's recurring preferences and the intent of a particular shoot, yet these signals are rarely evaluated separately. We study interpretable Lightroom adjustments in a longitudinal, private dataset of 3,193 edited photographs from 41 catalogs by one professional photographer, with holdouts formed at the batch level. We compare absolute and prior-relative targets, image-conditioned predictors, and estimators that receive a small set of edits from the target batch. Residual prediction improves validation error, but its advantage on held-out batches is small and not significant under a batch-clustered bootstrap (0.592 vs 0.582 EV exposure MAE). In a batch-shared protocol, closed-form shrinkage using 4, 8, or 16 same-batch anchors reaches 0.466, 0.457, or 0.453 EV with the best estimator at each k, compared with 0.586-0.592 EV for the prior-only baseline; the paired reductions are significant under a batch-clustered bootstrap. Learned anchor-conditioned heads do not improve on this closed-form estimator, and a negative-trained prior already yields a low cold-image false-edit rate, leaving little benefit for an additional gate. The recommended estimator is closed-form and auditable, and it requires only a handful of same-batch edits. A render-space check against 383 native Lightroom exports reproduces the ranking: at k=8, online EB lowers Delta E00 against the prior by 0.41 (p=0.042), and the photographer's true parameters lower it by 0.78 (p=0.034). An appendix extends the action space from six to thirteen global controls: a same-batch median look lowers rendered chroma toward the export, while a per-photograph residual head again fails to transfer. The study is limited to one photographer and does not claim that slider accuracy measures aesthetic preference.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29307) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29307)

## 270. CT-Navigator: Towards Interpretable and Generalizable CT Understanding through Agentic Perception and Tool-Invocation

- **作者**：Yibing Fu, Huidong Xie, Yihao Niu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Clinical computed tomography (CT) interpretation inherently requires progressive visual exploration, where radiologists navigate from whole scans to individual slices and regions of interest and interact with external tools when necessary. However, existing 3D medical multimodal large language models (MLLMs) typically connect a specialized 3D visual encoder to a language model, without fully leveraging the native 2D visual and agentic capabilities of generalist MLLMs. Trained on large-scale one-hop CT instruction-following data, these models also have limited ability to perform iterative reasoning and explicitly ground their conclusions in visual evidence, limiting their support for traceable CT interpretation. To solve these problems, we propose \textbf{agentic perception} as an alternative paradigm inspired by radiologists' interpretation workflows. We introduce \textbf{CT-Navigator}, a framework that harnesses generalist MLLMs' 2D visual and agentic capabilities to progressively ground CT interpretation in explicit, traceable visual evidence through hierarchical visual exploration and radiology tool invocation. To build these capabilities, we curate 8,904 visual chain-of-thought (V-CoT) trajectories for cold-start agentic supervised fine-tuning. We then optimize the policy through reinforcement learning on 33,219 questions, with data selection and dynamic rollout sampling prioritizing informative learning signals. Finally, we introduce CTNavi-Bench, a 3D CT visual question answering benchmark comprising 7,000 questions spanning anatomical regions throughout the human body. CT-Navigator achieves 79.50\% accuracy on CTNavi-Bench, surpassing the strongest baseline by 17.59\%, and demonstrates strong generalization across three external CT benchmarks. The code, model, and benchmark will be released on \href{https://github.com/fyb99/ct_navigator}{GitHub}.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29305) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29305)

## 271. RSLaA: Self-Reconfiguring Roadside LiDARs as Agents for 3D Traffic Perception

- **作者**：Yang Ma, Yichen Sun, Yubing Zheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages (including 8 supplementary files), 8 figures

Cooperative perception with roadside LiDARs (RSLs) extends the sensing range of autonomous vehicles and resolves onboard-sensor occlusions, particularly at curves and intersections. Because infrastructure deployment is costly, existing work optimizes RSL configurations once, against road geometry and aggregated traffic statistics, and treats them as fixed thereafter. Traffic flow, however, is inherently dynamic, and a placement optimal on average can be far from optimal at any given moment. We introduce RSLaA (Roadside LiDARs as Agents), a framework in which each RSL adaptively adjusts its pose to the evolving traffic scene over decision-time windows. Since ground truth is unavailable at decision time, the framework centers on surrogate metrics computed solely from a probabilistic occupancy grid of historical observations, which predicts the next-window perception performance of candidate poses. We construct a co-simulation benchmark pairing five candidate surrogates with detector-agnostic ground-truth perception labels at every decision window, identifying the multi-view coverage metric J_mv as the most reliable predictor. Upon this validated surrogate we build two adaptive configuration methods, a receding-horizon (MPC) optimizer and a lightweight MAPPO-based multi-agent reinforcement learning scheme with centralized training and decentralized execution. Across 10 sites and 89 six-hour traffic scenarios (890 evaluation runs), adaptive configuration consistently improves the composite perception score over static deployment. Our results indicate that RSLs could be benefitted from being treated as agents.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29294) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29294)

## 272. GALILEO: Geometric RoPE with Galilean Key Transport for Dynamic Scenes

- **作者**：Youngmin Kim, Seonghyun Jin, Jong Chul Ye
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Geometry-aware rotary positional encodings, such as RayRoPE, help attention reason about the 3D spatial relationships between tokens and the query camera. However, they implicitly assume a static scene, encoding each key at its observed position regardless of when it was captured. For dynamic scenes with temporally separated input views, this mismatch places the same moving object at a different position in each input frame, so it can be rendered at an inaccurate position. While temporal encodings inform attention when a token was observed, they do not resolve where its content should be at the query time. We introduce a simple and general approach that aligns positional encodings with dynamic scene geometry through Galilean transport. Before positional encoding, each key's 3D position is transported to the query time using its estimated velocity and the temporal offset. By modifying only key positions, our method integrates directly with existing encodings, including RayRoPE and URoPE, without altering their formulations. Velocities can be obtained from arbitrary sources. By default, we estimate them from token correspondences within the network. Integrated into LVSM, our method improves dynamic view synthesis on Syn4D, dynamic Objaverse, and real-world Stereo4D videos over baselines. Ablations show that encoding velocities as token features yields smaller gains, while attention visualizations reveal that our method attends to query-time locations of moving content, unlike RayRoPE. Beyond view synthesis, our method also reduces tracking errors on dynamic objects in a pretrained 3D point tracker and improves moving-vehicle generation in a video generator.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29293) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29293)

## 273. Robust Concept Erasure in Text-to-Image Models via Simulating Brain Functional Localization

- **作者**：Manyi Li, Yufan Liu, Bing Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Concept erasure in text-to-image models aims to prevent a target concept from appearing in generated images while preserving general image generation capability. However, erased concepts can still be reactivated by attacks. Prior attacks mainly search text or image input spaces, leaving the initial latent space underexplored. To address this gap, we introduce IVO (Initial latent Variable Optimization), a new concept reactivation attack that changes the starting point of image generation by optimizing the initial latent. Comprehensive experiments across 8 erasure methods show that IVO reactivates erased concepts more effectively than the attack baselines, revealing a blind spot in erasure robustness evaluation. This motivates us to localize model components associated with the target concept and then treat them as intervention sites for more robust concept erasure. We propose CeFlune (Concept Erasure via Functional Localization of Unit Neurons), which adapts brain functional localization to text-to-image models. CeFlune defines linear modules as Unit Neurons and localizes concept-responsive ones by comparing their response trajectories in the model's task and resting states. It then updates the localized Unit Neurons to constrain their responses and the erased model's predictions. Across U-Net diffusion and flow-matching transformer architectures, CeFlune achieves a favorable balance between concept erasure and general image generation capability in comparisons with up to 15 baseline methods. Under IVO and 4 other evaluated attacks, CeFlune also shows lower concept reactivation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29288) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29288)

## 274. RadioWorld: Transferring World Models to Radio-Frequency Radiance Fields

- **作者**：Hu Zhou, Jiazhou Zhou, Shuowen Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.NI, eess.SP

Electromagnetic (EM) wave propagation in the physical world constitutes a Radio-Frequency (RF) radiance field, providing rich information for 6G communication and sensing. However, existing neural RF field methods suffer from three fundamental limitations: (1) they predominantly rely on per-scene optimization and cannot generalize to unseen scenes; (2) they require costly reference RF measurements in each target scene; and (3) they degrade severely when extrapolating beyond the vicinity of reference measurements. To overcome these limitations, we present RadioWorld, a general framework that adapts a world model to synthesize RF radiance fields directly from physical scene geometry and transceiver locations, without requiring any reference RF measurements at inference. Specifically, RadioWorld couples two specialized modules within a dual-stream Mixture-of-Transformers (MoT) architecture: a Scene-Understanding Transformer that encodes 3D scene geometry and transceiver locations to extract environment-aware global EM propagation features, and a Field-Generation Transformer that attends to these features to synthesize the RF radiance field with the local query conditions. We also establish UrbanRF-Bench, a large-scale ray-traced benchmark spanning 657 urban scenes and 32,850 RF radiance fields across seven global cities. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RadioWorld significantly outperforms specialized baselines and accurately synthesizes RF fields at arbitrary locations in unseen cities. Code, datasets, and checkpoints will be released upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29282) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29282)

## 275. One-shot is Enough: Boosting SAM for Medical Image Segmentation through Structured Information Modeling

- **作者**：Guilin Guan
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In recent years, Foundation models such as the Segment Anything Model (SAM) have demonstrated strong generalization capabilities, but because they are primarily pre-trained on natural images, they perform poorly when faced with medical images. With sufficient labeled medical data, this challenge is somewhat alleviated. However, the difficulty and high cost of acquiring large amounts of labeled data make one-shot learning a highly attractive research paradigm. But within this paradigm, SAM struggles to fully extract its potential multi-granular information, leading to a sharp decline in performance. To address this, we propose OB-SAM, a one-shot enhancement framework for medical image segmentation. It aims to maximize the utilization of information from a single sample from the perspective of structured modeling. Specifically, we first designed a Multi-Frequency Decoupling (MFD) module, which divides features into different frequency domain components, allowing each branch to focus on information expression at different semantic levels, thereby alleviating overfitting and enhancing feature representation capabilities. Secondly, we introduce a Progressive Feature Integrator (PFI) module to promote complementary fusion among multi-level features, thereby avoiding the dominant effect of a certain level of feature in the fusion process. Furthermore, we propose an Selective Context Filtering (SCF) Module that adaptively selects key contextual information by modeling the contrast relationship between the foreground and background, thereby achieving precise localization of the lesion area. Extensive experiments across seven diverse datasets validate that OB-SAM achieves state-of-the-art performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29278) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29278)

## 276. $ζ_0$: A Framework for World Action Models with Joint Scene and Body Foresight in Humanoid Control

- **作者**：Yizhi Chen, Zhanxiang Cao, Yitong Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：18 pages (including appendix), 10 figures, 12 tables. Project page: https://chenyizhi123.github.io/zeta0-project/ ; Code & Data: https://github.com/chenyizhi123/Zeta0

World Action Models anticipate visual outcomes to guide robot actions, yet humanoid interaction also depends on how the robot's body responds. Visual foresight alone can miss body motion, while latent-distance losses do not directly supervise action responses. We present $ζ_0$, a framework that turns vision-language-action policies into World Action Models by foreseeing future visual features and measured joint trajectories in a shared latent representation. During training, a teacher conditioned on recorded futures provides a reference action response, and dual-response transfer trains the causal predictor to match it through the teacher and the executing policy. All components are trained jointly, and deployment uses only causally predicted futures. On seven HumanoidArena tasks, $ζ_0$ built on $Ψ_0$ and GR00T N1.7 reaches 63.6% and 79.5% mean success, 18.8 and 9.0 points above these bases. On six real-world tasks, it reaches 78.3% and 85.0%, gains of 16.7 and 10.0 points. On GR00T, its 79.5% tops all compared seven-task joint policies. Project page: https://chenyizhi123.github.io/zeta0-project/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29277) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29277)

## 277. ContinuLoc: Continuous Pose Inference over Neural Fields for UAV Geo-Localization

- **作者**：Weikun Zhou, Jinliang Lin, Yu Zang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026

Cross-view UAV geo-localization involves three design choices: how to represent the UAV observation, how to represent the geo-referenced map, and how to infer pose from their interaction. The dominant paradigm organizes the map as a discretized 2DoF candidate library, coupling map representation to pose inference and thus bounding spatial localization precision by candidate sampling resolution. We decouple map representation from pose inference by encoding the geo-referenced map as a continuously queryable semantic neural field, enabling localization to be formulated as explicit optimization over a continuous 4DoF pose space parameterized by 2D ground position, in-plane heading, and viewing scale. Pose hypotheses are evaluated on demand using global descriptors in a shared semantic feature space, bypassing the sampling bottleneck of retrieval. We learn 4DoF pose-sensitive visual representations under full 4DoF supervision provided by ContinuScenes, a multi-city benchmark with dense near-nadir UAV imagery and complete pose annotations. Inference proceeds hierarchically, progressing from broad pose-space coverage to adaptive concentration on high-compatibility regions. Experiments demonstrate substantial gains over representative localization paradigms under progressively stricter 4DoF accuracy criteria. Code is available at https://github.com/ZhouWeikun/ContinuLoc/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29276) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29276)

## 278. DriveOddsBench: Are Driving Video World Models Aligned with Real-World Odds?

- **作者**：Jiaxi Liu, Keshu Wu, Bin Ran et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：22 pages, 16 figures, 8 tables. Project page: https://hhjj233.github.io/DriveOddsBench-page/ Code: https://github.com/hhjj233/DriveOddsBench

Video world models predict how a scene will evolve, and their sampled futures are increasingly used in place of the real one, from planning to simulation. These uses assume that the samples are aligned with real-world odds, with each future appearing among them as often as it would follow the same history in reality, yet current evaluations only score how real each sample looks. Testing this assumption on real driving data is hard for three reasons. Each recorded history has only one real future, so the true probabilities are unknown. Physical quantities must be read from video, where reading errors can look like model errors. Finally, the many possible analyses invite selective reporting. We introduce DriveOddsBench, a pre-registered benchmark that addresses all three. First, if the samples follow the real distribution, the rank of the real outcome among them is uniform in every history, so pooled ranks test them without probability labels. Second, one validated instrument reads the same quantities, such as the lead-vehicle gap and the ego motion, from the generated futures and from the real future, which first passes through the world model's autoencoder so that both are read alike. Third, all confirmatory analyses were frozen before any test clip was measured. We test eleven general and driving world models on 200 held-out Argoverse 2 histories in five scenario families, with 35,200 generated futures. None is aligned, and every model underestimates its uncertainty. The real outcome falls outside the range of all 16 samples in 28-61% of cases instead of the 11.8% expected under the real distribution. Five models are significantly skewed, in opposite directions, and the largest errors are in the ego motion. Nine models beat climatology without being aligned, and among these models neither type nor scale predicts which comes closest.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29275) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29275)

## 279. Dually Constrained Parameter Projection for Continual Learning

- **作者**：Bowyn Tan, Zhilong Zheng, Yang Guan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables

Continual learning requires a model to learn multiple new tasks sequentially while retaining performance on previous tasks. Projecting model parameters onto the Jacobian null space of previous tasks after unconstrained training on a new task has emerged as an effective paradigm. However, unidirectional projection merely minimizes the parameter distance to the new-task optimum, overlooking the new-task loss landscape. To address this limitation, we propose DUET, a bidirectional projection algorithm that imposes Jacobian null-space constraints from both previous and new tasks during post-training projection. Specifically, DUET formulates the post-training adjustment as a dually constrained optimization problem. It minimizes the distance between parameters before and after training on a new task, subject to loss preserving constraints on both tasks. To solve the optimization problem, we develop an alternating bidirectional projection algorithm, whose convergence is guaranteed by the contraction mapping theorem. For reliable multi-step projection, we rectify existing step size selection criteria via an effective-rank aware formulation, overcoming their failure under rank-deficient Jacobians. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DUET consistently outperforms existing unidirectional projection methods and achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29269) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29269)

## 280. CFANet: Learning Conflict-Free Weather Adapters for All-Weather Image Restoration

- **作者**：Minglong Xue, Wenchao Yan, Guoan Guan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

All-weather image restoration aims to recover clean images from diverse weather degradations such as rain, haze, and snow. Most existing all-in-one methods handle different weather types with a unified network or rely on single-label weather modeling. However, real-world adverse weather is often compositional, where multiple known weather primitives may coexist in a single image. To this end, we propose Conflict-Free Adapter Network (CFANet) for open-compositional all-weather image restoration. CFANet first employs a Weather Mixture Router (WMR) to predict non-exclusive composition weights for rain, haze, and snow from shallow features. Guided by these weights, a Shared-Private Weather Adapter (SPWA) dynamically composes a common adapter with weather-private adapters inside each Conflict-Free Adapter Block, preserving shared restoration capability while providing weather-specific residual adaptation. Moreover, a lightweight Natural Color Adapter (NCA) performs constrained color calibration to improve color fidelity and visual naturalness. Extensive experiments demonstrate that the proposed method achieves competitive adverse weather image restoration performance and benefits downstream visual tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/wenchao-tech/CFANet-main

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29257) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29257)

## 281. MM-DataLoop: Closing the Data-Side Loop for Recursive Self-Improvement in MLLM Post-Training

- **作者**：Likang Peng, Chen Ju, Xu Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Recursive self-improvement (RSI) offers a path toward improving models through repeated evaluation and revision. We study its data side in multimodal language model (MLLM) post-training, where heterogeneous operations and cross-round evidence must be coordinated. MMDataLoop organizes data interventions into five operation families and connects them through an Observe-Diagnose-Plan-Execute-Verify-Reflect cycle with shared rule memory. Across 34 candidate rounds on Qwen3.5-0.8B and five benchmarks, we hold the base initialization, shared training schedule and evaluation protocol fixed while varying data recipes. The five-benchmark mean reaches 63.04 at R33, up 5.32 points from the zero-shot baseline; R29, R32, and R33 form a close top cluster within 0.09 points. In eightcandidate comparisons, guided search reaches the preselected target in six candidates and identifies the top observed candidate, while random arms show higher means and lower dispersion. The selected R29 recipe also scores above two full-pool baselines under the same epoch schedule. Together, these findings establish MM-DataLoop as a traceable workflow and provide an empirical basis for studying how data-side decisions shape outcomes across MLLM post-training recipes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29254) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29254)

## 282. Complementary Geometry in Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Mingle Jiang, Jikui Zhou, Chenghan Lyu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 Pages, supplementary material included

Gaussian Splatting has achieved high visual quality in novel view synthesis, but the accuracy of its rendered geometry remains a challenge. In particular, the rendered depth is computed as an opacity-weighted average along each ray, which may not correspond to the actual scene surface. In this paper, we observe that depth predictions from different views of the same frozen Gaussian representation exhibit complementary errors, allowing us to improve novel-view geometry using information from other views without modifying the original representation. Based on this observation, we propose REVEAL, a general framework that selectively refines novel-view depth using complementary geometric information. Specifically, we first reproject depth predictions from source views to the target view and combine them using robust multi-view consensus. We then introduce a learned selection mechanism to determine whether the candidate depth is more accurate than the original prediction, based on the expected reduction in depth error. In addition, we introduce RIG, a real-world benchmark captured with synchronized RGB-D cameras, which provides ground-truth depth for evaluating both static and dynamic reconstruction models. Experiments on 6 Gaussian Splatting backbones across 3 benchmarks show that REVEAL consistently improves depth accuracy while maintaining comparable rendering quality.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29236) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29236)

## 283. Why Adaptive Optimizers Struggle with Large Convolution Kernels

- **作者**：Tianyu Ding, Jiachen Jiang, Jinxin Zhou et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Large convolution kernels enlarge the receptive fields of ConvNets at a modest computational cost, but they are notoriously difficult to train. Existing large-kernel networks therefore rely on costly training techniques, such as parallel small-kernel branches with batch normalization. In this paper, we trace this difficulty to adaptive optimizers such as Adam, which normalize the update of every weight separately. Since input features rarely average to zero, all weights of a kernel share a common gradient, and these optimizers move the mean of a $k\times k$ kernel $k$ times as far as a single parameter. We find this mean amplification in widely used ConvNets and vision transformers, and show that it can force the maximal learning rate to fall as $1/k^2$. We further prove that the basis of the discrete cosine transform (DCT) isolates the mean and is the only orthonormal basis that diagonalizes every stationary gradient statistic with mirrored boundaries. We therefore train large kernels through their DCT coefficients and convert them back after training. The resulting method, DCT-Rep, requires only a few lines of code and adds no parameters, no inference cost and negligible training overhead. Extensive experiments on image classification, object detection, semantic segmentation and image restoration show that DCT-Rep consistently improves large-kernel ConvNets and vision transformers, by $+0.79\%$ top-1 accuracy for a large-kernel ConvNeXt-T and $+0.43\%$ for ViT-S/16 on ImageNet-1K, $+1.4$ box AP on COCO, $+1.2$ mIoU on ADE20K and up to $+0.4$ dB on image deblurring, where the gain grows with the kernel size.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29232) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29232)

## 284. Ego-HMB: Human Motion Bridging from Egocentric Images via A Motion Bridge Diffusion Model

- **作者**：Gaoge Han, Yongkang Cheng, Shaoli Huang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026

We introduce Egocentric Human Motion Bridging, a task that generates a coherent 3D human motion sequence from two endpoint egocentric images. Unlike conventional motion in-betweening, which requires structured 3D endpoint poses, this setting uses raw first-person observations available from wearable devices in VR/AR and robotics. It presents challenges due to viewpoint variations, partial body visibility, and temporal ambiguity under cross-modal endpoint constraints. To address these challenges, we propose Ego-HMB, a unified diffusion-based framework that learns endpoint pose estimation and temporally consistent motion infilling within a single model through two training stages. First, Egocentric-to-Canonical Pretraining uses a hierarchical curriculum with progressively increasing temporal spans to strengthen endpoint pose priors. Second, Motion Bridge Diffusion Training incorporates endpoint and physics awareness, diffusing from neighboring poses to promote temporal continuity while preserving global endpoint constraints. Extensive experiments compare our approach with one-stage methods that directly generate motion from two egocentric images and two-stage pipelines that combine endpoint pose estimation with motion interpolation. Ego-HMB consistently outperforms these baselines in endpoint accuracy and motion generation quality. Code is available at https://github.com/gaogehan/Ego-HMB.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29218) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29218)

## 285. PartPO: Part-Level Policy Optimization for Shape-to-Program Generation

- **作者**：Hannuo Zhang, Zhixiang Chi, Xuanchen Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

We present PartPO, a reinforcement learning method for generating structured shape programs from 3D point clouds. Existing shape-to-program models are trained with supervised learning on procedurally generated data and generalize poorly to more diverse shapes, while reinforcement learning from execution feedback assigns a single scalar reward to a long program that builds many parts, making credit assignment difficult. PartPO instead builds on a core property of the task: shape programs are self-describing, with each semantically named part produced by its own code block. It segments each generated program by its parts and applies part-level rewards only to the code of the corresponding part, a category reward only to the object name, and a shape-level reward to the whole program. The required part supervision is obtained without manual annotation, by grounding part names on target meshes with an open-vocabulary segmentation model prompt-tuned on the model's own outputs. Applied to MeshCoder, PartPO improves reconstruction accuracy and execution success on ShapeNetCore and ABO over the base model, rejection-sampling fine-tuning and trajectory-level RL, and generalizes to the unseen HSSD dataset. Code will be released at https://github.com/HannuoZ/PartPO.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29213) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29213)

## 286. DriveTTO: Understanding Search and Selection in Test-Time Trajectory Optimization for End-to-End Driving

- **作者**：Xinyao Rao, Zecheng Tang, Zheyuan Lai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, including supplementary material. Project page: https://rtwotwo.github.io/DriveTTO/ Code: https://github.com/Rtwotwo/DriveTTO

In difficult driving scenes, a learned scorer can select a poor trajectory from a fixed pool even when a better candidate is available. Nearby vehicles and road boundaries can also leave only a narrow range of high-scoring maneuvers. These constraints vary with the scene and are difficult to cover exhaustively during training. We introduce DriveTTO, a training-free method that searches around native proposals and checks each proposed refinement against a retained trajectory. A frozen scorer evaluates completed paths at the endpoints they actually reach, guiding resampling in progressively smaller neighborhoods. From a shared reference, one branch draws on planner proposals and another perturbs vehicle controls. DriveTTO blends their poses at matching times and accepts a branch or fusion update only when both frozen scorers predict a gain. On Navtest, DriveTTO recovers nonzero predictive driver model scores (PDMS) in 51.35% of native zero-score cases on average. Accounting for new failures, the zero-score rate falls from 1.63% to 1.13%. Benchmark-specific configurations achieve 95.54 PDMS and 92.30 extended PDMS (EPDMS) on Navtest, 57.10 EPDMS on Navhard, and 40.1 HD-Score on HUGSIM under observed commands. With a single frozen scorer, DriveTTO-S improves all six evaluated planners on both Navtest and Navhard.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29205) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29205)

## 287. Unleashing the Value of Raw Point Clouds for 3D Understanding with LLMs

- **作者**：Tailai Chen, Jiawei Yu, Yiming Cheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 4 figures. Tailai Chen and Jiawei Yu contributed equally

Standard 3D point cloud language models typically rely on pretrained point encoders to extract 3D features and project them into representations processable by large language models (LLMs). However, these encoders are often originally optimized for pre-defined tasks, resulting in a representation bottleneck that may lead to a substantial mismatch with downstream 3D understanding. Moreover, existing models largely follow a single pass closed-book generation, limiting their ability to actively inspect task-relevant 3D regions or retrieve necessary external knowledge. In this work, we present PointTool, an encoder-free and tool-augmented framework for 3D reasoning. PointTool introduces Alignment-Free Patch Tokenization (AFPT) to locally aggregate raw point clouds while preserving fine-grained geometric structure, and Decoupled Point-Text LoRA (DPL) to facilitate global aggregation and 3D knowledge internalization within a frozen LLM. Building upon these designs, Program-Supervised Tool Reasoning (PSTR) enables the model to explicitly operate on raw 3D data and external resources through executable tools, supporting interactive 3D reasoning grounded in spatial evidence. We further introduce PointCloud-TR, a benchmark comprising 13K 3D objects, 39.8K questions, and 35.6K tool trajectories to facilitate training and evaluation of complex 3D reasoning. Experiments demonstrate that PointTool achieves strong performance on both standard and complex 3D understanding tasks, validating the effectiveness of the proposed framework.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29194) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29194)

## 288. PhyCoVE: Benchmarking Physical Counterfactual Video Editing across Diverse Material Dynamics

- **作者**：Conghan Yue, Yuanjie Chen, Yunyan Xiao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Physical counterfactual video editing turns a source video and a language-specified physical edit at a given execution frame into a counterfactual video that shows how the scene would evolve after the edit. Existing evaluations with counterfactual target videos mainly cover object removal or specific rigid-body motions, although real scenes also involve elastic deformation, fluid flow, and interactions among rigid bodies, elastic solids, and fluids. We introduce PhyCoVE, a benchmark of 533 cases on 110 source videos that covers rigid, elastic, and fluid dynamics in five interaction patterns, with edits that remove an object, change a physical quantity, or insert a new object. For the simulated cases, a language model proposes scene designs, coding agents build them with solvers suited to each material and physically based rendering and then simulate each edit from the source scene state, and humans check every stage. For the real cases, we record continuously from one camera position before and after each edit. We also propose counterfactual fidelity, a metric that applies across materials and their interactions and measures how much of the physical change from the source video to the target video a generated video reproduces. We evaluate seven open-source and commercial video editing models and find that counterfactual fidelity ranks them nearly as human preference does, but even the best model reproduces only about one seventh of the physical change. The models change how a scene looks but rarely how it evolves, whereas a physical edit requires understanding the dynamics that it changes. Physical counterfactual video editing thus remains far from solved, and PhyCoVE provides a foundation for developing and evaluating methods for it.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29184) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29184)

## 289. loopxDINO: Dense Visual Prediction via Iterative Latent Reasoning

- **作者**：Xiangjing An, Zhibin Jiao, Aiting Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Standard dense visual prediction architectures scale representation capacity by stacking deep feedforward decoders (6-16 transformer layers with 50M-200M parameters). This explicit layer stacking brings substantial activation memory overhead during backpropagation, requires high off-chip memory bandwidth for non-tied weights, and uses static computational allocation that cannot adapt to spatial image complexity. We propose loopxDINO, which replaces physical decoder depth with iterative latent reasoning. It has two decoupled components: (1) a frozen DINOv2-Base backbone extracting spatial features in one forward pass and caching static Key-Value representations in GPU memory; (2) a compact weight-tied Looped Cross-Attention Decoder (7.96M core, 14.85M total trainable parameters) that recurrently updates queries across T iterations. Recurrence is stabilized by step-conditioned adaLN, LayerNorm-based hyper-ellipsoid manifold regularization, and learnable residual contraction damping. Intermediate mask predictions are fed back as spatial attention biases to guide queries toward ambiguous contours with no extra parameters. On Pascal VOC 2012 semantic segmentation with a single RTX 5060 Ti 16GB GPU, loopxDINO (T=2) achieves 85.66% mIoU and 65.01% Boundary IoU. It outperforms 6-layer DINO-Trans6L (54.06M params) by +1.09% mIoU and +7.41% Boundary IoU with 72.5% fewer parameters. Compared to 4-layer DINO-FCN4L probe (2.43M params, 83.82% mIoU), loopxDINO improves boundary delineation by +6.43% bIoU and occlusion resilience by +6.76% mIoU (60% patch masking). With Adaptive Computation Time (ACT), it reaches 87.3 FPS and retains 29.64% mIoU under 60% occlusion (+5.83% over DINO-Trans6L). Source code, pre-trained weights and evaluation suites are open-sourced under Apache 2.0 at https://github.com/loopxDINO/loopxDINO

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29180) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29180)

## 290. SpatialDuet: Bayesian task routing between a metric expert and a geometry expert for spatial intelligence

- **作者**：Duy Tran Thanh
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：11 pages, 3 figures, 8 tables

Embodied assistants and wearable devices need 3D spatial reasoning, and their limited hardware restricts them to small vision-language models (VLMs). Small spatial VLMs acquire different abilities from their training: a model trained on large spatial question sets estimates object and room sizes well, whereas a model with a 3D geometry encoder is better at relative directions and absolute distances. Instead of training a single model for all spatial tasks, as published methods do, we build one system that uses each frozen model for the tasks it handles best. We propose SpatialDuet, which pairs a metric expert with a geometry expert under a Bayesian task router. The metric expert is a data-driven spatial VLM run with three training-free modules: training-aligned sampling, metric-aware minimum Bayes risk decoding and per-type metric calibration. The geometry expert is a VLM with a 3D geometry encoder. For each question type, the router estimates from labelled development questions how likely each expert is to be better, and pools their answers with that probability as weight, in log space for numeric answers and over the option distributions for multiple-choice answers. Extensive experiments on four spatial benchmarks covering video and single images validate SpatialDuet; it outperforms the best published small VLMs by 3.17% on VSI-Bench and, excluding a model trained on CV-Bench-style data, by 1.44% on CV-Bench, without training any model weight.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29168) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29168)

## 291. Decoupling Perception from Generation for Agentic 3D CT Report Generation

- **作者**：Chenyu Wang, Mingyu Chen, Wenchao Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Chest CT reports repeatedly assess the same anatomical regions for findings and their attributes, such as whether a lung nodule is present and where it is located. These assessments suggest learning to answer visual questions directly, rather than coupling perception to open-ended text generation. Yet current CT report generation models largely follow generic vision-language modeling, which may miss subtle abnormalities when training data are limited. We therefore formulate report generation as a set of visual question-answering decisions coordinated by a large language model (LLM) agent. This decouples visual perception from reasoning and report writing: perception can be learned as a discriminative, non-generative task, while the LLM focuses on reasoning over patient context and posing relevant visual queries. We introduce Rad-VL-JEPA, which unifies report retrieval, finding recognition, and fine-grained attribute characterization through vision-language joint-embedding prediction. Building on this perception module, an agent trained with reinforcement learning (RL) acquires patient-specific visual evidence, while a separate editor incorporates the evidence into the final report, further separating evidence acquisition from report realization. Experiments show that Rad-VL-JEPA achieves strong performance on fine-grained CT understanding tasks with lower amortized per-query inference time than a generative VLM baseline trained with the same CT encoder and VQA data. The resulting reporting system outperforms strong VLM-based and agentic baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29167) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29167)

## 292. Training-Free 3D Editing via Feature-Divergence Localization and Trajectory Correction

- **作者**：Tri Ton, Siwoo Lim, Joshua Tian Jin Tee et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026

Modifying 3D assets requires enacting targeted structural transformations while strictly preserving the geometric identity of unedited regions. While recent probability flow models enable training-free editing, bounding these modifications within a high-dimensional latent space presents severe mathematical challenges. Current inversion-free trajectories rely on global velocity updates, which often cause spatial bleed into preserved structures and induce trajectory under-commitment, causing edits to stall mid-path. To resolve these dual failures, we introduce FocusFlow, a segmentation-free framework that structures the integration trajectory into two disjoint phases. During early coarse structure formation, FocusFlow applies Feature-Divergence Localization (FDL) to spatially gate the velocity field using intrinsic cross-attention. Once coarse structure has formed, FocusFlow applies Reference Generation Guidance (RGG), a trajectory-correction term that drives convergence to the target shape by pulling the trajectory toward an explicit clean-latent target. Evaluated on the joint Google Scanned Objects and PartObjaverse-Tiny benchmarks, FocusFlow obtains the best preservation scores and the largest edit magnitude among compared methods, suggesting that it can reduce the traditional preservation-modification trade-off. By providing stable, localized geometric control without manual spatial annotations, this phase-scheduled approach lowers technical barriers to the modification of synthetic 3D media.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29165) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29165)

## 293. Inclusive Hand Pose Estimation via Topology-Aware Distillation and Progressive Kinematic Reasoning

- **作者**：Kanglei Zhou, Zikai Hao, Liyuan Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR, cs.HC
- **备注**：Accepted to IEEE Transactions on Visualization and Computer Graphics (TVCG)

Hand pose estimation aims to recover the spatial configuration of hand joints from visual observations and serves as a fundamental perception module for downstream systems that rely on complete and topologically consistent hand representations. Most existing methods are developed under the assumption of fully articulated hand structures and tend to produce unstable or implausible predictions when parts of the kinematic structure are persistently absent, resulting in degraded performance or even system failure despite available partial observations. To study this challenge, we construct IncNYU, a physiologically constrained benchmark that simulates structural incompleteness through morphological modification of complete hand models and retains reference locations for both intact and synthetically removed regions. Based on this benchmark, we propose InsHaPE, an Inclusive Hand Pose Estimation framework designed to remain robust under structural incompleteness while preserving performance on standard, fully articulated hands. Built on hierarchical kinematic modeling, InsHaPE introduces two complementary components. First, a topology-aware distillation strategy transfers joint-level and relational priors from fully articulated hand representations, providing anatomically consistent initialization under missing structures. Second, a progressive kinematic reasoning module performs structured inference along the kinematic chain, enabling complete and anatomically consistent pose estimation under severe structural variations. Extensive experiments on NYU/IncNYU, together with external validation on MSRA, demonstrate that InsHaPE improves robustness under structural incompleteness while maintaining competitive performance on complete hands. System-level evaluations further show that InsHaPE improves robustness for downstream applications that require fixed-topology hand pose representations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29163) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29163)

## 294. MVAgent: Per-Video Agents under Global Coordination for Multi-Video Understanding

- **作者**：Kewei Wei, XueXiang Wen, Bocheng Hu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

Multi-video understanding requires systems to combine evidence from different videos for cross-video reasoning. Answering such questions involves two kinds of decisions. Observation decisions must adapt to each video's content, whereas cross-video judgments require relating evidence from different videos based on the question. Most existing agents make both kinds of decisions in one controller, whose context accumulates observations from every video. We separate them in MVAgent, a training-free Multi-Video Understanding Agent framework with one LocalAgent per video. Each LocalAgent plans observations of its own video and keeps a private observation history. A GlobalAgent coordinates cross-video reasoning by instructing the LocalAgents and combining their reports with joint observation. We evaluate MVAgent on CVBench, MVU-Eval, and CrossVid, using three Qwen3.5 models of different sizes and GPT-6-sol. With Qwen3.5, our framework improves aggregate benchmark scores by 5.1 points on average over Single-pass inference with the same model and by 5.0 points over the strongest prior agent using the same model. With GPT-6-sol, the average gain over Single-pass inference with the same model is 4.3 points. On the same hardware, MVAgent also runs 1.8-2.7 times faster than prior agents of comparable size.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29154) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29154)

## 295. Can an Agent Trust Its Own Zoom? Cross-Magnification Provenance Verification for Whole-Slide Images

- **作者**：Yan Wang, Yiran Song, Zizhuo Gong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Visual agents navigate whole-slide images through magnified observations associated with claimed locations. However, a visually plausible observation does not necessarily originate from its claimed location, particularly across magnifications and in regions with similar tissue structures. We formulate cross-magnification provenance verification (CMPV) to evaluate whether visual evidence supports a claimed spatial association, recover the source when possible, and abstain when evidence is insufficient. We introduce PyraFault, a benchmark of 3.06 million coordinate-supervised examples from 2,200 cases across five TCGA cohorts, covering within-context substitutions, absent-source faults, and geometric perturbations. We further propose ZoomTrace, a claim-independent verifier that estimates the observation's source before evaluating its agreement with the claimed location. It combines physical-scale alignment, visual retrieval, geometric refinement, and reliability estimation using spatially disjoint competing matches. On 503,320 test examples, ZoomTrace achieves 0.969 AUPRC and 0.987 AUROC, compared with 0.789 and 0.903 for Efficient LoFTR. Further evaluations reveal trade-offs in reliability estimation and limitations in vision-language models, cross-dataset retrieval, and calibration. Integrating ZoomTrace into visual agent tool paths reduces exposure to corrupted observations and silent failures, while downstream accuracy improvements depend on the agent and verification threshold.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29150) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29150)

## 296. ArborNet: Volume- and skeleton-aware completion of tree quantitative structure models from incomplete point clouds using anchor-conditioned graph learning

- **作者**：Ge Jiang, Tiangang Yin, Wei Yao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Terrestrial laser scanning (TLS) enables 3D reconstruction of trees and quantitative structure models (QSMs) for estimating woody volume and structural traits. However, occlusion in single-station or sparsely sampled TLS causes incomplete branch observations and biased volume estimates. Existing point-cloud completion methods recover plausible geometry but do not explicitly preserve branch topology, cylinder radii, or woody volume. We propose ArborNet, a volume- and skeleton-aware framework for completing tree structures in the QSM domain. Each tree is represented as a TreeQSM cylinder graph encoding cylinder geometry, branch level, parent-child topology, and branch identity. A multi-relation graph encoder propagates information across parent, child, and intra-branch connections. An anchor-conditioned decoder generates missing cylinders through terminal-branch continuation and new-branch generation. Geometry, existence, branch, radius, axis-smoothness, and volume losses encourage structural consistency. During inference, generated cylinders are selected under predicted level-wise missing-volume budgets. We evaluate ArborNet on DART-simulated TLS trees and urban TLS trees from Hong Kong, using single-station QSMs as partial inputs and multi-station QSMs as references. Under budget-constrained inference, ArborNet achieves mean total-volume relative error of 0.081% and mean level-wise volume error of 0.676% against reference QSMs, while reducing skeleton geometry error relative to evaluated point-cloud completion baselines. These errors quantify agreement with reference QSMs, not accuracy against destructively measured woody volume. Ablation results show that joint volume supervision is particularly important for volume control. These results demonstrate the potential of QSM-domain completion to recover explicit woody structure while constraining geometry and volume.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29148) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29148)

## 297. RVLM: Fewer Tokens, Better Resolution Scaling, Fine-Grained Spatial Grounding

- **作者**：Savya Khosla, Chris Dongjoo Kim, Alex Schwing et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) tokenize images using fixed spatial grids, so visual token count grows with pixel area rather than scene content. We propose to group neighboring patches based on their visual appearance, allowing tokens to represent coherent image regions rather than fixed spatial windows. We introduce RVLM, a VLM trained to use regions rather than patches as its visual units. Analyzing region construction for general-purpose VLMs, we find that regions must cover the full image, stay spatially connected during end-to-end training, and preserve fine structure such as small text, while semantic grouping adds little. RVLM therefore partitions images using training-free Felzenszwalb-Huttenlocher segmentation, assigns each ViT patch to a region, and pools patches within each region into a variable-length sequence of visual tokens. The patch-to-region assignment is retained to support spatially precise prediction through coarse-to-fine decoding. Across tasks spanning VQA, pointing, counting, segmentation, and depth estimation, RVLM matches or improves example-weighted task performance while using 21.5% fewer visual tokens than an identically trained patch baseline. The advantage grows with image resolution, reaching a 5.3-point gain while using 51.8% fewer visual tokens at the highest-resolution setting. The gains are largest on spatially demanding tasks, where prior token-reduction methods degrade most sharply. RVLM adds no model parameters and improves inference throughput by up to 35.9%. On unseen pathology and satellite images and on video benchmarks without any video training, RVLM performs on par with the patch baseline while using 2-3$\times$ fewer visual tokens. These results establish regions as a scalable alternative to patch-based visual representations while preserving the spatial precision required for grounded prediction.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29146) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29146)

## 298. RAVEC: Reward-Aligned Visual Evidence Correction

- **作者**：Junjie Yang, Rui Qian, Xutao Mao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards improves multimodal reasoning, but response-level feedback provides limited guidance on which intermediate decisions to correct. Visual interventions reveal sensitivity to image content, yet this sensitivity alone does not establish reward alignment or support across questions. We introduce RAVEC (Reward-Aligned Visual Evidence Correction), which selects visual corrections using local and cross-question reward support and jointly determines their strengths within each response. Paired Visual Evidence Contrast (PVEC) compares predictions from an exponential moving average teacher at the same sampled response prefix under the original image and a version with its annotated evidence region suppressed, yielding signed correction directions. Credit-Risk Allocation (CRA) screens these directions using local reward alignment and reward support from other questions, excluding all responses to the source question. It then solves a response-level budgeted allocation problem, assigning additional cost to directions that locally reduce predictive entropy. The resulting signed visual loss augments Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) during training. Inference uses only the original image and question. Across ten multimodal benchmarks, RAVEC achieves the highest average accuracy among the compared methods with both 3B and 7B Qwen2.5-VL-Instruct models, outperforming GRPO by 2.67 and 1.39 percentage points, respectively. Component ablations support the combined design of evidence alignment, credit qualification, and risk-aware allocation. The project is available at https://yangjj007.github.io/ravec/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29142) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29142)

## 299. Supply the Global, Supervise the Local: Combining Representation Conditioning and Alignment in Diffusion Transformers

- **作者**：Chaojie Ji, Sergio Quiroga-Sandoval, Ruxin Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

A pretrained image representation helps train a diffusion transformer in several roles, including as an alignment target, which the model's hidden states learn to match, as in representation alignment (REPA), and as a condition, an extra input describing the image to be generated. Combined, they would give the model an input and a target, but whether they conflict is unknown. To test this, we let the condition carry only part of REPA's target: we split the representation of each image patch into an image-level (global) part, shared by all patches of the image, and a within-image (local) part, the patch's difference from the image-level part, and use one token carrying the image-level part as the condition. Then, the two roles overlap on the image-level part, where they could conflict, while only REPA supervises the within-image part, where we can see whether the token helps REPA. We find that the token and REPA together teach both the image-level part and the within-image part, the main source of REPA's FID improvement, far more than the sum of what each teaches alone, so they cooperate rather than conflict. These findings give one design rule for combining REPA with an image-level condition, supply the global, supervise the local, which we implement as Global-Condition REPA (GC-REPA): REPA with the token as an extra input; the token carries no information beyond REPA's target and is drawn at sampling from a separately trained token prior. GC-REPA greatly accelerates convergence: on ImageNet 256*256, SiT-XL/2 + GC-REPA reaches REPA's 4M- and SiT-XL/2's 7M-iteration FIDs within 200K and 100K iterations (20 and 70 fewer).

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29138) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29138)

## 300. SilentWAM: Planning in the Masked State of a Diffusion World Model

- **作者**：Ziyu Zhao, Casey Sun, Zeyu Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

World-action models learn from future prediction, but accessing their future representations directly at test time can require costly frame generation. Methods that generate future frames before planning incur additional forward passes, while future co-training methods omit explicit future predictions at inference. We introduce \method, a planner that reads the hidden states of masked future tokens without decoding future frames. We pretrain MMaDA-Parallel-M on driving video using frame-level masked world modeling, which predicts a masked suffix of future frames and explicitly includes inputs with all future frames masked. Learned planning queries attend to these masked positions and the observed context, and a continuous head predicts the trajectory in the same forward pass. With a single front camera, \method reaches 92.0 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 89.3 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2, with an end-to-end latency of 60.3\,ms.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29125) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29125)

## 301. GAG-Net: Graph-Based Anatomical Guidance Network for Pharyngeal Area Segmentation in VFSS Images

- **作者**：JunYeong Park, Myung Woo Park, Byung Chan Lee et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：30 pages, 7 figures, 4 tables

Diagnosing sarcopenic dysphagia typically requires costly supplementary imaging such as CT or MRI to assess muscle atrophy, burdening elderly patients. The pharyngeal area at rest (PA_rest), measurable directly from videofluoroscopic swallowing study (VFSS) images, is a promising biomarker that could eliminate this dependency, but repeated manual measurement has limited its adoption. PA_rest segmentation is further complicated by indistinct soft-tissue boundaries and clinical rule-based delineation criteria that data-driven models capture only imprecisely. We formulate automated PA_rest segmentation as a clinically defined measurement task, translating an implicit clinical measurement into an explicit landmark specification and annotation protocol developed with a rehabilitation medicine specialist. Applying this protocol, we constructed, to our knowledge, the first dataset for this task: 2,255 single-center VFSS frames from 137 patients, with PA_rest contours and seven landmarks verified by a senior specialist. We then introduce GAG-Net, an automated rest-frame measurement pipeline combining deformable convolutions, for irregular pharyngeal boundaries, with a graph-based anatomical guidance module that models the geometric dependencies among landmarks prescribed by the delineation rules. On a patient-level held-out test set (237 frames, 67 patients), GAG-Net achieved the lowest area measurement error (mean absolute percentage error: 8.17% against specialist-verified references; patient-level ICC 0.97) and the highest Dice score (0.8862) among all evaluated methods, with 67.2% fewer parameters and 74% less training memory than the two-stage JOINED model. Integrating clinician-defined anatomical knowledge may enable automated PA_rest measurement from VFSS images alone, supporting quantitative assessment of sarcopenic dysphagia without supplementary imaging.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29118) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29118)

## 302. A Camera-Based Single-Volume, Monolithic Compton Camera

- **作者**：Alex Bocchieri, Devanshi Jain, Andreas Velten
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：physics.ins-det, cs.CV, eess.IV

A Compton camera measures two events in a detector from individual gamma-rays to reconstruct the radiation source. Conventional scintillator-based Compton cameras consist of two separate scintillator volumes or a single segmented scintillator, resulting in drawbacks regarding detector bulkiness and efficiency. Their scintillator readout techniques struggle to resolve two nearby events in one monolithic scintillator. Single-photon avalanche diode (SPAD) cameras have recently been proposed for measuring scintillation events in various configurations. SPAD array sensors enable high-resolution photon-counting acquisition at high rates that is easily scalable to millions of pixels. Prior work has explored different optics and measurement arrangements as well as demonstrated measurement of single interaction locations. In this work, we extend these capabilities to demonstrate a practical radiation imaging device in the form of a Compton camera consisting of a SPAD camera and one compact, monolithic scintillator. Our Compton camera prototype successfully reconstructs a gamma-ray source over multiple locations that span a 4$π$ steradian field of view. Our approach is able to resolve two simultaneous events in one monolithic scintillator and enables Compton camera devices with compact, monolithic scintillator volumes for enhanced portability.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29116) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29116)

## 303. Bridging Blind Spots With Words: Decentralized Multi-View Spatial Reasoning via Language-Constrained Communication

- **作者**：Soumyabrata Chaudhuri, Joydeep Ghosh
- **投稿**：2026-11-22 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.IR, cs.LG
- **备注**：17 pages, 13 tables, 4 figures

As multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) get increasingly used as collaborative partners across embodied robotics, AR/VR, and multi-user systems, spatial reasoning is now often distributed across agents with partial environment visibility, necessitating spatial reasoning integration. Prior work in language-mediated spatial integration is however strictly limited to simplified two-agent scenarios. To address this gap, we first formalize the general problem of decentralized multi-view spatial reasoning, where N agents observe distinct viewpoints, exchange budget-constrained natural language messages, and collaborate to enable a listener to answer spatial queries. We then introduce a novel multi-view spatial benchmark to isolate queries that demand non-trivial multi-view integration. Through an extensive empirical study, we discover that the penalty of restricting decentralized spatial reasoning to natural language with limited budget is far smaller than suggested by prior two-agent literature. Nevertheless, we observe that substantial headroom still remains for multi-agent spatial reasoning in this setting, as evidenced by a significant gap between current centralized performance and an oracle best-of-K ceiling. Moreover, we show that reinforcement learning with credit assignment substantially bolsters the decentralized model, effectively closing the gap with the centralized baseline.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29112) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29112)

## 304. TesseraFlow: Scalable Video Diffusion for Dynamic Medical Imaging Inverse Problems

- **作者**：Atakan Topcu, Mohammadhossein Momeni, Alfredo De Goyeneche et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Generative priors are state-of-the-art for modeling medical images in image reconstruction tasks, but reconstruction with video priors takes tens of seconds to minutes per frame. Latent priors inherit the errors of their autoencoder, the memory of pixel-space whole-frame priors grows with the frame size, and pixel-space patch priors are slow for two reasons. First, their deep networks process every frame at full resolution at every sampling step, even though early steps resolve only low frequencies. Second, their samplers spend many network evaluations per sampling step on overlapping windows, multiple re-noising rounds, or backpropagation through the prior. We introduce TesseraFlow, a complex-valued pixel-space flow prior that reduces the first cost. A global encoder reads a low-resolution view of every frame once per sampling step. A single shallow decoder operates on this view during early steps and on full-resolution space-time tiles during later steps, so no network processes a whole frame at full resolution. Our sampler, ReTile, addresses the second reason. We use randomized grid shifting at every sampling step along with re-noising, which acts as a stochastic gradient instead of computing overlapping tiles, so a single network pass per sampling step suffices. On its own, ReTile speeds up an existing global-context patch prior by 6-9x, and our prior adds a further 3-5x speedup. TesseraFlow reconstructs about one 256x256 frame per second, 20-367x faster than patch and latent video priors, and can match or exceed the best generative baseline on cardiac, brain, and abdominal MRI and lung CT, at a fraction of its GPU time.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29099) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29099)

## 305. Projecting Beauty: Transferring Image Aesthetic Analysis onto 3D Heritage and Urban Scenes

- **作者**：Sophia Kovalenko, Alexander M. Rusnak, Vittoria Meroni et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CY

Judgments about the beauty of buildings and streets inform architectural design, urban planning, and heritage conservation, yet increasingly rely on scanned 3D models over material fabric. Automatic aesthetic assessment remains largely confined to 2D photographs. We ask what survives when image aesthetic assessment crosses this dimensional gap to produce dense, per-point aesthetic descriptions of heritage and urban point clouds. We examine three routes that sidestep costly, and sometimes ethically dubious, human labeling at scale: (1) lifting scalar scores from image aesthetic models (NIMA, SAAN) onto geometry; (2) training Aesthetic Visual Analysis (AVA) regression heads on frozen vision backbones (DINOv3, TIPS, SigLIP2, PE, RADSeg) or, in a 3D-native variant, a generalist point encoder on feed-forward AVA reconstructions; and (3) querying lifted vision-language features with a natural-language aesthetic label, taking similarity as a score. The two supervised routes transfer poorly: heads fitting AVA in-domain produce architectural class profiles that neither reproduce one another nor track other signals, while the 3D-native probe stays near chance due to reconstruction geometry quality. Text querying is markedly more coherent. Projecting out the class-text direction from point features raises mean cross-model agreement on architectural part profiles from +0.035 to +0.352, and from +0.113 to +0.293 per point; the four backbones passing a semantic sanity check correlate at 0.31-0.73, while the failing one remains an outlier. Since model agreement is not agreement with human aesthetic perception, we detail a part-aware rating protocol to clarify where human and automatic ratings disagree. We provide a reproducible transfer protocol, an account of which pseudo-labeling routes carry signal, and dense aesthetic pseudo-labels meant as hypotheses rather than ground truth.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29071) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29071)

## 306. Single-Transformer Latent Diffusion

- **作者**：Hao-Yu Hsu, Shenlong Wang, Alexander G. Schwing
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://haoyuhsu.github.io/Unified-LDM/

Latent generative models typically rely on three separate networks: an encoder that tokenizes pixels, a generator that models the latent distribution, and a decoder that maps latents back to pixels. UNITE recently merged the first two into a single generative encoder trained in one stage, but its decoder remains a separate network. We ask whether this last network can be folded in too, so that one diffusion transformer encodes, generates, and decodes. Simply letting the shared backbone decode does not help. What makes it work is to cast all three modes as position-aligned sequence maps over one fully shared backbone, distinguished only by a mode token and the timestep. The resulting model, UNIFIED, is simple: one network trained from scratch in a single stage with only reconstruction and flow-matching losses, without adversarial losses or pretrained encoders (e.g., DINO). On ImageNet-1K 256x256 at matched parameters, UNIFIED improves gFID over UNITE at three sizes (~60M, ~115M, and ~215M) and reaches 2.08 with 215M parameters, the lowest among compared models under 250M parameters. On text-to-image generation, UNIFIED-B (218M) reaches a GenEval score of 0.87 at 512x512, exceeding single-stage pixel diffusion models with around one billion parameters. Full unification of latent diffusion is thus not merely feasible but beneficial.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29064) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29064)

## 307. Recurrent Point Transformer via Test-Time Training

- **作者**：Wentao Zhou, Xuweiyi Chen, Charles Saunders et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Real-world point clouds are not naturally bounded: 3D scans grow as sensors move through rooms, LiDAR surveys extend across forest plots, and 4D captures can span thousands of frames. Yet most point-cloud encoders are designed for fixed-size inputs, forcing inference-time cropping or tiling that loses long-range context and requires ad-hoc output stitching. We formulate arbitrary-extent point-cloud segmentation as recurrent sequence-of-chunks processing and introduce PoinTTT, a recurrent point-cloud encoder built on Point Transformer V3 (PTv3). PoinTTT augments PTv3 with residual Large Chunk Test-Time Training (LaCT) layers in both the encoder and decoder. Each LaCT layer maintains compact fast weights as recurrent state: tokens from each chunk update the fast weights, which are then applied to produce chunk features and passed to the next chunk. This design allows PoinTTT to train on fixed-size chunks while processing scenes of arbitrary spatial or temporal extent, with activation memory bounded by the chunk size. A pilot study on ScanNet and nuScenes shows that recurrent TTT improves segmentation over PTv3 baselines. On FOR-instanceV2, PoinTTT achieves state-of-the-art forest semantic segmentation of 87.9 mIoU with nearly constant memory. On HOI4D, online full-sequence recurrent inference carries state across clips rather than resetting at benchmark boundaries, reaching 42.6 mIoU; a matched full-sequence Mamba control reaches approximately 39.9. These results suggest that test-time training is a practical primitive for scalable spatial and temporal point-cloud understanding.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29059) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29059)

## 308. DiRGeo: Learning Directional Geometry of Visual Representations

- **作者**：Muhammad Ahtesham, Xin Zhong
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
