Conventional geometric measures of visual representations are symmetric, assigning equal costs to forward and reverse motion along the same path. We propose DiRGeo, a framework for learning directional geometry over frozen visual representations. Grounded in Randers geometry, DiRGeo learns a state-dependent directional field from ordered image trajectories through self-supervised contrast, without semantic labels. Sphere-tangent correction and attractor-relative decomposition distinguish learned directional structure from normalization artifacts and common convergence. Experiments across 17 visual processes, three encoder families, and multiple representation depths demonstrate consistent directional discrimination and whole-path asymmetry beyond simple attractor-based explanations. The learned fields become increasingly process-specific in deeper representations, while natural-video experiments reveal event-dependent directional preferences. As functional validation, DiRGeo guides restoration selection. These results establish directional geometry as a useful complement to symmetric representation analysis.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29057) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29057)

## 309. T2ISpec: Benchmarking Speculative Decoding for Text-to-Image Generation

- **作者**：Yifan Zhang, Shatong Zhu, Hui Shen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Text-to-image (T2I) generators produce detailed images but remain slow at inference. Speculative decoding can reduce this cost, yet its behavior across the rendering requirements of T2I prompts remains insufficiently understood. We introduce T2ISpec, a benchmark of 1,400 prompts balanced across seven rendering topics and two difficulty levels. It evaluates autoregressive and diffusion/flow accelerators by latency, work performed by the target model, and image quality. Our study reveals three key findings: (1) aggregate results hide large differences between topics, (2) alignment and preference scores can disagree with prompt constraint satisfaction, and (3) large reductions in target work can yield limited latency gains. Motivated by these findings, we propose TopicRoute, a plugin that combines prompt routing with speculative and cache controls without retraining the generator. Across six model--method pairs, it achieves a geometric mean speedup of 1.0308x over the native methods, and the speedup carries over to external prompts with the same settings, while quality gains and losses vary across configurations.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29029) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29029)

## 310. 3D-EMCAD: Efficient Multi-scale Convolutional Attention Decoding for Volumetric Brain Tumor Segmentation

- **作者**：Iftakhar Hossain, Israt Jahan Sinthia, Md. Hadiur Rahman Khan
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：7 pages, 4 figures, 1 table. Submitted to ICCIT 2026

Encoder-decoder networks are widely used for volumetric brain tumor segmentation, yet recent trends favor high-capacity decoders that incur steep computational costs in 3D at high spatial resolutions. The Efficient Multi-scale Convolutional Attention Decoder (EMCAD) is a lightweight decoder that achieves strong accuracy at very low cost, but it has been validated only in 2D. Here, we extend EMCAD to volumetric segmentation (3D-EMCAD) by converting all operations to 3D and replacing standard normalization layers with group normalization to suit the small batch sizes of volumetric training. To isolate the impact of decoder choice, we evaluate five decoders: the proposed 3D-EMCAD, a vanilla SegResNet decoder, an Attention U-Net decoder, UNet++, and a plain U-Net decoder using an identical 3D SegResNet encoder and training protocol on BraTS 2020 (369 cases, three-fold cross-validation). The five decoders yield statistically indistinguishable performance: their mean Dice scores span only 0.0071, less than the fold-to-fold variation of most models, and a paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test shows no significant difference between 3D-EMCAD and three of the four baselines. Importantly, 3D-EMCAD achieves this shared accuracy with the fewest decoder parameters (1.15 M) and operations (8.6 G), requiring $7.3\times$ fewer operations than UNet++, which scores no higher. These findings demonstrate that decoder capacity beyond a modest budget offers no measurable benefit for volumetric brain tumor segmentation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29027) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29027)

## 311. RAGA: Refreshable Animatable Gaussian Avatars from a Single Photo

- **作者**：Sadia Mubashshira, John Quarles, Kevin Desai
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Avatars created from a single photo are fixed once made: regions the photo never showed, such as the back of a garment, are hallucinated and remain that way. We present Refreshable Animatable Gaussian Avatars (RAGA), a full-body Gaussian avatar that is created from a single image in about one second and can be refreshed whenever new images of the same person become available, without retraining or gradient-based optimization. RAGA maps each new observation into canonical UV space, accumulates it as visibility- and angle-weighted running sums, and re-decodes the fused map with the same decoder used for the photo. Although this decoder is trained only on single images, it makes use of the fused evidence. On 163 clips from five datasets, RAGA created from one photo outperforms LHM, LHM++ and IDOL, including on three datasets never used in training. Refreshing from later frames adds 0.49 dB PSNR overall and 0.68 dB on views turned at least 135° from the photo, where SSIM and LPIPS also improve. Given the same 16 images on all clips, LHM++ gains 0.15 dB, against 0.48 dB for RAGA. Creating an avatar takes 1.08 s and animating it 48.1 ms per frame on a laptop RTX 3080 GPU, with body fitting and matting included in creation and pose estimation included in animation. Code will be released on GitHub.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29026) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29026)

## 312. Demystifying Classifier-Free Guidance for Auto-Regressive Image Generation

- **作者**：Zhiling Zhou, Jiachun Pan, Fengzhuo Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026. 62 pages, 14 figures

Classifier-free guidance (CFG) has been widely adopted in autoregressive (AR) models for high-quality image generation. Despite its strong empirical performance, its mechanism in AR models remains unclear. This paper demystifies the mechanism of CFG through both empirical and theoretical studies. By examining the top-ranked semantics of different components in CFG, we show that texture information is primarily encoded in the difference between the conditional and unconditional logits, whereas both logits share previous-token repetition as their leading semantics. This strong repetition semantics obscures the desired texture information, causing greedy decoding from the conditional logits alone to produce nearly pure-color images. Through a training-dynamics analysis of shallow transformers, we prove that this shared repetition semantics does not arise from limitations or failures of pretraining, but instead originates from the texture sparsity of images. We further show that CFG improves generation quality by rectifying this repetition bias during inference. Motivated by the shared semantics between conditional and unconditional logits, we propose Attention Weight Reuse (AttnReuse), which reuses intermediate attention computations from the conditional-logit forward pass to accelerate the unconditional-logit computation. AttnReuse reduces about $25\%$ of attention computation with little performance degradation across different models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29002) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.29002)

## 313. Elicitation format changes zero-shot classification accuracy in a medical vision-language model: a two-center evaluation on colorectal histopathology

- **作者**：Anay Sinhal, Amit Sinhal, Arpana Sinhal
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.LG
- **备注**：34 pages, 6 figures; Supplementary Table S1 and Supplementary Material S2 in ancillary files. Corrects one result reported in the companion preprint "Diagnosing Verbalized Confidence in a Medical Vision-Language Model for Selective Prediction on Colorectal Histopathology Patches" (Sinhal et al., arXiv, 2026)

Background. Verbalized confidence from a hosted medical vision-language model is usually treated as packaging around an unchanged prediction. Methods. We held a nine-class colorectal histopathology prompt fixed except for the requested response format and evaluated MedGemma-27b-it on 10,004 validation and 7,180 external-center PathMNIST patches, with preregistered endpoints, randomized ties, and random floors recomputed for each patch set. Results. Requesting probabilities over all nine classes instead of a ranked top-3 reduced accuracy by 14.8 points on validation, -0.1475 [-0.1594, -0.1353], and 13.4 at the external center, -0.1341 [-0.1484, -0.1196], against chance accuracy of 0.1111. The nine-class prompt uniquely forbade commentary, reasoning, and a chosen answer; removing that prohibition at matched cardinality and token budget recovered +0.1878 [+0.1623, +0.2134] and +0.1911 [+0.1648, +0.2181], while changing cardinality with the prohibition absent moved accuracy by -0.0013 [-0.0300, +0.0280] and -0.0214 [-0.0494, +0.0071]. The nine-class format returned no parseable distribution on 7.6 and 7.7 percent of patches, mostly zero vectors in the two least-textured classes, and deferring these failures first explained its measurable selective-prediction gain. Across four vision-capable models, accuracy did not grade the cost: two models matched on accuracy differed five- to sevenfold. First-token logit entropy routed better than any elicited signal, +0.1820 [+0.1722, +0.1974] versus +0.0493 [+0.0377, +0.0649] relative to each signal's random floor. Conclusions. Response format changes a medical vision-language classifier, and the operative component here was the instruction suppressing an answer. Use logits when available; otherwise request a short ranked list without prohibiting reasoning or a chosen answer.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28997) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28997)

## 314. Diagnosing Verbalized Confidence in a Medical Vision-Language Model for Selective Prediction on Colorectal Histopathology Patches

- **作者**：Anay Sinhal, Amit Sinhal, Ashish Pandey et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：39 pages, 7 figures; supplementary material in ancillary files. Under review at Scientific Reports. Code and artifacts: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23676823

Flagging uncertain histopathology patches for specialist review needs a confidence that is higher when the model is right. We test whether an open medical vision-language model emits one. MedGemma-27b-it and its base Gemma-3-27b-it were run zero-shot under one prompt on 10,004 PathMNIST validation patches and 7,180 patches of a patient-disjoint external cohort. Both are too inaccurate to deploy (40.6% and 35.5%); the object of study is the confidence. MedGemma's verbalized confidence puts 96% of patches at one value; its confidence-correctness AUROC is 0.499 and 0.528, and routing by it moves selective-accuracy AUC by -0.004 and +0.037. Gemma-3 spreads its confidence over nine values, reaches AUROC 0.70, and gains +0.114 and +0.115. MedGemma's label-token probabilities do not rescue it: their validation advantage reverses where the top-20 alternatives include every class. On 1,800-patch subsets of each cohort, stratified on correctness and reweighted to the full splits, agreement across five prompt variants beats random by +0.123 and +0.106, and wording-only paraphrases give +0.100 and, externally and in direction only, +0.054; agreement's external margins over confidence do not survive slide-level clustering. Under one restructured prompt variant, MedGemma's stated confidence routes (+0.112, +0.141), so the failure is tied to a prompt chosen for macro F1.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28993) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28993)

## 315. CLASP: Complementary Layer Selection and Profiling for Zero-Shot OOD Detection using Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Saahil Islam, Adarsh Bhandary Panambur, Teodora Spasojevic et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Vision-language models (VLMs) enable zero-shot out-of-distribution (OOD) detection in medical imaging, yet existing approaches often rely on final-layer confidence or entropy-based layer selection, potentially missing complementary information. We show that low in-distribution (ID) confidence entropy does not reliably predict OOD discrimination. We introduce CLASP (Complementary Layer Selection and Profiling), an ID-only framework that jointly models layer--prompt responses and visual features. CLASP ranks visual encoder layers using Gaussian conditional mutual information and selects a compact subset through anchored spectral selection, without fixing the number of layers. It then combines the selected layer--prompt profiles with PCA-compressed visual embeddings and fits a single shrinkage-regularized Gaussian. OOD scores are computed using squared Mahalanobis distance, without fine-tuning, OOD training data, or test-time adaptation. Across histopathology, chest X-ray, and brain MRI benchmarks with UniMedCLIP and BioMedCLIP, CLASP performs strongly under varied distribution shifts. On MIDOG near-OOD, it achieves 87.54% AUROC with UniMedCLIP, outperforming confidence-based baselines; with BioMedCLIP, it remains competitive but trails visual-only Mahalanobis modeling. Ablations highlight the value of layer--prompt interactions and cross-feature dependencies, while exposing shift-dependent contributions and limitations under semantic shifts.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28986) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28986)

## 316. AlignHand: Multi-View Hand Reconstruction from a Frozen Monocular Prior

- **作者**：Zhilu Yang, Kun Yang, Shiyan Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：18 pages, 6 figures. Project page and code: https://ada-zy2425.github.io/AlignHand/

Modern monocular hand estimators recover detailed 3D meshes from individual images, yet effectively leveraging their predictions across multiple views remains challenging. Independently predicted meshes can disagree in scale, placement, and articulation, limiting the integration of complementary multi-view information. Hence, we present AlignHand, a calibrated multi-view framework that builds on a frozen monocular hand reconstruction prior to align and enhance its predictions. Specifically, AlignHand refines cross-view joint observations and estimates their reliability to establish a shared metric reference for alignment. Once the meshes are brought into a common frame, their complementary surface evidence is selectively fused across anatomical regions, followed by pixel-aligned refinement that predicts bounded residual displacements from local image features. This global-to-local integration enables AlignHand to exploit complementary observations while preserving the strong monocular prior. Extensive experiments on DexYCB-MV, HanCo, and HO3D-Eval-MV demonstrate that AlignHand achieves the lowest MPVPE and MPJPE among the compared methods under the respective evaluation protocols, reducing MPVPE by 30.1%, 29.1%, and 8.0%, respectively, compared with a matched geometry baseline.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28979) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28979)

## 317. World Models for Ocean Engineering: A Critical Review of Maritime Autonomy, Adaptive Latent Prediction, Digital Twins, and Biodiversity Monitoring

- **作者**：Basma Dokkara, Esteban W. G. Cluab, Luiz M. G. Goncalves et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV

Marine robots face partial observability, non-stationary dynamics, and sparse communication, motivating WMs that learn compact predictive states for planning. This review develops a functional taxonomy of WMs for ocean engineering, focusing on Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs) and AdaJEPA's test-time adaptation within model predictive control, mapping them to navigation, adaptive sampling, digital twins, and biodiversity monitoring, and separating demonstrated evidence from prospective applications. The synthesis identifies key Gaps: benchmarks, uncertainty calibration, safe adaptation and proposes a research agenda for physics-informed, deployment-adaptive marine WMs validated through simulation and field missions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28978) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28978)

## 318. What the Background Hides: Thresholds, Vocabularies and Collisions in Training-Free Open-Vocabulary Segmentation

- **作者**：Wendong Yao, Binhua Huang, Soumyabrata Dev
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：33 pages, 11 figures, 13 tables. Submitted to Computer Vision and Image Understanding. Code: https://github.com/WendongYao/ovss-background-audit ; data: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24338613

Training-free open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) built on CLIP has advanced rapidly, with methods typically ranked by mean intersection-over-union (mIoU) across standard benchmarks. On datasets with a background class, however, implementations rely on per-dataset thresholds and background word lists that are rarely documented or unified (11 of 13 surveyed methods configure per-dataset background decisions). We audit configurations and papers of 13 training-free OVSS methods, re-run seven from official codebases, and compare official settings against a strict unified protocol under pre-registered contracts. The protocol gap depends strongly on the benchmark---ranging from 22.8 mIoU points on PASCAL VOC (VOC21) to 0.1 points (indistinguishable from zero) on ADE20K---and alters pairwise method rankings. Despite large shifts in absolute mIoU, most of the reported improvement of later CLIP-only methods over SCLIP remains under the unified protocol (roughly 79% of the published gain). Decomposing official background handling shows that it combines numeric settings and class-name files whose dominant component reverses across benchmarks, while no tested tuning-free rule transfers reliably across all datasets. Moreover, exact collisions between background words and target names---present in six of seven official COCO-Object configurations through "bed"---make affected classes unpredictable under official post-processing, while the main per-class drops under language-model-generated vocabularies on VOC21 are largely attributable to a few generated terms. We offer reporting recommendations and release our evaluation code, frozen configurations, contracts, and a collision-check tool.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28966) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28966)

## 319. Learning Land-Surface Hydrology World Model from Satellite Observations

- **作者**：Suya Li, Zekun Qi, Xiaochong Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Tech Report

A world model should tell us not only what will happen, but what would happen under a different action. Learned Earth models now forecast remarkably well, yet forecast skill says little about what their state means or how it responds to a changed driver. We build LandWM, a world model of land-surface water in which rain is the action, learned from seven years of SMAP and CYGNSS observations: physical observation operators tie its latent state to soil water, and a mass-conserving balance governs how that state responds to rain. Over the U.S. Southern Great Plains, the model, never shown a soil-moisture value, tracks 28 in-situ probes as well as the operational SMAP Level-4 product, even from CYGNSS alone, and a storm added to the forcing of a station's cell wets its soil as natural storms wet the probes. Replace either piece and the forecasts stay, but the meaning goes: with neural operators, only a probe fitted to the stations finds the soil water; with a generic recurrent transition, rain on a single cell barely moves it. Yet when the same rain covers the whole region the two transitions swap roles, and the conserving one responds too weakly. Grounding makes a world model readable; its response to actions must be tested under more than one kind of intervention.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28963) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28963)

## 320. Don't Sleep, Keep Watch: Proactive Perception for Computer-Use Agents

- **作者**：Jiaqi Su, Jie-Jing Shao, Yichen Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CL, cs.CV

Computer-use agents (CUAs) built on vision-language models are increasingly entrusted with long-horizon tasks in live desktop, web, and mobile environments, where the screen keeps changing on its own. However, current CUAs lack real-time situational awareness: they perceive the environment through discrete snapshots, and the substantial inference latency of VLMs, compounded by highly dynamic interfaces, opens a perception-action gap in which the observed state frequently becomes stale before the corresponding action is executed. To bridge this gap, we present NightWatch, a plug-and-play framework that endows any CUA with continuous, temporally grounded perception without changing the agent's model, prompt, or action space. NightWatch couples the VLM agent with NightWatch-0.8B, a lightweight System-1 model that performs streaming monitoring of the interface state and keeps the agent synchronized with the evolving environment at low computational cost and latency, thereby grounding each decision in the current state rather than an outdated snapshot and reducing both stale actions and idle waiting while preserving task success. Experiments on desktop and dynamic web environments (OSWorld-Verified and DynaCU-Bench) show that NightWatch, driven by a watcher of only 0.8B parameters, reduces non-reasoning wall-clock time by 33% and wasted time by 53% on OSWorld while keeping task success on par, raises success on DynaCU-Bench from 39.5% to 44.2% with the large VLM agent, and is more efficient than passive-watching baselines such as AOI and PUSV under the same screenshot-only input, as well as a System-1 decision model more than $30\times$ its size. Overall, NightWatch demonstrates that the timing of perception is as critical as its content, marking a step toward agents that maintain continuous awareness of their environment rather than perceiving it turn by turn.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28947) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28947)

## 321. SANA-Robot: Efficient World Action Model with Hybrid Memory

- **作者**：Yuyang Zhao, Zekai Li, Yicheng Pan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

World action models (WAMs) leverage the world knowledge of video generation models to improve action generation by aligning visual prediction with action prediction. However, their prediction overhead prevents real-time deployment on edge devices, and they usually lack an effective long-term memory. We introduce SANA-Robot, a 4.5B WAM with minute-level memory that runs in real time on edge devices, built on the following core designs: (1) hybrid memory at constant cost: our WAM relies on linear attention to maintain a global memory with a fixed-size state, and on sliding window softmax attention layers that propagate previous information, enabling high success rates on tasks requiring whole-episode memory while keeping an efficient and constant inference speed; (2) a unified model: by dynamically applying different noise levels to action and video tokens, SANA-Robot can serve not only as a policy but also as a world simulator; (3) a full-stack infrastructure for both memory training and inference, including efficient data packing and shuffling as well as memory-phase-based asynchronous execution. SANA-Robot achieves the best performance on the memory dimension of RoboDojo among models of medium size (about 5B) and the highest success rates on LIBERO-Plus, LIBERO-Pro and LIBERO-Max. In real-world deployment, SANA-Robot takes 135 ms per action chunk on an RTX 5090 with FP8 GEMMs and 115 ms with NVFP4 GEMMs, and achieves closed-loop, real-time asynchronous robot control at 30 Hz on edge devices, demonstrating both the efficiency and the effectiveness of SANA-Robot. Project page: https://nvlabs.github.io/Sana/Robot/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28943) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28943)

## 322. XShot: Cross-Shot Video Object Insertion from a Reference Video

- **作者**：Bangxun Tang, Chen Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：20 pages, 14 figures, 7 tables

We present XShot, a framework for cross-shot video object insertion, which adds objects to a shot by watching another shot of the same scene. Video productions show a scene through many shots, and objects added to one shot must appear consistently in the others. Most video editors take the appearance of new objects from reference images and their location from a text prompt, a mask or an edited keyframe, leaving this consistency to the user. XShot reads both from a reference video of another shot, which need not be aligned with the target, and needs no mask, box or camera pose. We build on a video Diffusion Transformer and formulate the task as in-context generation over the reference video, a few images of the objects and the target video. A geometry-aware cross-shot attention estimates the epipolar geometry between the two shots from the model's own correspondences and uses it to guide where the target reads the reference and the exemplars. We train XShot with an erase-and-restore data engine: objects are erased from one shot of a film or a multi-camera recording and restored from another shot of the scene. Experiments show that XShot places objects more accurately and renders them more faithfully than open-source and commercial video editors, while leaving the rest of the shot intact.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28942) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28942)

## 323. PIAE: Physics-Informed Continuous Autoencoding for Compact 3D Human Motion

- **作者**：Osama Alshammari, Omar Mohammed, Kerven Durdymyradov et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Temporal compression reduces the sequence length that motion generators must model, but can damage articulated detail and global trajectory. These errors have unequal consequences: root-velocity and heading errors propagate through trajectory recovery and affect the placement of the entire body. We propose PIAE, a physics-informed continuous autoencoder that allocates a fixed token budget between explicitly retained kinematic signals and complementary learned features. At 8 x temporal compression, each 256-dimensional token contains 224 learned values and a 32-dimensional physical carrier. The complete token conditions neural decoding, while a parallel recovery path constrains selected outputs. On the official HumanML3D benchmark, PIAE achieves 8.936 mm global MPJPE, an 82.0% reduction relative to a same-width learned-only model. The benefit extends beyond trajectory restoration: facing-local neural-body error before analytical correction decreases by 76.6% in a frozen-checkpoint validation diagnostic, and supplying the same true trajectory to both models preserves the body-quality advantage. Targeted root-only and explicit-root hybrid controls establish strong trajectory-aware comparisons; alternative carriers reveal that torso references favor position accuracy, whereas stance anchors favor support quality. With the original 8 x AEs frozen, matched text-to-motion generators predict every carrier value and improve FID from 0.506 to 0.163. Frozen reconstruction also transfers to AIST++ without adaptation. The compressed carrier interface reduces measured generation time by 27.8% relative to its 1 x counterpart. Together, these findings demonstrate that selective preservation can use a fixed representation budget more effectively, improving neural articulated reconstruction and downstream generation in compact human motion.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28922) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28922)

## 324. Div3DAfford: Diverse 3D affordance grounding

- **作者**：Haomeng Zhang, Raymond A. Yeh
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

3D affordance grounding aims to localize 3D regions specified by an interaction query. Existing methods predict a single affordance mask, overlooking the inherent diversity of human-object interactions. We introduce diverse 3D affordance grounding, a new task that requires predicting multiple plausible regions for a given object and affordance query. We then propose Div3DAffordNet, a transformer-based set prediction model capable of generating diverse affordance regions. To address the scarcity of diverse 3D affordance data, we develop an automated pipeline leveraging 2D generative models and 3D reconstruction techniques to synthesize 3D affordances, followed by lightweight human verification (reducing the annotation time by 72X), yielding the Div3DAfford benchmark. Extensive experiments show Div3DAffordNet effectively captures multi-mask affordance regions. Our method achieves the SOTA affordance grounding performance in the multi-mask setting, reducing GED-SIM from 0.745 to 0.492, while maintaining competitive single-mask performance on the standard benchmark of 19.0 mIoU.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28917) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28917)

## 325. From Dense to Sparse: Morton-Ordered Sparse Mapping for Efficient 3D Mesh Generation

- **作者**：Junjie Yang, Rui Qian, Yijiang Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Unified 3D understanding and generation require compact sequences preserving spatial structure. Dense grids yield long sequences, while serialization can separate spatial neighbors. We propose Sparse Proximity Aware Compression (SPAC), a shared sparse SDF vocabulary combining sparse encoding with reversible spatial merging. A Sparse SDF VQ-VAE quantizes near-surface features while retaining explicit coordinates, using reconstruction supervision for surface fidelity and detail. 3D Byte-Pair Encoding (3D-BPE) merges recurring face-adjacent patterns using relative offsets before serialization. Macro tokens expand exactly into leaf tokens and coordinates. After Morton serialization, a shared LLM reads geometry for captioning and predicts tokens that decode into meshes for text-conditioned generation. With fixed geometry, architecture, and band width in voxels, sparse encoder cost grows approximately quadratically with resolution. 3D-BPE reduces mean complete LLM sequence length by 45.4 percent, including coordinates, text, and control tokens. SPAC achieves strong results in both 3D captioning and text-conditioned mesh generation. Ablations show gains over leaf tokens and Morton 1D-BPE in both tasks. The project page at https://yangjj007.github.io/spac/ .

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28913) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28913)

## 326. Gaze Graphs: A Scene-Level Representation of Visual Attention in the Operating Room

- **作者**：Séraphin Baributsa, Keqi Chen, Vinkle Srivastav et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Purpose: Analyzing clinicians' visual attention from exocentric camera views can support surgical scene understanding without requiring wearable eye trackers. However, existing gaze-following research in the operating room (OR) estimates only 2D gaze locations without identifying attended scene entities or localizing gaze targets in 3D space. In this work, we investigate gaze graphs as a scene-level representation of visual attention that connects clinicians to attended scene entities, and evaluate their utility for fine-grained surgical action recognition and semantic scene graph (SSG) generation. Methods: We extend the public EgoExOR dataset with gaze annotations to create EgoExOR-Gaze, the first benchmark for multi-view gaze graph generation in the OR. We propose a novel architecture that jointly estimates 3D gaze targets and predicts attended entities using only exocentric camera views. Specifically, our approach exploits 3D gaze geometry to sample local visual features across multiple views for attended entity prediction. We further utilize predicted gaze graphs for fine-grained surgical action recognition and SSG generation. Results: For gaze graph generation, our model outperforms both exocentric-only and combined exocentric-egocentric baselines. For surgical action recognition, our model incorporating predicted gaze graphs outperforms both exocentric and egocentric baselines across all evaluated metrics, with particularly strong gains in temporal metrics. For SSG generation, incorporating gaze graphs improves macro-F1 from 0.42 to 0.52 compared to exocentric inputs alone, although performance remains below that achieved with egocentric inputs. Conclusion: Our results demonstrate the value of gaze graphs for surgical action recognition and SSG generation, highlighting the importance of explicitly modeling scene-level visual attention even when egocentric views are unavailable.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28901) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28901)

## 327. Controlled One-Click Benchmarking and Post-Fusion Recalibration for Support-Conditioned Medical Segmentation

- **作者**：Wang-Chi-Shiun, Wei-Huan Hu, Liu-En-Chi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at the European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026)

Sparse-prompt medical segmentation is hard to compare when prompts, supports, thresholds, and splits differ. This paper uses a controlled one-click protocol: each test image receives one positive foreground click, training-only support, validation-only checkpoint selection, and no test-time threshold tuning. We propose {Support-Aware Recalibration (SAR)}, a late post-fusion recalibration block for MultiverSeg that combines large-kernel spatial gating with squeeze-and-excitation channel recalibration after support--query fusion. BUSI, NerveUS-BP-present, and Kvasir-SEG are evaluated with matched 697 foreground-present sample budgets, shared splits/clicks, and threshold-free Dice-AUC/IoU-AUC. Compared with a protocol-matched raw-support MultiverSeg baseline, the main SAR configuration shows favorable mean Dice-AUC/IoU-AUC performance on BUSI, NerveUS-BP-present, and Kvasir-SEG, while reducing logged training time in this implementation. These controlled results indicate that late post-fusion recalibration is useful under a leakage-controlled one-click protocol.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28890) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28890)

## 328. Eidetic Code World Model

- **作者**：Wenqing Tian, Zijian Zhang, Qiang Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://gabrielinger.github.io/Eidetic-Code-World-Model/

Executable code can preserve a world's structural layout, yet video renderers frequently alter the appearance of previously visited places once they leave the camera view. We present Paintress, a code-conditioned world model that couples an executable geometric proxy with an independently parameterized 3D Gaussian appearance memory in a shared metric coordinate frame. While the proxy enforces structural and visibility constraints, 3D Gaussians capture the visual realization synthesized by emitted observations. To balance stable incremental fusion with consistent readout, Paintress separates writing from reading: newly emitted frames update a persistent raw Gaussian map under confidence-gated selection, whereas a cloned reader copy is globally refined against the observation archive and rendered with coverage masks to condition generation. We also introduce Expedition-33, a benchmark for generated-appearance retention across 11 static scenes, five route families, and 15, 30, and 60 s target absences (165 settings per system). Excluded from the opening image, targets are synthesized during a shared prefix and scored against each system's own first visit at matched return cameras. Across ten systems (1,650 videos), Paintress achieves 0.1220 ROI-LPIPS, 0.0613 MAE, and 52.12% detail retention (vs. 0.2731, 0.1401, and 10.91% for CWM), with lower discrepancy at 60 s (0.1630) than CWM, AgentGarten, and Lyra 2.0 achieve at 15 s.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28878) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28878)

## 329. SENSA: Visual-to-Brain Generation with Individualized Subject-State Transfer and Adaptation

- **作者**：Yihang Zou, Chunmei Qing, Ruixi Liang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Includes supplementary material

Visual-to-brain encoding predicts neural responses to visual stimuli, supporting visual cognition research and neural decoding. However, challenges in disentangling shared from image-specific activity, together with inter-subject distribution shifts and representational heterogeneity, limit accurate encoding and cross-subject generalization with limited calibration data. We introduce SENSA, a structured continuous neural response generation framework that separates shared activity from image-dependent responses and calibrates a compact subject state. An independent response prior captures shared activity, while complementary visual descriptors drive separable sensor-time bases and a shared Response Refiner. Subject-Adaptive Factor Transport adjusts the spatiotemporal expression of shared bases, while Source-Grounded Residual Memory predicts image-dependent corrections in source-response coordinates. Together, they personalize a frozen generator using limited paired support. Experiments demonstrate that the generation backbone outperforms recent generators across three datasets, with image-identity perturbations confirming stimulus specificity. Using only 400 paired images, electroencephalography (EEG) and the Natural Scenes Dataset (NSD) calibration achieve residual Pearson correlations of 0.1091 and 0.2995, respectively, demonstrating effective brain-response generation and cross-subject adaptation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28872) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28872)

## 330. KineState-SWAM: A Surgical World Action Model with Explicit Kinematics and Causal Interaction Dynamics

- **作者**：Rulin Zhou, Yujie Ma, Rui Tang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Existing surgical world models often leave implicit how instrument motion drives localized tissue responses and how interaction history informs subsequent actions. We introduce KineState-SWAM, a surgical world-action model coupling action prediction and video generation through a persistent interaction state, separating kinematically determined instrument motion from learned scene evolution. A flow-matching policy predicts joint trajectories conditioned on interaction state, robot configuration, and task instruction. Kinematic Geometry Conditioning converts these trajectories into surface-motion tokens and spatially aligned instrument renderings using calibrated URDF kinematics. Causal Interaction Dynamics recurrently updates the state through geometry-guided attention and motion-aligned feature transport, carrying interaction history into subsequent action predictions without intermediate video synthesis. The State-Geometry Adapter combines state-dependent spatial modulation with localized geometric residuals to guide video generation. Interaction-aware supervision emphasizes tissue responses near instrument working regions. We introduce dVRK-Chole5.2k, comprising 5,255 videos spanning 17 surgical tasks. Experiments demonstrate improved action accuracy and video fidelity over evaluated baselines within matched action-conditioning settings. Ablations support preserving and transporting interaction states for long-horizon prediction.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28871) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28871)

## 331. CivilCADBench: Benchmarking Multimodal LLMs for Understanding, Reconstructing, and Designing Civil Engineering Drawing Sets

- **作者**：Zelin Zheng, Difei Wu, Xinyan Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.SE
- **备注**：Project page: https://agenticivil.com/civilcadbench/

Civil infrastructure is designed, built, and maintained through engineering drawing sets, yet preparing and revising them remains labour-intensive and calls for automation. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are promising candidates, but assessing them requires a benchmark grounded in real engineering practice. We introduce CivilCADBench, a benchmark for understanding, reconstructing, and designing civil engineering drawing sets, built from real infrastructure drawings and source CAD files containing over 250,000 CAD entities. Unlike parametric 3D CAD models, these drawings are dense, layered, and multi-sheet; and since prints are lossy projections with no pixel trace of layers, a file can print correctly yet remain wrongly organised. CivilCADBench comprises three task families of increasing difficulty: understanding tasks are evaluated with 2,183 expert-reviewed questions ranging from single sheets to whole sets, while reconstruction and design tasks are agentic, requiring models to rebuild printed pages as editable CAD files, either as single pages or entire drawing sets, and to produce new designs. These tasks are performed across 232 pages, 28 sets, and 19 design tasks, and evaluated by a deterministic scorer that compares model outputs with the designers' source files on geometry, layers, text, and dimensions. We evaluate seven frontier MLLMs on CivilCADBench, and find that they read and redraw printed content accurately but recover the file structure poorly: the best model scores above 90% in understanding and single-page reconstruction, yet only 60% on layer assignment and 48% on design. Grounded in real engineering practice, CivilCADBench exposes both the capabilities and the limitations of frontier models in professional civil engineering, laying a foundation for next-generation models and agentic systems for intelligent civil engineering.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28853) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28853)

## 332. MQCODet: Modality Quality-Guided Collaborative Optimization for UAV-Based RGB-T Tiny Pedestrian Detection

- **作者**：Hui Li, Jianbo Ma, Hui Luo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-based RGB-thermal (RGB-T) remote sensing provides complementary visual and thermal information for pedestrian perception under complex aerial observation conditions. However, detecting tiny pedestrians in UAV-based RGB-T imagery remains challenging due to cross-modal spatial heterogeneity, manifested as modality quality imbalance and spatial position misalignment. Existing RGB-T detectors primarily address these challenges at the feature fusion stage, without establishing an effective coupling between feature fusion and object localization, potentially resulting in suboptimal detection performance. To address this limitation, we introduce a Modality Quality-Guided Collaborative Optimization Detector (MQCODet), which extends modality quality from a fusion prior to a localization supervision cue. Specifically, we propose a Dynamic Calibration and Region-Aware Fusion (DCRF) module that progressively refines cross-modal spatial alignment and performs region-aware fusion to generate reliable multi-scale features and a scene-level modality quality prior. We further design an Efficient Contextual Interaction (ECI) encoder to enhance multi-scale feature interaction while preserving fine-grained spatial details. Moreover, we propose a Modality-Aware Adaptive (MAA) loss that incorporates the modality prior and object scale to adaptively balance spatial consistency and geometric similarity for localization supervision. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MQCODet surpasses state-of-the-art methods on two UAV-based RGB-T tiny pedestrian detection benchmarks while achieving real-time inference on the edge platform.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28850) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28850)

## 333. Learning How a Body Moves: Few-Shot Body Inference for Visual World Models of Animated Characters

- **作者**：Amarnath R
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.GR, cs.LG
- **备注**：28 pages, 19 figures, 8 tables

Two visually identical animated characters can respond differently to the same action command because their body dynamics differ. For example, a stiff blob and its bouncy twin reach different heights when both receive the jump command. Given only the first frame and the action command, a visual world model has no information to distinguish these twins, because their initial appearances are identical. We therefore propose an in-context world model that encodes clips of a character's other actions into a body representation, using a contrastive loss to separate look-alike characters. We introduce ToonTwins, a benchmark of 144 2D cartoon characters that run, jump, fall, turn and crouch. Its characters form look-alike groups of four that differ only in their dynamics profile, and we also evaluate the model on 32 animated animals from DeformingThings4D. Even with a body code from these clips, the latent pixel world model we tested recovered at most 25 percent of the twins' jump height difference. On new shapes, our model identified the correct twin in 85.9 percent of cases, compared with 76.7 percent for a transformer baseline that reads the same clips without a body summary, and in a controlled ablation the contrastive loss gave more than half of this gain. On the animated animals, it reduced the mean path prediction error by 8.5 percent against a model without body information.

## 334. Task-Conditioned Detail Allocation for Synthetic Segmentation Data

- **作者**：Chunming He, Dingming Zhang, Jifan Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：10 pages, 6 figures, 10 tables

Synthetic images sharing a segmentation label can differ in their training value. We propose Task-Conditioned Detail Allocation (TCDA) to select useful candidates and locally edit some remaining images. We ask whether image details provide class-specific cues in the regions marked by the label. We train a small task reference on frozen image features from real training images. We compare its class responses before and after blurring each candidate, and score how well these response changes align with the labeled regions. This detail allocation score ranks candidates from the same source. Comparing their response--label alignment with that of the real source image also guides repair decisions and, in multiclass tasks, the choice of regions to edit. Selected candidates and accepted repairs are used for training with the original labels and the unchanged downstream segmenter. Experiments on camouflaged object detection, degraded salient object detection, and adverse-condition semantic segmentation show that our selection outperforms equally sized random and label-agreement subsets. Combining selection and repair improves over real-only training by up to 0.019 in $S_α$ on COD10K and 2.86 mIoU points on ACDC.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28841) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28841)

## 335. Spatially Grounded Latent Lookahead for Vision-Language-Action Models

- **作者**：Qihang Chen, Tengjie Li, Hongwei Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO

Vision-language-action (VLA) models benefit from strong semantic representations that support object recognition and instruction understanding. However, precise manipulation also depends on resolving object locations and workspace relations, and on selecting actions with an estimate of how the scene will evolve. Existing geometry-aware methods make the current scene more explicit, while predictive methods model future observations or representations. Yet future information is often neither grounded in a control-relevant spatial state nor directly used to guide action generation. We introduce Spatially Grounded Latent Lookahead (SG-LL), which uses the grounded present as the reference for predicting an actionable near-future representation. A frozen spatial teacher first distills geometric priors into policy-connected spatial tokens. A current-conditioned bottleneck then learns a compact latent that captures future information relative to the present scene. This latent is predicted from the current policy context and initial Action Expert features, without access to future observations or the spatial teacher. Finally, zero-initialized residual fusion incorporates the predicted latent into the Action Expert to refine action generation. Experiments on LIBERO-Plus, CALVIN, and three real-world manipulation tasks demonstrate improvements over in both simulation and real-robot settings.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28839) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28839)

## 336. Omni-H3-Image: Constrained Foundation Priors Usher in a New Era of All-in-One Blind Image Restoration

- **作者**：Zihao He, Songhua Liu
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：19 pages, 13 figures, 7 tables. Code: https://github.com/RedRoman/Omni-H3

Off-the-shelf unified image models such as 33B MiniMax-H3-Image, FLUX.1-Kontext, and Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 synthesize crisp textures yet collapse to 11.33-16.74 dB PSNR across 429 degraded public restoration scenes due to spatial drift, chromatic hallucination, and semantic fabrication. We present Omni-H3-Image (Omni-H3), an approximate three-stage constrained posterior projection framework over unmodified MiniMax-H3-Image and Qwen3-VL-32B using only a 0.92M-parameter auxiliary weather pre-conditioner (<0.003% of backbone weights). Omni-H3 unifies blind physics-VLM sensing with exact-32 px lattice anchoring, joint affine-Wiener-Butterworth projection in opponent YCbCr space, and closed-loop Z=4 text verification with sub-Nyquist abstention. Across 925 blind tasks against 11 external SOTA baselines -- five public benchmarks (N=647), Dev-160, and a held-out locked_test suite (N=118) -- Omni-H3 recovers +4.73 dB degraded-scene PSNR over Base H3 (21.47 vs. 16.74 dB; +6.27-10.14 dB over other omni backbones) while winning overall #1 DISTS (0.207), #1 NIQE (4.45), and #1 Edge-F1 (0.807) on all 429 degraded public scenes (MUSIQ 66.8 vs. 40-44 for discriminative SOTA), 76.59 dB on CBSD68 Clean, -91.6% Urban100 chroma drift, #1 2K Dev Non-Clean DISTS (0.202), and #1 locked_test 2K DISTS (0.173) and 132-ROI SubCER, cutting pre-annotated 132-ROI SubCER from 0.456 to 0.319 under non-Qwen RapidOCR (PP-OCRv4) (with recognizer-dependent tradeoffs under TrOCR-large-str).

## 337. PointNeXt Adapter Improved Model for BIM Component Defect Association under Construction Images and Design Model Attributes

- **作者**：Na Yu
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：13 pages, 8 figures

Construction site defect analysis increasingly requires linking field evidence to the correct BIM object, because a crack, spalling area, missing sleeve or misaligned support is useful for quality control only when it is connected with component identity, location, responsibility and design intent. This paper proposes a PointNeXt Adapter improved model for BIM component defect association under construction images and design model attributes. The method aligns site image crops, local point cloud patches, BIM component identifiers, geometry attributes, material categories, installation stage, inspection distance, viewpoint quality and source reliability tags into component level inspection windows. PointNeXt learns local geometry and deviation from as built point evidence, while the adapter injects BIM attributes so the same geometric irregularity can be interpreted differently for structural, envelope, MEP and embedded components. The fusion head outputs component link, defect class, defect probability, confidence, dominant evidence and recommended review action. A synthetic multisource BIM inspection dataset is used to evaluate repeated layouts, occlusion, sparse point clouds, registration errors and unseen component arrangements. Results show that attribute aware point learning improves association accuracy, defect recall and confidence calibration compared with image only, point only, simple fusion and adapter free baselines. The framework is positioned as traceable BIM issue review support, requiring manual confirmation and staged field validation before operational deployment.

## 338. ViTrust: Learning 3D Observation Trust for Deformable Physical World Models

- **作者**：Zhihao Guo, Zhihao Li, Xu Ge et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：28 pages, 6 figures, 7 tables, including appendix

Learning deformable physical world models requires dynamics that generalize beyond observed motion. Accurate reconstruction alone does not ensure accurate future prediction, motivating us to learn how observations should influence physical identification. However, visual reliability alone does not determine how informative an observation is about physical dynamics. We propose ViTrust to learn 3D observation trust, determining how strongly each tracked point influences physical identification. Its key design is a visual-contact trust adapter that combines pointwise spatiotemporal evidence with interaction history on a persistent contact graph to predict time-varying observation weights. A single-stage, shared-forward objective jointly optimizes these weights and physical parameters through weighted geometry and correspondence losses, with constraints to prevent observation-weight collapse. All learning uses only the observed prefix. The fitted physics remains fixed for future prediction under supplied controls. Experiments on real-world sequences demonstrate improved future prediction over the evaluated baselines, alongside qualitative demonstrations of novel-control rollouts and cross-object transfer.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28815) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28815)

## 339. RadAG: Reward-Adversarial Attention Guidance for Efficient Inference-Time Alignment of Diffusion Models

- **作者**：Hyeongchan Jeon, Kwanyoung Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.CV
- **备注**：25 pages, 13 figures, 14 tables

Diffusion and flow-based models have achieved remarkable success in generation, yet their samples often remain suboptimal in fidelity and text alignment. Two lines of inference-time methods address this: extrapolation guidance, such as classifier-free guidance (CFG) and its variants, and reward guidance, which aligns samples with reward models of human preference. Extrapolation guidance needs only forward passes, but it builds its reference by heuristic rules unrelated to the reward and therefore cannot steer sampling toward a given reward. Gradient-based reward guidance steers sampling directly toward a higher reward, but backpropagates through the entire generator at every step. This trade-off raises the question of whether extrapolation guidance can steer sampling toward a higher reward without a full backward pass. To answer this, we introduce Reward-Adversarial Attention Guidance (RadAG), which constructs the reference by perturbing the attention logits of a single block against their reward gradient and extrapolates away from it. We prove that this update increases the reward of the clean estimate to first order, while its backward pass stops at the perturbed block instead of traversing the entire generator. On FLUX.2-klein-4B and 9B with PickScore and aesthetic rewards, RadAG achieves the highest target reward in every setting with 30-65% less time than gradient-based and candidate-based baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28809) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28809)

## 340. Not All Directions Are Flat: Decomposed-Gradient Flatness for Sparse-View 3D Gaussian Splatting

- **作者**：Ungbae Yoon, Kwanyoung Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：19 pages, 13 figures, 11 tables

Many applications of novel view synthesis, such as augmented reality and robotics, can afford only a few input views, since dense capture is costly. With such sparse views, however, 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) overfits the training views and degrades at unseen viewpoints. Among the approaches to this problem, flat-minima optimization treats the Gaussians as trainable parameters and steers training toward flat minima, which generalize better, by perturbing the Gaussians with random noise or along the training gradient. However, these perturbations ignore their direction relative to the other training views. Interestingly, we find that the direction of the perturbation determines whether it improves generalization. Specifically, we decompose the gradient of each training view into a component parallel to the average gradient of all views, on which the views agree, and an orthogonal component, on which they disagree. Perturbing along the orthogonal component improves generalization, whereas perturbing along the parallel component does not. We further find that the flatness correction often opposes the reconstruction gradient, shrinking or even reversing the reconstruction step. Based on these findings, we propose DecoFlat, which perturbs each Gaussian along the orthogonal component together with random noise. Furthermore, we introduce a conflict-aware correction, which removes the part of the flatness correction that opposes the reconstruction gradient, and a rescheduling of the spherical-harmonics color, which suppresses the overfitting of view-dependent appearance. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DecoFlat, as a plug-in optimizer, consistently improves vanilla 3DGS and a wide range of sparse-view 3DGS methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28802) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28802)

## 341. HighwayVerse: A Chain-of-Inspection Benchmark for Highway Infrastructure Anomaly Understanding

- **作者**：Junyi Yang, Zheng Si, Yilin Zhai et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL

Practical highway infrastructure inspection entails progressive diagnostic reasoning from visual perception to maintenance decisions, yet existing benchmarks remain confined to isolated tasks, leaving Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) insufficiently evaluated across sequential inspection stages. Meanwhile, current datasets focus primarily on highway pavement defects, falling short of real-world inspection across diverse infrastructure components such as highway pavements, guardrails, glare screens, and drainage systems. To address these gaps, we formulate the Highway Infrastructure Inspection Progression Chain (HIIP-Chain), spanning Anomaly Detection, Anomaly Recognition, Condition Analysis, and Maintenance Resolution, and introduce HighwayVerse. HighwayVerse comprises 14,143 real-world Ground and Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) inspection records spanning 100 and 17 anomaly categories, respectively, with 84,858 expert-reviewed question-answer pairs. We evaluate 12 open-source and closed-source MLLMs using the chained metric Final, which credits a correct maintenance decision only when all preceding diagnostic stages are completed correctly. Results show that although models achieve relatively high accuracy when initially judging whether an anomaly is present, their markedly lower Final scores expose struggles across downstream recognition, analysis, and decision-making. This discrepancy indicates that current MLLMs do not fully comprehend specific highway anomaly patterns beyond superficial initial judgments. Furthermore, we propose LaneRAG, a training-free retrieval-augmented framework that provides hierarchical reference guidance achieving consistent performance improvements across diverse MLLMs. Our dataset, evaluation benchmark, and source code will be publicly released.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28800) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28800)

## 342. NavDecision: Parallel Multi-Step Action Prediction with Persistent Self-Referential Learning for Vision-and-Language Navigation

- **作者**：Kai Wu, Yufan Hu, Quan Fang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Vision-and-language navigation (VLN) requires timely decisions based on instructions and visual observations. However, autoregressive VLN models generate action descriptions token by token, introducing substantial inference overhead. Decision models offer an efficient alternative but face challenges in maintaining short-horizon action coherence and long-horizon goal alignment. To address these challenges, we propose NavDecision, an efficient multi-step VLN decision model. We introduce parallel multi-step action prediction (PMAP) to predict multiple action distributions from a shared vision-language state, and memory-augmented self-referential policy optimization (M-SRPO) to learn from unsuccessful trajectories using successful trajectories as references. A cross-iteration success memory further accumulates historical successful trajectories to provide informative training feedback. NavDecision achieves success rates of 64.60% on R2R-CE and 64.57% on RxR-CE with a 4B backbone. Compared with existing autoregressive VLN methods, it delivers significant inference speedups of up to $22.28\times$ and $18.96\times$ while maintaining comparable navigation performance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28796) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28796)

## 343. Inference Dynamics: Image Classifiers Keep Using Visual Cues After They Are Removed

- **作者**：Ruiqi Wang, Mingming Lu
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Image classifiers are typically evaluated by their final predictions, treating inference as a static image-to-label mapping. Reliability, however, also depends on whether a model can relinquish evidence after that evidence disappears. We reveal a counterintuitive phenomenon: removing a local visual cue from its original location may not remove its influence on the prediction. Once captured by early layers, the cue can become distributed across later representations, making ``no longer visible'' different from ``no longer relied upon.'' We introduce Inference Dynamics, a process-level framework for tracing how cue influence evolves and remains withdrawable across network depth. Given an image and a matched cue-removed reference, we replace cue-aligned states in the original branch with their reference counterparts at successive layers and measure recovery toward the cue-free prediction. The resulting trajectory separates evidence locality from decision-level withdrawability. Across datasets and architectures, we find locality declines before withdrawability collapses: cue-induced information becomes less concentrated at its original support while its effect remains active, before entering an evidence lock-in stage. Consequently, fixed late-stage correction may alter the final readout without removing accumulated cue dependence. We therefore propose Evidence Reset, which selects an input-dependent layer before lock-in and reconstructs cue-aligned states toward their cue-free counterparts. Our results establish evidence withdrawability as a process-level dimension of classifier reliability and suggest a new intervention principle: correction depth should follow the dynamics of evidence rather than be fixed in advance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28795) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28795)

## 344. STAC++: Plug-and-Play Adaptive Spatio-Temporal Aware Cache Compression for Streaming 3D Reconstruction

- **作者**：Boyi Zhu, Youcheng Cai, Dong Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.GR, cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables

Streaming 3D reconstruction must preserve geometry under limited memory, yet STAC's fixed-resolution spatial key-value (KV) cache grows as scenes expand. We propose STAC++, a plug-and-play, training-free framework that compresses and reuses spatial memory for long-term reconstruction in unbounded scenes. Adaptive Spatial KV Cache (ASKC) organizes KV states into an octree with adaptive resolutions across regions and layers. Response-Aware Cache Compression (RACC) guides KV merging by measuring attention-output changes, balancing storage savings with geometric preservation. Temporal Context Refinement (TCR) reuses cached intermediate features and recomputes only a short backbone suffix to refine historical predictions with later observations. Integrated into four pretrained streaming backbones, STAC++ reduces spatial memory consumption while maintaining competitive reconstruction quality on long sequences and large scenes, with further quality gains from refinement at limited computational overhead. The code will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28793) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28793)

## 345. ArgsWorld: Unifying 3DGS Scene Reconstruction, Generation and Extrapolation with Autoregressive Diffusion Transformer

- **作者**：Weiming Chen, Shuaiyan Liu, Xinyu Shao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Autoregressive diffusion world models deliver strong video generation performance, yet they typically output only RGB frames rather than explicit 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) scene representations, creating a gap between learned visual world dynamics and usable 3D assets for downstream robotics, simulation and rendering. To bridge this gap, we introduce ArgsWorld, an autoregressive diffusion model that also produces an explicit 3DGS scene. For each generation chunk, the diffusion transformer jointly processes clean historical image latents and noisy latents for new views. It keeps the historical image latents fixed and refreshes the scene features so that the Gaussian representation can be revised as new regions come into view. Our 3D Self-Forcing Distillation trains the model on its own generated histories using a mixture of single-view and multi-view data. Distribution matching supervises direct image latents and latents of Gaussian renderings at both generation and additional cameras. ArgsWorld unifies 3D scene reconstruction from posed images, scene generation from image or text prompts, and scene extrapolation across camera segments with disconnected endpoints. Experimental results demonstrate that ArgsWorld achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple 3D scene modeling tasks. Project page: https://m4a1tastegood.github.io/ArgsWorld/

## 346. 3DCodeFactory: Scaling Training Data for 3D Code Agents

- **作者**：Zhaoyu Wang, Xueru Wen, XingYu Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

As code agents evolve, their scope is expanding from generating simple static 3D meshes to constructing structurally complete, complex, interactive 3D scenes. However, existing datasets mainly rely on converting or captioning pre-existing 3D assets, consisting mostly of single-turn code data for static single objects and struggling to scale task diversity and scene complexity, leaving such training data scarce. We present 3DCodeFactory, an automated framework that directly constructs diverse, complex 3D training data from abundant raw text (e.g., diffusion prompts and product descriptions). The framework first leverages an image generator to synthesize 3D reference images from raw text as visual anchors, guiding the generation of concrete modeling specifications and interactive functional requirements; meanwhile, it jointly constructs visual, specification, and functional rubrics from the reference images and requirements to automatically score and filter multi-turn agent rollouts. Based on this framework, we release 3DCodeFactory-Data, comprising 5,045 high-quality task instances and multi-turn interaction trajectories. Fine-tuning Qwen3.5-9B and Qwen3.8-27B on 3DCodeFactory-Data significantly improves performance on BlenderWorld, P3D-Bench, and 3DCodeBench, consistently outperforming models trained on existing open-source 3D code datasets, with our 27B model further surpassing the same-series 2.4T flagship Qwen3.8-Max in overall performance.

## 347. Where Does a Weight-Editing Agent Help? Blind Diagnosis and Repair of Deployment Faults in Vision Models

- **作者**：Zhuangzi Li, Shilv Cai, Jian Jin et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Language-model agents can read and edit the weights of neural networks without gradients, and this is increasingly presented as a new way of repairing models. We ask what such an agent adds, in a setting with a known answer. A vision model moved into a new system may be broken by a preprocessing, weight-conversion or output-convention fault; an agent restricted to forward passes must decide whether a fault exists, name it, and undo it by editing the weights. Over 183 runs on classification, segmentation and detection, scored against the fault-free model, we find three things. When the kind of fault is known, the agent is not needed: a script written afterwards repairs 30 of 48 faults against the agent's 33. But the agent did not have to be told: on five fault types outside every script it repairs 15 of 15 where that script repairs 4, and it never edits any of 21 fault-free models. And its limits are predictable: it needs a trustworthy per-layer reference for the model's activations and a fault that is an invertible structured transformation; without them, small faults are missed and repairs stay partial. We derive guidance on when to use an agent and when a script.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28781) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28781)

## 348. Imagine Smarter, Drive Faster: Efficient World Modeling for Decision-Aware Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Feiyang Jia, Lin Liu, Zhicheng Bao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.RO
- **备注**：23 pages total, 8 pages main text (4 figures, 8 tables), 12 pages supplementary material (9 figures, 5 tables)

World models hold great promise for autonomous driving planning, yet their ability to imagine the future often comes at a formidable computational cost. Efficiency efforts, such as knowledge distillation and domain-specific pre-training, often shift this burden to costly teachers or training pipelines, leaving lightweight world modeling an open challenge. We introduce FastWorld, a natively lightweight world-model framework for efficient autonomous driving planning. FastWorld reduces computation through compact scene representations, short-horizon action-conditioned prediction, and selective candidate verification and score refinement. These designs support efficient future-informed planning without teacher distillation or additional domain-specific pre-training. On NAVSIMv1, FastWorld achieves 92.2 PDMS at 132 FPS on a single 4090 GPU. It also achieves 89.4 EPDMS on NAVSIMv2 and an average L2 of 0.40 over 3 seconds at 17 FPS on nuScenes. Code and models can be found at https://github.com/MoyangSensei/FastWorld.

## 349. AdapToC: A Unified Framework for Multi-Task Visual Token Communication

- **作者**：Zhicheng Liu, Chenliang Wu, Zhouxiang Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.IT
- **备注**：13 pages, 8 figures

Pretrained discrete visual tokenizers supply compact latents for synthesis, recognition, and embodied perception, yet wireless token streaming remains fragile. Under fading and noisy pilots, hard cyclic redundancy check (CRC) erasures turn corrupted blocks into missing tokens while frozen decoders amplify edge errors, which can collapse unequal error protection (UEP) into a binary choice between strong coding and omission under severe fading. Soft bit posteriors break this collapse. We present AdapToC, a single-pass transceiver that restores multi-tier rate-distortion tradeoffs without altering tokenizer weights or differentiating through pixel decoders. We align codebook geometry to the channel via offline frequency-weighted Pseudo-Gray relabeling and Halton dispersion to contract bit and burst errors with zero overhead. Because soft recovery pulls intermediate rates onto the three-tier convex hull, our unit-gated AdaLN sender schedules protection tiers across channel budgets, while our pilot-calibrated receiver feeds factorized codebook posteriors into a ConvNeXt-attention restorer with CRC bypass. We co-train AdapToC solely in latent space via counterfactual utility estimation and codebook cross-entropy regularization. On identical VQGAN-f16 tokens, AdapToC outperforms TONIC and adaptive TokenCom+AMC, cuts multi-SNR Rayleigh LPIPS from 0.6551 to 0.3312 within the frozen-tokenizer PSNR ceiling, and saves 43.8% Rician-K6 symbols at matched quality. Our single-pass restorer runs 8.3x faster than iterative resampling, raises accuracy while accelerating decoder-free recognition 20.6x, and improves fidelity plug-and-play across TiTok, VAR, and zero-shot NuPlan.

## 350. A high-speed and high-resolution 3D velocity map imaging system with a neuromorphic camera

- **作者**：Sulaiman Abubakar, Blessed Oguh, Akaninyene Udoikono et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：physics.ins-det, cs.CV, nucl-ex, physics.chem-ph

An improved camera-based 3D-velocity map imaging (VMI) system was demonstrated with a neuromorphic camera. We show a machine vision event-driven camera can achieve an unprecedented latency of 2 microseconds when detecting single ion/electron with a microchannel plate imaging detector, which boosts the speed of the momentum imaging system to more than 100 kHz, while the system with a conventional frame camera is limited to a few kHz. The high spatial resolution of the camera offers additional performance boost while maintaining excellent multi-hit capability and a high time resolution.

## 351. JevNavAgent: A Single-Forward Visual Navigation Agent with Trajectory-Level RL for Gigapixel Pathology

- **作者**：Yonghuang Wu, Zhenyang Liang, Pengfei Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Gigapixel whole-slide images (WSIs) exceed single-pass vision-language model (VLM) capacity at cellular resolution, while multiple instance learning relies on query-agnostic offline patching without interactive reasoning. Multi-step VLM navigation agents inspect regions on demand, yet face two bottlenecks: (1) autoregressive action decoding incurs heavy latency, throttling exploration and online multi-trajectory RL; and (2) offline distillation suffers from an existential-versus-universal stopping asymmetry between positive detection and negative exclusion, inducing thumbnail and kinematic shortcuts while leaving policies prone to passing metastases without stopping. Under the Jev (System One) paradigm, a model predicts distributions over predefined decision spaces in one forward pass (0 generated tokens) while deterministic code executes navigation. We build JevNavAgent, a 2B-parameter agent combining factorized stopping, spatial-candidate, and contextual-bilinear answer heads with overview modality dropout. We pair Stage-1 offline counterfactual trajectory alignment (mitigating depth and panning shortcuts) with Stage-2 online trajectory-level GRPO (Traj-GRPO), using decoding-free rollouts to optimize branch-level lesion discovery, in-polygon stopping, and step-wise confidence gain. On Clean Visual WSI-VQA, JevNavAgent achieves 51.57% multi-scale (50.43% terminal-step) overall and 51.39% focal accuracy, outperforming frontier VLM agents and 4B-11B open-source cascades while running nearly 12x faster than frontier APIs (5.63s vs. 66.64-70.04s). On CAMELYON16 Official Test, Traj-GRPO reaches 22.45% localization (converting 11/12, or 91.7%, of visited tumors into grounded stops) with 93.75% specificity and 69.77% slide accuracy, surpassing Stage-1 SFT under matched stopping rules alongside zero-shot budget scaling.
