## 385. OKB-CFM: Observation-Keyed Barycentric Conditional Flow Matching for Unpaired Sparse-to-Dense Cardiac Shape Completion

- **作者**：Kangneng Zhou, Shuang Song, Yitian Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 8 figures

Completing cardiac segmentations from sparse cine magnetic resonance labels is challenging because acquired planes do not uniquely determine the underlying anatomy and patient-matched dense annotations are rarely available. We propose observation-keyed barycentric conditional flow matching for completion between clinically unpaired cohorts. Independently trained variational autoencoders represent sparse observations and dense shapes. A fixed memory associates simulated sparse keys with their source dense encodings, enabling candidate retrieval within the sparse latent space. A matching network aggregates the retrieved values into a query-dependent barycentric endpoint, toward which an endpoint-conditioned flow transports the sparse encoding. Individual candidate values provide alternative completion endpoints. On 327 real unpaired inputs, the method obtains encoder-feature Fréchet and squared maximum mean discrepancy scores of 7.93 and 2.02 after scaling by 10,000 and 10,000,000, respectively, corresponding to reductions of 47.0\% and 94.2\% relative to contrastive unpaired translation. On a 34-file simulated test partition, evaluation against dense-VAE-reconstructed references yields a five-class mean Dice of 0.889, squared Chamfer distance of 6.15 square voxels, 95th-percentile surface distance of 3.88 voxels, and average symmetric surface distance of 1.50 voxels. These results characterize cohort-level feature agreement, observed-label consistency, and reconstruction under the stated reference construction. By complementing routine cine-based functional assessment with inferred multi-chamber anatomical context, this framework provides a basis for investigating how chamber morphology and spatial relationships relate to cardiac function.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28600) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28600)

## 386. When Fog Becomes a Weapon: Atmospheric Attacks on Visible-Infrared Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Chengyin Hu, Dehe Zhao, Dingyi Lu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 8 figures. Supplementary material (23 pages) is available as an ancillary file

Existing adversarial attacks on vision-language models (VLMs) and cross-spectral perception primarily rely on conspicuous, unnatural artifacts, such as vivid adversarial patches or active light beams, that lack physical stealthiness, whereas prior weather attacks largely overlook how one atmosphere simultaneously affects heterogeneous sensors. This raises a key scientific question: can a purely natural, shared fog environment simultaneously disrupt complementary visible and thermal infrared cues without artificial surface modifications? We introduce Fog Manipulation Attack (FMA), a zero-gradient attack that optimizes a common 3D fog state to induce simultaneous semantic failures in visible-infrared VLMs. FMA links shared, depth- and layer-aware atmospheric propagation along scene paths with band-specific imaging physics---visible airlight scattering and thermal path radiance---allowing one realistic fog state to erode both modalities' distinct cues. Experiments show that FMA more than doubles simultaneous failure rates over fidelity-matched random fog, transfers to captioning and VQA, and removes cross-modal compensation, where restoring a clean companion view recovers 64%--74% of lost dual-input VQA answers.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28592) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28592)

## 387. CAR3T: Generalizable Calibration-Guided Reference-View Attention Reuse for Multi-View 3D Transformers

- **作者**：Lan Wei, Haosong Peng, Sicheng Tian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Feed-forward multi-view 3D reconstruction relies on global attention for cross-view aggregation, but the attention cost grows quadratically with the number of views, making inference increasingly expensive for larger inputs. Existing acceleration methods compress tokens or sparsify attention, potentially losing geometric detail or restricting cross-view aggregation while leaving redundancy among attention outputs underexploited. We observe similar cross-view attention distributions in certain layers, revealing an opportunity to reduce repeated global aggregation through output reuse. However, the layers suitable for reuse vary with the input, and reusing one layer alters the features received by later layers. Similarity at individual layers therefore cannot characterize the combined effect of multiple reuse decisions on output drift. To address these challenges, we propose CAR3T, a training-free framework combining reference-view attention output reuse with offline risk calibration and online dynamic layer selection. Calibration models cumulative reuse risk, while online selection adapts reuse to each input under a calibrated budget. CAR3T retains all input tokens and attends to the keys and values of all views, reducing the core attention computation in selected layers from quadratic to linear. Experiments on VGGT, DA3, and $π^3$ show forward speedups up to $1.41\times$ with at most 100 views and $1.75\times$ on longer sequences, with accuracy trade-offs across backbones and datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28591) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28591)

## 388. CLOSE: A Calibrated Label-free Online Self-Evaluating Verifier for Object Hallucination in Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Zhaoxi Wang, Jiahao Lai
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL
- **备注**：41 pages (8-page main text and appendix), 25 figures, 24 tables

Vision-language models frequently describe objects absent from the image because of language priors. Yet the model itself is already a strong judge of its own output: asked separately whether an object is present, it rejects 44%-62% of its hallucinations, and its yes probability reaches an AUROC of 0.90-0.95 without training. This judgment, however, is not directly usable: (1) its probabilities lack a common scale across models; (2) for the remaining 38%-56%, the model still answers yes with probabilities that overlap those of real objects, so no threshold separates them. We therefore propose CLOSE, a Calibrated Label-free Online Self-Evaluating verifier that enforces this judgment during generation. Its threshold is a quantile of a memory rather than a probability and thus depends only on ranks; the memory is cold-started from unlabeled images and updated online. A scorer self-trained on pseudo-labels from four hidden-state signals is fused with self-asking by rank to separate what self-asking leaves mixed. Refused objects are rolled back and banned through a laddered back-off, and a recognition table intercepts their reappearance under other wordings. On COCO, refusing 20% and 40% of object mentions reduces the CHAIRs of LLaVA-1.5-7B by 42% and 87% (F1 +1.9 and -6.1 points); refusing 20% and 35% reduces the reachable hallucinations of Qwen3-VL-8B (official counts minus CHAIR-script miscounts) by 79% and 96% (F1 +0.5 and -5.3). An external vocabulary further extends the check beyond the official vocabulary.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28589) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28589)

## 389. CLASP: Deciding Structure and Claims Before Decoding for Whole-Slide Pathology Report Generation

- **作者**：Ziaullah Khan, Rashadul Islam Sumon, Md Ariful Islam Mozumder et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL

Whole-slide pathology report generation has advanced through stronger slide encoders, retrieved knowledge, and, recently, slide-level multimodal LLMs. However, these generators leave every report-level choice to the decoder: how long the report is, how it is organised, and which findings it states and where. On the standard PathText-BRCA benchmark, we find that these choices dominate n-gram overlap, as reports from the query's hospital selected without learning approach the state of the art, while decoders under-generate and fall back to the most frequent findings. We present CLASP, a decide-then-decode generator that makes these choices explicitly, once per slide. Before decoding, an Exemplar Structure Prior (ESP) takes the sentence plan and length from reports of image-similar cases, a Calibrated Claim Decision (CCD) selects the findings from calibrated slide posteriors, and a Claim-to-Sentence Placement (CSP) assigns each finding to a planned sentence. A Plan-Conditioned Decoder (PCD), refined by structure-anchored sequence training, then writes the report under these decisions. With 35M parameters, CLASP outperforms retrained report generators and slide-level LLMs of up to 7B parameters, matches the strongest published results, and generalises to lung cancer from unseen hospitals. Its decisions change what the reports state, not only their length, transfer to an LLM decoder, and can withhold what H&E cannot show: withholding receptor status leaves 23% fewer contradictions with clinical records than the same network without decisions.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28576) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28576)

## 390. LoopSight: Looped Vision Transformers with Gated Prediction Memory

- **作者**：Zhaozhi Wang, Mingrui Wu, Jiaming Zhao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 10 figures

Looped vision transformers extend computation for visual reasoning by repeatedly applying a shared Transformer core. A challenge is to preserve useful prediction information across loop iterations while allowing the computation state to evolve. In this paper we introduce LoopSight, a looped vision transformer that uses Gated Prediction Memory (GPM) to maintain a persistent prediction state for readout, separate from the core's evolving computation state. At each iteration, a cross-attention gate uses the core's candidate features, the previous prediction state, and the current task state to control writes at the patch and channel level. The model decodes its prediction from the updated memory. We condition the shared core on the current iteration using asymptotic loop encoding and train the model by supervising its prediction at loop counts sampled from a truncated Gaussian distribution. LoopSight outperforms LoopViT across three model scales on ARC-AGI-1 and ARC-AGI-2. On ARC-AGI-1, LoopSight-Small achieves 67.4% pass@2, a gain of 6.6 percentage points over LoopViT-Small with comparable parameter counts and FLOPs. LoopSight also outperforms LoopViT on Visual Sudoku and ImageNet classification. Further analyses on ARC-AGI-1 show that LoopSight preserves more already-correct predictions and maintains more stable readout features as its computation state continues to evolve. The code will be released at https://github.com/feufhd/LoopSight.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28575) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28575)

## 391. MACE: Memory-Augmented Crop-Disease Evaluation of Frozen Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Tirtho Roy, Shreyan Ganguly, Muhammad Arbab Arshad et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.MA, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：7 pages, 6 figures, 3 tables. Project page: https://mace-bench.github.io/

In plant phenotyping, crop disease diagnosis has traditionally relied on expert assessment of visible symptoms and on models tailored to specific crops or diseases, and both are difficult to scale across field conditions. Recent methods provide frozen vision-language models with source-grounded knowledge bases that summarize the typical symptoms of each disease but describe little of how symptoms vary across individual cases. We introduce MACE (Memory-Augmented Crop-Disease Evaluation), a training-free method that supplements the knowledge base with a memory of symptom descriptions paired with known disease labels. MACE matches a description of the query image against this memory and retrieves only text descriptions and disease labels, so the query is the only image that the diagnosis model sees. Across ten crops, MACE with Claude Sonnet 5.5 improved accuracy over the knowledge-base baseline from 42.8% to 62.2% in distribution and from 50.7% to 57.0% on held-out image collections. Retrieved disease labels accounted for most of the gain, and adding the corresponding text descriptions improved in-distribution accuracy by about six percentage points.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28573) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28573)

## 392. WorldWeave: Rethinking Embodied Navigation through Cross-Task World Modeling

- **作者**：Xuan Yao, Jiahao Zhang, Rongjunchen Zhang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Mobile embodied agents continuously alter their observations through their own motion, while external dynamics further shape future interactions. Different mobile embodied tasks therefore capture complementary patterns of state evolution, yet jointly learning world dynamics across such interactions remains less explored. We investigate this problem through Vision-and-Language Navigation (VLN) and Embodied Visual Tracking (EVT), which respectively involve navigation toward a stationary goal and interaction with a moving target. We propose WorldWeave, a cross-task world model that couples shared predictive dynamics with task-specific adaptation. Instead of conditioning state transitions on heterogeneous physical actions, WorldWeave learns latent actions from observed state changes via inverse-forward dynamics, establishing a shared transition representation across tasks. The resulting future-state predictions are adaptively integrated into task-specific representations for downstream decision making. Experiments across multiple VLN and EVT benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements over specialized baselines.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28553) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28553)

## 393. Analyzing Atypical Local Features in Museum Artifacts using Generative Image Inpainting

- **作者**：Maho Kinoshita, Wakana Kuwata, Ryota Mibayashi et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：BESC 2026; 20 pages

This study proposes a method for analyzing local visual features in museum artifacts that may appear atypical or unexpected. Objects in the same category often share a similar overall shape, while local details such as patterns, decorations, and facial expressions may reflect regional or cultural characteristics. We use the appearance distribution learned by an image generation model as a reference for what is generally expected within an artifact category. For each target region, we exclude images from that region and fine-tune a generative model using only images from the remaining regions. We then mask selected parts of artifact images from the target region and ask the region-excluded model to complete them. If the completed result differs greatly from the original image, the masked part may contain features that are difficult to predict from the appearance learned from other regions. We measure this difference using LPIPS and treat it as reconstruction difficulty for the generative model. We conduct experiments on images of kokeshi dolls, drums, and masks from the collection of the National Museum of Ethnology. For each category, we select several target regions, build region-excluded LoRA models, and perform inpainting on predefined local parts. The results show that typical shapes and simple decorations are often reconstructed consistently, whereas some region-specific colors, patterns, cord arrangements, and painted details are replaced with more common appearances. These results suggest that region-excluded inpainting can serve as an exploratory tool for finding region-specific local visual features in museum artifact images.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28549) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28549)

## 394. Reconstruction-Guided Dual-Branch Learning for LiDAR Anomalous Object Detection

- **作者**：Guanyu Jiang, Junjie Cao, Jinshan Pan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：9 pages, 3 figures, 5 tables

Detecting unexpected objects is essential for autonomous driving safety. Most existing LiDAR anomaly detectors rely on semantic discrimination, which primarily captures category-discriminative features but may overlook deviations from broader feature relationships in normal scenes. Reconstruction offers a complementary perspective by assessing consistency with learned normal patterns. However, pointwise reconstruction errors alone struggle to distinguish anomalies from normal variability in complex outdoor point clouds. We propose Structured Residual Reasoning (SRR) to extract additional anomaly evidence from residual structure across feature channels, time sequences, and geometric space. We further establish that semantic discrimination and normal-pattern reconstruction capture fundamentally different yet complementary anomaly evidence: category compatibility and consistency with learned normal patterns, respectively. At matched background false-positive rates, SRR recovers more objects missed entirely by the semantic branch while producing fewer false positives in normal-only scenes. Building on this finding, we propose Reconstruction-Guided Dual-Branch (RGDB) learning, in which SRR-derived normal-pattern consistency cues guide a semantic-conditioned anomaly decoder to complement reconstruction-based predictions during training. At inference, the two branches jointly exploit their complementary anomaly evidence. Experiments show that standalone SRR establishes a strong reconstruction-based baseline without semantic labels from the target-scene training data, while RGDB achieves state-of-the-art performance on the STU validation set.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28545) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28545)

## 395. Centerline-Guided Multi-Class Road Surface Segmentation

- **作者**：Xu Ouyang, Lin Gan, Di Ma et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Road-surface extraction from satellite imagery supports geo- graphic mapping, but a binary mask leaves road axes, transverse extent, and junction structure implicit. Learning these proper- ties together is challenging because narrow corridors, merging roads, and occlusions require different geometric cues. We present a centerline-guided multi-class segmentation framework that makes these dependencies explicit. A hierarchical encoder sup- plies shared features, while predicted centerline features and soft probabilities condition specialized regular, intersection, and over- pass decoders. A two-component width branch supplies transverse geometry to regular-surface decoding, and learned feature fusion produces the complete road mask. Unlike independent prediction heads or a fixed union of component masks, this design exchanges geometric information during decoding while retaining separately inspectable outputs. Evaluation of the four-semantic-output con- figuration on CHN6-CUG achieves 65.53% complete-surface IoU, improving on a binary SegFormer-B4 baseline by 1.01 percentage points and reducing false-positive pixels by 11.94%. The results support geometry-aware surface refinement and a richer raster representation for road-map inspection.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28544) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28544)

## 396. Where the Image Sits: Vision-Text Order Bias in Jev

- **作者**：Youzhi Zhang
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Jev-style system-1 decision models read a choice from a single prefill. They restrict the next-token distribution to the supplied option labels and return a distribution over the options, with no generation and no parsing. Recent work shows that generative vision-language models are sensitive to the relative order of image and text tokens. We ask whether that sensitivity reaches this Jev-style readout. The answer is always read at the end of the prompt, so moving the image changes the distance from the visual evidence to the decision, but never the position at which the decision is read. Using four placements---prefix, pre-question, pre-options, and suffix---and holding the option listing fixed, we measure argmax flips, total variation, accuracy, calibration, and label marginals. Suffix placement flips 41.0% of decisions and lowers accuracy by 18.6 points. Averaged over the four slots, the curve is monotone, not U-shaped. That shape differs from prior results on generative vision-language models. The effect survives a battery of controls. We also introduce prefix--question averaging (P+Q), a two-forward readout that averages the prefix and pre-question distributions. It is at least as accurate as prefix and outperforms the prior baselines. Our code and data are publicly available at https://github.com/Youzhi333/Visual_Tex_Order_Bias_Jev.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28535) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28535)

## 397. Walks2Maps: Learning Indoor Geometry from Crowdsourced Wi-Fi RTT and Motion Trajectories

- **作者**：Enguang Fan, Binh Minh Tran, Fulin Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：This paper is accepted to NeurIPS 2026 PhysWorldAI Workshop

Everyday smartphone walks offer a scalable source of indoor geometry, but their motion traces drift and their radio measurements are distorted by multipath. We present Walks2Maps, a physics-guided framework that reconstructs a shared metric map from Wi-Fi round-trip-time (RTT) and motion observations, without a floor plan, surveyed access-point (AP) coordinates, or position labels. Walks2Maps jointly estimates pedestrian trajectories, AP locations, and per-trajectory stride scales, then recovers the pathway layout from the reconstructed paths. Shared AP observations connect walks recorded in different local frames, while metric ranges constrain their relative geometry and scale. An asymmetric range loss accommodates multipath-induced excess delay. On 22 phone trajectories collected along 11 routes on an office floor with 30 APs, Walks2Maps achieves median AP and trajectory errors of 0.87 m and 0.89 m, respectively, and a walked-area F1 score of 0.985. Ablations show that radio similarity can recognize places while still producing a distorted map; retaining the metric information in RTT resolves this ambiguity. Asymmetric modeling further reduces AP error by 54% relative to a symmetric loss. These results demonstrate how radio and motion measurements can jointly reveal indoor geometry without a dedicated mapping survey. We will release the dataset and toolkit.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28532) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28532)

## 398. VG3R: Video Generation Model for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction

- **作者**：Jingong Chen, Qingwen Zhang, Lin Lian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video generation models are widely used as 3D priors for novel view synthesis and 3D scene generation. Fine-tuned for perception, generation models also excel at dense prediction such as depth estimation. However, methods that apply video generation models to 3D reconstruction either predict depth without cameras or generate geometry and estimate cameras through many iterative denoising steps, which makes them slower. In this paper, we present $\textbf{VG3R}$, which turns a pretrained video generation model into a feed-forward 3D reconstruction model. Our key observation is that such a model already captures the depth and camera motion of a video, but not in the form required for reconstruction. We introduce two components: $\textit{clip-level shift prediction}$, which corrects depth distortion caused by the unknown shift in decoded inverse depth, and a $\textit{multi-layer camera head}$, which predicts per-frame cameras parameters by combining features across transformer blocks. Using Wan2.1 as its video backbone, VG3R reconstructs a scene and estimate the camera of every frame without iterative denoising. It achieves the lowest ATE and reconstruction errors among methods built on video generation models, runs about 4 and 14 times faster than Aether and Geo4D. VG3R also demonstrates competitive depth estimation and 3D reconstruction performance compared with geometry foundation models. Project page: https://github.com/KTH-RPL/VG3R

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28520) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28520)

## 399. AlpaSafe: Risk-Aware Trajectory Selection for Vision-Language-Action Models in Autonomous Driving

- **作者**：Qiren Wang, Mingyu Guo, Weisong Shi
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Reasoning-based driving vision-language-action (VLA) models propose multiple candidate trajectories at each decision, yet only one is executed. Standard evaluation scores the best candidate, assuming an oracle selector that deployment lacks, and leaves the risk of the executed candidate unexamined. Existing online evaluators typically close this gap with a purpose-trained scene or world model, often rolling out each candidate through it at inference time. We introduce AlpaSafe, a risk-aware trajectory selector that reuses the scene representation a frozen driving VLA computes while proposing its candidates. AlpaSafe reads the last hidden token of the VLA's prompt prefill. A lightweight head, trained on the closed-loop simulation outcomes of the candidates, fuses this token with each candidate trajectory and predicts a relative score and collision/offroad logits. A fixed rule removes candidates dominated on the two risk logits and selects the highest-scoring candidate among those remaining, adding neither rollouts nor VLA training. Experiments on 525 AlpaSim scenes show that AlpaSafe outperforms Alpamayo's default first-sample rule and uniform random selection in selected regret and top-1 agreement, and reduces the collision-at-fault or offroad rate from 6.41% and 6.10% to 4.93%. The selector has 1.7M parameters and takes 0.75 ms per decision.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28509) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28509)

## 400. State of Mind: Learning and Evaluating World Models of Naturalistic Brain Dynamics

- **作者**：Yuxiang Wei, Zundong Wu, Weiyu Sun et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CV

Understanding naturalistic brain activity requires going beyond the stimulus alone, as external inputs continuously interact with an individual's evolving brain state. A world model captures such dynamics in a latent state by inferring it from neural observations, predicting stimulus-driven evolution, and updating with new observations. However, existing brain world models fail to maintain a persistent latent state over time, and standard metrics of neural prediction accuracy cannot tell whether a model tracks an individual's brain state or relies solely on the stimulus. To directly test state inference, prediction, and updating, we introduce BrainStateBench, which evaluates a model's latent state through a frozen linear probe on whole-cortex, movie-driven fMRI. Its State task tests whether a model captures trial-to-trial variability in an individual's responses to repeated stimuli, while Forecast and Updating evaluate future activity predictions without and with new observations, respectively. To track individual brain state across time, we propose Spectator, a linear latent world model trained with a joint-embedding predictive objective. Spectator encodes short- and long-horizon observation histories into a two-timescale latent state and predicts state transitions linearly from the current state and stimulus. Stimulus-only encoders reach 0.320 in fMRI prediction correlation without observing the brain (0% on State), while prior deep brain world models reach 0.239 yet score at most 0.4% on State. In contrast, Spectator achieves 0.349 correlation alongside 5.5% on State, 16.0% on Forecast, and 20.3% on Updating, outperforming all baselines while forecasting over 20x faster than deep forecasting models.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28508) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28508)

## 401. Simple Baselines for Fetal Brain Unsupervised Anomaly Detection

- **作者**：Hongjia Yang, Mingxuan Liu, Yingqi Hao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.IV
- **备注**：Accepted for publication in the proceedings of the 2026 IEEE International Conference on Bioinformatics and Biomedicine (BIBM)

Unsupervised anomaly detection (UAD) offers a promising approach to fetal brain magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) analysis, where pathological data are scarce. Existing methods rely mainly on brain age prediction or image reconstruction, but are limited by noisy gestational age (GA) labels, lack of lesion localization, or poor cross-domain generalization. We propose FetalBUAD, a UAD framework based on voxel-level brain age estimation. A joint multi-task network predicts global brain age, voxel-level brain age maps, and brain segmentation to establish an internal biological brain age reference, reducing reliance on clinically recorded GA. Uncertainty-aware voxel-level brain age gap maps are then used for anomaly localization. Experiments on 1,317 internal fetal brain MRI volumes and an external benchmark demonstrate state-of-the-art anomaly detection performance and improved cross-domain generalization. FetalBUAD also generates anatomically interpretable maps that localize diverse fetal brain abnormalities. Our code is publicly available at: https://github.com/birthlab/FetalBUAD.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28505) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28505)

## 402. Open Vision Banana: An Open Framework for Unified Visual Perception through Generation in RGB Space

- **作者**：Xu Lin, Ruilin Yao, Haoran Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Vision Banana showed that a closed image generator, instruction-tuned to paint perception outputs as RGB images, becomes a generalist vision model. Does this carry over to \emph{open} generators, and if not, what fails? We answer with \textbf{Open Vision Banana (OVB)}, an open framework that adapts the 7B diffusion generator of Qwen-Image-2.1 into one instruction-driven model for metric depth, semantic, instance, and referring segmentation, using a single shared low-rank adapter and public data. Each task is a \emph{task contract} whose RGB encoder, instruction, and decoder share one specification and are checked before training. Studying 42 externally evaluated checkpoints from two 100k-update runs, six exact weight-space merges, and eight controlled shorter runs, we find that the gap to the closed system is specific and measurable. Geometry is learned early while absolute scale is not: median-aligned NYUv2 $δ_1$ stays at 0.89--0.93 throughout training as unaligned $δ_1$ wanders between 0.11 and 0.50. The scale is not committed by a single sample: a ten-seed ensemble lifts unaligned $δ_1$ from 0.348 to 0.516 without retraining. Depth and 2D segmentation compete for the shared adapter, and weight merging moves along this trade-off without escaping it. Finally, lower validation flow loss comes with lower Cityscapes accuracy (Spearman $ρ\approx-0.9$ between loss reduction and mIoU), so the loss cannot select checkpoints. We release code, contracts, training data, and all evaluation outputs.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28489) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28489)

## 403. VICA: Video Contextual Agreement for AIGC Video Detection and Localization

- **作者**：Runheng Lin, Jialiang Shen, Jiyang Zheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

The rapid advancement of AI-generated content (AIGC) video generation and editing technologies has raised growing societal concerns about the authenticity and reliability of digital videos. Existing video detection methods primarily exploit temporal inconsistencies through holistic frame-level representations, whereas manipulation localization methods focus on artifacts within individual spatial regions. However, holistic representations may obscure localized artifacts through global feature aggregation, while region-centric approaches may overlook disagreements between individual regions and their surrounding spatiotemporal context. To address these limitations, we propose VIdeo Contextual Agreement (VICA), a unified framework that masks individual video regions and leverages a frozen V-JEPA model to predict their representations from the surrounding spatiotemporal context. Complementary masking ensures complete regional coverage, while context-conditioned predictions establish references for assessing regional agreement with the broader video context. By comparing predicted and observed representations, VICA derives spatial residuals that characterize intra-frame discrepancies and bidirectional temporal residuals that capture inconsistencies across preceding and subsequent frames. These residuals characterize contextual disagreements, providing a shared forensic representation for both tasks and shifting the focus from identifying isolated manipulation artifacts to assessing regional agreement with expectations inferred from the broader video context. Extensive experiments demonstrate that VICA achieves state-of-the-art performance on both tasks across multiple benchmarks, with strong generalization to unseen video generators and editing methods.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28485) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28485)

## 404. CAMA: Calibrated Anti-Collapse Marginal Alignment for Test-Time Adaptation of Vision-Language Models

- **作者**：Jinho Kang, Yunhui Guo
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：30 pages, 11 figures, 18 tables

Test-time adaptation (TTA) updates a model using unlabeled test data to help it handle distribution shifts. However, prior work has found that image corruption causes a vision-language model (VLM) to produce more similar features for different images, a phenomenon known as feature compression. Under compression, entropy minimization, the most common TTA objective, can cause the model to favor a few classes and perform worse than before adaptation. Despite these observations, a detailed analysis of how these failures are connected and an effective strategy for preventing them are still lacking. In this paper, we first analyze why entropy minimization fails for VLMs. We then propose CAMA, a new test-time adaptation objective that maximizes batch mutual information to encourage confident predictions for individual images while maintaining diverse predictions across the batch. CAMA also estimates a reference class distribution early in the test stream and aligns each batch's average prediction with this reference during adaptation. We prove that feature compression biases the predictions and that entropy minimization amplifies this bias into collapse, whereas CAMA is guaranteed to preserve every class. Across standard corruption benchmarks, we observe collapse on nearly every VLM backbone evaluated. CAMA prevents collapse in every observed case and, in turn, achieves the best accuracy on every backbone. Quantitative and qualitative analyses confirm that CAMA reverses the feature compression behind the collapse, and ablations show that each of its components contributes.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28461) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28461)

## 405. Decoding Frozen Vision Representations for Training-Free Few-Shot Pineapple Detection

- **作者**：Manfred Gonzalez-Hernandez, Danny Xie-Li, Fabian Fallas-Moya et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-21 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Accepted in WACV HARVEST Vision Workshop 2027

Few-shot object detection in agricultural imagery is hampered by dense vegetation, repeated patterns, and scarce annotations. We present a training-free detector that turns dense features from frozen visual foundation models directly into detections, without fine-tuning, a learned head, or external proposals: a whitened foreground-background direction estimated from annotated support images scores every query patch, and the score field is decoded with an adaptive threshold and support-derived geometry. We also test whether predicted relative depth can be fed directly to RGB-pretrained frozen encoders. On aerial pineapple imagery, the detector outperforms PET-DINO and GeCo2 at every evaluated support size; at K=40, predicted depth alone reaches 92% of the RGB performance with DINOv2, and RGB-depth fusion lowers counting error for both encoders. We release the Pineapple Plantation dataset (4,908 aerial frames, 1,995 annotated with 17,609 boxes, predicted depth, leakage-aware splits) and analyse transfer to two further agricultural datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28442) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28442)

## 406. Center-Aware Ordinal Learning for Prostate Cancer Grading from Histopathology Images

- **作者**：Mohammad Mahdi Abootorabi, Parvin Mousavi, Purang Abolmaesumi
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：6 pages, 3 figures. Accepted as an oral presentation at SPIE Medical Imaging 2027

Prostate cancer grading from histopathology whole-slide images (WSIs) is complicated by the ordered nature of ISUP grades, class imbalance, and center-dependent variations in staining, tissue preparation, and scanning. Most weakly supervised methods treat grade groups as independent categories and may therefore learn representations that do not transfer reliably across institutions. We propose a center-aware ordinal learning framework that combines a pretrained pathology encoder with attention-based multiple instance learning. The framework uses an Earth Mover's Distance loss to account for ordinal errors, an ordinal supervised contrastive objective to align same-grade slides across centers while separating grades according to disease severity, and curriculum learning to reduce the dominance of benign cases. We evaluate the framework on approximately 9,100 biopsy WSIs from the two-center PANDA dataset. The proposed framework achieves an accuracy of 0.842 and a quadratic weighted kappa of 0.958 on the held-out test set. Its main benefit is observed in cross-center transfer: when a linear probe is trained on Karolinska representations and evaluated on Radboud, accuracy increases from 0.643 to 0.823 and quadratic weighted kappa from 0.899 to 0.930. Performance remains stable in the reverse direction. UMAP projections further show improved mixing between centers while preserving an ordinal grade trajectory. These results suggest that combining ordinal and center-aware objectives produces more transferable slide representations for multi-center prostate cancer grading.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28419) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28419)

## 407. Occupancy Planar Patch: Incremental Probabilistic Bounded-Plane Reconstruction from LiDAR Data

- **作者**：Jiahao Wang, Chaehyeon Song, Dongjiao He et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV

Online surface reconstruction from LiDAR scans requires observation models that respect the sensor's physical measurement geometry. Unlike unordered point sets, LiDAR returns carry anisotropic range noise along known rays, which also implicitly identify free space. We introduce the Occupancy Planar Patch (OPP), an incremental bounded planar patch representation that evaluates surface likelihoods and free space directly in range-space, allowing sensor noise, plane uncertainty, and ray clearance to be explicitly modelled during reconstruction. A single ray-to-patch association weight jointly updates plane geometry, refines surface boundaries, and blends camera imagery. The method is evaluated using six sequences from the Oxford Spires and Multi-Campus datasets. OPP achieves the highest precision and lowest reconstruction error among evaluated methods, while producing stored maps among the most compact. By conservatively admitting surfaces only where geometric evidence is certain, OPP prioritises structural precision over unconstrained completeness, which is well suited for architectural modelling. Geometric reconstruction operates in real time, requiring 81 ms per frame on a representative sequence, with texture projection adding 134 ms.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28403) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28403)

## 408. Plan Big, Paint Small: Semantic-Aware Computation Allocation for Efficient Autoregressive Video Generation

- **作者**：Yixuan Luo, Yanjing Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Autoregressive (AR) video diffusion streams a video chunk by chunk, but large AR models are too slow for real-time use. We ask where in an AR rollout a large model is actually needed. Pairing Krea-Realtime-14B with Self-Forcing-1.3B, which share their latent space and denoising schedule, we find that the order of the two models matters: when the 14B model generates only the first chunk and the 1.3B model continues, the rollout keeps 95\% of the 14B model's semantic advantage, whereas the reverse allocation keeps 16\% at about ten times the cost. The large model decides what the video shows; the small model only has to carry it forward. We turn this into BigSmall, a training-free planner--renderer sampler. The 14B model plans the opening chunk and sketches the chunks in which content changes, which preserves motion, and the 1.3B model renders the rest; a faster variant, BigSmall, plans only the opening chunk. A cross-model KV cache that is approximate, windowed and lazy lets the planner leave and re-enter the rollout at low cost. On VBench, BigSmall is $3.02\times$ faster than the 14B model at an unchanged semantic score and motion, and $1.55\times$ faster than SDVG, which lets the small model draft first; BigSmall reaches $3.77\times$ at the same semantic score, trading away 10\% of the motion. On minute-long videos (LoCoT2V-Bench), where the 14B model degrades when it reads its own long history, BigSmall is $3.14\times$ faster than the 14B model and scores higher ($+1.78$).`

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28402) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28402)

## 409. TOGO: Take One Image Editor, Get One Video Editor for Free

- **作者**：Yixuan Luo, Jose Caballero, Yanjing Li
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Instruction-based image editors are trained on millions of edit pairs and follow instructions far more reliably than current video editors. We show that this ability can be transferred to video editing without any training. Our key observation is that a growing number of image editors and video generators share the same latent space. Their velocity fields can therefore be combined at sampling time: the difference between the editor's velocity under the instruction and under a neutral prompt, which we call the \emph{edit velocity}, can be added to the sampler of a video model. Added naively, however, it blurs the video, alters the background and flickers. An analysis of the edit velocity explains why. It is small and reliable only at coarse scales and high noise levels, not concentrated on the edited object, and uncorrelated across frames. From these properties, we derive a simple guidance operator that amplifies the edit velocity and restricts it in noise level, space, and time. The video model then turns the incoherent per-frame signal into a temporally coherent edit. Our method, TOGO, combines Qwen-Image-Edit with the Wan2.1 video model without training or fine-tuning. On FiVE-Bench, it raises the edit success rate from 47.0\% (the same video model without the editor) to 65.6\%, above the best published method (59.2\%). At the same time, it maintains the background PSNR and obtains the best LPIPS and SSIM of all methods. Because the video editor is assembled at inference time, it can be upgraded to any newer image editor that shares the latent space with no cost.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28397) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28397)

## 410. Two Actions from One State: Fork Futures Teach JEPA World Models to Compare

- **作者**：Yihao Wu, Runjia Zeng, Tongyan Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

JEPA world models are trained to predict logged transitions but used to compare counterfactual actions from one state. The logged behavior data show one action per state, and under a local linear approximation we prove the action response is unidentified along any direction the policy never varies within a state. We therefore supplement behavior data with measured outcomes of alternative actions from the same state. Our fork futures execute two actions from one restored state, and their outcome difference cancels the state term, identifying the action response on the sampled span. The resulting model, Fork-JEPA, matches predicted to measured differences, anchors the logged successor with behavior prediction, and adds a lookback head that keeps the predecessor recoverable, all at training time, leaving planning unchanged. On three visual control tasks, behavior-only training leaves the predicted action effect no better than a constant guess. Fork-JEPA makes it readable and raises planning success by up to 14\% over LeWM, its base model. Where the action response changes across hidden zones, it nearly doubles cross-zone success, and 1,024 fork pairs beat 1.2M extra behavior steps. Our project is available at https://fork-jepa.github.io/.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28350) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28350)

## 411. Defending Vision-Language-Action Models against Transferable Attacks via Visual-Proprioceptive Consistency

- **作者**：Jiahao Wang, Ming F. Li, Han Xu
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 10 figures

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have demonstrated strong capabilities in a wide range of robotic tasks. However, adversarial attacks in VLA models have shown strong transferability across robotic tasks, posing significant threats to robotic systems. Despite their different attack strategies, we observe that these transferable attacks share a common consequence: they disrupt the VLA model's perception towards the robot itself. Motivated by this observation, we propose a proprioception-grounded defense framework that leverages trusted proprioceptive information to detect and mitigate transferable adversarial attacks. Specifically, we utilize the robot's proprioceptive states to establish a trusted spatial reference, and compare it with the robot's perceived location inferred from visual features. Discrepancies between these two sources provide a reliable signal for detecting adversarial attacks. Extensive experiments demonstrate effective detection of transferable adversarial patches and improved closed-loop performance. Real-robot experiments further demonstrate the effectiveness of our defense on real-world robotic tasks.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28346) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28346)

## 412. Alignment under Hard Negative Re-Pairing: Cross-Modal Similarity, Scale and Retrieval

- **作者**：Charles Weng, Alexander Martin, Sandra Arcos-Holzinger et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：52 pages, including references and supplementary material

Do vision and language models represent the world alike? That question hides three: what structure each modality has, whether the two modalities organize images and their captions alike, and whether a model ranks an image's own caption highly. Centered kernel alignment (CKA) and neighborhood agreement answer the second, yet matching and model selection use them as if they identified individual pairs and answered the third. To test this reading, we introduce hard negative re-pairing: every image receives the caption of a similar image, and nothing else changes. Although no image keeps its own caption and annotators judge a majority of the substituted captions wrong, CKA stays higher than under a random re-pairing that keeps 80% of the original pairs. In all 22 image-text models we test, neighborhood agreement loses far less than top-one retrieval, which collapses. The reason is scale: these diagnostics pool over many neighbors, matching and retrieval are decided among the nearest ones, and an exact decomposition shows that near-neighbor pairs contribute almost nothing to CKA. In the matching problems we test, an exact maximizer of CKA would not return the original pairing. On COCO and Flickr30k, the model with the highest diagnostic value retrieves worse on held-out data than the model chosen by validation recall, even where the two correlate positively. What re-pairing removes is the informative part: that share tracks retrieval on both datasets and ranks encoder pairs before alignment training. A diagnostic should be reported with its question and its scale, and tested against hard negative re-pairing.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28336) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28336)

## 413. GlaucomaMarker - Biomarker-Grounded Glaucoma Vision-Language Model

- **作者**：Ahammed Sakir Nabil, Sadia Siraz, Nikita Chincholkar et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Glaucoma is a leading cause of irreversible blindness. In the current clinical setting, glaucoma diagnosis is generally conducted by assessing optic disc cupping and asymmetry. Vision-language models (VLMs) have been utilized in recent years for glaucoma, mostly trained on fixed templates or AI-generated captions, as no public text describes the biomarkers. Our proposed framework, GlaucomaMarker, hypothesizes that retinal structural biomarkers can ground glaucoma-based VLMs. It converts public segmentation masks into sentences describing eleven biomarkers, to teach retinal foundation models to read them. We applied this framework to RETFound (GlaucomaMarker-R) and FLAIR (GlaucomaMarker-F), training on 9,778 images that yielded 45,190 image-text pairs. On a real-world clinical cohort, GlaucomaMarker-F detects glaucoma with an AUROC of 0.905, against 0.848 for FLAIR, and both read the biomarkers where baselines stay near random guessing. Since the biomarker text is computed from existing masks, it comes at no extra cost.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28332) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28332)

## 414. Click and Count: Multimodal Prototype Adaptation for Class-Agnostic Object Counting

- **作者**：Amani Aifi, Wassim Bouachir, Ahmed Zgaren et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：7 pages, 2 figures, 4 tables. Accepted for publication in the Proceedings of the 2027 IEEE Symposium Series on Computational Intelligence (IEEE SSCI 2027). Code: https://github.com/ameniifi/Click-and-Count

We propose a multimodal, class-agnostic, density-based object counting framework that specifies the object of interest through visual exemplars, a text description, or both, within a single iterative refinement pipeline. Existing counters typically fall into two groups: methods that fuse two modalities, a hand-drawn bounding box and a text description, to specify the target object, and single-modality methods that rely on either a box exemplar or text alone. A few recent methods replace the box with a point or click. We expand a single click into a precise object mask via a frozen visual-prompt segmentation model, feeding an iterative prototype-refinement process that also fuses a text description at every refinement step. We make two contributions: first, a click-derived exemplar extraction module that removes the bounding-box requirement of prior exemplar-based counters; second, a per-step text-fusion mechanism that combines visual and textual prompts throughout refinement. Combined with text guidance, our model achieves state-of-the-art counting accuracy, outperforming prior click-based counting methods across all metrics and surpassing the box-exemplar base architecture in mean absolute error. The code is available at github.com/ameniifi/Click-and-Count.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28321) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28321)

## 415. Learning a Food World Model That Predicts the Next State from Production Data

- **作者**：Inkyu Sa, Gaurav Kuppa, Steve Van der Hoeven et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：12 pages, 5 figures, 9 tables: a 4-page paper with appendices. This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication

Food assembly robots pick ingredients from a pan, and every pick must deliver a target weight. We learn a world model of food picking from 61,773 production picks, labeled automatically by a camera and a scale (self-supervised learning). From the pre-pick heightmap of the pan on a 2.5 mm grid, the commanded pick action, the food and the utensil, the model predicts the post-pick heightmap and the picked mass with uncertainty. We evaluate the mass prediction on 12,294 held-out picks of nine foods. The production controller keeps each pick near its commanded target. The model's root-mean-square error (RMSE) of 10.93 g is 9% lower than that of the commanded target or of a per-food mean. This RMSE is 24% lower than that of the controller's internal mass estimate. Gradient-boosted trees on 20 hand-crafted features have a 0.2 g lower RMSE, but they predict only the mass, not the next state that planning and policy learning need. Using the predicted uncertainty, we decline the most uncertain picks and answer 89.4% of picks with an RMSE of 10.28 g. The prediction barely responds to the commanded volume and depth, because they vary little in closed-loop production. The model also fails without warning at target weights not seen in training. Future directions include action-primitive conditioning and a more general model across foods and utensils. We give recommendations for learning world models from production data.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28319) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28319)

## 416. Task-Agnostic Incremental Vision-Language Object Detection via Prompt Augmentation and Distribution-Aware Fusion

- **作者**：Yonghan Jiang, Zhengyuan Xie, Wenchu Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Incremental Vision-Language Object Detection (IVLOD) empowers pre-trained open-vocabulary detectors to continuously learn new visual concepts from sequential tasks without forgetting foundational knowledge. However, existing methods predominantly rely on oracle task identities during inference, severely limiting their open-world practicality. In this paper, we formalize Task-Agnostic IVLOD (TA-IVLOD), a more realistic setting where such priors are unavailable, requiring the model to simultaneously recognize classes from all learned tasks. This unconstrained setting exposes severe performance degradation due to cross-task semantic interference and parameter conflicts. To tackle these challenges, we propose TADA, a novel modular framework. Specifically, TADA introduces Stochastic Prompt Augmentation to mitigate semantic interference via training-time noise injection, and Test-Time Distribution-Aware Fusion to dynamically weight class-specific LoRA experts, effectively resolving parameter conflicts. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms baselines in standard IVLOD and yields substantial improvements in the rigorous TA-IVLOD setting on the ODinW-13 benchmark, while effectively preserving zero-shot generalization on the MS COCO dataset. The code is available at https://github.com/yonghanjiang/TADA.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28291) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28291)

## 417. When Video Verifier Scores Mislead: Capability Assessment and Verifier Selection

- **作者**：Charles Weng, Zixi Huang
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL
- **备注**：55 pages, including appendices

Video verifiers judge whether a candidate answer or trajectory agrees with a video, and are themselves chosen by a score. We show that this score depends on two choices in the test, the type of negative and the judgment interface, strongly enough to reverse conclusions about what a model can do and which verifier to deploy. We separate the two with controlled constructions that have exact truth conditions: endpoint-matched negatives, wrong only in their internal states, and a truth-switch test, in which the same two candidates swap correctness between two videos. Under direct judgment, where the verdict is read from answer scores, models that reject a visibly wrong start fall to near chance against endpoint-matched negatives. When the frames disclose each event, writing out reasoning before the answer lifts three of four models from under 10% to 70-100% on the truth-switch test, and staged inputs trace the direct failure to one step, composing events into states. But when the event must first be found, on human-validated natural video, the same change brings no significant gain under a protocol common to all models and can lower accuracy if one candidate denies that the event occurs. The negative type also changes which verifier is selected: one chosen on event-denial negatives is 8.9 points less accurate against negatives that keep the event and change a detail, the errors a generator mostly makes, and recovers about half as much accuracy when it reranks that generator's answers. A verifier should be evaluated on the errors it will face, and neither a low direct score nor a gain from reasoning should be read as a property of the model alone.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28287) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28287)

## 418. HandXFM: Semantic-Structural Distillation for Hand Radiograph Foundation Models

- **作者**：Yuxi Long, Ganlin Feng, Lianghong Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026 (Main Conference)

Hand radiographs support diverse clinical tasks, including skeletal maturity assessment, rheumatoid arthritis scoring, abnormality screening, and anatomical localization, yet existing models are typically trained separately for each task and dataset. We present HandXFM, a domain-specific foundation model that leverages cross-modality knowledge for comprehensive hand X-ray understanding. HandXFM distills semantic priors from BiomedCLIP and structural cues from a chest X-ray Vision Transformer (ViT) through a unified alignment framework, integrating complementary medical knowledge in an interpretable way. After pretraining, HandXFM can be adapted to a range of musculoskeletal tasks, including bone age estimation, SvH score prediction, abnormality detection, joint localization, bone segmentation, and visual question answering. It consistently outperforms task-specific and single-expert baselines, achieving up to 0.11 improvement in Pearson correlation coefficient (PCC) for bone age prediction and a 12\% accuracy gain in abnormality classification. Moreover, HandXFM generalizes to unseen datasets and yields cross-attention maps linking clinical terms to anatomical regions. These results demonstrate that multi-expert distillation effectively unifies semantic and structural supervision within a single pretrained model, establishing HandXFM as an interpretable and generalizable foundation model for hand radiograph analysis.

## 419. Learning a Rule Bank to Revise 3D CT Reports with Independent Evidence

- **作者**：Renjie Liang, Mengxian Lyu, Zijian Xu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Vision--language models can generate fluent radiology reports from 3D chest CT scans, yet they may still omit abnormalities or misstate findings. Independent image analysis using models separate from the report model provides additional observations, but acting on uncertain evidence may introduce new errors. We propose an agent system that learns a revision policy represented as an executable rule bank for each report model. Inspired by experience banks, the agent proposes and refines rules using revision outcomes across development cases. During inference, the retained rules guide local edits using independent classifier predictions and quantitative image measurements without retraining the report model. Unedited sentences are preserved, and each change is linked to the rule that triggered it. On CT-RATE, revision increases CE-F1 by 11.5 points for SelfCue-KD and 0.9 points for NV-Reason-CT, with gains across five draft-sampling seeds. SelfCue-KD's CT-FineBench score also increases by 15.2 points. Draft-only rule learning yields limited gains, while the two image-evidence sources provide complementary benefits for abnormality detection. Measurements also supply numerical details missing from the drafts. These results support learning model-specific rules to control how independent image evidence informs report revision. Code is available at https://github.com/renjie-liang/CT-RuleBank.

## 420. eSPG: The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Event Speckle Plethysmography

- **作者**：Aniket Dashpute, Anirudh Bindiganavale Harish, Bhargav Ghanekar et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Non-contact measurement of vital signs such as heart rate (HR) is valuable for patient monitoring and rescue operations. However, existing methods struggle with ambient illumination and motion, and have been demonstrated only at distances of tens of meters. Here we introduce event speckle plethysmography (eSPG), which illuminates a body part with a low-power (0.5-5 mW) laser and captures the resulting dynamic speckle pattern using an event camera. Event cameras, with their high dynamic range, temporal resolution, and sensitivity to intensity changes, are well-suited to capturing rapid speckle fluctuations that encode the physiological signal, even under challenging illumination and imaging conditions. We demonstrate HR estimation at 1 km with a mean absolute error of 5 beats per minute, and 90% of estimates satisfy an AAMI-defined HR estimation criterion. This distance exceeds previous remote plethysmography demonstrations by an order of magnitude and previous speckle plethysmography demonstrations by over three orders of magnitude. We also study and characterize robustness to motion and ambient illumination. Our theoretical analysis and experimental results demonstrate that resolving dynamic speckle fluctuations enables accurate heart rate estimation even at kilometer-scale distances, establishing a new direction toward robust, non-contact physiological monitoring.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28275) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28275)

## 421. DeFlowCritic: Dense Latent Reward Alignment for Text-to-Image Flow Matching Models

- **作者**：Zeeshan Khan, Xin Yu, Shizhe Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted to NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://zeeshank95.github.io/deflowcritic/

Online reinforcement learning (RL) has emerged as a powerful paradigm for aligning text-to-image flow matching models with complex user intent. However, existing methods typically rely on sparse rewards computed only from final decoded images. This delays credit assignment across the denoising trajectory and makes training computationally expensive. Moreover, simply combining multiple rewards does not reliably produce a Pareto trade-off, where gains in compositional accuracy often degrade aesthetic quality. In this work, we propose DeFlowCritic, an efficient online RL framework based on Dense Latent Reward Alignment. Unlike prior work, our method provides dense supervision on intermediate latents, enabling early-stage guidance for the denoising trajectory. To achieve this, we introduce an internal critic that evaluates noisy latents using features extracted directly from the diffusion model, a representation inherently better suited for latent-space reward estimation than standard image encoders. We construct a high-quality reward-modeling dataset with continuous scores for critic training, capturing compositional correctness on high-quality reference images. The resulting dense reward enables efficient online RL training and inference-time scaling. Experiments show that our method achieves a superior balance between prompt faithfulness and visual quality on GenEval2 and T2I-CompBench++ benchmarks. The approach also generalizes across text-to-image flow matching models, including SD3.5-Medium, SD3.5-Large, and FLUX.1-dev.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28265) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28265)

## 422. FlowPAC: Flow-Guided Visual Pruning and Action Caching for Efficient VLA Inference

- **作者**：Jintao Cheng, Yipu Zhang, Haozhe Wang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at DATE 2027. 7 pages, 6 figures

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for embodied AI, yet their high inference latency and memory footprint remain major barriers to edge deployment. Existing training-free acceleration techniques, such as visual token pruning and action caching, typically rely on static resource allocation. This mismatch with dynamic robotic interactions not only degrades control performance but also causes severe hardware inefficiencies, including fragmented KV-cache layouts and wasted compute on non-critical regions. To address this, we propose FlowPAC, a locality-aware framework that leverages task-aware optical flow as a shared physical signal to jointly optimize visual and action computation. Specifically, we introduce Direction-Aware Trajectory Propagation (DATP) and Locality-Aware Contiguous Token Selection (LACT) to jointly handle visual token dynamics and enforce spatial locality, reducing memory fragmentation and enabling efficient KV-cache compression. This is complemented by Flow-Conditioned Action-Block Refresh (FCAR) for adaptive action computation based on motion dynamics. Extensive closed-loop evaluations on LIBERO demonstrate that FlowPAC achieves a 2.13$\times$ end-to-end speedup over the dense baseline while essentially maintaining the success rate with 30% visual-token retention. These results validate that aligning computation allocation with both physical dynamics and hardware constraints is essential for efficient VLA deployment on edge systems.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28256) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28256)

## 423. A Closed-Form Temporal Readout for Surgical Triplet Recognition

- **作者**：Lemar Abdi, Amaan Valiuddin, Fida Mohammad Thoker et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Surgical triplet recognition requires identifying which instrument performs which action on which anatomical target, and following these interactions as they unfold over time. The task is challenging because several triplets can occur at once, their components span very different durations, and the evidence for an interaction is rarely complete in a single frame. We introduce STRIDE, a post-hoc temporal readout that adds temporal context to any trained recogniser. It averages the recogniser's features and logits over a few fixed temporal windows and combines them with the current frame through ridge regression, solved in closed form on a CPU. Applied as a plug-and-play readout to nine existing methods, STRIDE improves $AP_{IVT}$ in 24 of 27 method-dataset pairs, by up to 7.4 points. Moreover, we propose a simple pipeline consisting of an adapted foundation vision encoder with a linear classifier and STRIDE. Across thirteen encoders, STRIDE improves 37 of 39 encoder-dataset pairs by up to 7.2 points. STRIDE reduces false alarms by 41%, at the cost of a slower response at interaction boundaries. Code will be made publicly available upon acceptance.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28240) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28240)

## 424. Video-as-Weights: Compiling a Video Once into Parametric and Token Memory

- **作者**：Wentao Zhang, Ian Miao, Chen Qu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Vision-language models (VLMs) represent videos as thousands of visual tokens, creating a large key-value cache whose memory grows with video length. At the same time, small VLMs that fit on limited hardware understand video far worse than large models. Existing compression methods shorten visual tokens into a brief prompt to save memory, yet the small model still processes them through fixed weights and cannot close this capability gap. To address both challenges, we introduce Video-as-Weights (VaW), which compiles a video once without seeing any question into the parametric and token memory of a frozen interpreter VLM. Specifically, a compiler VLM watches the video, and a single weight decoder jointly generates the interpreter's LoRA weights and optional prefix tokens from every compiler layer, enabling both same-model compilation and strong-to-weak transfer across model sizes and depths. Across six video QA benchmarks, a 0.8B interpreter beats full in-context reading by 1.7 points when compiled by itself and by 4.3 points with a 9B compiler, reducing context from up to 8,192 visual tokens to 64 prefix tokens while using 5.5x less memory than the video's key-value cache. Generated weights add 3.9 points over token compression alone, and all-layer decoding outperforms layer-wise generation by 2.9 points. Mechanistic analysis shows that with prefix tokens, the weights mainly alter attention queries and MLP down projections to guide retrieval and store a baseline summary, whereas without tokens, they shift to attention output projections to write video content directly into the residual stream.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28221) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28221)

## 425. EID-Net: Endoscopic Illumination-aware Degradation Correction Network

- **作者**：Junzhi Liu, Yinghao Zeng, Yang Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：7 pages, 5 figures

Endoscopic images captured under complex illumination conditions often suffer from spatially non-uniform exposure degradation, including local under-exposure, over-exposure, and illumination-induced color variation. These degradations are usually entangled with tissue structures, making it challenging for general-purpose low-light enhancement and image restoration methods to improve visibility while preserving local structural and chromatic cues. In this paper, we propose EID-Net, an endoscopic illumination-aware degradation correction network for structure-preserving endoscopic illumination correction. Rather than treating illumination correction as a global intensity adjustment, EID-Net explicitly extracts an endoscopy-oriented degradation prior from the input image to characterize illumination-related variations in a unified representation. This prior is then introduced into the decoder through an illumination-modulated transposed attention mechanism, where it adaptively guides feature aggregation by modulating the key-value feature subspace. Extensive experiments on CEC, Endo4IE, EndoVis17, and EndoVis18 datasets demonstrate that EID-Net performs favorably against recent endoscopic enhancement and general image restoration methods. The code is available at https://github.com/JUNZHIlius/EID-Net.

## 426. From Point Clouds to Engineering Models: Benchmarking Agentic 3D Reconstruction in the Built Environment

- **作者**：Changyu Du, Stavros Nousias, Syed Salman Ahmed et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.CV

Coding agents can translate 3D measurements into CAD geometry, but reconstructing engineering models for the built environment demands semantic interpretation and consistent construction constraints beyond surface fitting. We formulate scene-level Scan-to-BIM (Building Information Modeling) as an agentic 3D coding task and introduce S2Bench, comprising public and proprietary scans of 128 real-world scenes across five domains, paired with expert-curated reference models spanning diverse spatial scales. A shared framework lets agents measure scans in Python, build class-labeled components in a live CAD/BIM kernel, and iteratively refine their code. We also propose parallel asynchronous collaboration, where a lead delegates versioned tasks, continues modeling while workers execute, and integrates revision-bound proposals. Evaluating five agents and a collaborative team across 1,028 reconstructions shows that scan-to-model residuals directly guide geometric repair, whereas inferring semantic classes and construction relations requires joint geometric, visual, and engineering reasoning that agents struggle to ground and verify. Consequently, the leading agent fits scan geometry closely ($96\%$ coverage within $5\,\text{cm}$) yet reaches only $63\%$ mIoU, and no agent passes all engineering checks on more than $38\%$ of scenes. Coverage and engineering conformance drop with scene scale, whereas running a smaller model as a team improves all three axes with greater coverage gains on larger scenes. The project page is available at https://tumcms.github.io/S2Bench.github.io/

## 427. torchtsetlin: Fast, Interpretable Tsetlin Machines for PyTorch

- **作者**：Vajira Thambawita, Pål Halvorsen, Ole-Christoffer Granmo
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

The Tsetlin machine learns propositional rules with teams of Tsetlin automata that are driven by reward and penalty rather than gradients, so its models are interpretable by construction and cheap to evaluate. Its software, however, consists of C and CUDA kernels behind scikit-learn-style wrappers that sit outside the deep learning ecosystem. torchtsetlin implements the Tsetlin machine family as ordinary PyTorch modules: vanilla and weighted, coalesced, regression, convolutional over sequences, images and volumes, and dense per-pixel segmentation. The automata are a tensor buffer, so a model moves with .to(device), is saved with state_dict() and learns from a mini-batch with one update(x,y) call. Its core technique is a batched formulation of Type I and Type II feedback, in which automaton transitions become event counts realised as binomial draws, with one commit per mini-batch. On ten benchmarks taken from the existing libraries, it matches their accuracy, and on all but the two smallest it trains 2.4-10.9X faster than PyTsetlinMachineCUDA and 10.3-81.2X faster than tmu on the same GPU. The package is pure Python, MIT-licensed and on PyPI; every number in this paper is reproduced by one script. Code: https://github.com/vlbthambawita/torchtsetlin; package: https://pypi.org/project/torchtsetlin/.

## 428. FastD4RT: Training-Free Acceleration of Dynamic 4D Reconstruction and Tracking

- **作者**：Meng Bi, Yili Jin, Kaiyuan Hu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Query-based 4D reconstruction models such as D4RT encode a video once into a global scene representation and obtain outputs by decoding independent point queries. However, inference is often influenced by dense outputs and long videos. Although D4RT separates encoding from decoding, a dense output still requires one decoder query for every pixel of every frame, and a long video is still encoded as many overlapping segments. In this work, we analyze the inference cost of D4RT and show that a large part of it is redundant. In particular, we find that the later decoder blocks attend to a small set of tokens shared by all queries. Based on these findings, we propose FastD4RT, a training-free framework that accelerates D4RT. By exploiting the smoothness of the outputs over time and across pixels, FastD4RT encodes and decodes only at sparse anchors and recovers the remaining outputs via interpolation. It decodes the remaining queries against a shared token set selected once per segment. We further characterize the errors introduced by both components. Experimental results show that FastD4RT accelerates D4RT by about 5 times on average across all its tasks with competitive accuracy, and further improves accuracy on long videos. Our code is available at https://github.com/Lycheemeng/fastd4rt.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28193) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28193)

## 429. BReD: BEV Residual Diffusion for Feed-Forward Road Gaussian Reconstruction

- **作者**：Baizhi Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Accurate road surface reconstruction is essential for autonomous-driving mapping and simulation, but existing methods trade reconstruction quality for cross-scene inference. Optimization-based methods recover detailed surfaces through costly per-scene optimization, while feed-forward methods lack road-specific constraints and often produce large elevation errors. We propose \textbf{BReD}, a feed-forward framework for reconstructing road elevation, appearance, and semantics from calibrated six-camera sequences. BReD first estimates coarse elevation through LiDAR-supervised depth learning and BEV lifting, using only cameras at inference. A lightweight BEV residual diffusion model then conditions elevation refinement on a camera-derived depth--elevation discrepancy and elevation-aware image features. Diffusion-indexed perturbations expose the model to different discrepancy corruption levels during training, while inference performs one deterministic refinement from the clean discrepancy. The refined elevations are temporally aggregated to anchor road-aligned Gaussians, whose attributes are predicted by a geometry-prior-guided decoder without test-time optimization. On four held-out nuScenes scenes, BReD achieves 7.34 cm elevation RMSE and 90.92\% semantic mIoU, compared with 14.43 cm for RoGS without test-time LiDAR and 8.88 cm for its test-time-LiDAR-assisted variant; both RoGS variants require per-scene optimization.
