## 430. SAMA: Training-Free Consensus Ridge Tracking for Camera-Based Heart Rate on Five Public Datasets

- **作者**：Sudhakar Mishra
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.AI, cs.CV

Remote photoplethysmography (rPPG) is usually benchmarked by one heart rate per video, a figure that hides the short windows in which a tele-consultation would actually read the pulse. We present SAMA, a training-free estimator that (i) forms a quality-weighted consensus of POS and CHROM chrominance spectra, (ii) discounts frequencies occupied by pulse-free references derived from the same frames (frame motion, skin-mask geometry and background brightness), (iii) follows the heart rate through time with a Viterbi ridge under a random-walk prior, and (iv) returns a track-filtered, amplitude-normalised waveform with a per-window confidence. No parameter is learned and no dataset-specific tuning is used. On 218 recordings from 111 participants in five public datasets (UBFC-rPPG, PURE, SUMS, M3PD and a two-participant subset of MMPD), all processed with the same fixed face box and scored with a re-implementation of the rPPG-Toolbox protocol, SAMA has the lowest mean absolute error (MAE) averaged over datasets at every protocol level: 4.40 against 5.13,bpm for POS per video, 4.80 against 5.43 on 30,s windows and 5.05 against 6.59 on 10,s windows. Pooled over participants, the bootstrap interval of its paired difference from POS excludes zero on 30,s and 10,s windows (Wilcoxon p=0.11 and 0.004) but not per video, and it does not beat POS on SUMS per video or on the MMPD subset. Withholding the 20\% least confident windows lowers the pooled 10,s error from 4.06 to 2.33,bpm. We also show that the per-video protocol has a floor of 1.2--1.6,bpm on datasets where heart rate drifts, and that skin tone cannot be recovered from uncalibrated video, which motivates the instrumented, skin-tone-stratified collection we propose for the Indian population. The method does not surpass the best published neural networks on UBFC-rPPG; we report where it stands against them without re-running them.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28185) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28185)

## 431. Beyond Outlier Rejection: Transient-Aware Reconstruction of Multi-Stack MRI

- **作者**：Anika Knupfer, Maximilian Lindholz, Maria Camila Bustos-Vivas et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 6 Figures

Clinical MRI typically acquires the organ of interest as consecutive stacks of 2D slices in different orientations. Reconstruction methods such as SVRTK and NeSVoR fuse them into one volume and down-weight what does not fit, although in living tissue the change during the scan is often the signal itself and can contain clinically relevant physiological information, such as fetal motion and uterine contraction. We propose an analysis-by-synthesis decomposition that explains every acquired voxel through its exact acquisition geometry as a static anatomy shared by all stacks, a rigid and smooth non-rigid motion per stack, and a sparse transient field per stack for changes during one acquisition only. A robust reweighting keeps such changes out of the anatomy. The model is fitted per subject without training data, with one configuration for all datasets. On a digital phantom with known ground truth, static changes are kept entirely in the anatomy, and the transient field of the stack that saw a transient recovers 68% of it at 12mm radius. From identical inputs, the anatomy predicts held-out stacks better than SVRTK and NeSVoR on uterine (NCC 0.897 against 0.874 and 0.863, 123 patients) and on prostate MRI from unseen sites and scanners (0.847 against 0.795 and 0.757, 85 patients), and on fetal brain MRI as well as SVRTK and better than NeSVoR (0.771 against 0.763 and 0.747, 83 fetuses). The transient fields flag corrupted fetal slices with an AUROC of 0.94 against expert raters. As the input of a frozen anomaly detector trained on healthy subjects only, the anatomy localizes radiologist-delineated uterine lesions better than the SVRTK and NeSVoR volumes (AUROC 0.76 against 0.71 and 0.71, 37 patients).

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28184) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28184)

## 432. GenPA: Image Generators as Efficient Robot Policies

- **作者**：Zian Deng, Tianyi Zhang, Zilong Zheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.CV
- **备注**：Project page: https://deng-zian.github.io/GenPA-website/

Pretrained visual generation models have shown great promise in robotic manipulation. However, a representative line of work still treats future visual prediction as the central link between visual generative pretraining and action learning, retaining it as a training objective even when future scenes are no longer generated at inference time. We argue that the instruction-following, spatial understanding, and visual imagination capabilities acquired through image generation pretraining can support control without future visual prediction: image generation models themselves can be directly adapted into powerful action learners. Building on this perspective, we propose GenPA, which fine-tunes an image generation backbone with a lightweight 19M-parameter action regression adapter and reuses its native generative objective for perception co-training. The native image-editing interface accommodates different visual targets without task-specific perception heads, enabling comparisons of current-scene perception, future prediction, and their combinations within a common policy architecture. At deployment, GenPA requires only a single backbone forward pass and action regression, without visual generation or iterative action denoising. Without additional large-scale robot-data pretraining, GenPA demonstrates strong control performance and robustness to perturbations across simulation benchmarks (99.10% success on LIBERO and 83.79% on LIBERO-Plus) and real-world manipulation tasks. Under compiled inference, GenPA takes 20.9 ms per action chunk on a single H100 GPU, achieving 1.7-3.4x speedups over strong VLA and WAM baselines. Our latency analysis further highlights architectural advantages of visual generation backbones for efficient robot control. Ablations across visual tasks and their combinations identify current-depth supervision as a simple, effective training recipe.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.27446) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.27446)

## 433. ADEPT: Adaptive Monocular Depth Estimation via Model-In-The-Loop Photometric Tuning with Capture Risk Prediction

- **作者**：GiHyun Kim, JinMo Yang, Hyung-Sin Kim
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.CV

Exposure and gain determine the visual evidence available to monocular depth estimation (MDE), particularly when robot motion creates a tradeoff between light collection and motion blur. We present ADEPT, a framework that adapts these settings using learned feedback from a frozen depth model. During training, ground-truth depth identifies the lowest-error reference within a group of physically evaluated camera settings. A compact predictor learns reference-relative disagreement (RRD) between each candidate's depth prediction and the reference prediction using regression and ranking losses. At deployment, it scores observed captures using frozen-model features and camera, motion, and illumination context, without ground truth or reference images. A contextual Gaussian-process controller uses recent scores to select subsequent exposure and gain settings under motion and photometric constraints. We evaluate offline camera selection with relative and metric Depth Anything v2 and demonstrate online control with the affine-invariant variant on a mobile robot. Across nine equally weighted scene-illumination conditions, ADEPT reduces mean scale-and-shift-aligned $\mathrm{Abs}_{\mathrm{rel}}$ by 14.6% relative to built-in auto-exposure and by 10.0% relative to the best-performing evaluated prior controller.

## 434. Quality-Control Policies Shape Model Behaviour in Computational Pathology

- **作者**：Mario Parreño Centeno, Yu Guan, Pedro Montero Pavón et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：50 pages, 7 main figures, 22 supplementary figures, 1 table; includes supplementary methods and results, and 5 ancillary data files

Quality-control policies determine which image regions are used by computational pathology models, but their effects are difficult to isolate when quality estimation and input selection are coupled. We make these policies explicit, configurable and testable. We introduce PathQC-3C, comprising 1,661 densely annotated regions from 1,194 haematoxylin and eosin whole-slide images across 32 cancer/tissue categories, and TileNet, a framework that separates dense tissue/background/artefact estimation from task-specific input-selection policies. We evaluated the default predictor across three held-out cohorts and against harmonised quality-control comparators, assessed policy decisions in a blinded single-pathologist audit, and tested alternative policies in three downstream settings. In retrospective CAMELYON16 multiple-instance learning, background-aware selection improved the pooled out-of-fold area under the receiver operating characteristic curve from 0.758 to 0.839 compared with random sampling from the same exhaustive candidate pool under a fixed tile budget. In weak-label lung classification, strict artefact exclusion reduced the pre-matching candidate pool by approximately 43%, with small metric-dependent differences after matching training-set size and patient contribution. PathQCAug, which composites real artefact components during tumour-stroma model adaptation, improved macro Dice on high-artefact tiles under severe held-out composited artefacts across three training seeds and outperformed random-erasing and clean-tissue controls. Separating quality estimation from policy execution provides an auditable way to control which image content enters computational pathology models; the appropriate policy is task specific, and clinical utility remains to be established.

## 435. Don't Test Where You Trained: Geographic Overlap Inflates End-to-End Driving Scores

- **作者**：Willi Poh, Kevin Rösch, Christoph Stiller
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.RO

In NAVSIMv1, the standard benchmark for end-to-end driving, $89.6\%$ of the test scenes lie within 4m of a training scene. We show that this geographic overlap inflates the reported scores. We propose a geographically disjoint split of NAVSIMv1 and an evaluation that compares models trained on either split on the same scenes, which neither has seen in training. All sensor-based models we test, DiffusionDrive, Latent TransFuser, and DrivoR, lose 3.4 to 6.1 PDMS points on these scenes, mainly by leaving the road and coming close to other objects, against 1.5 points for a baseline without sensors. The loss concentrates where a training drive of the original split passed the same place. At all other places, models trained on the original split are no better than those trained on the geographic split. Models trained on the original split predict road geometry that no camera can see. The gap persists when the auxiliary map loss is removed, so the models memorize locations even without being supervised to. On nuScenes, the geographic split cuts SparseDrive's mapping accuracy by $59\%$ and also degrades its planning. Driving scores on logged scenes thus partly measure how well a model remembers the test locations. We release the split to evaluate generalization to new places.

## 436. Med-Agentic: Distilling Agentic Medical Reasoning with Internalized Meta-Capabilities

- **作者**：Yucheng Zhou, Junwei Sheng, Jianbing Shen
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：NeurIPS 2026

Large language models (LLMs) in expert domains face a trade-off between agentic tool use, which externalizes expertise behind brittle multi-turn runtimes, and end-to-end specialization, which internalizes expertise only implicitly at task-level granularity. We propose internalized meta-capabilities: atomic, parametrically internalized, and composable reasoning units that an LLM can select and combine within a single chain-of-thought (CoT). We instantiate this idea as Med-Agentic for medical visual diagnosis, where the model emits a capability-tagged structured CoT in one forward pass, turning capability invocation into tagged token generation rather than external tool calls. For training, Med-OPD performs multi-teacher on-policy distillation with token-level dual-factor teacher routing conditioned on both the image and the active capability tag, strictly generalizing per-prompt teacher routing. We show that this routing gives an unbiased estimator of the same expected teacher-KL gradient. Across public medical imaging datasets spanning nine anatomies and eight modalities, Med-Agentic consistently improves in-distribution and out-of-distribution diagnostic accuracy over the strong competitors.

## 437. VideoJSON: A Minimal Harness Beats Purpose-Built Memory for Ultra-Long Video Understanding

- **作者**：Wenqi Marshall Guo, Kuan-Hao Huang, Junda Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Answering questions over days or weeks of video requires searching for scattered clues across long timelines. Existing video agents rely on complex memory graphs and fixed retrieval pipelines, which restrict how the agent can search. We propose VideoJSON, a minimal harness for ultra-long video understanding that keeps the memory simple and lets a coding agent write its own search programs. During indexing, a vision-language model turns each minute of video into a single JSON record containing a dense caption and structured fields such as events, entities, places, and speech transcripts. At query time, the agent writes Python or Bash programs in a sandboxed container to filter, link, and read these records over multiple steps, without task-specific training or raw video access. On four ultra-long video benchmarks, VideoJSON with DeepSeek V4.1 Flash outperforms MAGIC-Video by 7.0, 20.0, and 24.75 points on EgoLifeQA, Ego-R1-Bench, and MM-Lifelong Week, and beats the best prior EgoMemReason score by 12.6 points. Across four policy models of increasing capability, VideoJSON's average score rises by up to 11.58 points over the weakest model, compared with at most 5.50 points for MAGIC-Video. Ablations show that both agentic code search and the structured JSON records drive these gains, while adding hourly summaries, extra tools, or video access changes the average score by less than 1.5 points. In addition, fine-tuning Qwen-3.5-Flash on 64 VideoJSON trajectories from an unrelated 100-hour livestream improves its MM-Lifelong scores by up to 8.25 points. Code will be open-sourced after publication.

## 438. The UNIFESP Chest CT Fatty Liver Dataset

- **作者**：Augusto Sarquis Serpa, Mariana Ramos Scolari, Eduardo Moreno Judice de Mattos Farina et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：7 pages, 3 figures, 2 tables. Presented in part as a poster at the EuSoMII Annual Meeting 2022

Hepatic steatosis is common, usually asymptomatic, and frequently visible on CT examinations performed for other reasons, including chest CT, in which the upper abdomen is partly included. We describe the UNIFESP Chest CT Fatty Liver Dataset, a public collection of 285 unenhanced CT studies (344 series, 99,883 images) from 264 patients examined at a single academic center in Brazil: 152 patients with chest CT and 112 patients with abdominal CT. Each patient has a binary label for the presence of hepatic steatosis, assigned by a radiology resident who compared the attenuation of three regions of interest in the liver with one in the spleen. Images are provided in de-identified DICOM format. The dataset was used in the UNIFESP Chest CT Fatty Liver Competition, hosted on Kaggle in 2022, with 226 patients for training and 38 patients with chest CT for testing. In a technical validation, the criterion of liver attenuation lower than spleen attenuation, measured automatically, agreed with the labels in 206 of the 226 training patients (91%). The dataset is available at https://www.kaggle.com/competitions/unifesp-fatty-liver.

## 439. Duplicate- and Patient-Overlap Leakage in Brain MRI Tumor Classification: A Multi-Model Evaluation

- **作者**：Xin Li, Samson Oluwadare
- **投稿**：2026-11-20 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 6 figures, 2 table

Background. Repeated MRI images and distinct slices from the same patient can both compromise evaluation of brain tumor classifiers. Objective. We audited image duplication and examined how patient overlap relates to discrimination, probability error and model ranking. Methods. The Cheng/Figshare dataset contains 3,064 contrast-enhanced T1 MRI slices, three tumor classes and 233 recorded patient IDs. The dataset author advised treating the IDs as individuals, but original DICOM records were unavailable for verification. Four CNN architectures were trained under image-level and recorded-ID-disjoint protocols with five folds matched on size and class counts (40 original runs). A patient-cluster bootstrap quantified uncertainty. The extension added 60 split-seed runs, 40 training-seed runs and 72 fixed-test sibling-exposure runs with class-matched other-patient controls. Results. We found no exact duplicate images and little evidence of cross-patient near-duplicates among screened pairs. Image-level minus recorded-ID-disjoint balanced accuracy ranged from +0.0318 to +0.0462 across models. Expected calibration error and multiclass Brier scores were lower on the image-level evaluation sample. The balanced-accuracy difference persisted across new seeds for ResNet50 and DenseNet121, but their original ranking reversal did not replicate. Only ResNet50 in fold 2 passed Holm correction in the five-test exposure family. All 16 correlated exploratory subgroup estimates were positive, with no consistent dose-response. Conclusions. Patient overlap was associated with higher apparent performance on this benchmark. Fixed-test comparisons could not isolate patient identity from shared anatomy or acquisition characteristics. No independent external clinical validation was performed.

## 440. Which Vertebral Landmark Errors Change a Cobb Angle? Geometry-Weighted Supervision for Radiographic Landmark Regression

- **作者**：Zizhao Peng
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages, 7 figures, 3 tables

A network that localizes vertebral corners can reduce pixel error while rotating the line used to measure spinal curvature. We derive a training loss from the geometry of a three-angle reader. Of the eight corner-displacement degrees of freedom of a vertebral quadrilateral, one local direction rotates its left-to-right chord. The angular sensitivity in this direction is inversely proportional to chord width. An orthonormal Hadamard basis lets us give this residual extra weight during training while retaining supervision on the other seven directions. In five-fold image-level development experiments on 372 anteroposterior radiographs, we compared three objectives across 45 fits of the same landmark network. The three-seed corner ensemble reduced pooled symmetric percentage error from 18.45% with coordinate supervision to 17.20% with geometry weighting. Adding a soft pairwise-angle term yielded 16.89%, a paired difference of $-1.56$ percentage points from coordinate supervision (95% image-bootstrap interval $[-2.51,-0.61]$). The interval for the increment from adding the soft term included zero. In a single-fold comparison, direct unit-chord supervision also reduced error, and its difference from geometry weighting was uncertain. Across single-seed runs, rotational residual error fell by 36.1% while normalized corner error rose from 2.60% to 2.71%. Perturbing one vertebra at a time also showed when a small rotation changes the selected end pair. These results identify a measurable trade-off between landmark fidelity and agreement with a specified geometric reader. The evaluation concerns an internal image-level split and landmark-derived target angles.

## 441. Beyond Fidelity: Evaluating Synthetic Medical Imaging Data

- **作者**：Seyed Kahaki, Alexander R. Webber, Tahsin Rahman et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：eess.IV, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages

The growing use of synthetic medical imaging datasets (SMD) in the development and evaluation of machine learning models has motivated interest in systematic methods to assess dataset quality across different imaging modalities. Common evaluation strategies rely heavily on fidelity-oriented metrics developed for general computer vision and may not capture clinically relevant properties such as anatomical plausibility, dataset coverage, metadata completeness, subgroup consistency, or documentation quality. We present the ScoreCard Toolkit that operationalizes six of the seven dimensions found within the 7Cs approach for SMD evaluation across Congruence, Coverage, Completeness, Consistency, Constraint, and Comprehension. The ScoreCard Toolkit provides a modular pipeline for preprocessing, feature extraction, metric computation, visualization, and structured scorecard generation. This approach complements standard fidelity-based evaluation by exposing quality characteristics not captured by those measures alone, including constraint violations, anatomically implausible shape artifacts, and metadata-related consistency issues. The toolkit supports appearance, texture, and shape features and is designed to accommodate additional feature representations, including radiomic features, deep-learning embeddings, and foundation-model representations. We demonstrate the end-to-end workflow across three imaging modalities: synthetic digital pathology whole-slide image patch data, synthetic digital mammography, and synthetic head CT. Across all three modalities, the resulting scorecards reveal dataset-specific quality characteristics, such as distributional biases and coverage gaps that may impact their effectiveness for model development or testing. ScoreCard provides a practical, extensible, and modality-agnostic approach for evaluating and transparently reporting SMD quality.

## 442. Close the Loop on the Retrievability Gap

- **作者**：Guangyuan Dong, Wenhao Li, Zhen Tian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.IR

A frozen vision-language encoder now guides image restoration and later indexes what it produced. Over a fixed set of eighteen pansharpening systems on one WorldView-III test set, the fidelity index that ranks them, $Q2^n$ with a reference and HQNR without one, picks the less retrievable of two systems one time in three and one in two; the disagreement lies between the pixel-supervised and the language-conditioned family, and even the best system falls short of the reference, small objects first. We call this the retrievability gap and trace it to the area weighting of position-averaged losses. LEDGER closes the loop around the encoder: it indexes the input into a set of named concepts, generates, re-encodes the output alone with the same frozen encoder, and minimises a set-level soft-Jaccard loss on the coverage of that set, a label-free target available at test time, so that every present concept of a scale receives a gradient share independent of its area. Measured outside the loop by label-grounded probes, held-out retrievers, language-free evaluators, detectors and human raters, LEDGER matches or exceeds the strongest baselines in fidelity on four sensors, raises retrieval by seven to nine points within its vocabulary and two to five outside it, closes two thirds of the reduced-resolution gap to the reference, lifts extraction on raw SpaceNet imagery above the vendor product in each of four cities, and transfers to existing generators as a training-only loss at no inference cost.

## 443. Dynamics-Guided Conditional MeanFlow from Regional Interaction to Internal Revision and Its Application to CT Imaging

- **作者**：Md Shifatul Ahsan Apurba, Md Selim, Jin Chen
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Conditional image enhancement requires spatially varying corrections, yet fixed-depth models allocate similar computational budgets regardless of where additional refinement is needed. Although one-step flow models reduce inference costs and iterative restoration methods introduce subsequent corrections, how predicted transformation dynamics can guide internal feature refinement remains underexplored. We introduce IRCMF, a dynamics-guided Conditional MeanFlow framework that combines regional interaction with recurrent internal revision. The decoder predicts signed interval velocity and a learned velocity-change cue. A soft-region graph aggregates contextual information for velocity estimation, while a dynamics-conditioned graph establishes regional relationships to guide recurrent bottleneck updates. The revised features are processed by the shared decoder to recompute velocity, establishing feedback between transformation dynamics and internal representations. An actor-critic policy controls regional updates and further revisions to balance enhancement quality and computational cost. We evaluate IRCMF on two computed tomography (CT) datasets, NLST for STANDARD-to-LUNG kernel enhancement and LDCT-and-Projection-data for simulated low-to-routine-dose enhancement. With nine decoder evaluations, IRCMF achieves slice-level PSNR of $36.24 \pm 3.49$ dB and SSIM of $0.97 \pm 0.01$ on NLST, and PSNR of $32.45 \pm 1.97$ dB and SSIM of $0.84 \pm 0.05$ on LDCT-and-Projection-data. The learned policy reduces average decoder evaluations to 4.42 and 3.45, respectively, lowering per-slice latency from 97.00 to 51.82 ms and from 97.12 to 41.95 ms, with PSNR reductions of only 0.20 and 0.06 dB.

## 444. Choose What Returns: Object-Aware KV Memory for Selective Recall in Interactive Video Generation

- **作者**：Hyunwook Kang, Junoh Park, Hyunggyu Lim et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Interactive long-video generation lets users build a story through successive prompts and reuse earlier characters and settings under new instructions. Existing memory methods retain past content, yet they often fail at precise selective recall, returning the wrong appearance of a person, mixing different people, or keeping an unrequested background. Based on our analysis of memory form and retrieval, we introduce an object-aware key--value (KV) memory framework for a frozen autoregressive diffusion generator. It keeps the original KV of tracked object regions, selects background KV outside them by attention-scores, and links each memory with a readable record of its source scene and observed appearance. An LLM selects the requested records, and a memory integrator supplies their KV for generation under the current prompt. We also introduce a six-scene evaluation that tests selective attribute modification, time-specific appearance recall, and multi-object composition, in which each person's requested past appearance changes across requests. Experiments show that our framework more reliably preserves each person's identity while reproducing their requested past appearance than existing memory methods, under both explicit scene references and natural-language cues.

## 445. ENGRAM: Reliability-Weighted Latent Fusion and Occlusion-Aware Layered Readout for Video World Models

- **作者**：Chenhao Wu, Zhaolong Su, Yang Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：22 pages, including supplementary material

Camera-conditioned video world models aim to generate 3D-consistent videos from a single image along specified camera trajectories. However, set-union updates in latent spatial memory leave repeated observations unconsolidated, while frontmost-only readout limits simultaneous access to stored evidence at different depths. To address these limitations, we present ENGRAM, a latent spatial memory framework combining reliability-weighted latent fusion and occlusion-aware layered readout. Specifically, recursive fusion integrates geometrically and visually compatible observations in a channel-normalized latent space, consolidating repeated evidence into a shared scene representation. Layered readout exposes multiple depth-ordered latent candidates with their reliability and availability at each target cell. This gives the generator access to information from multiple surfaces as visibility changes. Explicit camera rays provide viewpoint information independently of memory coverage, while geometric consistency constraints guide memory updates. Memory retrieval uses a single projection at latent resolution, avoiding intermediate RGB rendering and VAE re-encoding. We adapt a pretrained video generator using only 256 training scenes. On 100 RealEstate10K test scenes, ENGRAM improves novel-view-synthesis and closed-loop PSNRs over Spatia by 0.82 and 4.48 dB, respectively. On a 200-case WorldScore evaluation subset, it achieves an average score of 67.78, exceeding Spatia by 3.31 points. Project page: https://wchkoutian.github.io/engram-website/.

## 446. Beyond Output Suppression: Mitigating Privacy Risks in LVLMs via Visual Intrinsic Privacy Erasure

- **作者**：Xiangkui Cao, Jie Zhang, Meina Kan et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Large vision-language models can extract personally identifiable information from images, making privacy protection essential for their deployment in perception-oriented tasks. Existing targeted fine-tuning and model-editing methods usually suppress sensitive answers by aligning the model toward safe responses. In this paper, we show that such protection can be illusory: although the fine-tuned model refuses to disclose private information under normal decoding, privacy leakage still occurs in sentence completion scenarios. This indicates that existing algorithms only change the model's output distribution, but the model still retains the ability to perceive private information. Private information therefore still remains inside the model. To address this problem, we propose Visual Intrinsic Privacy Erasure (VIPE), which targets privacy-related visual representations to weaken the model's ability to perceive sensitive information. VIPE constructs paired privacy-sensitive and privacy-masked images, uses their internal feature residuals to identify privacy-related visual components, and suppresses these components before they are perceived by the model. To preserve benign visual representations, the erasure objective projects feature differences onto a relaxed benign subspace and adaptively controls the erasure strength. VIPE compels the model to ignore the extraction of sensitive information in the visual representation space, thereby blocking the source of privacy leakage more fundamentally rather than merely altering the model's output distribution. Experiments on Qwen2.5-VL and LLaVA-OneVision show that VIPE substantially alleviates prefix-triggered privacy leakage while maintaining competitive utility on general visual-language benchmarks.

## 447. SceneTether: Activity-Gated Value Anchoring for Long-Horizon Autoregressive Image Animation

- **作者**：Matiur Rahman Minar, Seunghun Oh, Ganghyeon Jeong et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-19 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI
- **备注**：Project page: https://minar09.github.io/scenetether/

Long-horizon image animation must keep the scene fixed while natural material (e.g., water, smoke, or fire) continues to move. Autoregressive video generators read recent frames for temporal continuity, but this history also carries accumulated appearance errors, and sparsifying it disrupts motion. We introduce SceneTether, a training-free attention-read policy that keeps where attention reads recent memory but changes what appearance that memory returns. SceneTether keeps every key of the newest latent, blends its low-activity values toward co-located source-image values, and leaves high-activity values untouched so motion can evolve; an activity map estimated from the first generated latents and then frozen decides where to blend. It adds no denoising evaluations and leaves the cache-update rule unchanged. On a Causal Forcing++ generator, SceneTether raises source-support PSNR by 5.15 dB over its framewise Echo host across two seeds with 1.5x its late activity, and by 7.31 dB over spatial Echo-Forcing on a preselected four-minute subset. At equal recent-memory coverage, value anchoring alone adds 8.14 dB. A post-hoc pixel composite with the same map matches static fidelity but retains nearly twice the moving-region color drift, and viewers in a small blinded study preferred SceneTether for its natural scene and motion preservation. Project page: https://minar09.github.io/scenetether/

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28134) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28134)

## 448. Urban State Representation for Autonomous Mobility: A Survey of Multimodal Perception, Architectures, Datasets, and Benchmarks

- **作者**：Binay Kumar Singh, Niels Da Vitoria Lobo
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.RO
- **备注**：Accepted to the UrbanState Workshop at WACV 2027. 8 pages + references + 5-page supplementary appendix. Code and data: https://github.com/binayksingh/Awesome-Urban-State-Representation

Cities are sensed by many platforms at once: on-board cameras, LiDAR and radar on autonomous vehicles, roadside units at intersections, drones and satellites overhead, and the GNSS traces of millions of trips. Yet most learned representations are built for one platform, one city, and one task. We survey this fragmented landscape through a single lens, the Urban State Representation (USR): a georeferenced, layered, time-varying, uncertainty-aware state that any sensing platform can write to and any downstream task can read from. We (i) formalize USR and five desiderata for it, and relate it to bird's-eye-view (BEV) features, occupancy, vectorized maps, neural fields and 3D Gaussians, scene graphs, road-network graphs, language tokens, and world-model latents; (ii) organize sensing platforms, cross-view (ground-aerial) matching, fusion operators, 3D geometric foundation models, end-to-end and vision-language-action models, and generative world models and data engines in one taxonomy that spans sensors to decisions; (iii) compile 26 urban datasets and simulators across ego, cooperative (V2X), aerial, trajectory, and language settings, with a metric glossary, a cross-benchmark results snapshot, a trend analysis, and a platform-coverage gap analysis; and (iv) review applications and propose, as an outlook, USR-5, a five-test protocol with explicit references and one aggregate score for measuring whether a state is shared, together with a research agenda of six open problems covering cross-city and cross-sensor transfer, uncertainty, efficiency, sim-to-real evaluation, and governance. This survey is an entry point for computer vision, autonomous driving, transportation, and urban computing researchers; the paper list, datasets, and USR-5 code are at https://github.com/binayksingh/Awesome-Urban-State-Representation.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28129) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28129)

## 449. Robust Trajectory Prediction Under Sensing and V2X Noise: Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies

- **作者**：Shahab Salehi, Luca Lusvarghi, Miguel Sepulcre et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.NI, cs.RO

Anticipating the future behavior of surrounding objects is key for the safe and efficient navigation of autonomous vehicles. State-of-the-art Transformer-based interaction-aware trajectory prediction models are typically trained and evaluated on large-scale, highly curated datasets obtained by filtering any object detection and tracking noise through extensive offline post-processing. However, in real-world deployments, the detected objects' state (position, heading, and speed) estimates are inherently affected by sensing and localization uncertainties. These uncertainties can be even higher for connected and automated vehicles receiving information from other road users through Vehicle-to-Everything (V2X) communications. In this paper, we first investigate the sensitivity of five state-of-the-art Transformer-based interaction-aware trajectory prediction models to realistic noisy object state estimates. Numerical results show that prediction accuracy degrades by up to a 6x factor as the noise level increases, highlighting the models' sensitivity to the mismatch between the noise conditions encountered during training and those experienced at deployment. We then show that retraining models on a more realistic noise-augmented dataset is a more effective strategy to mitigate the impact of noisy object state estimates on trajectory prediction accuracy than using denoising modules at the input. Retrained models limit the prediction accuracy degradation below a 1.5x factor. The noise-augmented dataset is openly released to support further research.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28128) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28128)

## 450. DINO-KNN: Training-Free Multiclass In-Context Segmentation via Nearest Neighbors

- **作者**：Hussain Alasmawi, Numan Saeed, Mohammad Yaqub
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

In-context segmentation (ICS) segments a target image given a few annotated reference images. Recent training-free methods built on frozen foundation models segment one class at a time and gain little from additional references. We study the multiclass, multi-reference setting and ask how far a simple joint decision rule can go. We introduce DINO-KNN, which stores every labeled reference patch of a frozen DINOv3 encoder in a single memory and labels each target patch by a distance-weighted vote of its k nearest neighbors. All classes are predicted in a single pass, with one label per pixel, without training or a mask decoder. We evaluate on eight datasets, four natural and four medical, with R=1 to 15 reference images and three reference seeds. DINO-KNN achieves the best average mIoU at every R; it is the best method on six of the eight datasets from R=3 and on seven of the eight from R=7. Its accuracy continues to grow with the number of references, from 37.8 mIoU at R=1 to 56.2 at R=15, while the strongest baseline, INSID3, rises from 36.1 to 42.9. DINO-KNN is also 2.1x faster than INSID3. The code will be made public.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28127) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28127)

## 451. ReMiC: Representation-Driven Token Pruning for 3D VLMs via Minimax Hierarchical Clustering

- **作者**：Bangfeng Yan, Chaoning Zhang, Yuanhao Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：10 pages, 3 figures, 6 tables

3D vision-language models (3D VLMs) have demonstrated strong capabilities in 3D scene understanding and spatial reasoning. However, their reliance on multi-view inputs produces long visual token sequences, imposing substantial computational overhead during inference. Existing pruning methods often rely on token salience or feature diversity as proxies for representativeness, without explicitly modeling the representation gap between the original token set and the retained subset, thereby leading to suboptimal representation of the original scene. In this paper, we propose ReMiC, a training-free, representation-driven token pruning framework based on minimax hierarchical clustering. ReMiC treats retained tokens as prototypes for the original observations, formulating pruning as minimizing the maximum representation error under a fixed token budget. Local prototype initialization allocates tokens according to regional representation needs, while global prototype refinement updates the selected prototypes using cross-region information to further reduce the maximum representation error. Extensive experiments conducted on three representative 3D VLMs across five 3D scene understanding benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and generalization of our method. Notably, by retaining only 10% of visual tokens, ReMiC preserves 93.2% of the performance of Video-3D LLM and achieves a $2.04\times$ faster inference speed.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28124) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28124)

## 452. Seeing the Invisible: How Vision-Language Models Read, Predict and Trace Radio Fields

- **作者**：Yiyang Zhu, Xin Li, Guangyuan Liu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Operating in the physical world requires reasoning beyond what cameras directly capture. In a city, radio signals are invisible to the lens, yet their strength is governed by the buildings and streets an image does show. Given only these visible structures, can vision-language models (VLMs) infer the invisible radio field they shape? Probing true physical reasoning requires exact fields in real cities, alongside controlled interventions where a single physical factor changes. We build a label-free engine that turns any coordinate on Earth into a ray-traceable city, simulates matched single-factor counterfactuals, and computes every answer exactly. This yields RadioVISTA, a benchmark and training dataset for visual reasoning about invisible fields. Physically, a radio field is the sum of its propagation paths, yet across six continents we uncover a striking field-path dissociation: frontier VLMs accurately judge where a field is strong, but fail to tell whether a single direct or one-wall path exists. Open models fail even earlier at reading the field itself, and are systematically wrong, scoring below random guessing even when credited for any of four attempts. On unseen cities, fine-tuning brings a 9B open model close to frontier proprietary models on fields, yet neither supervised nor reinforcement fine-tuning improves path tracing. Supervising only final answers teaches models to approximate the field, yet leaves single-path geometry out of reach.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28123) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28123)

## 453. RALC: Risk-Controlled Local Adoption of Segmentation Updates with Few Calibration Images

- **作者**：Shize Yin
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Fine-tuning a road-scene segmenter for rain and nighttime driving can introduce errors on normal daytime images. With few labeled normal images for calibration, a whole-image risk check may reject changes that would pass if adopted locally. Risk-Controlled Adoption of Label Changes (RALC) selects local changes from a fixed model update, using confidence differences and an image score to form nested candidates. On each normal calibration image, RALC measures how many more errors each candidate makes than the original model, assigning zero loss when errors stay the same or fall. At test time, each replacement can add at most one error, so the replacement count bounds the net increase without test labels. RALC combines this candidate-dependent bound with recorded calibration losses to select the largest set that passes the risk check. If the models and adoption rules are fixed before calibration and normal calibration and test images are exchangeable, the expected per-image loss stays within budget. On held-out BDD100K, we evaluated three weight-interpolated updates with 10 additional normal images per calibration draw. At a common fixed budget, adverse-scene mIoU was 1.89--3.19 percentage points higher with RALC than with whole-image adoption, while measured mean normal loss was also higher. After matching this loss post hoc at 1,000 pixels per image, RALC still led by 1.135--2.428 points. Selecting changes in $8\times8$-pixel cells recovered 93--98% of this advantage. With 250 calibration images at the original budget, both rules adopted all three updates in full.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28122) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28122)

## 454. Separating Module Gain From Transport Plan Contribution in Audio-Visual Dynamic Facial Expression Recognition

- **作者**：Vu Nhat Minh Pham, Cong Khanh Nguyen, Minh Tuan Le et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, eess.IV

A cross-modal routing module can improve over its backbone while its transport plan accounts for no demonstrable part of that improvement. In ABVnet with DFEW, Sinkhorn cross-modal routing (SCR) exceeds the retrained baseline by 0.87 unweighted average recall (UAR) points, a resolved difference, yet sits 0.17 points above an individually trained, parameter-matched pooled variant, an unresolved difference that establishes no equivalence; under the development protocol the comparison with the baseline reverses. Separating the two needs comparisons a module-against-backbone test cannot supply: parameter-matched variants changing only the plan rule, and interventions replacing the plan without retraining. The evaluation covers MMA-DFER and ABVnet on DFEW and MAFW, and MMA-DFER on CREMA-D, with a single training run per configuration and paired fold intervals. ABVnet with DFEW motivated the follow-up after the broader results were available. Replacing the plan everywhere with a uniform one reduces UAR by 0.72, whereas permuting its rows gives an unresolved difference of -0.20 UAR points; permutation is less damaging by 0.51 UAR points, a resolved contrast. Replacing the plan with the product of its own marginals, preserving both marginals and removing only frame-specific mixtures, reduces UAR by 0.70. These reductions concern reliance at inference; attribution of the module gain to the transport plan remains unestablished. Under the 1.5 fold-dependence widening, the baseline gain remains resolved, whereas the replacement reductions and the paired contrast are unresolved.

## 455. Seeing Beyond Pixels: Radar Feature Generation from Images and Sparse Returns

- **作者**：Hongyu Zhou, Yubo Peng, Luping Xiang et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI

Camera-radar fusion benefits from complementary visual and geometric information but often relies on complete radar representations, leading to substantial computing overhead. However, the extent to which visual information can compensate for limited radar measurements remains insufficiently explored. Therefore, we propose Vision-informed Radar Token Generation (VRTG), which exploits cross-modal information to generate task-relevant radar features from camera images and sparse radar measurements. Specifically, VRTG first leverages image-derived object cues to select a limited number of informative radar points. It then establishes calibrated one-to-one associations between visual objects and radar returns, using matched measurements to refine object ranges while retaining visual estimates for unmatched objects. Finally, the associated object features and visual context are transformed into radar tokens compatible with existing camera-radar fusion networks. Experiments on RADIal demonstrate that VRTG achieves 90.76\% vehicle-detection F1 and 86.68\% free-space mIoU using only four radar points per frame, approaching the performance of the full-radar model.

## 456. StateBind: Exposing the Symbol-to-State Binding Gap in Visual Measurement

- **作者**：Qing Mu, Jiani Zheng
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：14 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Accepted at MMM 2027. Code: https://github.com/mqingcs/StateBind

Reliable vision systems should connect numerical claims to visible evidence. In visual measurement, this requires mapping candidate values to image-specific physical states. StateBind-200 augments 200 real images with human-refined local geometry and matched Numeric, Calibrated, and Physicalized interfaces. Across a 128-image held-out evaluation and 6,696 controlled calls, physicalizing identical candidates raises three-model majority accuracy from 75.54% to 91.13%, a source-image-balanced gain of +15.30 points. Calibration and additional reasoning leave most of the gap intact; target localization can succeed despite incorrect value-lattice binding, with a larger burden for two-endpoint ruler spans. An independent 40-image geometry audit yields a 0.08-tick median target discrepancy and preserves the main effect under disagreement sensitivity. We further propose Factorial StateBind, a training-free selective verifier. Two VLMs propose target and lattice components, an independent VLM validates their evidence, and valid combinations are resolved deterministically. A precision mode selected on 69 development images combines component validity, four-way agreement, and state separation. Applied unchanged to held-out evaluation, this high-precision evidence gate reaches 91.67% candidate precision across 36/128 images (28.12%) and 9.68% of ordered cells. The results identify instance-specific symbol-to-state binding as a distinct reliability bottleneck and provide an interpretable evidence gate for numerical visual claims.

## 457. PhoneQC: Benchmarking Multimodal LLMs for Order-Level Smartphone Trade-in Inspection

- **作者**：Zihan Deng, Xinpei Lin, Du Tiantian et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

As vision-language models enter remote smartphone trade-in, sellers already upload six views and a diagnostic screen, which makes it easy to treat the human quote as a step a model can take over. A model may mark a crack or read the screen, yet the quote is a staff form: appearance fill stays near 0.25, and the closed models that score a little higher do so only on the orders they answer, so human appraisal remains necessary. Two obstacles stand in the way. (1) Single-view defect sets and attribute benchmarks do not score an order-level form under a fixed input contract, so a box or a leaked field can be mistaken for a finished quote. (2) The unread half of the form is the staff fields, and a model left to emit boxes or captions does not learn them. We present PhoneQC, a 335-order iPhone-heavy cut that ranks systems by a 0 to 100 form score (Score100) only inside matched protocols (images alone; images plus a detector sidecar), with a declared upper bound that is not ranked as a peer. As a case study on that board, field-only LoRA freezes the backbone, adapts attention projections, and supervises staff fields while leaving the defect list empty; the adapter sees the same images as the zero-shot model. On image-only T1 the 3B model rises from 26.6 to 33.3 at coverage 0.928; without the empty-blocker item the open ranking still favors the adapter, while the remaining gap to a person stays large on this cut.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28117) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28117)

## 458. Is It the Agent or the Retry? Separating Selection from Agency in Text-to-Image Generation

- **作者**：Jian Xu, Delu Zeng, Qibin Zhao
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Agentic text-to-image systems wrap generators in loops of planning, routing, verification and refinement, and report large gains on compositional benchmarks, almost always relative to a single sample. We ask how much of the gain survives against Single-tool Rejection Retry (\ours): the strongest generator already in the system's pool, an off-the-shelf verifier, and retries that stop at the first accepted image under the system's own budget. Auditing four recent systems with official evaluators, we find that on GenEval SRR matches the accuracy of OctoT2I (0.962 vs.\ 0.96), recovers 63 to 90% of the gain of GEMS depending on the verifier, and exceeds GenPilot by 12 points with 2.3 generations per image; the effect persists with generators not tuned to the benchmark and on T2I-CompBench++. A closed-form analysis predicts the accuracy of SRR at every budget and shows that its gain is capped by the verifier's likelihood ratio. The same analysis also exposes genuine agentic gains: on GenEval 2, even the best of five samples chosen by the evaluator stays 15 points below the score GEMS reports (12 at the upper end of its 95% interval). We prove a ceiling on the accuracy that routing across generators can add, bracket it between one and three points on OctoT2I's own pool, and show that what OctoT2I's routing buys is compute (about 2.5 less than SRR at equal accuracy), not accuracy. In our offline distillation experiments selected samples teach the generator no more than random ones, which a short information argument explains. We end with a reporting checklist for agentic generation papers.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28116) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28116)

## 459. NOVA: Normalization-Aware Output-Guided Attention Acceleration for Video Diffusion Transformers

- **作者**：Wenxuan Miao, Haosong Liu, Aiyue Chen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Video diffusion transformers (vDiTs) have become the main backbone for video generation. The quadratic cost of self-attention limits their efficiency at high resolutions and long durations. Existing sparse attention methods often rely on preserving high attention weights. However, these criteria do not fully capture the fidelity of the final value-weighted output, whose error depends jointly on changes to the attention numerator and the softmax denominator. We propose NOVA, a coarse-to-fine sparse attention framework that first approximates the full normalized output using size-weighted key/value centroids. Building on this estimate, we construct a residual proxy that accounts for coupled numerator-denominator errors by centering value contributions around the estimated output. This proxy guides the selection of query-key blocks for refinement under a predefined block budget for each query cluster. Selected blocks are evaluated through exact token-level computation, while the remaining blocks contribute through their centroids under a shared softmax normalization. On five vDiTs, NOVA achieves the best PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS among sparse attention methods, improving PSNR by up to 1.55\,dB over the strongest baseline, while delivering up to 1.82$\times$ end-to-end speedup on an H100 GPU.

## 460. Temporal Persistence for Stylized Rendering: Historical Image Storage and Reconstruction

- **作者**：Daniel Szelogowski
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.GR, cs.CV, eess.IV
- **备注**：19 pages, 5 figures, 2 tables

The overlapping silhouettes and gradual fading in The Legend of Zelda: Majora's Mask (released April 27, 2000) produce a distinctive depiction of motion blur, in which earlier scene states remain visible as part of the current image. Reproducing this appearance in a modern renderer involves decisions about when historical images are sampled, how their contributions are retained, and how the resulting history can be represented within the available memory and processing budget. In this paper, we investigate these decisions through complete image histories, shared image regions, and selectively retained pixel changes, using Godot engine implementations evaluated against independently composited references. For a ten-image history sampled at 20 Hz on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060 Ti, half-float storage halves history allocation and reduces median storage-pass duration by factors of 2.40 at 720p and 1.73 at 1080p, preserving the supplied source precision. The adaptive experiments examine how memory savings depend on reconstruction metadata and allocation, including a sparse representation that reduces allocation by 83.81% at low resolution with 445.25 times the GPU duration.

## 461. Benchmarking Foundation Models for Bone Scaffold Pore Detection: A Comparative Analysis of Few-Shot Performance

- **作者**：Tri Tin Vo, Vu Thien An Ngo, Nhi Kieu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 5 figures, 3 tables. Accepted at the Australasian Data Science and Machine Learning Conference (AusDM 2026)

Evaluating the mechanical properties of bone scaffolds conventionally requires destructive testing or costly Finite Element Analysis, while deep learning alternatives are constrained by the scarcity of labeled biomaterial imaging data. This study introduces the first expert-annotated, pixel-dense dataset of biopolymer bone-scaffold SEM images, spanning five magnification series, and uses it to benchmark two state-of-the-art pre-trained foundation models under a 12-shot fine-tuning regime. SAM3, an instance segmenter, and YOLOv26, a bounding-box detector, feed the predicted pore geometry to regression heads that estimate Young's modulus and compressive yield strength. Our central finding is that both models fail by pore density rather than magnification: YOLOv26's predicted pore counts saturate and porosity estimation collapses on the most crowded frames, whereas SAM3 over-fragments sparse pore fields and merges adjacent pores in dense ones, producing eight catastrophic failures against three for YOLOv26. Averaged across the 44-image evaluation set, YOLOv26 achieves lower porosity error (19.9% vs. 23.1% MAE), higher precision, and a higher F1 score, whereas SAM3 offers pixel-level instance segmentation that YOLOv26's bounding boxes cannot reproduce. We conclude that model selection should be driven by pore density rather than SEM magnification alone.

## 462. ROSE-SAM3: Response-Oriented Prompt Selection for Open-Vocabulary Remote Sensing Segmentation

- **作者**：Yanghui Song, Chengfu Yang, Yongpeng Wen et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：13 pages, 11 figures, 7 tables. Submitted to IEEE Transactions on Geoscience and Remote Sensing

In open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) of remote-sensing imagery, class-name prompts can miss objects with varied appearances, while richer descriptions can activate competing classes. We propose Response-Oriented Prompt Selection for SAM 3 (ROSE-SAM3), a training-free framework that selects descriptions using frozen SAM 3 responses on unlabeled target-domain images. We screen candidate descriptions for coverage beyond the native prompts while protecting reliable native class responses, retaining candidates that pass these checks on at least four of five image folds. An offline directed acyclic graph (DAG) search then evaluates candidate combinations and builds a compact PromptBank for each class. Each bank retains the native prompts and adds selected descriptions, which we call residual prompts. At inference, the bank is fixed, and Semantic-Instance Cooperative Head (SICH) uses instance evidence to refine semantic responses before assigning pixel labels. We also introduce RS-OVSS-C to evaluate robustness under six image corruption families across eight remote-sensing datasets, preserving their class vocabularies and reference labels. ROSE-SAM3 achieves 57.3% mean mIoU across eight clean benchmarks and 54.7% on RS-OVSS-C, achieving state-of-the-art performance among training-free methods. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/YanghuiSong/ROSE-SAM3.

## 463. GC-GS: Optimizing 3D Gaussian Splatting with Gradient Conflict as a Geometry Signal

- **作者**：Chenhao Zhang, Fengze Zhang, Fengqing Zhu
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：WACV2027

3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is commonly optimized with multiple reconstruction losses, which naturally forms a multi-objective optimization problem. This issue becomes more pronounced when perceptual losses are introduced to improve visual quality, as different objectives may impose competing update directions on Gaussian primitives and make it difficult to simultaneously improve fidelity, perceptual quality, and representation efficiency. Based on this view, we reformulate 3DGS training as a multi-objective optimization problem and derive selective conflict-aware position updates that reduce interference between competing objectives while preserving the contribution of each loss. We further observe that gradient conflicts persist throughout training and are most severe in Gaussian position parameters, indicating that objective conflict is closely tied to geometry optimization. We therefore treat position-gradient conflict as a geometry-aware signal to guide densification and pruning. Experiments show that our method achieves better rendering performance while improving representation compactness and rendering efficiency over existing 3DGS variants.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28111) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28111)

## 464. Tangent incompleteness under coupled image transformations: margin reconstruction and decision consequences

- **作者**：Karim Bounja, Abdeljalil Sakat
- **投稿**：2026-11-18 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG
- **备注**：16 pages, 10 figures, 2 tables

Can accurate responses to individual geometric image transformations recover their combined effect on a classifier's decision? For identity-aligned bilinear resampling, coordinatewise assembly may fail at first order. Projecting the implemented transformation onto a fixed logit-margin gradient, we derive a linear term plus signed absolute-value ridges. An exact even--odd identity and ridge-cancellation conditions characterize the missing information. Under independent parameter laws, a noncancelling off-coordinate ridge obstructs even unrestricted additive fits. The coupled correction has $O(s^2)$ projected error, versus a possible $Θ(s)$ axiswise defect; transfer to realized network margins requires local smoothness. Projected RMS errors fall by 82.96% on CIFAR-10 and 82.38% on ImageNetV2 relative to axiswise assembly; realized fixed-pair margin RMS errors fall by 39.75% and 42.73%. The representation prospectively identifies nine strict nonadditive network-margin sign configurations and permits exact minimization of a three-parameter threat surrogate, with conditional selection-regret guarantees. In an independent CIFAR-10 search, coupled-selected transformations uncover 51 top-1 changes versus 41 for axes. On 256 independent ImageNetV2/ConvNeXt-Tiny images and 16,384 transformed decisions at the prespecified scale $s=.40$, multiclass top-1 flip-prediction errors fall from 11.97% to 10.23% (paired improvement 1.74 percentage points; 95% interval [0.93, 2.61]). Directional finite differences are slightly more accurate (10.00%) at higher measured model-construction cost. These results connect first-order sampling interactions to margin reconstruction, decision prediction, and threat selection, without claiming finite-scale network certification.
