## 465. Cyclic Discrete Diffusion: A Novel Multi-Class Segmentation Refinement Technique

- **作者**：Libo Chen, Souvik Ghosh, Vinay P. Namboodiri
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. 25 pages

We cast image segmentation refinement as a discrete diffusion process. To solve this task we introduce Cyclic Discrete Diffusion (CDD), a novel forward-reverse formulation with a cyclic label topology that maps a discrete label set to the uniform distribution and back. Instead of injecting Gaussian noise as is done for continuous diffusion, CDD evolves segmentation masks through label jumps: at each step, every pixel either remains in its current class or jumps to the next label with a fixed rate. This jump-or-stay mechanism defines a finite-state Markov process with bounded forward and reverse steps, featuring analytically consistent and tractable reverse dynamics. CDD operates natively in a multi-class label space, enabling joint refinement of all classes in a single pass without requiring class-wise decomposition, as commonly needed in prior binary refinement approaches. The proposed formulation admits a finite and controllable number of refinement steps, for which we analyze the convergence properties through spectral gap analysis. We evaluate CDD across diverse segmentation tasks, including multi-class semantic, instance-level, and binary segmentation refinement. Our method consistently improves both region accuracy and boundary quality across multiple backbones and datasets.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28107) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28107)

## 466. Gradient-Free Few-Shot Adaptation of Vision Models via Agentic Coding

- **作者**：Khayrul Islam
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV

Adapting a vision model to new classes from a few labeled images usually relies on gradient fine-tuning, which is unstable with so few images. We ask whether a coding agent can replace gradient descent for this task. We propose the Agent edit, a method in which a coding agent writes a program that sets the new class weights in the class head of a frozen COCO-trained detector, with no gradient step. The agent runs the detector on the labeled images a limited number of times and sees how much its edit lowers COCO mAP; the program rebuilds the weights without the agent. In the same head and with the same labels, the Agent edit outperforms full-decoder fine-tuning at five shots on 2 of four fine-grained sets, by 11.2 and 12.3 F1 points, and ties on the rest. At ten shots, fine-tuning ties with or outperforms it. Moreover, when the new classes are scored jointly with the COCO classes, the AP of the COCO class to which they belong falls by up to 33.3 points, and limiting that loss lowers F1 on the new classes. Most of the agent's programs share three parts, which we write as a formula that needs no agent. A classifier on frozen backbone features reaches a higher F1 than every method inside the head on every set. Hence a coding agent can replace gradient descent only in part: inside the head and at five shots.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28105) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28105)

## 467. Intervene or Trust CLIP? Competence-Gated Adaptation for Source-Free Cross-Domain Few-Shot Learning

- **作者**：Haoyue Liu, Chenhao Zhu, Yu Huo et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

Source-free cross-domain few-shot learning (SF-CDFSL) adapts a pre-trained vision-language model such as CLIP to a distant target domain from a handful of labeled images, without access to source data. Recent methods improve the fine-tuning of CLIP with token-level interventions and apply them in the same way to every target domain. In this paper, we find that whether an intervention helps depends on how well frozen CLIP already recognizes the target domain: in 5-way 1-shot tasks, intervening improves accuracy by 0.72 points where frozen CLIP misclassifies most support images, but lowers it by 1.31 points where frozen CLIP already classifies them well, and the support set of a single episode suffices to tell the two cases apart; a bias-variance analysis explains both. Based on this finding, we propose Competence-Gated Adaptation (CoGA), which measures frozen CLIP's accuracy and margin on the labeled support set and decides, before training, whether to intervene on the visual tokens or to trust CLIP by fusing the adapted prediction with the frozen one. CoGA adds no trainable parameters and uses no query labels. On the four BSCD-FSL target domains, CoGA achieves the best average accuracy, 59.56% (1-shot) and 68.96% (5-shot), outperforms ATHA and TIR by at least 2.82 and 1.39 points on the same episodes, and consistently improves three further backbones, three parameter-efficient fine-tuning frameworks and five further target domains.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28104) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28104)

## 468. VideoGym: An Adaptive RL Environment for Long-Video Agents

- **作者**：Jing Wang, Yi Ke Yun, Yifeng Yao et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM

Reinforcement learning (RL) trains video agents to locate sparse evidence for long-video question answering through structured memory retrieval and temporal re-observation. This training, however, faces a long-tail modality distribution in the data. In our source pool, text-heavy questions account for 58% of samples, compared with 15% graph-heavy, 17% visual-heavy, and 10% time-range questions. Fixed-distribution RL therefore allocates fewer rollouts to underperforming question types. Reweighting these types could increase their sampling frequency but repeatedly reuses the same finite banks of original questions. To solve this problem, we introduce VideoGym, a curriculum-based RL environment that jointly constructs video-grounded training tasks and allocates rollouts among them. Within the environment, a memory-augmented VideoAgent is trained to coordinate retrieval from text, graph, and visual memories with temporal re-observation. Across episodes, VideoGym adapts VideoAgent's training distribution online: it casts per-period sample selection as an adversarial bandit over (modality, mode) arms and applies the EXP3 update to shift sampling toward arms with low answer accuracy. To supply the augmented-mode arms, a TaskGenerator-Verifier pipeline produces harder variants of existing questions that remain answerable within the same video. Across five long-video benchmarks, VideoGym lifts RL accuracy from 60.4% to 64.8% on average and by up to 10.5 pp on underrepresented graph-heavy questions with 3.3x sample efficiency. Together, these results show that task expansion and adaptive allocation can improve video agent learning from limited, imbalanced training data.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28102) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28102)

## 469. Scale-Aware Reference Attention for Mannequin-to-Model Gown Generation

- **作者**：Guang Yang, Fengchen Liu
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：11 pages, 4 figures, 4 tables

Formal gowns are sold online with photos of models wearing them, and buyers zoom in to inspect sequins, lace and beading. Image editors can turn a photo of a gown on a mannequin into a convincing photo of a person wearing it, but they receive every reference photo without any notion of its magnification, and the fine detail of the gown is lost when the output is viewed up close. We propose scale-aware reference attention (SARA), which tells an open diffusion transformer the zoom level of the output and of every reference photo, biases the attention of the output toward references of a similar zoom level, uses macro photos of the fabric as references, and is trained on a mix of whole-garment and zoomed targets. To train and evaluate it, we introduce Kiss3000, a dataset of 915 high-resolution mannequin photos and 2274 on-model photos of formal gowns with cross-domain pairs and splits by style, and zoom-crop evaluation, which compares garment crops at full, half and local scale without pixel alignment and judges local fidelity with two vision-language models gated on control crops. With this protocol, Nano Banana Pro at 4K, the highest-resolution commercial editor we test, loses or redraws local detail on 14 of 20 test gowns. At 1536x2048 pixels, both judges prefer SARA over a LoRA fine-tuning baseline on 18 test items and the baseline on 6 (p=0.023), and over the base model on 26 against 4 (p=5.9e-5), while SARA keeps the gown design as often as the baseline.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28101) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28101)

## 470. You Only Think Once: Rescuing Hybrid Vision-Language Models Lost in Thought

- **作者**：Tianyi Tan, Wentao Wang, Xiaohan Li et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.LG

Thinking at length before answering is expected to strengthen the visual reasoning of vision-language models (VLMs), yet hybrid VLMs often get lost in thought: their thoughts loop or exhaust the budget without an answer, so thinking can score below answering directly. Existing early-exit methods watch a thought for the moment its answer is ready and force the answer out of it, but in a hybrid VLM that moment often never comes. We find that the remedy is not to stop a failing thought earlier, but to stop taking its answer: once a thought is given up, the model's own direct answers decide. Based on this, we propose **YOTO (You Only Think Once)**, a method that answers first and thinks at most once. YOTO draws direct answers until a probe-weighted vote settles; only an unsettled problem receives a single thought, which a failure head on the hidden state monitors and stops as soon as it predicts failure, handing the problem back to direct answers. At a matched 20k-token budget, YOTO is up to 5.9 points more accurate than the strongest early-exit baseline, using one failure-head threshold across model sizes. Keeping the released weights frozen, it adds only two linear probes. On the full MathVision benchmark, YOTO improves Qwen3.5-2B from 40.1% to 57.3% and Qwen3.5-4B from 68.3% to 73.7%, above the 72.8% that Qwen3.5-9B, a model more than twice its size, reaches with thinking. Its gains carry over to five further benchmarks and a second model family. These results suggest that hybrid VLMs reason better by answering first and thinking once than by thinking longer.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28100) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28100)

## 471. VidRouteBench: A Benchmark for Budgeted Video-Generation Routing

- **作者**：Dayu Wang
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.CL

Text-to-video generators differ by two orders of magnitude in price, and a cheap configuration of a strong model is often good enough for a prompt. Routing prompts across such arms (model x sampling configuration) should buy quality at fixed budget, but no dataset supports studying it: public video-preference sets contain only closed models, one configuration each. We release VidRouteBench: 16 arms from 7 open families spanning 121x in measured cost ($0.0025-$0.298 per clip), 27,110 clips (1,421 A800-hours) over 1,625 grouped prompts, a quality score frozen after calibration against human preferences (68.7% CV accuracy vs 66.2% for the best single scorer), per-clip scores on 9 identifiable quality dimensions, repeated seeds, an encrypted test split, and a sealed newest family for cold start. The evaluator compares a router against the best prompt-agnostic mixture of arms, not the best single arm. Per-prompt specialisation here is real and predictable (oracle gap 0.642, 47% reproducible across seeds, prompt-arm interaction F=4.47 at p=1.6e-63), unlike LLM-routing arms, where the same pipeline finds none. We then propose OPMU (one probe, many utilities): label a new arm on K probe prompts, predict its 9 dimensions once, and serve any scenario utility by scalarising afterwards. Over 215 utility directions OPMU Pareto-dominates the prompt-agnostic baseline for 94% of directions versus 90% for per-scenario routers, with no per-scenario retraining. Three baselines fail instructively: bag-of-words features lose almost all routing gain in the low-data regime (0.009 vs 0.168); crediting a new arm with its probe average is harmful (-3.32 recovery) although it is the strong baseline on RewardBench-style arms (0.786); Chebyshev scalarisation does not help, our cost-utility front being convex.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28099) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28099)

## 472. Causal Dioramas: Controllable Video Generation, Revision and Editing via Intervention

- **作者**：Yifan Shen, Xinyi Yang, Peiyuan Zhu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM

Two centuries ago, an early cinema-like theater called the Diorama could already control its scenes at will. Today's video generation models produce videos of near-cinematic quality, yet they still lack this controllability. This limitation stems from the inherent semantic gap between text and pixels, since a prompt can describe a video only coarsely and leaves its details and relations beyond control. We bring this ability to video generation with Causal Dioramas, modules that decompose a video into a concept graph of variables that can be intervened on individually. Each instruction is translated into an intervention on the concept graph, modifying the specified concepts and their dependents. A connector then conveys this intervention to the video generator, which reuses the original noise to produce a counterfactual video while preserving all other content. Building on Causal Dioramas, we develop a unified framework for controllable generation, counterfactual revision of generated videos and editing of real videos. We outperform both open-source and commercial models in controllability on T2V-CompBench, CoVEBench and OpenVE-Bench.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28098) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28098)

## 473. ORDO: Fine-Grained Motion Understanding in Video LLMs via Order-Dependent Supervision

- **作者**：Jiaye Luan, Chaoning Zhang, Pengcheng Zheng et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：16 pages, 5 figures, 9 tables. Includes supplementary material

Video large language models (video LLMs) answer a wide range of questions about real videos, yet they remain weak at fine-grained motion such as whether an object speeds up or slows down. Existing methods train these models on synthetic motion clips, on order pretext tasks such as reordering shuffled clips, or with rewards that compare ordered and perturbed frames. None of them requires that the label be unrecoverable from the frames once their order is discarded, so a model can fit such data from static cues without learning to use frame order. We propose ORDO, a framework that builds this requirement into the training data. We define order-necessary supervision as data in which the unordered set of frames has the same distribution under competing labels, and we realise it for motion attributes with three constructions, time-reversal pairing, matched spatial extent and uniform interval coverage. A procedural curriculum of six motion primitives instantiates these constructions, and low-rank adapters of the language model are trained on it with an answer-level objective and self-distillation replay on real videos. With 4,000 synthetic clips, ORDO raises the mean accuracy over four motion benchmarks by +7.6, +4.9 and +5.1 points on Qwen3.5-9B, Qwen3-VL-8B and Molmo2-8B, while the same training with shuffled frames changes it by less than one point. Code and generator will be made publicly available.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28097) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28097)

## 474. LightOnOCR for AMNLT: Reading Chant Notation and Lyrics in One Pass, from Single Staves to Whole Pages

- **作者**：Robert Klugseder
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.SD

Aligned music notation and lyrics transcription (AMNLT) asks for the notes of a chant, its sung text and the assignment of syllables to notes in one output. Fuentes-Martínez et al., who defined the task, found that the one vision-language model they tested fell far behind specialised recognisers. We fine-tune LightOnOCR-3-1B, a page OCR model with one billion parameters, end to end, vision encoder included, to write a staff as a GABC string whose letters denote staff positions. Trained on the published AMNLT training split alone, it more than halves the best published aligned music and lyrics error rate (AMLER) on Solesmes, the one directly comparable dataset (9.9 against 21.0 %); on Einsiedeln and Salzinnes, measured in a different encoding, its figures are also lower. The benchmark does not measure generalisation to unseen manuscripts: while the pitch error on AMNLT validation stays between 1.7 and 1.9 %, on 200 staves from five unseen manuscripts it falls from 24.9 to 1.3 % as we add chant aligned through CantusDB, Transkribus ground truth and silver staves kept only where two readers agree. Trained also on multi-staff images, it reads whole pages without staff detection (pitch error 3.3 to 3.8 % on validation and test pages, 1.9 % on pages of unseen manuscripts). Remaining errors include Gothic F clefs read as C clefs, notes dropped with abbreviated or illegible text and lower accuracy on pages with small notation. Controlled ablations are needed to explain the gains.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28094) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28094)

## 475. VTree: Question-Driven Growth of Hierarchical Memory for Agentic Long-Video Understanding

- **作者**：Xiaoyu Ma, Lianyu Hu, Zixuan Hu et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM

Long videos spanning tens of minutes to hours far exceed the context window of today's vision-language models, and the standard remedy of sparse uniform frame sampling discards exactly the fine-grained evidence that specific questions depend on. Existing video agents explore on demand but re-explore from scratch for every question and discard their exploration state once an answer is produced, so per-question cost scales with video length. Hierarchical-memory methods instead fix the scope of each depth in advance and deepen along a pre-committed pyramid rather than toward the question. We observe that a video's coarse structure (where its scenes and topics begin and end) is question-independent, whereas which part a question must zoom into, and how deep, is question-dependent. We propose VTree, which builds a coarse hierarchical memory tree over a video once and then, for each question, grows branches only where that question needs depth. The growth is globally routed so any part of the timeline is reachable, sufficiency-gated so drilling stops when the evidence suffices, and query-conditioned so a branch unfolds into the sub-intervals that answer \emph{this} question. Crucially, every expansion is written back into the video memory, allowing later questions to reuse previously discovered structure and evidence without repeating the corresponding expansion process. Across five long-video understanding benchmarks, VTree consistently improves the accuracy of diverse vision-language backbones while substantially reducing the context length and execution time required for each question.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28093) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28093)

## 476. When Recognition Meets Discovery: Learning Need Not Be Entangled in One Representation Space

- **作者**：Chenglin Liu, An Tian, Mingxuan Du et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV
- **备注**：5 pages, 4 figures

Generalized Category Discovery (GCD) aims to recognize known classes while discovering novel categories from unlabeled data containing both old and novel samples. Existing methods typically optimize recognition and discovery within a shared representation space, implicitly assuming that the same representation geometry can serve both objectives. Through controlled spectral analysis, we uncover a striking asymmetry: old-class discrimination is increasingly concentrated in the dominant singular directions, while novel-class structure is better preserved in the complementary residual space. More importantly, strengthening the former can even degrade the latter, revealing that recognition and discovery compete for representation capacity rather than merely for optimization weight. Motivated by this observation, we propose Dual-Space Spectral Routing, which dynamically decomposes the representation via online SVD and routes supervised recognition and unsupervised discovery to complementary discriminative and general subspaces. We further exploit their distinct responses to old and novel samples to construct a dual-space novelty signal, which guides Subspace Relation Aggregation (SRA) for organizing candidate novel categories. Extensive experiments across multiple GCD baselines, backbones, and benchmarks demonstrate a consistently improved recognition-discovery balance without introducing additional inference parameters. Code will be open source.

[arXiv 页面](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28092) · [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.28092)

## 477. Teacher-Guided Multimodal Dual Representation Reconstruction for Egocentric Human Activity Recognition

- **作者**：Yuzhe Hao, Ikuro Sato, Rei Kawakami et al.
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.MM

Multimodal Egocentric Human Activity Recognition (Ego-HAR) leverages complementary cues from first-person vision (V) and inertial sensor data (S) to recognize daily activities, where video mainly captures appearance and object-related context while inertial signals capture motion cues that video may miss. However, existing pre-training methods typically supervise the fused representation with a single joint objective, which may underemphasize the complementarity between the two modalities and limit the model's ability to learn from distinct modality-oriented signals. To address this issue, we propose Multimodal Dual Representation Reconstruction (MDR2), a teacher-guided pre-training framework in which frozen, label-informed video and inertial teachers each provide dense, modality-specific targets to a multimodal student trained on temporally sparse inputs. On the EvIs-Kitchen dataset, MDR2 improves noun/verb F1-macro over the supervised M-Baseline by 9.20/6.17 points at K=4 video key frames and by 1.21/1.82 points at K=32. These results show that MDR2 consistently achieves higher mean recognition performance across the evaluated video-observation settings under the unified backbone setting, with more pronounced gains under sparse inputs.

## 478. Every IVUS Frame in Its Place: A Zero-Install Browser Tool for Registering Intravascular Ultrasound to the Coronary Angiogram, and a Path to Angiography-Only Plaque Imaging

- **作者**：Prahlad G. Menon, Srilakshmi Adhyapak
- **投稿**：2026-11-17 ｜ **分类**：cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.ET, cs.SE
- **备注**：19 pages, 10 figures, 4 tables. Live tool with one-click demo: https://menonpg.github.io/ivus-xa-registration/?demo

Intravascular ultrasound (IVUS) shows plaque composition, calcium and stent apposition that the coronary angiogram cannot, but relating each IVUS cross-section to its position on the angiogram is still largely done in the operator's head. Commercial co-registration is tied to vendor consoles and to set-up at acquisition, so archived angiograms and pullbacks cannot be co-registered, and paired data for training AI models remain scarce. We present a semi-automatic registration tool that runs entirely in a web browser, with no installation and no upload. The clinician picks a contrast-filled reference frame (aided by automatic contrast and cardiac-phase analysis), selects or traces the vessel centerline (aided by Frangi vesselness and minimal paths), and pins the pullback ends and visible landmarks; a monotone piecewise-linear map then places every IVUS frame on the vessel, and each case exports as a frame-level training record. The original Python analysis runs unchanged in WebAssembly and videos are decoded frame-accurately with WebCodecs. On 6 angiographic runs the in-browser analysis was bit-identical to the server pipeline on identical frames; browser decoding differed by at most 2 grey levels, suggested reference frames agreed in all runs and the default automatic centerline in 4 of 6. A 546-frame pullback decoded in 5.3 s. On 36 phantoms with known ground truth, the clinician-guided trace stayed inside the lumen along 87-99% of the vessel, whereas the fully automatic centerline fell from 83% to 0% as noise rose and contrast fell. In a registered Left Main case, one side-branch landmark moved mid-pullback positions by 17.5% of the segment. We map where AI can enter each step, up to predicting a virtual IVUS from the angiogram alone, and make the tool freely available online with a one-click demo.
